# Miziki changelog

Step-by-step history of every refactor and feature PR in this project, moved out of CLAUDE.md (which otherwise grew too large to load in a session's context) to keep that file focused on durable conventions, architecture, and commands. Read this file for the "why" behind a specific past decision, or to see exactly what changed and how it was verified in a given step; read CLAUDE.md for what's true right now.

### What changed in step 1 (tooling)

No behavior change — this step only adds build tooling and moves code, per the refactor rules above.

- Added Vite (`package.json`, `vite.config.js`) with a GitHub Actions workflow (`.github/workflows/deploy.yml`) that builds and deploys `dist/` to GitHub Pages on every push to `main`.
- The inline `<style>` block became 21 files under `src/styles/`, referenced via `<link rel="stylesheet">` tags in the same order, immediately after the Google Fonts links (unchanged).
- The vendored copy of the social client inside `miziki.html` is gone; it now loads `src/miziki-social.js` directly, as a classic script (not a module) so timing stays identical — the main script right after it still does a synchronous `typeof MizikiSocial` check.
- Verified (see the PR): confirmed the two social-client copies were byte-identical before removing the vendored one; diffed the built `dist/miziki.html` against the pre-step-1 original with the CSS and social-script regions normalized out — the only remaining difference was a stale comment folded into the new one above the script tag. Nothing in the ~10,100-line main script changed.

### What changed in step 2c (lowest-coupling extractions + first unit tests)

No behavior change — a move is a move, per the refactor rules above.

- Extracted the five lowest-coupling sections out of `main.js`, in order: record-art quality tiers + variant selection (`src/record-art/tiers.js`), solar (`src/sundown/solar.js`), the crate-view constants block (`src/crate/constants.js`), location & motion (`src/sundown/location.js`), and the sleep timer (`src/player/sleep-timer.js`). Section comments and spec references (e.g. "SLEEP spec §5", "CRATE spec §3") moved with the code.
- `normKey()`, defined inside the tiers section but used ~40 times app-wide, moved to its own `src/util/text.js` instead of staying in `tiers.js`.
- `clamp()` moved to `src/util/math.js`, and the local `$()` shorthand moved into `src/util/dom.js` alongside `el()` — both were still defined locally in `main.js` and used by the newly-extracted modules, so leaving them in place would have meant copying them instead of moving them.
- `location.js` and `sleep-timer.js` each import a few functions back from `main.js` (`drawSun`/`queueSave`, and `pause`/`drawTime`/`setPathNote` respectively) — a deliberate circular import. Those functions got `export` added in `main.js` with no other change; this is the standard, safe shape for extracting a section that leaf-level code elsewhere in `main.js` still depends on, before that code is extracted too.
- Added the first player unit tests: `tests/unit/tiers.test.js` (`trackTier`) and `tests/unit/solar.test.js` (`solarEvent`, checked against a published sunset time for Columbia, MD plus a polar no-sunset case), run via the new `npm run test:unit` (Node's built-in `node:test`, no new dependency). Wired into both CI pipelines ahead of the build, so a failing unit test blocks both deploys.
- Verified lossless: each new module was diffed against the exact original lines it was extracted from (modulo only `export`/`import` additions), and the full `git diff` of `main.js` for this step was reviewed end to end — every removal matches a verified new-module file, and every other change is either an added import line or a single `export` keyword.

### Node version

Node **22** (bumped from 20 in step 3a — Node 20 reached end-of-life in April 2026). Set in `.github/workflows/deploy.yml` (`node-version`), `netlify.toml` (`NODE_VERSION`), and `package.json`'s `engines` field. `npm run test:unit` also needs 22 for `--experimental-test-module-mocks` (see Commands).

### What changed in step 3a (audio graph into `src/audio/engine.js`)

No behavior change — a move is a move, per the refactor rules above.

- Extracted the audio graph (`makeContext`, `buildGraph`, `satCurve`, `applyCharacter`, `routeSource`) and `ensureContext()` (the one function pulled out of the much larger "file loading" section) into `src/audio/engine.js`.
- `ensureContext()` calls `stop()` and `applyOutputRoute()`, which stay in `main.js` for now (rule 8 above) — imported back as a deliberate, temporary circular import, same shape as `location.js`'s and `sleep-timer.js`'s from step 2c. `applyVolume()` was already available from `location.js`.
- Added `tests/unit/engine.test.js`: `satCurve` (identity curve at 0, in-range/monotonic/trimmed-below-1 at 0.35 and 1 — with a small tolerance for a real, intentional overshoot the asymmetry term produces, not float noise) and the Pure/Vinyl chain-order promise (`buildGraph`'s wobble → sat → satTrim → dc → tone → comp → makeup → master wiring, and `routeSource`'s Pure-vs-Vinyl branch), using a fake `AudioContext` that just records `connect()` calls.
- `engine.js`'s circular import back to `main.js` means loading it for real needs a browser (`main.js` has DOM-dependent code at module top level, not just inside functions). The test uses Node 22's `mock.module()` (`node --experimental-test-module-mocks`) to replace `main.js`'s exports with no-ops first — not just the two `engine.js` imports directly, but every export any module in that import chain needs from `main.js` (`location.js`, loaded for `applyVolume`, also needs `drawSun`/`queueSave`), since `mock.module` replaces the whole module by resolved path, not per-importer.
- Upgraded Node 20 → 22 (separate commit) — see "Node version" above.

### What changed in step 3b (transport into `src/player/transport.js`)

No behavior change — a move is a move, per the refactor rules above. This is the step where the iOS audio-gesture rules (rule 9 above) mattered most: nothing about *when* anything runs changed, only *where it's defined*.

- Moved `load`, `startSource`, `play`, `stop`, `pause` and `seek` — sitting at the end of the "launch intro" section with no header of their own — into `src/player/transport.js`. Verified lossless: diffed the moved block against the exact original lines (modulo only the `export` keywords it already had, from step 2c/3a, plus the new ones rule 8's list needed) — identical.
- Moved `current()` (`S.tracks[S.index] || null`) into `src/state.js`, next to `S`. It's used ~47 times across the app, so this removes a `main.js` dependency from every module that calls it, present and future.
- `engine.js` imported `stop` from `main.js`; now imports it from `transport.js` instead. `sleep-timer.js` imported `pause` from `main.js`; now imports it from `transport.js` instead. Both still import their other `main.js` names (`applyOutputRoute`, `drawTime`/`setPathNote`) as before.
- `transport.js` itself imports ~20 functions back from `main.js` — see rule 8's list and its future-cleanup note. Confirmed no `await` was added, removed or moved, and no function's sync/async-ness changed, by diffing the six functions' bodies against the pristine originals.
- `tests/unit/engine.test.js` needed a second `mock.module()` call, for `transport.js` (replacing its one export, `stop`, with a no-op) — otherwise loading `engine.js` would transitively pull in `transport.js`'s whole ~20-function `main.js` dependency list too. Mocking `transport.js` directly, rather than also listing everything *it* needs from `main.js`, is simpler and keeps the test from having to track `transport.js`'s dependency list as it grows.

### What changed in step 3c (playback clock and spin-to-scrub)

No behavior change — a move is a move, per the refactor rules above. **Step 3 is now complete** — see the "Revised extraction order" note above for what's left in `main.js`.

- Moved the "render loop" section — `tick`, `settleTrackEnd`, `frame`, the hidden-page `setInterval`, the `last`/`lastUI`/`lastSky` variables, `fmt`, `drawTime`, `drawSun` — into `src/player/clock.js`. `sleep-timer.js`, `location.js` and `transport.js` now import `drawTime`/`drawSun` from there instead of `main.js`.
- Moved the "spin-to-scrub" section — `wireSpinToScrub` and its helpers/constants — into `src/player/scrub.js`. `setPathNote`, which happened to sit in that section but is a general on-screen note helper used across the app, got its own module instead: `src/ui/path-note.js`.
- The clock's behavior is unchanged: the `maxDt` caps (0.1s frames, 120s hidden-page timer), the order of work in `tick` (sleep deadline check, then rate, then position, then played ranges and completion), and the 90ms/220ms redraw throttles are all byte-for-byte identical. Verified lossless the same way as step 3b: diffed the moved blocks against the pristine original lines (modulo only `export` additions and the one deliberate `scrubbing`→`S.scrubbing` change below).
- `scrubbing` was a top-level `let` declared in the render loop and reassigned by the `#scrub` slider's wiring handlers — the exported-`let` rule (rule 7) meant it had to become `S.scrubbing` (default `false` in `state.js`) once it crossed a module boundary. Every read and write was updated. The gatefold/crate-rail view has its own function-local `scrubbing` that already shadowed the global one; that one is unrelated and was left alone.
- Added `tests/unit/clock.test.js`: `angleDelta` (wraparound across ±180°, both directions, plus a plain in-range case) and `tick` (advances `S.pos` by `dt × S.rate` while playing, doesn't while paused, never passes the track's duration) — `current()` isn't mocked directly; the tests just set `S.tracks`/`S.index` and let the real `current()` read them back, which is simpler than mocking `state.js` for the same effect.
- `clock.js`'s circular import back to `main.js`, and its real (non-circular) import of `sleepCheckDeadline` from `sleep-timer.js`, meant loading it for real in the test would transitively pull in `sleep-timer.js` → `location.js`/`transport.js` → `main.js`'s DOM-dependent top level. `main.js`, `sleep-timer.js` and `transport.js` are each mocked directly in `clock.test.js` to stop that chain at the door, same approach as `engine.test.js`.
- `clock.js`'s top-level `setInterval` (the hidden-page fallback clock) is a real OS timer the moment `clock.js` is imported directly — harmless in the browser (the page has other reasons to stay alive), but it kept `clock.test.js`'s Node process from exiting on its own, hanging the test run. Fixed in the test file only, with Node's `mock.timers.enable({ apis: ['setInterval'] })` before importing `clock.js`, so a real timer is never created; `clock.js` itself is untouched.
- `engine.test.js` needed a third `mock.module()` call, for `clock.js` (replacing its one export that `location.js` needs, `drawSun`, with a no-op) — same reasoning as the `transport.js` mock added in step 3b: otherwise loading `engine.js` would transitively reach `clock.js`'s own dependency chain (`sleep-timer.js`, more of `main.js`) through `location.js`.

### F1: Pure means untouched (feature, not a refactor)

Unlike the steps above, this one **does** change behavior on purpose: Sundown's rate change (auto or manual) now only ever reaches the recording in Vinyl. Pure always plays at exactly 1.0×. See "How the app is put together" above for the one-line rule and why Car Mode volume/the sleep-timer fade/the user's own volume are exempt (level changes, not changes to the recording).

- `computeRate()` in `src/sundown/solar.js` returns `1` outright when `S.mode === 'pure'`, before even checking `S.auto` — the only place this rule lives. `tick()` in `clock.js` already glides `S.rate` into the audio node via `setTargetAtTime` whenever the computed rate changes, and `frame()` already drives the disc's spin off `S.rate` — both needed zero changes for mode switches to glide smoothly and for the disc to visibly follow the real rate; `computeRate()` returning a different number was the whole fix.
- Fixed three places whose wording implied Pure was still affected by Sundown: the Sundown panel's "Now playing at" line (`drawSun()` in `clock.js`) now reads "Pure — always full speed (Sundown applies in Vinyl)" in Pure, rather than reporting a rate that's always 1.000× with no explanation; the Character panel's Pure note gained a line about the rate; and `setMode()`'s mode-switch note no longer claims "the slow-down still works" in Pure. The Sundown panel's other controls (auto/manual, full-night rate, pacing, the preview slider) stay live and usable in Pure — they describe what Vinyl will do.
- `tests/unit/solar.test.js`: `computeRate()` returns `1` in Pure regardless of `S.auto`/`S.target`; Vinyl is unchanged (same values as before this PR).

### F2: true scratching in spin-to-scrub (feature, not a refactor)

Spinning the disc by hand in Vinyl, with the Character menu's new **Scratch** toggle on (default On), now plays the real track forward or backward at the hand's speed, instead of just moving the position silently. Scratch off, or Pure mode, is exactly today's silent fast-scrub, unchanged.

- Safari can't reliably play an `AudioBufferSourceNode` backward (negative `playbackRate`), so this is an `AudioWorkletNode`: `src/audio/scratch-processor.js`, loaded lazily the first time a scratch gesture happens via `ctx.audioWorklet.addModule(new URL('../audio/scratch-processor.js', import.meta.url))` in `src/player/scrub.js`. The `new URL(..., import.meta.url)` form is what makes Vite copy the file as a plain, unbundled asset (confirmed in the built output: `dist/assets/scratch-processor-*.js`, not inlined, not run through the JS bundler) so it resolves correctly and loads under both `/Miziki/` and `/` bases. It is reloaded into a fresh `AudioContext` if `ensureContext()` ever rebuilds one for a different sample rate (tracked by `moduleLoadedCtx` in `scrub.js`).
- **The worklet file has no `import` or `export`, not just no imports.** It originally exported its pure helpers for the unit test, which worked in every desktop browser tested but silently produced no scratch audio on an iPhone: Safari's AudioWorklet module loader has not reliably supported ES module syntax in the processor file itself, so an `addModule()` that resolves fine in Chrome/Firefox can still fail to actually register the processor in Safari — and `scrub.js` already treats that failure as "no scratch audio this session," by design, so there was nothing to see without a device's remote inspector. Fixed by making the file a plain classic script (no `export` at all); `tests/unit/scratch-processor.test.js` now loads its source with Node's `vm` module instead of `import`, since there's nothing left to import — a classic script's top-level `function` declarations become properties of the sandbox object it runs against, which is how the test reaches the pure helpers, and `registerProcessor()`'s own call is still how it reaches the processor class (same as the real worklet; `class` declarations never become global properties, imported or not).
- **The playhead follows position, not velocity.** `scrub.js` sends the worklet a `target` (absolute track seconds) on every `pointermove`; the worklet moves its own `playhead` toward that target with a one-pole follower (`onePole()`, time constant `FOLLOW_TIME_CONSTANT`) and reads the loaded window at the resulting (possibly fractional, possibly decreasing) sample index with linear interpolation (`sampleAt()`). Reading a decreasing index plays the track backward — there's no separate "reverse" code path. **This is also where the pitch comes from**: advancing two samples of source per output sample plays an octave high, half a sample an octave low, exactly like vari-speed playback on a real turntable — no separate pitch-shifting step was needed or added. `FOLLOW_TIME_CONSTANT` was retuned from an initial `0.012s` up to `0.035s` because the tighter value let the playhead snap to each new touch sample almost instantly, which made real motion too brief to read as a held pitch — more a click per touch event than a scratch. `VELOCITY_FLOOR` (`0.05` → `0.03`) and `ENVELOPE_TIME_CONSTANT` (`0.01` → `0.02`) were loosened to match, so slow deliberate scratches stay audible instead of gating to silence between touch events.
- **Holding the disc still is genuine silence, not a frozen sample.** A fixed `target` would otherwise mean reading the same sample forever — a non-zero constant level, not silence. Loudness is tied to how fast the simulated platter is actually turning (`velocityEnvelopeTarget()`, floor `VELOCITY_FLOOR`, smoothed by its own one-pole so it doesn't buzz) the same way a real record is silent with the motor off and the needle resting on one groove point. This is deliberate, not just click-avoidance — see the comment at the top of `scratch-processor.js`.
- **Only a window of audio is ever copied to the worklet**, not the whole decoded track (tens of MB, and GitHub Pages can't send the `SharedArrayBuffer` headers): `loadScratchWindow()` in `scrub.js` copies ±10s (`SCRATCH_WINDOW_SEC`) of channel data around the current position via `Float32Array.slice()` (a real copy, so transferring its buffer to the worklet never detaches the track's own decoded `AudioBuffer`) and re-sends a new window once the position gets within `SCRATCH_REFETCH_MARGIN_SEC` of either edge.
- **Ratio:** `SCRATCH_SEC_PER_ROTATION = 1.8` (the real 33⅓ rpm speed) replaces `SCRUB_SEC_PER_ROTATION = 30` only when claimed as a scratch (Vinyl + Scratch on); otherwise the existing fast 30s/rotation navigation ratio is unchanged. Both constants are exported for their unit test.
- **Gain ramps (5–10ms)** at gesture start/end run on a plain `GainNode` between the worklet and `S.nodes.wobble` (not inside the worklet), scheduled with `linearRampToValueAtTime`. The node and gain are freed (`disconnect()`) once the end-of-gesture ramp finishes, so an idle worklet is never left running between gestures.
- **iOS:** `S.ctx.resume()` is called synchronously at the very top of the `pointerdown` handler, before anything else and before any `await` — the same rule `play()`'s `bgPrime()` follows, since iOS only allows resuming audio from inside the real user gesture. No `await` in the existing `play()`/`seek()` path was touched; release still goes through the unchanged `seek(S.pos)` then `play()` (only if it was already playing).
- **The toggle:** `S.char.scratch` (default `true` in `state.js`), alongside the other Character sliders in the saved prefs' `char` object. `applyPrefs()` special-cases it (the existing `char` merge loop only accepts numbers, since the sliders are percentages) and a saved value's absence leaves the default (On) in place — no IndexedDB version bump, since it's just a new field inside the existing `prefs.char` object. New row in the Character panel (`#scratchBtn`, styled like `#sleevePullBtn`).
- **Falls back safely:** if `addModule()` or the `AudioWorkletNode` constructor ever throws (unsupported browser, bad build), `scrub.js` logs a warning, sets a module-level `scratchSupported = false`, and every future gesture behaves exactly like Scratch-off — scratching is never allowed to break ordinary spin-to-scrub.
- `tests/unit/scratch-processor.test.js`: reads the worklet file's source and runs it with Node's `vm` module in a sandbox stubbing the AudioWorkletGlobalScope globals (`AudioWorkletProcessor`, `registerProcessor`, `sampleRate`), then exercises both the pure math (`clampIndex`, `sampleAt`, `onePole`, `velocityEnvelopeTarget`, reached as properties the sandbox's global object gained from the script's top-level `function` declarations) and the full `process()` loop on fake buffers: a continuously-advancing target produces forward motion, a continuously-retreating one produces reverse, a target held still after one small move settles to near-silence, and the playhead/reads never leave the loaded window or the track. `tests/unit/clock.test.js` gained one more test confirming the 1.8s/30s rotation ratio constants.
- Verified: both builds (`npm run build`, `MIZIKI_BASE=/ npm run build`) emit the worklet as its own asset, confirmed free of `export`/`import` in the built output; `npm run test:unit` passes (34/34); a Playwright script dragged the disc in a real Chromium past the 8° claim threshold against both built targets with `AudioWorklet.prototype.addModule`/`AudioParam.prototype.linearRampToValueAtTime` spied from outside the module (nothing is exposed on `window`) — Vinyl + Scratch on produced a matched ramp-up/ramp-down pair per gesture (while paused and while playing), Scratch off and Pure mode produced none, and `?social=demo` stayed clean. No console or page errors beyond the known baseline 404. The ESM-syntax fix and the retuned constants are desktop-Chromium-verified only — Playwright can't stand in for Safari here, so the actual sound and whether scratching now works at all on an iPhone still needs Ken's device.

### Step 4a: database module plus "never lose the library" tests

No behavior change — a move is a move, per the refactor rules above. First of three step-4 PRs putting storage behind an interface (see the "Revised extraction order" note above, item 2); this one is the safety net, added before anything about *how* the app saves actually changes (4b, 4c).

- Moved `DB` (`db`, `ok`, `req`, `open`, `store`, `put`, `get`, `all`, `clear`, `del`) out of `main.js`'s "persistence" section into `src/storage/idb.js`, unchanged — database name, version 4, the eight stores and key paths, the non-destructive `onupgradeneeded`, the 4-second open timeout and the `onblocked` handling are all byte-for-byte identical. Verified lossless: diffed the moved block against the exact original lines (modulo only the `export` keyword). `main.js` now imports `DB` from there; every one of its ~100 existing `DB.put`/`DB.get`/etc. call sites, and the two `MizikiSocial.mizikiAdapter({S, DB, …})` call sites, are untouched (replacing them is step 4c, not this one).
- Added `fake-indexeddb` as a dev dependency (the second dependency, after Vite) and `tests/unit/idb.test.js`: a fresh install creates all eight stores with the right key paths; an upgrade from a v3-shaped database (seeded with only `tracks`/`meta`/`sessions` and real records in each, via the same fake `IDBFactory` instance so it's a genuine upgrade, not a fresh install) keeps every old record and adds exactly the missing stores; with `indexedDB` undefined, every method no-ops safely (`open`/`put`/`del` return `false`, `get` returns `null`, `all` returns `[]`, nothing throws); and `put`/`get`/`all`/`del`/`clear` round-trip correctly, including a `Blob`. Each test gets its own fresh `IDBFactory` instance assigned to the global `indexedDB` `idb.js` reads, so tests can't see each other's databases.
- Added two persistence rules and a known-gap note to "How the app is put together" above: future version bumps need their own upgrade test in `idb.test.js`, and there's no `onversionchange` handler yet (an old tab open during a future upgrade would block it, not get out of the way) — noted for when version 5 actually ships, not fixed now.
- Verified: `npm run test:unit` passes (38/38 — 34 before this step + 4 new); both builds succeed; a Playwright script added a track, reloaded the page (closing and reopening is what actually exercises `DB.open()` reopening the same database and `restoreMeta()` reading it back, not just a first load) and confirmed the track was still there, against both built targets. No console/page errors beyond the known baseline 404.

### Step 4b: saved settings — parse, then apply

No behavior change — a move is a move, per the refactor rules above. Second of the three step-4 PRs (see the "Revised extraction order" note above, item 2).

- Split `main.js`'s `applyPrefs(p)` (which both validated each saved field *and* applied it — `setMode`, `cycleRepeat`, `toggleShuffle`, `bgUpdateUI` and the rest) into `serializePrefs(S)`/`parsePrefs(raw)` (`src/storage/prefs.js`, pure, no `main.js` import) plus `applyPrefs`, which stays in `main.js` completely unchanged — same body, same order of screen/side-effect calls, just fed `parsePrefs(raw)`'s output instead of the raw saved object. `saveMeta()` now calls `serializePrefs(S)` instead of building the prefs object inline.
- `parsePrefs` mirrors every one of `applyPrefs`'s existing per-field checks exactly: `char`'s four sliders must be numbers and `char.scratch` a boolean (step 4b hardcodes those field names rather than deriving them from `S.char`'s current keys, since `parsePrefs` never receives `S`); `target`/`auto`/`sleevePullEnabled`/`bgAudio`/`launchIntro`/`albumSides`/`sealedRecords`/`playInFullPlayer` must be the right primitive type; `libraryLayout`/`crateGroup`/`shareStyle`/`shareFormat` must be one of their listed values; `pace`/`librarySort`/`crateAnchor`/`repeat`/`mode` keep `applyPrefs`'s original truthy-only checks (no stricter than today). The one deliberate exception: `shuffle` moved from a truthy-only check to a real `typeof === 'boolean'` check, because `applyPrefs`'s own `if(parsed.shuffle && !S.shuffle)` already treats a present-but-`false` value exactly like an absent one — so this doesn't change what `applyPrefs` does with it, it just lets a saved `shuffle: false` round-trip instead of silently vanishing (caught by the round-trip test below).
- `tests/unit/prefs.test.js`: a full round trip through a fake `S`-shaped object; an old saved object missing `char.scratch` (pre-F2) leaves it at its default (On); a saved object missing newer fields (`shareStyle`, `playInFullPlayer`) or holding only a handful of very old fields loads without throwing; wrong types, unlisted enum values, and `null`/`undefined` input are all ignored rather than thrown on; unknown fields are dropped.
- Added a persistence rule to "How the app is put together" above: every new saved setting goes into both `serializePrefs` and `parsePrefs`, gets a default in `S`, and gets a test in `prefs.test.js` that an old saved object without it still loads.
- Verified: `npm run test:unit` passes (50/50 — 38 before this step + 12 new); both builds succeed; a Playwright script changed mode (Pure), Scratch (Off), shuffle (on), repeat (cycled), and library layout (list), reloaded the page, and confirmed every one of them was exactly as left, against both built targets. No console/page errors beyond the known baseline 404.

### Step 4c: a named storage interface in place of direct `DB` calls

No behavior change — a move is a move, per the refactor rules above. Last of the three step-4 PRs (see the "Revised extraction order" note above, item 2) — **step 4 is now complete.**

- Added `src/storage/repo.js`: one small object per store (`tracks`, `meta`, `sessions`, `achievements`, `collection`, `overlays`, `artwork`, `profile`) on top of `DB`, each with only the methods something in `main.js` actually calls — no method added that nothing uses. `storage.available()`/`storage.open()` replace direct reads of `DB.ok` and the one `DB.open()` call at boot. Every method is a thin forward to `DB`, so today's storage-unavailable behavior (`false`/`null`/`[]`, never throwing) is unchanged by construction.
- `meta` is the one store whose own shape didn't match what callers wanted: `DB.get('meta', key)` returns the whole `{k, v}` row, and every call site in `main.js` was unwrapping `.v` (or checking it was there) by hand. `meta.get(key)` does that once, returning the bare value (or `null` for a missing key or unavailable storage — callers never had a reason to tell those apart). `meta.put(key, value)` takes the key and value separately and builds the row itself. `meta.thumbs()` is the one place `main.js` reads `meta` by scanning all its rows rather than by one key (artwork thumbnails, keyed `thumb:<kind>:<id>`); it returns `{key, blob}` pairs with the `thumb:` prefix already stripped, same as `restoreThumbs()` always did with the slice itself, so it gets its own named method instead of a generic `all()` nothing else would call.
- Replaced every direct `DB.*` call in `main.js`, one commit per section: sealed records + thumbnails/display snapshots; core persistence (`queueSave`/`saveMeta`/`restoreMeta`); session tracking + metal-tier persistence (`restoreSessions` and its dozen `meta.get`s); metadata-edit overlays/artwork (`loadOverlayFor`, `applyEdit`, `revertTrack`, `applyEditWithUndo`, `undoLastEdit`, `storeTrack`); deletion, forget-library and clear-history (`forgetLibrary` needed an actual rewrite, not just a rename — it used to read and reinsert `meta`'s raw `{k, v}` rows directly across a `DB.clear()`, which `meta.get`'s bare-value return and `meta.put`'s key/value signature don't support directly, so it now tracks `{key, blob}` pairs itself across the same clear/reinsert sequence); crate art tiers; profile persistence and the boot sequence's `DB.open()`/`DB.all('tracks')`. The boot sequence's order (open, then load all tracks, then `restoreMeta()`) and every other section's `await` order are byte-for-byte unchanged — confirmed section by section, running the unit tests and both builds after each one.
- `grep -n "DB\." src/main.js` now finds only the two `MizikiSocial.mizikiAdapter({S, DB, …})` call sites (`localStaffPicks()`, `installSocialDemo()`), each with a comment explaining why: the adapter's own signature expects `DB`, and it's social-client code, not `main.js`'s own storage access — the one exception the step's instructions called for. No other direct `DB.*` call remains in `main.js`.
- Added `tests/unit/repo.test.js`: round-trips every store (including a `Blob` through `artwork`/`meta`), confirms `meta.get` returns the bare value (checked against a raw `DB.get` read of the same row) and that `meta.thumbs()` filters correctly, and confirms every method's storage-unavailable return matches `DB`'s own (`false`/`null`/`[]`, nothing throws) — same fresh-`IDBFactory`-per-test isolation as `idb.test.js`.
- Recorded the App Store plan as a note at the bottom of `repo.js` (not built): in a native shell, the audio bytes (`tracks.blob`), artwork blobs and the `thumb:*` blobs currently kept inside `meta` rows should live as files on the device, with only small records (not bytes) left in the database. This module is where that split would happen, since callers already never touch a blob's storage directly.
- Verified: `npm run test:unit` passes (59/59 — 50 before this step + 9 new); both builds succeed; `grep -n "DB\." src/main.js` confirms only the two adapter-comment lines remain.

### Step 5a: library model, queue and the decoded-audio cache

No behavior change — a move is a move, per the refactor rules above. First of the five step-5 PRs (see the "Revised extraction order" note above, item 3) — the step 5 prompt's own table maps each part to its new module; this is 5a.

- Three new modules, each one cohesive section of `main.js`:
  - `src/library/model.js`: `byName`/`trackSort` (comparators), `groupBy`, `allIdx`, `albumTracks` (moved from "library", "favorites" and the top of "persistence" respectively), plus `idOf`/`idxOf`. All pure — no DOM, no `main.js` import.
  - `src/player/queue.js`: the whole "queue, shuffle, repeat" section (`buildOrder`, `setQueue`, `reorderQueue`) plus `preloadNextTrack`/`advance` from "visible queue" (the rest of that section — `queueInsert`, `queueRemoveAt`, the queue-sheet UI — stays in `main.js` for 5e, per the step 5 prompt's own table).
  - `src/player/buffers.js`: `touchLRU`/`ensureBuffer`, the decoded-audio LRU cache, out of the old "clear listening history" section.
- `transport.js` imported `advance`, `preloadNextTrack` and `ensureBuffer` from `main.js` (step 3b's circular-import list) — all three are now real imports from `queue.js`/`buffers.js` instead, shrinking that list from ~18 to ~15. `queue.js` itself picks up a *real* circular import with `transport.js` (it imports `load`/`play`/`pause`/`seek`; `transport.js` imports `advance`/`preloadNextTrack` back) — the same shape already established between `engine.js` and `transport.js` in step 3a, not a new pattern.
- `queue.js` still needs six functions from `main.js` (`invalidateActiveSessionIfAny`, `runStartSequence`, `openPlayerViaSheet`, `shouldPutAway`, `putAwayInstant`, `runPutAwaySequence` — session invalidation and the start/put-away ceremony sequences), added to rule 8's list with `export` added to each in `main.js` and no other change. `main.js` itself now imports `byName`/`trackSort`/`groupBy`/`allIdx`/`albumTracks`/`idOf`/`idxOf` from `model.js`, `touchLRU`/`ensureBuffer` from `buffers.js`, and `setQueue`/`buildOrder`/`reorderQueue`/`advance`/`preloadNextTrack` from `queue.js` — the screens and sections that still live there (library rendering, next/prev buttons, shuffle/repeat toggles, the start sequence) call these the same way they always did.
- No `await` was added, removed or reordered anywhere in `advance`/`setQueue`/`ensureBuffer` — confirmed by diffing each against its pristine original lines (modulo only `export` additions and import-path changes).
- Added `tests/unit/library-model.test.js` (10 tests: `byName`'s case/accent-insensitive natural-number order, `trackSort`'s disc/track/title order, `groupBy`, and `albumTracks`'s auto/title/manual modes including the manual-order append-new/drop-stale behavior) and `tests/unit/queue.test.js` (12 tests: `buildOrder`'s shuffle, `reorderQueue`'s shuffle on/off, `advance`'s repeat off/all/one branches — using `mock.fn()` on the mocked `transport.js` exports to assert which of `play`/`pause`/`seek` actually ran — and `ensureBuffer`/`touchLRU`'s LRU eviction with stubbed decoding, including that the currently-playing track is never evicted even when it's the one that would otherwise be dropped). `queue.test.js` mocks `main.js`, `transport.js`, `clock.js`, `sleep-timer.js`, `audio/engine.js` and `ui/path-note.js` directly, the same `node --experimental-test-module-mocks` approach as `engine.test.js`/`clock.test.js`, since `queue.js`'s circular imports would otherwise transitively pull in `main.js`'s DOM-dependent top level.
- Verified: `npm run test:unit` passes (81/81 — 59 before this step + 22 new); both builds succeed (`npm run build` and `MIZIKI_BASE=/ npm run build`); a Playwright script imported a 3-track library, played through it, skipped forward/back (exercising the LRU cache), toggled shuffle on/off, cycled repeat through all three modes, and confirmed repeat-all wraps at the end of the queue while repeat-off stops — against both built targets, with the library still intact after a reload. No console/page errors beyond the known baseline 404.

FLAC/ID3 byte-format correctness itself is covered by `tags.test.js`'s synthetic fixtures instead. ### Step 5c: play history

No behavior change — a move is a move, per the refactor rules above. Third of the five step-5 PRs (see the "Revised extraction order" note above, item 3) — builds on 5a/5b.

- `src/history/` is new, with three modules matching the step 5 prompt's own table:
  - `played.js`: `addPlayedRange`, `playedCoverage`, `checkTrackCompletion` (the tail of the old "record art: session tracking" section) plus `incrementTrackPlay` (out of "metal tiers," its only caller's module).
  - `sessions.js`: the rest of "record art: session tracking" — `albumExpectedOrder`, `invalidateActiveSessionIfAny`, `touchActiveSession`, `sessionOnLoad`, `sessionTrackComplete`, `restoreSessions`, and the six `persist*` functions the prompt named (session counts, metal eligibility, track play counts, rare unlocks, album look preference), plus `persistAlbumLastPlayed`.
  - `clear.js`: `clearListeningHistory` and its helpers (the "clear listening history" section, minus the decoded-audio cache that moved separately in 5a).
- The prompt asked me to decide, and explain, where `persistAlbumLastPlayed` and `persistSealedAlbums` belong. `persistAlbumLastPlayed` moved into `sessions.js`: `S.albumLastPlayed` is written only by `sessionTrackComplete`, right there in the same section, and it was only sitting in the old "profile tab" section because that's the one place that happens to *read* it for display — not because profile-tab code owns it. `persistSealedAlbums` **stayed** in `main.js`'s "sealed records" section: it's tightly coupled to `restoreSealedAlbums`/`isAlbumSealed`/`breakSeal` there, none of which are session-tracking, and `S.sealed` is deliberately independent of listening history by design (SEALED spec §2, "Clear listening history must never reseal an album") — moving just the persist function away from its siblings would split a cohesive, already-correct unit for no benefit.
- `persistTrackLastPlayed`, `persistAlbumDisplay`/`persistTrackDisplay`, `snapshotAlbumDisplay`/`snapshotTrackDisplay` and `checkAlbumAchievementAndCollection` were **not** moved — the prompt's table didn't name them, and each still has other callers/siblings in `main.js`'s "sealed records" section that haven't moved yet. `played.js` and `clear.js` import them back from `main.js` as temporary circular imports instead (rule 8).
- `transport.js` and `queue.js` imported `sessionOnLoad`/`touchActiveSession` and `invalidateActiveSessionIfAny` (respectively) from `main.js` since steps 3b/5a; both are now real imports from `history/sessions.js` instead. `clock.js` imported `addPlayedRange`/`checkTrackCompletion` from `main.js` since step 3c; both are now real imports from `history/played.js`. All three modules' `main.js`-import lists in rule 8 shrink accordingly — `transport.js`'s drops from ~15 to ~13, `queue.js`'s from six to five, `clock.js`'s loses two of its five names. `main.js` itself now imports `albumExpectedOrder`/`persistAlbumLookPref`/`invalidateActiveSessionIfAny`/`touchActiveSession`/`restoreSessions` from `sessions.js` and `clearListeningHistory` from `clear.js`, for the handful of call sites (deletion, the boot sequence, settings wiring) that still live there.
- Added the persistence rule to "How the app is put together" above: `src/history/` is the only place that writes listening counters.
- No `await` was added, removed or reordered anywhere in `sessionOnLoad`/`sessionTrackComplete`/`restoreSessions`/`checkTrackCompletion`/`clearListeningHistory` — confirmed by diffing each against its pristine original lines (modulo only `export` additions and import-path changes).
- Added `tests/unit/history.test.js` (11 tests): `addPlayedRange`/`playedCoverage` (contiguous merges, a scrub-skip leaving a real gap, overlapping replays not double-counted, the 0.05s joining tolerance on both sides); `checkTrackCompletion` (counts once at ≥95% coverage, never twice for the same play-through, never below 95% even once playback reaches the track's end, never for a zero-duration track); and `clearListeningHistory` against `fake-indexeddb` (full scope wipes every counter — session/metal/rare/play/display — and leaves the library and other settings alone; orphaned-only scope drops just the identities no longer in `S.tracks`, keeping the rest). The test file mocks `main.js` with the union of what `played.js`/`sessions.js`/`clear.js` need from it (since `played.js` and `clear.js` load for real, and `played.js` itself pulls in the real `sessions.js`), plus `util/dom.js`'s `$`/`el` so `clear.js`'s one `$('#route-profile')` call doesn't need a real `document` — same `node --experimental-test-module-mocks` approach as the other step 3/5 test files.
- Preview verification used the two 1-second WAV fixtures (a full play-through completes fast enough to watch end to end) and the 20-second one for the scrub-skip case. Reading exact counter values wasn't possible from outside (nothing is exposed on `window`, by design — see `src/main.js`'s repo-layout entry), so Playwright checked the outward behavior instead: a 1s track plays through and auto-advances without error, scrubbing past most of a track and dismissing the player doesn't error, playing both 1s tracks in sequence (an "album," session-tracking-wise) completes cleanly, the profile tab (where session/metal progress renders) opens without error, and "Clear listening history" (everything) leaves the library's track count unchanged. The precise counting rules themselves (the actual subject of this step) are covered by `history.test.js`'s unit tests instead, which can assert on `S` directly.
- Verified: `npm run test:unit` passes (102/102 — 91 before this step + 11 new); both builds succeed; the Playwright script above ran clean against both built targets, library still intact after reload. No console/page errors beyond the known baseline 404.
