### Fix: saveMeta's missing `idOf` import (step 5d regression)

Step 5d's claim above — "`main.js` no longer calls `idOf`... anywhere in its own remaining code" — was wrong. `saveMeta()`, in the persistence section, also calls `idOf` (to serialize `S.playlists`/`S.albumOrder` by track id rather than by position), and that call was missed when `idOf` was dropped from `main.js`'s `./library/model.js` import line. The bug only surfaces once `S.playlists` or `S.albumOrder` is non-empty — favoriting a track, or setting a manual album order — and the debounced save (`queueSave()`, 600ms) actually fires: `idOf is not defined` (a `ReferenceError`, since nothing in scope resolves the bare identifier). Step 5e (above) didn't touch this — `saveMeta()` and its import line are untouched by that PR.

- Fixed by restoring `idOf` to the import line: `import { byName, trackSort, groupBy, allIdx, albumTracks, idOf, idxOf } from './library/model.js';`. One line, no other change.
- Confirmed the bug reproduced on `main` as of the step 5d merge (commit `1124582`), via a throwaway `git worktree` build, independent of any later work — so this really was a step 5d regression, not something introduced afterward.
- Verified: `npm run test:unit` still passes unchanged (123/123 on this fix's own base; 172/172 once rebased past step 5e); both builds succeed; a Playwright check imported a track, favorited it, and confirmed no `idOf` (or other) console/page error after the debounced save fires — against both built targets.

### Step 6a: lint safety net

**This step does change behavior, in two small, deliberate places — see "Real bugs fixed" below** — everything else is tooling. First of step 6's five PRs. The reason this step exists at all: step 5d's `idOf` bug (above) went live for an hour or two before PR #55 caught and fixed it, purely because nothing checked for a missing import until a user actually hit that code path. A linter catches the same class of bug in seconds, before merge, rather than after.

- Added **ESLint** (`eslint.config.js`, flat config) plus `globals` as dev dependencies. The rule set is deliberately small and focused on this one class of bug, not style: `no-undef` (error — the one that would have caught `idOf`), `no-unused-vars` (warn; `_`-prefixed args/vars ignored), `no-redeclare`/`no-dupe-keys`/`no-unreachable`/`no-const-assign`/`no-import-assign` (all errors — `no-import-assign` is what catches refactor rule 7, reassigning an imported `let`). No formatting rules — nothing in this PR reformats any code.
- Five config blocks cover the repo's actual mix of module styles (see CLAUDE.md's own repo-layout entries for why each exists): `src/**/*.js` as ES modules with browser globals; `src/miziki-social.js` as a classic script (no import/export) with browser globals; `src/audio/scratch-processor.js` as a classic script with `globals.audioWorklet` instead of browser globals (no DOM in `AudioWorkletGlobalScope` — the `globals` npm package ships this exact global set, including `registerProcessor`/`AudioWorkletProcessor`/`sampleRate`/`currentTime`, so no hand-written list was needed); `tests/unit/**/*.js` as ES modules with Node globals; and `tests/client.test.js`/`vite.config.js`/`eslint.config.js` as CommonJS with Node globals. A sixth block declares `MizikiSocial` as a known read-only global (never imported — it's loaded as a real global via a classic `<script src>` ahead of `main.js`, see the repo-layout entry) for the six modules that reach it: `main.js`, `history/sessions.js`, `library/delete.js`, `library/edit.js`, `library/import.js`, `player/transport.js`.
- Added `npm run lint` (`"eslint ."`) and wired it into both CI pipelines ahead of `npm run test:unit` — `.github/workflows/deploy.yml` and `netlify.toml`'s build command (`npm run lint && npm run test:unit && npm run build`) — so a lint error blocks both deploys, same as a failing test.
- Added refactor rule 10 above: every extraction PR must pass `npm run lint` with zero errors.

**Running the linter on the as-is codebase found exactly two `no-undef` errors — both real bugs, both fixed in this PR:**

1. **`src/library/edit.js`'s `renderEditArtPreview` wasn't exported, and a call site for it was left behind in `main.js`.** Step 5d moved the whole edit-sheet section into `edit.js`, including `renderEditArtPreview` (called internally by `openEditSheet` to draw the art thumbnail) — but `main.js`'s own wiring section, which step 5d didn't touch, also calls `renderEditArtPreview` directly, from the `#editArtFile` change handler, to live-update the preview after the user picks a new cover image. That call was missed, so `main.js` referenced a name step 5d had made module-private. **What a user would have seen:** opening a track's edit sheet, tapping to pick a new cover photo, and the preview thumbnail never changing to show it — the `ReferenceError` is thrown inside a `try/catch` that was already there for "bad image" handling, so it fails silently. The artwork *is* still correctly staged in `S.editArtBlob` and would save correctly on tapping Save, which made this easy to miss by just checking the save worked — the preview was the only thing actually broken. Fixed by adding `export` to `renderEditArtPreview` in `edit.js` and importing it in `main.js`. Verified via Playwright: before the fix (confirmed against the pre-6a build) the preview never updates; after, picking an image shows an `<img src="blob:...">` immediately — against both built targets, zero non-baseline console/page errors.
2. **`src/main.js` called a never-implemented `openBookletViewer(item.albumId)` from the gatefold's left panel.** Unlike the bug above, this one isn't a dropped import — `openBookletViewer` never existed anywhere in the codebase. It was reachable only when `S.booklets[item.albumId]` was truthy, and `S.booklets` itself has no default in `state.js` and is never written anywhere — a half-built, never-wired "booklet viewer" feature stub (a `.gf-booklet-btn` CSS rule was the only other trace of it) that was 100% dead code today: no user could ever trigger it. Asked the user how to handle it, since (unlike `idOf`/`renderEditArtPreview`) there was no "restore what was dropped" fix to mechanically apply — the user chose to remove the dead branch outright. Removed the `hasBooklet` check and the button it built from `buildGatefoldLeftPanel()` (two call sites, ~`main.js` lines 2728/2752 at the time), the now-dead `S.booklets` check from the gatefold's "left content cue" condition, and the orphaned `.gf-booklet-btn` CSS rule (`src/styles/11-gatefold.css`). Zero observable behavior change for any existing user, since the branch was unreachable before and after.

**Also fixed in this PR, as "obviously safe" unused-import/dead-code cleanup (not bugs — nothing threw, these just had zero remaining call sites):**
- 16 unused imports removed from `main.js`'s top-of-file import blocks (`trackSort`, `touchLRU`, `buildOrder`, `preloadNextTrack`, `applyEditWithUndo`, `updateFavoriteButtons`, `computeSun`/`sunProgress`/`easedProgress`/`computeRate`, `updateSleepUI`/`stopSleepState`/`sleepStopPlayback`/`sleepCheckDeadline`, `load`, `drawTime`) — leftovers from steps 3–5e where the only caller of each had already moved to another module. Confirmed each had exactly one occurrence in `main.js` (the import line itself) before removing.
- Two genuinely dead local functions in `main.js`, confirmed zero call sites anywhere: `sleeveFilled()` (an orphaned sibling of the still-used `crateFilled()` first-run gate — nothing currently toggles the sleeve-pull button's active state off this) and `shouldShowLaunch()` (superseded by two separate inline checks at the two points that actually gate the launch intro — `S.launchIntro` isn't known synchronously at boot, so `runLaunchSequence()` re-checks it itself rather than ever having called this function). Updated the launch-intro section's header comment, which named `shouldShowLaunch()` as the thing to see, to point at `runLaunchSequence()` instead.
- Two unused local variables in test files: `setQueue` (destructured but unused in `tests/unit/queue.test.js`) and `keep` (assigned via a side-effect-free `.slice()`, never read, in `tests/client.test.js`).

**Left as warnings, not fixed** (92 remain, 0 errors) — per the step's own instructions, fixed only where obviously safe: the overwhelming majority are `catch(e)` / `catch(err)` blocks that deliberately ignore the error (a very common, intentional pattern throughout this codebase, e.g. "bad image — leave whatever preview was already showing"); renaming ~80 of these to `_`-prefixed would silence the warnings but touch nearly every file for a purely cosmetic reason, which is out of scope for a safety-net PR. The rest are unused function parameters kept for a shared call signature (e.g. `duplicate-scan.js`'s `gi`, `search.js`'s `mode`, a handful of test-file callback params) and a few unused local assignments inside `src/miziki-social.js` (`sleep`, `clone`, `fp`, `spinKey`) — left untouched since that file is the social client's single source of truth (see its own repo-layout entry) and these predate this engagement.
- Verified: `npm run lint` passes with 0 errors (92 warnings, listed above); `npm run test:unit` passes (172/172, unchanged); both builds succeed (`npm run build` and `MIZIKI_BASE=/ npm run build`); Playwright confirmed the `renderEditArtPreview` fix works end to end against both built targets with zero non-baseline console/page errors.

### Step 6b: record-art display — metals, looks, sealed records, achievements

No behavior change — a move is a move, per the refactor rules above. Second of step 6's five PRs (see the "Revised extraction order" note above, item 4) — builds on 6a; `npm run lint` passes with zero errors throughout.

- Five new modules, matching the step 6 prompt's own table:
  - `src/record-art/metals.js`: the "metal tiers" section — `albumMetalFor`/`trackMetalFor` (and the threshold tables they read, now exported for their unit test), `metalBackground`, `setDiscBackground`, `METAL_COLORS`. Fully standalone — only `S`/`$`, no `main.js` import, not even a circular one.
  - `src/record-art/looks.js`: the "look switching" section — `resolveLook`, `applyDiscVariant`, `openLookPicker`/`closeLookPicker` (`availableLooks`/`setLookPref`/`lookLabel` stay module-private). `applyDiscVariant` living here rather than getting its own module was a judgment call: it's look-switching's own renderer, not a distinct concern, and keeping it with `resolveLook` meant the one thing every other module actually needed (a real, non-`main.js` import for `applyDiscVariant`) came for free.
  - `src/library/sealed.js`: "S5: sealed records" — `restoreSealedAlbums`, `persistSealedAlbums`, `isAlbumSealed`, `breakSeal`, `buildSealOverlay`.
  - `src/library/thumbs.js`: the "persisted artwork: thumbnails" section, plus `healDetails` (named in the step 6 prompt's table alongside `healThumbs`, even though it heals liner-notes/credits data, not thumbnails — its own comment already called out "same shape as `healThumbs()` above," and that shared batched-re-read shape, not subject matter, is why it landed here rather than a `library/tags/`-adjacent module of its own) — `restoreThumbs`, `healThumbs`, `pruneThumbs`, `persistAlbumDisplay`/`persistTrackDisplay`, `snapshotAlbumDisplay`/`snapshotTrackDisplay`.
  - `src/history/achievements.js`: the "profile tab" section's achievement/Collection/top-lists half — `checkAlbumAchievementAndCollection`, `achievementsList`, `collectionGroups`, `topAlbums`, `topTracks`, `crateFilled` (the last one left behind in step 5c as a follow-up, per the step 6 prompt). The *rendering* half of "profile tab" (`renderProfile` and friends) stays in `main.js` for step 6e, per the step 6 prompt's own heads-up about that header covering more than just demo code.
- `thumbURL` — the private `'album:<id>'`/`'track:<key>'` → object-URL map — needed to become `thumbs.js`'s one genuinely new export (not just an existing function gaining `export`): `main.js`'s own crate/gatefold/sealed-sheet rendering (none of it extracted yet) reads it directly by key in about half a dozen places that didn't move, so it's exported as a plain object rather than wrapped in an accessor — every one of those read sites keeps its exact `thumbURL['album:' + id]` expression unchanged. Nothing reassigns the object wholesale (in `thumbs.js` or anywhere importing it), so this doesn't trip refactor rule 7. `persistable` (the "strip the runtime `art` URL before persisting" helper `upsertAchievement`/`upsertCollectionEntry` need) is the other small new export, for the same reason — both are additions a human would make the same call on, not something to avoid.
- `applyDiscVariant` moving out of `main.js` is the one change with real leverage: six modules (`player/transport.js`, `library/edit.js`, `library/delete.js`, `history/played.js`/`sessions.js`/`clear.js`) were circularly importing it back from `main.js` since steps 3b–5c; all six now import it from `record-art/looks.js` as a real, non-circular import instead. `persistSealedAlbums` (three modules: `library/edit.js`/`delete.js`/`import.js`) and `restoreThumbs` (one: `history/sessions.js`) made the same move, to `library/sealed.js`/`library/thumbs.js` respectively. `history/sessions.js` is the one module that drops off rule 8's list entirely as a result — `applyDiscVariant`/`restoreThumbs` were its only two `main.js` names, and both are real imports now. It does pick up a new *real* circular import with `looks.js` (`looks.js` imports `persistAlbumLookPref` back from `sessions.js`), the same shape already established between `engine.js` and `transport.js` in step 3a — not a new pattern, just a new instance of it, and one step further from `main.js` than before.
- No `await` was added, removed or reordered anywhere in `breakSeal`/`restoreSealedAlbums`/`healThumbs`/`healDetails`/`checkAlbumAchievementAndCollection` or any other moved function — confirmed by diffing every moved block against its pristine original lines (modulo only `export` additions, import-path changes, and the `thumbURL`/`persistable` export additions above).
- Added `tests/unit/metals.test.js` (12 tests) and `tests/unit/sealed.test.js` (8 tests) — see the `tests/unit/` entry above for exactly what each covers, including the knock-on mock updates eight *existing* test files needed (`clock.test.js`, `queue.test.js`, `tags.test.js`, `history.test.js`, `edit.test.js`, `delete.test.js`, `credits.test.js`, `search.test.js`): each had been mocking `applyDiscVariant`/`restoreThumbs`/`persistSealedAlbums`/`checkAlbumAchievementAndCollection` as plain `main.js` exports, which stopped working the moment those became real imports from the four new modules instead — each test now mocks whichever of `record-art/looks.js`/`library/thumbs.js`/`library/sealed.js`/`history/achievements.js` its own real chain still reaches, for the same "stop the chain at the door before it needs a browser" reason the existing `audio/engine.js` mocks were already there for.
- Preview verification used two WAV fixtures sharing a real (non-"Unknown Album") RIFF `INFO` album tag, so the pair imports as one sealed album (SEALED spec §2 explicitly skips `Unknown Album`, confirmed by a first attempt with untagged fixtures correctly seeing zero `.new-tag` elements). A Playwright script imported them, confirmed the sealed `NEW` tag shows on both rows in the library's list layout, played the first track (the seal-breaking ceremony runs, no console/page error), confirmed the `NEW` tag is gone from both rows afterward (the whole album's seal breaks at once, matching `breakSeal`'s own per-album scope), confirmed `#lookBtn` correctly stays hidden (nothing earned yet, so `availableLooks().length` is 1), and opened the profile tab's Top Album/Top Song tiles and the Collection section, each rendering its sub-screen without error — against both built targets, zero non-baseline console/page errors.
- Verified: `npm run test:unit` passes (192/192 — 172 before this step + 20 new); `npm run lint` passes with 0 errors, 94 warnings — the exact same count as 6a's baseline, unchanged by this step's five new `src/` files or its two new test files; both builds succeed (`npm run build` and `MIZIKI_BASE=/ npm run build`); the Playwright script above ran clean against both built targets.

### Step 6c: background playback, lock screen and transport controls

No behavior change — a move is a move, per the refactor rules above. Third of step 6's five PRs (see the "Revised extraction order" note above, item 4) — builds on 6b; `npm run lint` passes with zero errors throughout. **This is the most iOS-sensitive part of step 6** — see the confirmations at the end of this entry.

- Three new modules, matching the step 6 prompt's own table:
  - `src/player/background.js`: the "background playback (optional)" section — `bgPrime`, `bgRouteActive`, `bgIdleNow`, `bgPosition`, `bgSync`, `bgUpdateUI`, `toggleBgAudio`, `applyBgAudioSetting`, `applyOutputRoute`, and the Media Session / lock-screen handlers (`mediaSessionSync`, `bgInstallHandlers`, `bgRemoveHandlers`, and the rest of the section's private helpers — `bgEl`, `bgSilentURL`, `bgStartEl`, `bgFallback`, `bgSwapGuard`, `bgTeardown` — stay module-private, exactly as they were module-private inside `main.js`).
  - `src/player/controls.js`: `nextTrack`, `prevTrack`, `toggleShuffle`, `cycleRepeat` — these four happened to sit under `main.js`'s "persistence" header (between `applyPrefs` and the navigation-shell section), not because they're persistence code, but because that's simply where they were in the file. `advance(1, false)`/`advance(-1, false)` is `nextTrack`'s/the tail of `prevTrack`'s entire body — nothing else in "visible queue" needed to move for these four.
  - `src/audio/mode.js`: `setMode` — similarly, it sat under "share image" (between `closePlayerShareSheet` and `isLibraryRouteVisible`) only because that's where it physically was, not because it's about sharing.
- Every one of these three modules turned out to need little or nothing from `main.js`: `controls.js` imports `seek` from `player/transport.js`, `advance`/`reorderQueue` from `player/queue.js`, and `invalidateActiveSessionIfAny` from `history/sessions.js` (all real) — no `main.js` import at all. `mode.js` imports only `routeSource` from `audio/engine.js` (real) — no `main.js` import at all. `background.js` needed exactly one: `queueSave`, for `toggleBgAudio`'s own save (added to rule 8's list).
- The bigger effect is on the three modules already circularly importing these names from `main.js` since steps 3a/3b/3c: `audio/engine.js` imported `applyOutputRoute`, `player/clock.js` imported `bgPosition`, and `player/transport.js` imported `bgPrime`/`bgRouteActive`/`bgIdleNow`/`bgPosition`. All of these are now real imports from `player/background.js` instead — `engine.js` drops off rule 8's list entirely (it had no other `main.js` import), and `transport.js`'s and `clock.js`'s lists both shrink. `engine.js` and `background.js` become a new circular *pair* of their own (`background.js` needs `ensureContext` for its Media Session "play" handler; `engine.js` needs `applyOutputRoute` for `ensureContext`'s own output-routing step) — the same shape already established between `engine.js` and `transport.js` in step 3a, not a new pattern. `transport.js` and `background.js` form a second such pair (`background.js` needs `play`/`pause`/`seek` for its Media Session handlers; `transport.js` needs `bgPrime`/`bgRouteActive`/`bgIdleNow`/`bgPosition` for `play`/`pause`/`seek` themselves).
- **iOS rules, confirmed untouched (rule 9):** `bgPrime()` is still the literal first statement in `player/transport.js`'s `play()`, before any `await` — only *where `bgPrime` is defined* moved, not *when it runs* or *what runs before it*. No `await` was added, removed or reordered anywhere in `play`/`pause`/`seek` (`transport.js`, unchanged by this move except its import lines) or in any function that moved into `background.js`/`controls.js`/`mode.js` — confirmed by diffing every moved block against its pristine original lines (modulo only `export` additions and import-path changes). **Lock-screen behavior (PR #42) is unchanged**: `bgInstallHandlers()` still wires `previoustrack`/`nexttrack` straight to `prevTrack`/`nextTrack` (skip, not seek) and leaves `seekforward`/`seekbackward` explicitly `null` so iOS's own ±10s default never takes over the Now Playing widget; `play`/`pause`/`stop`/`seekto` are wired in the exact same order as before, and `bgRemoveHandlers()` tears all eight down in the same order it always did. `mediaSessionSync()`'s own sequence (install handlers if needed → update metadata if the track/art changed → set `playbackState` → call `bgPosition()`) is byte-for-byte unchanged, as is `bgSync()`'s (clear the idle timer → start or arm the idle timer → sync the Media Session).
- `setMode` moving doesn't touch F1: the Pure-rate rule still lives entirely in `computeRate()` (`src/sundown/solar.js`), which reads `S.mode` the same way whether `setMode` is defined in `main.js` or `audio/mode.js` — nothing about F1 was expected to change here, and `tests/unit/controls.test.js`'s own `setMode` test confirms `computeRate()` still returns exactly `1` in Pure after moving it.
- Added `tests/unit/controls.test.js` (5 tests) — see the `tests/unit/` entry above for what it covers and how it's mocked. Four existing test files needed their `main.js`/`player/background.js` mocks adjusted the same way step 6b's record-art move required: `clock.test.js` dropped `bgPosition` from its `main.js` mock and let `background.js`/`controls.js` load for real (their own dependencies were already satisfied by that test's existing `transport.js`/`engine.js`/queue-chain mocks); `engine.test.js` moved `applyOutputRoute` out of its `main.js` mock and into a new, separate `player/background.js` mock, for the same "stop the chain at the door" reason `transport.js`/`clock.js` are already mocked there rather than loaded for real.
- Verified: `npm run test:unit` passes (197/197 — 192 before this step + 5 new); `npm run lint` passes with 0 errors, 94 warnings — unchanged from 6b's baseline; both builds succeed (`npm run build` and `MIZIKI_BASE=/ npm run build`). A Playwright script imported three WAV fixtures, switched Pure → Vinyl → Pure (confirming `#modeTag`/`aria-pressed` follow each click), toggled shuffle on and off, cycled repeat through all four reads (off → all → one → off, matching `cycleRepeat`'s own order), used next and previous from the full player, and toggled Background playback on and off in Character — against both built targets, zero non-baseline console/page errors. Lock-screen behavior itself (the Media Session handlers, and real locked-screen next/previous/play/pause) is only partly testable in a desktop Chromium tab, per the step's own "Preview focus" note — the real check is in the **After-merge check** below.

### Step 6d: share images

No behavior change — a move is a move, per the refactor rules above. Fourth of step 6's five PRs (see the "Revised extraction order" note above, item 4) — builds on 6c; `npm run lint` passes with zero errors throughout.

- Four new modules under `src/share/`, matching the step 6 prompt's own table and the section's own existing sub-headers:
  - `styles.js` — the "style x format system" (Part 2 §2): `SHARE_BG`, `SHARE_FORMATS`, `shareFitText`/`shareFitLines`, `shareSwatchGradient`, `loadImageEl`, `drawCover`, `roundRectPath`, `drawShareWordmark`, `shareGrainTile`, `shareDominantColor`/`hslStr`, `resolveShareStyle`, `shareChip`.
  - `crate-card.js` — the My Crate card (`composeShareImage`).
  - `now-playing-card.js` — the Now Playing card (`composePlayerShareImage`, plus its private `fillShareDisc`).
  - `sheet.js` — the share sheets' wiring (`openShareSheet`/`closeShareSheet`/`openPlayerShareSheet`/`closePlayerShareSheet`, the render-timer debouncing, `shareDoSave`/`shareDoShare`/`shareDoCopy`).
- **One judgment call, made once and applied consistently**: `hexMix` and `drawDiagonalWordmark` each sat physically between the two card composers in the pre-step-6d file, under no sub-header of their own, and — unlike every other helper in the "style x format system" section — each is used by *both* composers (`hexMix` for the metal-tier disc gradient in `fillShareDisc`, which `composePlayerShareImage` calls, **and** for the metals row in `composeShareImage` itself; `drawDiagonalWordmark` for the fallback jacket in both composers directly). Homing either one in just `crate-card.js` or `now-playing-card.js` would have made the other card module import across a card-to-card boundary for no real reason, and splitting them into a third micro-module seemed like more structure than two small functions warrant. Both moved into `styles.js` instead, alongside the rest of the shared, DOM-free drawing primitives that already lived there — a small, deliberate relocation (not a behavior change: identical code, just filed under a different module boundary than its physical position in the old file implied).
- `fmtCount` — a one-line pure helper that's lived in `main.js`'s "profile tab rendering" section since before this engagement started — needed `export` added, with no other change, so `crate-card.js` can reach it as a real but temporary circular import (rule 8): `composeShareImage`'s "top album" subtitle is the only place outside profile-tab rendering that ever needed it.
- The navigation-shell functions that sat under the old "share image" header (`isLibraryRouteVisible`/`isProfileRouteVisible`, `updateHeaderActionsAvailability`, `updateLibTabsForLayout`, `refreshLibraryTools`, the header search field, the header + menu, `showRoute`) only by physical accident — none of them are share-image code — stayed in `main.js`, per the step 6 prompt's own instruction, now under a correctly-named `navigation shell: header actions, search field, add menu` header instead of the stale `/* setMode moved... */` comment step 6c left behind in that spot.
- No `await` was added, removed or reordered anywhere in `composeShareImage`/`composePlayerShareImage`/`fillShareDisc`/`resolveShareStyle` or any other moved function — confirmed by diffing every moved block against its pristine original lines (modulo only `export` additions, import-path changes, and the `hexMix`/`drawDiagonalWordmark` relocation above).
- Added `tests/unit/share.test.js` (4 tests) — see the `tests/unit/` entry above for what it covers. No existing test file needed its mocks adjusted: nothing in the test suite previously imported any share-image function from `main.js` (the share-image section had no test coverage before this step), so this extraction is purely additive to the suite.
- Preview verification used two 2-second WAV fixtures. A Playwright script imported them, played the first track, opened the Now Playing share sheet, cycled through all three styles (Night/Cover/Paper) and confirmed the canvas resized correctly for all three formats (1080×1920/1080×1350/1080×1080, matching `SHARE_FORMATS` exactly), triggered "Save image" (a real download fired in headless Chromium), confirmed the Copy button is visible (`navigator.clipboard`/`ClipboardItem` are both present in Chromium) and that Share is correctly hidden (no Web Share API in headless Chromium — expected, not a bug), then closed it, opened the My Crate share sheet from the profile tab's own Share button, repeated the per-format canvas-size check, and triggered its own "Save image" — against both built targets, zero non-baseline console/page errors.
- Verified: `npm run test:unit` passes (201/201 — 197 before this step + 4 new); `npm run lint` passes with 0 errors, 98 warnings (94 before this step + 4 new `catch(e)` bindings in the four new `src/share/` files, the same long-standing, deliberately-unfixed pattern noted in step 6a — not a new kind of warning); both builds succeed (`npm run build` and `MIZIKI_BASE=/ npm run build`); the Playwright script above ran clean against both built targets.

### Step 6e: profile, social screens and demo mode (`?social=demo`)

No behavior change — a move is a move, per the refactor rules above. Last of step 6's five PRs (see the "Revised extraction order" note above, item 4) — builds on 6a/6b/6c/6d; `npm run lint` passes with zero errors throughout. **Step 6 is now complete.**

- `main.js`'s ~1,580-line "profile tab rendering" / "Social Step D" / "Social Step E" run (originally lines 3316–4894) moves out as seven new modules, matching the step 6 prompt's own instructions:
  - `src/profile/badges.js` — `metalBadge`/`collectionBadge`, the small DOM-building wrappers the profile tiles use around `metalBackground()`/`variantBackground()`. No `main.js` import, not even a circular one.
  - `src/profile/storefront.js` — the shared card primitives (`storeAwning`, `storeStat`, `storeSwitch`, `storePickTile`, `localAlbumArt`, `timeAgoShort`, `renderOpenStoreCard`, `renderStoreError`, `storeRequestsCard`, `STORE_TIER_LABEL`/`STORE_VIS_LABEL`, `renderStorefrontSection`) plus "my own storefront" (`localStaffPicks`, `renderLocalStorefront`, `loadAndRenderStorefront`, `cycleStoreVisibility`, `renderStorefront`).
  - `src/profile/settings.js` — the social sign-in flow and settings screen (`beginSocialSignIn`, `closeSocialAuth`, `renderSocialAuth` and its per-step renderers, `renderSocialSettingsScreen`).
  - `src/profile/screens.js` — `persistTrackLastPlayed`, `persistProfile`, `formatDisplayName`, `fmtCount`, `renderProfile`, `renderProfileHome`, `renderProfileExtras`, `renderTopAlbumsScreen`, `renderTopSongsScreen`, `renderCollectionScreen` — the real profile tab's own screens, which had been sitting physically under the demo-mode header in the pre-6e file.
  - `src/social/screens/friend-store.js` — the social nav stack, a friend's storefront (locked/unlocked), and the record sheet.
  - `src/social/screens/home.js` — the social home feed (`renderHome`, `feedEventText`, the people-search, the per-friend "now spinning" subscriptions).
  - `src/social/demo.js` — `installSocialDemo` alone, loaded via a **dynamic `import()`** instead of a static one.
- See the new `src/profile/` and `src/social/` repo-layout entries above for each file's exact exports and real imports — not repeated here.
- **Three judgment calls, each explained where made:**
  1. **The module split within `src/profile/`.** The step 6 prompt left this as "`screens.js`, `settings.js`, `badges.js` or similar," with no file named for the storefront-rendering code. Cross-referencing every function's call sites found `storeAwning`/`storeStat`/`timeAgoShort`/`localAlbumArt`/`storePickTile`/`renderOpenStoreCard`/`renderStoreError`/`storeRequestsCard`/`STORE_TIER_LABEL` are genuinely used by both the profile's own storefront code *and* the social friend-store screens — so a fourth file, `profile/storefront.js`, was added specifically so `social/screens/` could import them as real, non-`main.js` imports. The cost is a real 3-way circular cluster among `storefront.js` ↔ `settings.js` ↔ `screens.js` (each imports at least one name from another) — the same permitted peer-circular-import shape as `engine.js`/`transport.js` since step 3a, just three modules instead of two, and still strictly better than every one of those names staying a circular import back to `main.js`.
  2. **The module split within `src/social/screens/`.** The prompt gave no explicit sub-file breakdown here either. A naive 4-way split (nav / friend-store / record-sheet / home) would have produced three separate circular pairs, since nav, friend-store and the record sheet all call into each other with no clean one-directional boundary. Chose 2 files instead — `friend-store.js` (nav + friend-store + record sheet combined) and `home.js` — which reduces that to exactly one real circular pair (`friend-store.js` ↔ `home.js`, for `feedEventText`/`openFriendStore`).
  3. **`fmtCount`'s actual behavior vs. the step 6 prompt's own example.** The prompt's wording suggested a 999/1.2k-style number abbreviator; the function the codebase actually ships is just singular/plural word selection — `n + ' ' + (n === 1 ? singular : plural)`. Per the prompt's own "test the code's actual rules" escape valve, `tests/unit/profile.test.js` tests that real behavior, not the suggested one — changing `fmtCount` itself to add abbreviation would be a behavior change, out of scope for an extraction PR.
- `main.js`'s `restoreLibrary()` kept its exact `installSocialDemo()` call site and the exact `?social=demo` gate, just behind a dynamic import:
  ```js
  if(new URLSearchParams(location.search).get('social') === 'demo'){
    const { installSocialDemo } = await import('./social/demo.js');
    installSocialDemo();
  }
  ```
  This, not a static `import`, is what makes Vite emit `demo.js` as its own chunk (confirmed in both built targets: `dist/assets/demo-*.js`, 9.34 kB, separate from the ~240 kB main `miziki-*.js` bundle) rather than bundling ~700 lines of fixture data and `MizikiSocial`-monkey-patching into what every real user downloads.
- Six modules dropped their circular `main.js` import entirely once `src/profile/screens.js` existed for them to import from directly instead: `history/played.js` and `history/clear.js` (`persistTrackLastPlayed`, and `clear.js`'s `renderProfile`) drop off rule 8's list completely; `library/thumbs.js` keeps one remaining name (`invalidateCrateModel`) but loses `renderProfile`; `share/crate-card.js` (`fmtCount`) drops off the list completely. `profile/screens.js` itself picks up the one new rule-8 entry this step adds: `refreshLibraryTools`, for `renderProfile`'s own storefront-gear-button path — not extracted yet, since the navigation-shell functions it belongs with are step 7's job.
- No `await` was added, removed or reordered anywhere in `renderProfile`/`renderHome`/`installSocialDemo`/`beginSocialSignIn`/`loadAndRenderFriendStore` or any other moved function — confirmed by diffing every moved block against its pristine original lines (modulo only `export` additions, import-path changes, the dynamic-import rewrite of the one `installSocialDemo()` call site, and the `thumbURL`/`persistable`-style new-export additions noted in the repo-layout entries above).
- Added `tests/unit/profile.test.js` (7 tests: `fmtCount`'s real singular/plural rule, `metalBadge`'s rare-tier-with/without-art and metal-tier-with-label/with-image cases, `collectionBadge`, and a sanity check that the fixture data used stays in sync with the real `METAL_COLORS`/`VARIANT_DEFS` tables) — see the `tests/unit/` entry above for exactly what it covers and how it mocks the chain. Three *existing* test files (`clock.test.js`, `queue.test.js`, `history.test.js`) needed the same kind of mock-chain fix step 6b's and 6c's extractions required: each had been mocking `persistTrackLastPlayed`/`renderProfile` as plain `main.js` exports, which stopped resolving once `history/played.js`/`history/clear.js`/`library/thumbs.js` started importing them for real from `src/profile/screens.js` — each test moved those two names into a new, separate `mock.module` targeting `src/profile/screens.js` directly, the same "stop the chain at the door" pattern used throughout this test suite.
- Preview verification (Playwright, both built targets): the real app — imported two tracks, opened the profile tab, edited the display name (survives a reload), uploaded a profile picture (survives a reload), confirmed the local-only storefront shows no sign-in/open-store card (the calm "not set up" state, since `SOCIAL_SUPABASE_URL`/`SOCIAL_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY` are empty), and confirmed the demo chunk (`/assets/demo-*.js`) is never requested on a normal load. `?social=demo` mode — confirmed the demo chunk *is* requested, the Home tab becomes visible, and (wrapped defensively, since the deeper flow isn't this step's core requirement) walked through demo sign-in, a people search for a fixture friend, opening their storefront, opening a record sheet from one of their staff picks, and returning to the social nav root — all succeeded with no console/page errors on the final run.
- Verified: `npm run test:unit` passes (208/208 — 201 before this step + 7 new); `npm run lint` passes with 0 errors, 94 warnings (down from 98 — the ~1,580-line block this step removes from `main.js` took several of its own `catch(e)`-style warnings with it; no new warning introduced by any of the seven new files); both builds succeed (`npm run build` and `MIZIKI_BASE=/ npm run build`), each confirming the separate `demo-*.js` chunk; the Playwright script above ran clean against both built targets, zero non-baseline console/page errors.

### After step 6 (superseded — see "After step 7g" at the end of this changelog)

`main.js` had 14 modules still importing from it at the end of step 6 (rule 8's list at the time had the exact names for each): `library/credits.js`, `library/delete.js`, `library/edit.js`, `library/favorites.js`, `library/import.js`, `library/sealed.js`, `library/search.js`, `library/thumbs.js`, `player/background.js`, `player/clock.js`, `player/queue.js`, `player/transport.js`, `profile/screens.js`, `sundown/location.js`.

What was left in `main.js` for step 7, per the engagement's own plan: the persistence glue (`saveMeta`, `restoreMeta`, `applyPrefs`, `queueSave`), the navigation shell and routes, the start sequence and sleeve pull, the gatefold-to-player hand-off, put-away, launch intro, crate image tiers, the gatefold (~1,450 lines, the biggest single piece), and `wiring`. All of it has since moved — see "After step 7g" at the end of this changelog for where things stand now that all seven step-7 PRs are done.

### Step 7a: saving and restoring

No behavior change — a move is a move, per the refactor rules above. First of step 7's seven PRs (see the "Revised extraction order" note above, item 5) — step 7's own prompt flagged two header inaccuracies to watch for as the rest of step 7 proceeds: the "launch intro" section also holds the player overlay/morph and shared crate geometry, and the "S3: gatefold album view" header also covers crate rendering, the sealed-record sheet and `renderTracks` — move code by what it is, not by the header above it, in 7b/7c/7d/7e.

- `src/storage/meta.js`: the "persistence" section — `queueSave` (exported), the module-private `saveTimer`/`saveMeta`, and `restoreMeta`/`applyPrefs` (`restoreMeta` exported for the boot sequence's one call; `applyPrefs` stays module-private, exactly as it was in `main.js` — `restoreMeta` is its only caller). `saveMeta`/`restoreMeta` are otherwise byte-for-byte unchanged: same `idOf`/`idxOf` round trip for `S.playlists`/`S.albumOrder`, same `serializePrefs`/`parsePrefs` calls for everything else.
- `applyPrefs` keeps every one of its UI calls in the same order (`setMode`, `cycleRepeat`/`toggleShuffle`, `bgUpdateUI`/`applyBgAudioSetting`, `attachLaunchSettingRow`, `applyCharacter`, `applyVolume`), each now imported from the module that actually owns it — `audio/mode.js`, `player/controls.js`, `player/background.js`, `audio/engine.js`, `sundown/location.js` — all real, non-circular imports, since every one of those modules already existed from earlier steps. `attachLaunchSettingRow` is the one exception: the launch-intro section it belongs to hasn't moved yet (that's step 7c), so it's a temporary circular import back to `main.js` (rule 8) — `export` added to it in `main.js`, no other change.
- Six modules were circularly importing `queueSave` from `main.js` since various earlier steps (`player/background.js` since 6c, `sundown/location.js` since 2c, `library/search.js`/`delete.js`/`favorites.js`/`import.js` since 5d/5e): all six now import it from `storage/meta.js` directly instead. `background.js` and `location.js` pick up real circular *pairs* of their own with `meta.js` as a result (`meta.js` needs `bgUpdateUI`/`applyBgAudioSetting` back from `background.js`, and `applyVolume` back from `location.js`, for `applyPrefs()` itself) — the same peer-import shape already established between `engine.js`/`transport.js` in step 3a, not a new pattern. `search.js`/`delete.js`/`favorites.js`/`import.js` have no such reverse dependency, so their imports are simply real now, with no new circularity.
- The stale "profile tab" / S5 sealed-records / thumbnails / achievements / duplicate-scan leftover comments that sat in `main.js` just above the old persistence header (29 lines, flagged by the step 7 prompt) were removed outright rather than moved again: each was pure documentation of code already relocated in steps 5c/5d/6b, and every destination module (`profile/screens.js`, `library/sealed.js`, `library/thumbs.js`, `history/achievements.js`, `library/duplicate-scan.js`) already carries its own copy of the relevant note — confirmed by direct comparison before deleting, not assumed.
- No `await` was added, removed or reordered anywhere in `saveMeta`/`restoreMeta`/`applyPrefs` — confirmed by diffing the moved block against its pristine original lines (modulo only `export` additions and import-path changes).
- Added `tests/unit/meta.test.js` (4 tests) — the tests the step 7 prompt said would have caught the step 5d `idOf` regression: a full `saveMeta()`/`restoreMeta()` round trip (playlists, manual album order, album sort, read back by track id rather than position); a playlist entry whose track no longer exists is dropped rather than thrown on; `queueSave()`'s 600ms debounce collapses several calls into exactly one `saveMeta()` run; and with storage unavailable, all three functions quietly no-op. Uses a real 650ms wait rather than `node:test`'s `mock.timers` for the debounce test, since `saveMeta()`'s own body awaits real `fake-indexeddb` request callbacks regardless of whether `setTimeout` is faked — see the test file's own comment. Seven *existing* test files (`clock.test.js`, `controls.test.js`, `delete.test.js`, `engine.test.js`, `favorites.test.js`, `search.test.js`, `tags.test.js`) needed a `storage/meta.js` mock added for the same reason: each had been relying on `main.js`'s mocked `queueSave` to stop a chain that, since this step, runs through the real `storage/meta.js` instead — see the `tests/unit/` entry above for the exact fix applied to each.
- Preview verification (Playwright, both built targets): imported three WAV fixtures, switched mode to Pure, toggled shuffle and cycled repeat (via `page.evaluate(el => el.click(), ...)`, since these controls live inside the full-player overlay this script never opens — a direct DOM `click()` exercises the same handlers a real tap would), waited past the 600ms debounce, reloaded, and confirmed mode/shuffle/repeat all read back exactly as left and the library was still intact — against both built targets, zero non-baseline console/page errors.
- Verified: `npm run test:unit` passes (212/212 — 208 before this step + 4 new); `npm run lint` passes with 0 errors, 94 warnings — unchanged from the pre-7a baseline; both builds succeed (`npm run build` and `MIZIKI_BASE=/ npm run build`); the Playwright script above ran clean against both built targets.

### Step 7b: navigation shell, player overlay, start/put-away, hand-off

No behavior change — a move is a move, per the refactor rules above. Second of step 7's seven PRs (see the "Revised extraction order" note above, item 5) — builds on 7a. **This is the most iOS-gesture-sensitive step since 3b/3c** — see the confirmations at the end of this entry.

- Seven new modules, matching the step 7 prompt's own table (see their own repo-layout entries above for exact exports and imports): `src/util/motion.js` (`REDUCED`, `easeOutCubic`) and six files under `src/shell/` — `mini-player.js`, `routes.js`, `header.js`, `player-overlay.js`, `start-sequence.js`, `handoff.js`, `put-away.js`.
- **Two deliberate exceptions to the prompt's own file-name table, both explained where made:**
  1. **`easeOutCubic` and `REDUCED` went to `src/util/motion.js`, not `src/shell/put-away.js`.** The prompt's table listed `easeOutCubic` under put-away.js's target, since that's physically where it sat — but a separate, more specific instruction said to give both it and `REDUCED` a proper home in `util/motion.js` instead, and that's the one followed: `easeOutCubic` is only ever called by `player/clock.js`'s render loop, never by `put-away.js` itself, so filing it under put-away would have been grouping by old physical location, not by what the code actually is. `put-away.js` doesn't import `easeOutCubic` at all as a result.
  2. **`GATEFOLD_ANIM` stayed in `main.js`.** It's physically interleaved with `SLEEVE_ANIM` in the original file (the two animation-timing blocks sit back to back), but it's the gatefold's own open/close timing, not mini-player/overlay code, and the gatefold itself hasn't moved yet (step 7e) — dragging it into `mini-player.js` would have been a move by proximity, not by what the code is, the same judgment the step 6 prompt's own "wrong header" callouts warned against. Its CSS-custom-property side effects (`--gatefold-lift-duration` etc.) stayed right alongside it.
- Three new real circular/peer pairs, the same permitted shape already established between `engine.js`/`transport.js` since step 3a: `mini-player.js` ↔ `routes.js` (`mini-player.js` needs `isLibraryRouteVisible`; `routes.js` needs `updateHeaderActionsAvailability`/`closeSearchField`/`closeAddMenu` from `header.js`, which itself needs `routes.js`'s two visibility checks back — a 3-way cluster the same shape as `profile/storefront.js`/`settings.js`/`screens.js` from step 6e), and `start-sequence.js` ↔ `put-away.js` (for `waitAnimEnd`/`sleeveArtFor` and `clearSpinDown`). `player-overlay.js` → `start-sequence.js` is a real but one-directional import (no import back), since nothing in `start-sequence.js` ever needs to call back into the player-overlay functions.
- The biggest effect is on the five modules already circularly importing these names from `main.js` since earlier steps: `player/transport.js` (`updateShellAlbum`/`renderMiniPlayer`/`showRoute`/`clearSpinDown`), `player/queue.js` (`runStartSequence`/`openPlayerViaSheet`/`shouldPutAway`/`putAwayInstant`/`runPutAwaySequence` — all five, step 5a's entire list), `library/edit.js` (`renderMiniPlayer`), `library/delete.js` (`clearSpinDown`/`closePlayer`/`renderMiniPlayer`), `library/credits.js` (`closePlayer`/`showRoute`), `library/favorites.js` (`REDUCED`) and `player/clock.js` (`easeOutCubic`/`REDUCED`, its *entire* `main.js` list) and `profile/screens.js` (`refreshLibraryTools`, also its entire list). All of these are now real imports from the new `shell/`/`util/motion.js` modules instead — `player/queue.js`, `player/clock.js` and `profile/screens.js` drop off rule 8's list completely as a result; `player/transport.js`, `library/edit.js`, `library/delete.js`, `library/credits.js` and `library/favorites.js` all shrink.
- No `await` was added, removed or reordered anywhere in `runStartSequence`/`runPutAwaySequence`/`runGatefoldHandoff`/`runMorphOpen`/`openPlayerViaSheet`/`closePlayer` or any other moved function — confirmed by diffing every moved block against its pristine original lines (modulo only `export` additions and import-path changes). **The iOS audio-gesture rules (rule 9) are untouched**: `bgPrime()` is still the literal first statement in `player/transport.js`'s `play()` (unchanged by this step — `play()` itself didn't move), and every one of `runStartSequence`'s/`runGatefoldHandoff`'s/`runPutAwaySequence`'s own `await sleep(...)`/`await waitAnimEnd(...)` calls, and the `S.sleeveSeq` cancellation-token checks after each one, moved verbatim.
- Added `tests/unit/shell.test.js` (6 tests): `easeOutCubic` (the curve's fixed points at 0/1, the documented formula at its midpoint, monotonically increasing across the range) and `shouldPutAway` (`auto && S.sleevePullEnabled && !REDUCED` — true only when all three hold; false for a manual skip even with the setting on; false when the setting itself is off). `util/motion.js`'s `REDUCED` is read once, at module load, from `window.matchMedia` — there's no browser `window` in Node, so this file stubs `globalThis.window = { matchMedia(){ return {matches:false}; } }` before importing anything, letting both `easeOutCubic` and `shouldPutAway` run as the genuine, unmocked code (the same "stub only what a browser would otherwise provide" approach `idb.test.js`/`prefs.test.js` already use) rather than a re-implementation — the REDUCED-true branch isn't separately exercised, the same precedent `favorites.test.js` already set for this exact flag. `shell/put-away.js`'s own non-pure imports (`mini-player.js` for `SLEEVE_ANIM`/`renderMiniPlayer`, `start-sequence.js` for `waitAnimEnd`/`sleeveArtFor`) are mocked directly, since an ES module evaluates its whole import graph regardless of which export is actually used, and `mini-player.js`'s real chain would otherwise reach `player/background.js` → `player/controls.js` → `storage/meta.js` → `audio/mode.js` — the usual "stop the chain at the door" reasoning. **Not separately tested, per the step 7 prompt's own "not much here is pure" escape valve**: `waitAnimEnd`, `sleeveArtFor`, `runStartSequence`/`runPutAwaySequence`/`runGatefoldHandoff` themselves, and the morph/hand-off geometry math — these are choreographed, DOM- and `requestAnimationFrame`-driven async sequences with no meaningful pure core beyond the two decisions above, and the Playwright scenario below is what actually exercises them end to end.
- Eleven *existing* test files needed a mock-chain fix of the same shape step 6b's/6c's/6e's extractions each required: `clock.test.js`, `controls.test.js`, `credits.test.js`, `delete.test.js`, `edit.test.js`, `favorites.test.js`, `history.test.js`, `profile.test.js`, `queue.test.js`, `search.test.js` and `tags.test.js` had each been mocking `updateShellAlbum`/`renderMiniPlayer`/`showRoute`/`closePlayer`/`clearSpinDown`/`runStartSequence`/`openPlayerViaSheet`/`shouldPutAway`/`putAwayInstant`/`runPutAwaySequence`/`REDUCED`/`refreshLibraryTools` as plain `main.js` exports (whichever subset each one's own real import chain actually reached) — all of these stopped resolving the moment the modules that need them started importing the real `shell/*.js`/`util/motion.js` instead. Each test moved the affected names into new, separate `mock.module` calls targeting the specific `shell/` file (or `util/motion.js`) that now owns them, the same "stop the chain at the door" pattern used throughout this suite — none of them needed to load any `shell/` module for real, since none of them test shell behavior itself (`shell.test.js`, above, is the one exception).
- Preview verification used three WAV fixtures carrying real RIFF `INFO` album/artist/track tags (so imports land in named albums, not "Unknown Album" — the pull/hand-off ceremonies deliberately skip untagged tracks). A Playwright script, run against both built targets:
  1. started a track playing (sheet → pull ceremony), confirmed the full player opened, closed it (`#playerDismiss`), confirmed the mini-player strip became visible, tapped it (`#miniTap`) to reopen via the morph, and closed it again — exercising both ways into the full player and back out.
  2. opened the crate's first album's gatefold (keyboard `Home`/`Enter` — crate cards carry no text, only a lazily-loaded art image, so navigation goes through the crate stage's own keyboard handler rather than guessing pixel positions) and played a track from it, then jumped to the crate's last album (`End`/`Enter`, guaranteed a different album) and played a track from *its* gatefold — a real gatefold-to-player hand-off away from whatever was already on the platter. Confirmed the hand-off ghost ended hidden, the real disc ended visible (not stuck `disc-hidden`), and the track name changed to the second album's track.
  3. queued a single short track with repeat off and let it play to its natural end, confirming the sleeve closed over the platter (`#sleeve` display `none`), the disc went `disc-hidden`, and the mini-player's playing indicator cleared — `runPutAwaySequence` end to end.
  Zero non-baseline console/page errors on either built target across all three scenarios. One result is headless-environment-dependent rather than a regression: after the hand-off, `S.playing` sometimes read `false` in this environment even though every visual landing step completed correctly — `startAudio()` (`play`) is invoked several `await`s and real seconds after the original tap, and headless Chromium's autoplay policy can decline to resume an `AudioContext` that far from the gesture it's nominally still inside, a stricter read of the same iOS rule (rule 9) this step was careful not to disturb. This predates the refactor (the `await` sequencing inside `runGatefoldHandoff` is byte-for-byte unchanged) and is exactly the kind of thing the device checklist, not a desktop browser, is the real check for.
- Verified: `npm run test:unit` passes (218/218 — 212 before this step + 6 new); `npm run lint` passes with 0 errors, 94 warnings — unchanged from the pre-7b baseline; both builds succeed (`npm run build` and `MIZIKI_BASE=/ npm run build`); the Playwright script above ran clean against both built targets.

### Step 7c: launch intro and crate geometry/tiers/view

No behavior change — a move is a move, per the refactor rules above. Third of step 7's seven PRs (see the "Revised extraction order" note above, item 5) — builds on 7a/7b.

- Five new modules, matching the step 7 plan's own scope for this step ("launch intro, crate geometry/tiers/view") — see their own repo-layout entries above for exact exports and imports: `src/crate/geometry.js` (`flowPose`/`flowZIndex`), `src/crate/tiers.js` (the "S4: crate image tiers" section), `src/crate/model.js` (the crate data model), `src/crate/view.js` (the crate-shelf rendering), and `src/launch/intro.js` (the launch intro sequence).
- **Two misleading-header splits, both flagged by step 7's own prompt as something to watch for across this step and 7d/7e:**
  1. **`main.js`'s "S4: crate image tiers" header covered two distinct concerns.** Image-tier building (`buildCrateTier`, `getCrateArtURL`, `deleteCrateArtTiers`, `healCrateArt`, `prewarmCrateAnchorL`, the `crateArtCache` LRU) went to `src/crate/tiers.js`; grouping/sorting albums into the shelf's own data model (`ensureCrateModel`, `invalidateCrateModel`, `isNowPlayingAlbum`, `persistCrateAnchor`, `crateRailJumpTarget`, `libraryHasGenres`) — which had only ever sat under that header by physical accident — went to `src/crate/model.js` instead.
  2. **`main.js`'s "S3: gatefold album view" header covered four things, not one.** The gatefold itself, the sealed-record sheet, `renderTracks` and the crate-shelf rendering code were all physically interleaved under it. Only the crate-rendering piece (`applyCrateMiniInset`, `renderCrate`, `centerEmptyState`, plus the module-private `openCrateAlbum`/`updateCrateGroupToggleUI`/`renderCrateFlat`) moved here, into `src/crate/view.js` — the gatefold and sealed-sheet code is 7e's territory, `renderTracks` is 7d's, and both stay in `main.js` for now, reached from `crate/view.js` as a temporary circular import (rule 8).
- **Two smaller judgment calls:**
  1. **`flowPose`/`flowZIndex` got their own module, `src/crate/geometry.js`, rather than living in either `launch/intro.js` or `crate/view.js`.** Both the launch overlay's card stack and the crate shelf call it, and CRATE spec §3 is explicit that this has to stay "one geometry implementation" — filing it under either consumer would have made the other import across a view-to-view boundary for no reason.
  2. **`toggleScratch` moved to `src/player/scrub.js`, not `launch/intro.js`.** It had been sitting in the old launch-intro section only because that's physically where the Character menu's Scratch toggle wiring landed; `scrub.js` already owns everything else about Scratch (F2), so this is where it belongs.
- `crate/tiers.js`'s `crateArtCache` and `launch/intro.js`'s `resolveLastPlayedAlbum` are the two names that cross the launch/crate boundary: `launch/intro.js` imports `crateArtCache` read-only (for `makeLaunchCard`'s own art lookup), and `crate/view.js` imports `resolveLastPlayedAlbum` from `launch/intro.js` (for the crate's own cold-start anchor). Neither import goes both ways, so neither is a circular pair — `launch/intro.js` and `crate/view.js` each depend on the other's sibling module, not on each other directly, except for that one one-directional `resolveLastPlayedAlbum` import.
- `libraryReadyResolve`/`libraryReadyPromise` (a `Promise`/resolver pair, previously two top-level `let`s in `main.js`) moved into `launch/intro.js` as module-private state, per refactor rule 7 (an importing module can't reassign an exported `let`): `main.js`'s two `libraryReadyResolve()` call sites in the boot sequence now call a new exported wrapper, `markLibraryReady()`, instead — the same "export a small setter function from the module that owns it" shape rule 7 itself prescribes.
- The six other modules already circularly importing the crate-tiers/crate-model/crate-view names from `main.js` were repointed to the new real modules in the same commit as the main.js wiring itself, so nothing was left stale: `library/import.js` (`invalidateCrateModel`/`healCrateArt`), `library/edit.js` (`deleteCrateArtTiers`/`buildCrateTier`), `library/delete.js` (`deleteCrateArtTiers`/`invalidateCrateModel`), `library/thumbs.js` (`invalidateCrateModel`), `shell/mini-player.js` (`applyCrateMiniInset`/`centerEmptyState`) and `shell/header.js` (`libraryHasGenres`) all now import these for real from `crate/tiers.js`/`crate/model.js`/`crate/view.js` instead of circularly from `main.js`. `library/thumbs.js`, `shell/mini-player.js` and `shell/header.js` each had only that one name on their own `main.js` list, so all three drop off rule 8's list entirely as a result. `storage/meta.js`'s `attachLaunchSettingRow` import was repointed the same way, to the real `launch/intro.js` — dropping `meta.js` off rule 8's list too, and creating a new real circular pair with `launch/intro.js` itself (`intro.js` imports `queueSave` back from `meta.js`), the same peer-import shape already established between `engine.js`/`transport.js` since step 3a. `crate/view.js` picks up the one new rule-8 entry this step adds, for the gatefold/sealed-sheet/`renderTracks` functions noted above.
- No `await` was added, removed or reordered anywhere in `runLaunchSequence`/`ensureCrateModel`/`renderCrate`/`buildCrateTier`/`healCrateArt` or any other moved function — confirmed by diffing every moved block against its pristine original lines (modulo only `export` additions, import-path changes, and the `markLibraryReady()` wrapper noted above).
- Added `tests/unit/crate-geometry.test.js` (6 tests: `flowPose`'s centered/undeflected/opaque base case, direction sign following `d`'s sign not its magnitude, the opacity falloff past `|a|=3` floored at 0, the speed-stretch making farther cards move more than near ones at the same speed, `opts.originY`/`opts.spread`, and `flowZIndex`'s floor past `|a|=9`) — needs no mocking at all, since `geometry.js` has zero imports. Added `tests/unit/crate-model.test.js` (6 tests: `libraryHasGenres`, `ensureCrateModel`'s one-record-per-album grouping and its dirty-flag/group-change rebuild gate, `isNowPlayingAlbum`, `crateRailJumpTarget`'s divider-to-first-record landing, `persistCrateAnchor`) — stubs `globalThis.window = {devicePixelRatio: 1}` for `crate/constants.js`'s top-level `CRATE_DPR` read (the same "stub only what a browser would otherwise provide" approach `shell.test.js` already uses) and mocks `storage/meta.js`'s `queueSave`. ### Step 7e: the gatefold and the sealed-record sheet

No behavior change — a move is a move, per the refactor rules above. Fifth of step 7's seven PRs (see the "Revised extraction order" note above, item 5) — builds on 7a/7b/7c/7d. **Rule 8's module list goes to zero in this step** — see the confirmations at the end of this entry.

- Two new modules under `src/gatefold/`, matching the step 7 plan's own scope for this step ("gatefold + sealed-record sheet") — see their own repo-layout entries above for exact exports and imports: `view.js` (the gatefold itself, GATEFOLD spec §A2-A7) and `sealed-sheet.js` (the sealed-record sheet, SEALED spec §5).
- **The last piece of the misleading "S3: gatefold album view" header split**: that header had sat over four distinct things — the gatefold, the sealed-record sheet, the crate-shelf rendering (moved to `src/crate/view.js`, step 7c) and `renderTracks()` (moved to `src/library/view.js`, step 7d). This step moves the last two: the gatefold (`GF`, `isGatefoldOpen`, `openGatefold`, `closeGatefold`, `closeGatefoldInstant`, `updateGatefoldNowPlaying`, `updateGatefoldScale`, `gatefoldSwitchPanel`, and all the module-private rendering/gesture helpers) and the sealed-record sheet (`SEALSHEET`, `isSealedSheetOpen`, `updateSealedSheetAfterBreak`, `openSealedSheet`, `closeSealedSheet`, `sealedQuietPlay`, `sealedHandoffPlay`, `closeSealedSheetInstant`) — plus `GATEFOLD_ANIM`, which had sat physically just *above* the old "S3" header (next to `main.js`'s other animation-timing constants) rather than inside it, but is gatefold code through and through and moved alongside the functions that actually use it, the same judgment step 7b's prompt made for `HANDOFF_ANIM`/`SLEEVE_ANIM`.
- **One split decision, explained where made**: the gatefold and the sealed sheet went into two files, not one, even though they share `buildGatefoldTracklist()`/`gatefoldTrackIndices()` (SEALED spec §5's own "the sealed sheet reuses this exact rendering") — `sealed-sheet.js` imports those two (plus `GF`, read-only) from `view.js`, a real one-directional import, not a circular pair, since nothing in `view.js` ever needs anything back from the sheet. Keeping them separate matches the repo's existing precedent of splitting a data module from its own sheet UI (`library/sealed.js`'s sealed-records *data* stays separate from this sheet's UI, by design — see that module's own updated entry above) and keeps each file's own job description to one sentence.
- Five modules that were circularly importing gatefold/sealed-sheet names from `main.js` since steps 3b/6b/7b/7c are repointed to the two new real modules: `player/transport.js` (`updateGatefoldNowPlaying`), `library/sealed.js` (`updateSealedSheetAfterBreak`), `crate/view.js` (`openGatefold`/`openSealedSheet`/`sealedQuietPlay`), `shell/routes.js` (`GF`/`closeGatefoldInstant`), `shell/handoff.js` (`isGatefoldOpen`/`closeGatefoldInstant`). Each of these five had only gatefold-related names left on its own `main.js` list (the last one, in every case, per the running tally in rule 8's own module-list note) — **all five drop off rule 8's list entirely**, leaving it empty. `grep -rn "from '.*main.js'" src/` outside `main.js` itself confirms zero matches.
- `gatefold/view.js` picks up three real circular pairs as a result: with `player/transport.js` (it needs `play`; `transport.js` needs `updateGatefoldNowPlaying` back — the same shape as `engine.js`/`transport.js` since step 3a), with `shell/handoff.js` (it needs `runGatefoldHandoff`; `handoff.js` needs `isGatefoldOpen`/`closeGatefoldInstant` back), and transitively with `library/sealed.js` through `sealed-sheet.js` (which needs `buildSealOverlay`; `library/sealed.js` needs `updateSealedSheetAfterBreak` back). `crate/view.js` and `shell/routes.js` become real, one-directional imports instead — neither `gatefold/view.js` nor `gatefold/sealed-sheet.js` needs anything back from either of them, so neither is a circular pair.
- No `await` was added, removed or reordered anywhere in `openGatefold`/`closeGatefold`/`runGatefoldOpen`/`openSealedSheet`/`closeSealedSheet`/`sealedHandoffPlay` or any other moved function — confirmed by diffing every moved block (`GATEFOLD_ANIM` plus the full old "S3" gatefold/sealed-sheet content) against its pristine original lines (modulo only `export` additions, import-path changes, and the `gatefoldSideSplits` export addition below), and by a separate script-based normalization diff of the two new files' concatenated code against the exact original block, which came back identical but for that one export, the moved-file header comments, and the two sealed-sheet functions' position (both files' own content, not reordered within either file).
- `gatefoldSideSplits` gained `export`, previously private, for its own unit test — same precedent as `angleDelta` (`scrub.js`, step 3c) and `sampleStackAlbums` (`launch/intro.js`, step 7c).
- Added `tests/unit/gatefold.test.js` (6 tests: `gatefoldSideSplits`'s four branches — under 4 tracks, `S.albumSides` off, a single disc's midpoint split, multiple discs splitting by disc — plus `isGatefoldOpen`/`isSealedSheetOpen` both reading `false` with nothing open) — see the `tests/unit/` entry above for its mocking approach and for why opening either overlay isn't separately unit-tested (no meaningful pure core beyond the one exported decision function; the Playwright script below is what actually exercises the open/close/hand-off choreography). Four *existing* test files (`clock.test.js`, `history.test.js`, `queue.test.js`, `sealed.test.js`) needed the same kind of mock-chain fix every prior step's extractions required: each had been mocking `main.js`'s `updateSealedSheetAfterBreak`, which stopped resolving once `library/sealed.js` started importing it for real from `src/gatefold/sealed-sheet.js` instead — each test moved that one key into a new, separate `mock.module` targeting `src/gatefold/sealed-sheet.js` directly (dropping the `main.js` mock entirely in `history.test.js`/`queue.test.js`, where it was the only key left on it), the same "stop the chain at the door" pattern used throughout this suite.

A script: opened Album A's sealed sheet (first play — confirmed its title and 2-row tracklist), played a row (confirmed the sheet closed instantly — `closeSealedSheetInstant` — and the seal broke); reopened the crate's first record, now unsealed, confirming the *real gatefold* opened this time (correct title, a now-playing row marker present); switched panels via `#gfLeftCue` and back with no error; closed the gatefold, unsealed Album B the same way (confirming its own sealed sheet and tracklist, then that its track started playing); reopened Album A's gatefold while Album B's track was the one actually playing (confirming no now-playing row inside it, since it's a different album, and the correct title); then tapped a track row inside it to trigger the real gatefold-to-player hand-off (shell/handoff.js's `runGatefoldHandoff`) — confirmed the hand-off ghost ended hidden, the real disc ended visible, and the correct track from Album A started playing. Finally confirmed Escape closes the gatefold (`main.js`'s own keydown handler, calling `isGatefoldOpen`/`closeGatefold` from the real module now). ### Step 7f: wiring — `main.js` becomes a boot file

No behavior change — a move is a move, per the refactor rules above. Sixth of step 7's seven PRs (see the "Revised extraction order" note above, item 5) — builds on 7a/7b/7c/7d/7e. **Step 7 is now complete except 7g.**

- **The design question this step had to answer first, since it's the one step 7 PR with no file-list table in the plan**: "wiring, split by area." Before writing anything, every single function the ~430-line wiring IIFE called was checked against the real exports of every module steps 3-7e had already produced — and every one of them was already a real, existing export of its owning module. That single fact settled the shape: rather than inventing new exports on ~20 existing feature modules just to give them their own "wire myself" method, this step adds one brand-new top-level directory, `src/wiring/`, with 13 small files, each exporting one `wireX()` function that does nothing but attach event listeners by calling functions that already exist. Zero changes were needed to any module outside `main.js` and the 13 new files themselves — the lowest-risk shape available for this step, and the one that keeps rule 8's module list at zero (none of the 13 import from `main.js`; `main.js` imports from all 13, one-directional, exactly as a boot file should).
- The 13 files — `transport.js`, `library-view.js`, `search.js`, `import.js`, `library-management.js`, `gatefold.js`, `sundown.js`, `character.js`, `edit.js`, `account.js`, `share.js`, `overlays.js`, `routes.js` — are described in full in the `src/wiring/` repo-layout entry above, including the two deliberate groupings (`account.js` combining profile-picture + social sign-in/nav since both are small "identity" concerns; `routes.js` kept separate from `library-view.js` since top-level route switching isn't a library-screen concern) and why the global Escape-key handler lives in `gatefold.js` (it closes the gatefold and sealed sheet first, in priority order, before the add menu or search field).
- `main.js` itself shrank from the ~10,100-line file this engagement started with to ~145 lines: the trimmed import block (just what `restoreLibrary()` and the boot sequence itself need, plus the 13 `wireX` imports), the boot sequence (`renderSearchChips()`, the 13 `wireX()` calls in the same order the original IIFE ran their bodies in, `setMode('vinyl')`/`fallbackSun()`/`askLocation()`/`drawSun()`/`updateChromeInset()` plus its resize listener, `requestAnimationFrame(frame)`, `restoreLibrary()`, and the launch-intro gate), and `restoreLibrary()` itself — unchanged, since it was already a top-level function in the original file, not part of the wiring IIFE.
- **One pre-existing dead import, found and removed as "obviously safe" cleanup per refactor rule 10** while rewriting `main.js`'s import block: `persistAlbumLookPref` (from `history/sessions.js`) had no remaining call site anywhere in `main.js`'s own code — confirmed via `grep -c "persistAlbumLookPref\b" src/main.js` returning `1` (only the import line itself) against the pre-7f file, so this was already dead before this step's own changes, not something 7f introduced. Dropped from the import line, no other change.
- No `await` was added, removed or reordered anywhere in the moved wiring code or in `restoreLibrary()` — confirmed two ways: (1) a full-content diff of the original wiring IIFE's body against the concatenation of all 13 new files' function bodies, after fully dedenting (`.strip()`-ing) both sides and comparing them as multisets of stripped lines (a naive partial-dedent comparison produces a flood of false positives, since the code moved from inside an IIFE's indentation context to inside 13 separate top-level functions) — exact match, 386 lines each side, zero missing, zero extra, once the two pieces deliberately *not* moved to any wiring file (the `renderSearchChips()` call and the short boot-sequence tail, both staying in `main.js` by design) were excluded from the comparison; (2) a separate line-anchored diff confirming the retained boot-sequence lines and `restoreLibrary()` are otherwise byte-for-byte identical to the original (the only differences being the 2-space de-indentation that naturally follows removing the old `(function init(){...})()` wrapper — no content changed).
- No test file needed a mock-chain fix this step, unlike every prior extraction: `main.js` already had zero entries on any test's mock lists as of step 7e (rule 8's list was already empty), and none of the 13 new `wiring/*.js` files are imported by any test — they're pure DOM-wiring glue with no pure core to test, the same "not much here is pure" reasoning step 7b's prompt used for the shell's own choreography.
- Preview verification (Playwright, both built targets) ran one consolidated script exercising representative actions across essentially every wiring area: booted the app with no console/page error, imported two WAV tracks (`import.js`), played/paused/shuffled/cycled-repeat/skipped next and previous (`transport.js`), switched to the Songs tab and opened/closed select mode (`library-view.js`), opened the header search field and typed a query (`search.js`), toggled Sundown's auto/manual row (`sundown.js`), switched Pure/Vinyl, applied a Character preset, and toggled Scratch (`character.js`), opened the edit sheet from the player (`edit.js`), opened/closed the queue sheet (`overlays.js`), opened the Now Playing share sheet and confirmed a canvas rendered (`share.js`), opened a crate record via keyboard and confirmed the gatefold or sealed sheet opened, then closed it with Escape (`gatefold.js`), opened and closed the clear-history overlay without triggering it (`library-management.js`), and switched to the profile tab and back via the tab bar (`routes.js`). The two genuinely destructive actions (Forget Library, Clear All History) were deliberately not triggered, only their overlay open/close wiring. Zero non-baseline console/page errors on either built target.
- Verified: `npm run test:unit` passes (253/253, unchanged — this step added no new test file, since there was nothing new and pure to test); `npm run lint` passes with 0 errors, 93 warnings — unchanged from the pre-7f baseline (the three `catch(err)` warnings now living in the new wiring files are the exact same three that existed in the original `main.js`, just relocated, not new warnings); both builds succeed (`npm run build` and `MIZIKI_BASE=/ npm run build`, 114 modules transformed); the Playwright script above ran clean against both built targets.

### Step 7g: lint-warnings cleanup, then `npm run lint` blocks on warnings too

Almost entirely a move — the actual rename of every deliberately-unused identifier to a leading-underscore name is a cosmetic, behavior-preserving change, per refactor rule 1's own "no behavior change" bar read literally (not a line moved, but nothing a user, or any test, can observe differently) — **except for one place where the mechanical rename briefly changed real behavior, caught and fixed before it shipped; see below.** Last of step 7's seven PRs (see the "Revised extraction order" note above, item 5) — builds on 7a/7b/7c/7d/7e/7f. **Step 7 is now fully complete.**

- The 93 warnings outstanding at the end of 7f (`npm run lint`'s own count, unchanged start-to-finish of steps 6a through 7f) fell into four groups, found by running ESLint with JSON output and categorizing every message by rule and by what kind of declaration it flagged:
  1. **~80 deliberately-ignored `catch` bindings** (`catch(e){}`, `catch(err){}`, two already-`catch(_)`) — by far the largest group, spread across almost every `src/` file, each one the same "bad input, truncated bytes, a storage write that failed, a browser API that isn't there — leave whatever was already true and move on" pattern this file's own "Also fixed in this PR" note under step 6a already named as the reason these were left alone back then. `eslint.config.js`'s `no-unused-vars` rule had `argsIgnorePattern`/`varsIgnorePattern` (both `^_`) but no `caughtErrorsIgnorePattern` — so a caught error could never be silenced by naming convention at all, args/vars-style, until this step added one. Fixed by adding `caughtErrorsIgnorePattern: '^_'` to the rule config and renaming every one of these bindings to a leading-underscore name (`catch(e)` → `catch(_e)`, `catch(err)` → `catch(_err)`); the two already named `_` needed no rename at all, since `_` already matches `^_`.
  2. **A handful of unused function parameters kept for a shared call signature** — `library/duplicate-scan.js`'s `gi`, `library/search.js`'s `mode`, `profile/storefront.js`'s `host`, and five callback parameters across `tests/unit/credits.test.js`/`search.test.js` (`i`, `label`, `sub`, `idx`, `onTap`) — each one named in this file's own step 6a "left as warnings" note as "kept for a shared call signature," not dead code to remove. Renamed to `_gi`/`_mode`/`_host`/`_i`/etc., the same convention as group 1 — the parameter stays (callers elsewhere in the same file, or the mock's own call shape, need the position), only its name changes.
  3. **Four unused local declarations in `src/miziki-social.js`** (`sleep`, `clone`, a destructured `fp`, and the function `spinKey`) — named in step 6a's own note as "left untouched... predates this engagement," not touched there since a safety-net PR wasn't the place for even a cosmetic rename across the social client's one source-of-truth file. Renamed the same way (`_sleep`, `_clone`, `_spinKey`) now that this step's whole purpose is clearing every warning — **except** the `fp` case, which needed care: it's destructured out of an object (`const { fp, ...rest } = it;`), so a plain textual rename to `{ _fp, ...rest }` would have looked up a property literally named `_fp` on `it` instead of `fp` — a real behavior change (`rest` would then still carry the real `fp` field, since nothing stripped it). Caught in review before this PR's own verification pass, before any test or build ran against it, and fixed as `{ fp: _fp, ...rest }` — destructures the real `fp` property, same as before, just binds it to an ignorable local name. Mentioned here, not buried, because it's the one place in this step where a "pure rename" tool briefly wasn't one.
  4. **One genuinely dead import**: `src/main.js`'s `current` (from `state.js`) — unlike every name in groups 1-3, nothing in `main.js`'s own remaining code reads it at all (confirmed by grepping the file), so prefixing it would have hidden dead code rather than explained deliberately-unused code. Removed from the import line outright, the same "obviously safe" cleanup this file's own history already has precedent for (steps 6a, 7d, 7f).
- Mechanical, scripted application: every one-off rename in groups 1-3 (92 of the 93 original warnings, all but the `current` import) was applied by a small script that read ESLint's own JSON output — file, line, exact column, and the flagged identifier's name — and inserted a single `_` at that exact column in that file, rather than a hand-written regex per file. This is what caught group 3's `fp` case early: the script's job was only ever "insert a character at this exact position," so the destructuring-semantics risk was something to check for afterward (by reading every resulting diff line, not by trusting the mechanism), not something the mechanism itself could have silently gotten wrong across 90+ sites. Confirmed by re-running `npm run lint` after the script (0 warnings before the two fixes below) and then reviewing every changed line in `git diff` by hand — the only two corrections needed were the `fp` destructuring fix above and a cosmetic double-underscore (`catch(__)` instead of `catch(_)`) on the two already-`_`-named bindings, which the script still "fixed" since it ran against ESLint's pre-config-change output; both reverted to the minimal, correct form before committing.
- Added `caughtErrorsIgnorePattern: '^_'` to refactor rule 10's own description of the lint rule set, and changed `npm run lint` from `eslint .` to `eslint . --max-warnings=0` in `package.json` — the one real tooling change in this step, and the step's actual point: from here on, a new unused import/var/arg/catch-binding left behind by a future extraction fails CI immediately, the same way an error already does, rather than silently joining a warning count nobody is required to keep at zero. Sanity-checked by temporarily adding two throwaway unused variables (one `_`-prefixed, one not) to a scratch file: `npm run lint` passed on the first, failed with "ESLint found too many warnings (maximum: 0)" on the second — confirming the flag actually gates, not just that the warning count happens to be zero today — then reverted the scratch change.
