- No `await` was added, removed or reordered anywhere — nothing in this step touches a playback or persistence `await` sequence at all; the only occupied-vs-correct-behavior risk was the `fp` destructuring case above, and it's fixed.
- No new test file: nothing in this step is new, pure, and untested — every change is a rename of something a test (if one exists for that function) already exercises under its original behavior, and the two genuinely consequential files (`transport.js`'s `catch(_e)` renames, `miziki-social.js`'s `fp: _fp` fix) are both covered by existing suites (`tests/unit/*.test.js` for the former; `toWire()` itself is `miziki-social.js`-internal and reached only through `tests/client.test.js`'s Postgres-backed sync tests, not run in this environment, so this one fix leans on the destructuring-semantics argument above — provably equivalent to the original, not just "tests still pass" — rather than a fresh assertion).
- Preview verification (Playwright, both built targets): booted clean, imported two WAV tracks (exercising `library/import.js`'s renamed `catch(_err)`), played/paused/skipped next and previous (`player/transport.js`'s renamed `catch(_e)` in `stop`/`pause`/`seek`), toggled Background playback on and off (`player/background.js`'s/`audio/engine.js`'s renamed `catch(_e)`/`catch(_)` chain in `applyOutputRoute`/`ensureContext`), opened and closed the edit sheet (`library/edit.js`'s renamed `catch(_e)` around art-URL revocation), opened the Now Playing share sheet and confirmed a canvas rendered (`share/*.js`'s several renamed `catch(_e)` blocks around font-loading and art-drawing), and opened/closed select mode (exercising `library/delete.js`'s renamed `catch(_e)`). Zero non-baseline console/page errors on either built target.
- Verified: `npm run test:unit` passes (253/253, unchanged); `npm run lint` passes with 0 errors, 0 warnings (down from 93 — see above) under the new `--max-warnings=0` flag; both builds succeed (`npm run build` and `MIZIKI_BASE=/ npm run build`, 114 modules transformed, identical bundle sizes to the pre-7g build, confirming nothing behavior-affecting changed); the Playwright script above ran clean against both built targets.

### After step 7g

Step 7 — the entire `main.js`-splitting engagement, steps 2 through 7 — is complete. `main.js` is a short boot file; every module under `src/` passes `npm run lint` with zero errors and zero warnings; `npm run lint` itself now fails on any new one. Account backup work (the long-term plan's next phase, per the top of this file) can start from a clean baseline.

### Step 01 (P1): security hardening

First of the post-refactor hardening/backup plan (see the roadmap README the user pasted in — "01" there, not part of the numbered extraction steps above). An Oct 11 audit found one real bug and three gaps; this PR fixes all four, documented in full in "Security" above — this entry is the how and the verification.

- **Fixed two real tag-injection bugs**, both the same shape: untrusted file-tag text (an artist/title the user doesn't control) concatenated into an `innerHTML` string, which would run as markup if a tag contained something like `<img src=x onerror=…>`. Once sign-in is live (step 04+), that code would run as the signed-in user.
  - `src/library/duplicate-scan.js`'s `renderDupScan()` built a `<div>`'s inner HTML from `title + ' — ' + artist` joined with `'<br>'`. Fixed by extracting a new `duplicateNamesElement(tracks)` — a small, pure-ish DOM builder (no imports beyond the bare `document` global) that appends real text nodes and a real `<br>` element instead, called from `renderDupScan()` the same way.
  - `src/launch/intro.js`'s title-screen code built `#launchTitleSub`'s inner HTML from `targetArtist + '<br><span>LAST PLAYED</span>'`. Fixed the same way, extracted as `renderLaunchTitleSub(subEl, artist)`.
  - Both extractions exist for testability, not because the fix itself needed a new function — a secondary benefit, not the point: `runLaunchSequence`/`renderDupScan` are otherwise expensive-to-mock async choreography, while the extracted builders need nothing but a fake `document.createElement`/`createTextNode`. Same precedent as `gatefoldSideSplits`/`angleDelta`/`computeFlickPosition` — a small decision or rendering function pulled out, `export`ed, for its own unit test.
  - The two remaining `innerHTML` uses in these same files (`'Mi<span>zi</span>ki'`, a static wordmark with no tag data in it) were left exactly as they were — not the bug, and explicitly the shape rule 2 below still allows.
  - Added `tests/unit/duplicate-scan.test.js` (2 tests) and two new tests in `tests/unit/launch.test.js`, each feeding the literal payload `<img src=x onerror=alert(1)>` as a title/artist and asserting (against a tiny fake DOM that keeps text-node data separate from element tags) that it survives as literal text and that no `<img>`-or-other element was ever created from it — a regression here fails by producing *empty* collected text, not by rendering the payload, since the fake DOM has no `innerHTML` parser to misuse in the first place.
- **Added an ESLint rule that makes this bug class impossible to reintroduce silently**: `no-restricted-syntax` (`eslint.config.js`), two selectors — assigning `innerHTML`/`outerHTML` with anything other than a `Literal` string, or calling `insertAdjacentHTML` with a non-literal second argument. Sanity-checked against a scratch file with both a violating and a compliant pattern before relying on it (the violating one errored, the compliant one didn't), then confirmed it's zero-error against the real codebase once the two bugs above were fixed, with the handful of pre-existing static-literal uses (`= ''`, the wordmark) all still passing. Added to `SAFETY_NET_RULES`, so it applies everywhere `no-undef`/`no-unused-vars` already do.
- **Bundled `@supabase/supabase-js`** (exact-pinned `2.117.3`, a real `dependencies` entry in `package.json` — the project's first) instead of loading it from jsDelivr. `main.js` dynamically `import()`s it only when both social keys are non-empty, passed to `MizikiSocial.init()` via its existing `supabaseLib` option; `init()` itself lost its `loadScript()`/CDN-fallback path entirely (a behavior change, deliberately — the whole point was no more unpinned CDN fetch) and now fails closed if no library is available. See "Security" above for the exact before/after bundle-size numbers (today: zero bytes, since Rollup proves the empty-keys branch dead; once configured: a separate ~231 kB/~60 kB-gzip chunk, fetched only then).
- **Self-hosted the three Google Fonts** as `woff2` + `@font-face` (`src/fonts/`, `src/styles/fonts.css`) — see each one's own repo-layout entry above for exactly which files and why only the `latin` subset. Removed the Google Fonts `<link>` and both `preconnect` tags from `miziki.html`.
- **Added the Content Security Policy and `referrer` meta tags** to `miziki.html`'s `<head>` — see "Security" above for the exact policy and the step-04 note about widening `connect-src`/`img-src` once real Supabase keys exist.
- No behavior change beyond the two bug fixes themselves (tag text now renders as text, which is the fix) and the CDN-fallback removal in `miziki-social.js`'s `init()` (dead code today, since keys are empty — the real behavior change is deferred to whenever step 04 actually configures Supabase).
- Verified: `npm run test:unit` passes (257/257 — 253 before this PR + 4 new: `tests/unit/duplicate-scan.test.js` (2 tests) plus 2 new cases in `tests/unit/launch.test.js`); `npm run lint` passes with 0 errors, 0 warnings; both builds succeed (`npm run build` and `MIZIKI_BASE=/ npm run build`). Playwright, both built targets: booted clean; imported tracks (exercising blob: artwork/thumbnail URLs under the CSP's `img-src`/`media-src`); played and scratched a record (a direct `PointerEvent` dispatch past the 8° claim threshold, confirming `AudioWorklet.prototype.addModule` — spied from outside the page — resolved with zero `securitypolicyviolation` events, i.e. `worker-src 'self' blob:` actually covers the scratch processor); rendered a Now Playing share-image canvas; opened and closed the duplicate scan, a crate/gatefold record, and the profile tab (the social "not set up" state). Zero non-baseline console/page errors, zero CSP violations on either target throughout.
- **Not done here, and not Claude's to do**: the GitHub repo settings (Dependabot alerts, secret scanning, push protection, a branch-protection ruleset on `main` requiring the PR-checks workflow to pass) the hardening doc's own §"GitHub settings" section calls for — those are a five-minute github.com task for the repo owner, not a code change.

### Step 02 (F3): "Forget library" removes songs, never stats

Second of the post-refactor roadmap's numbered documents (see the roadmap README the user pasted in). A feature PR — it changes behavior on purpose, per F3's own framing: **"Forget library" removes your songs from Miziki and keeps your listening stats.**

- **The old behavior (step 5d) cleared the `tracks`, `meta`, `sessions`, `overlays` and `artwork` stores wholesale** — erasing play counts, sessions, metals, sealed state, playlists, edits and settings along with the library itself, keeping only achievements/Collection thumbnails via a manual copy-out-then-back-in dance across the `meta.clear()`. It also left several in-memory stats fields un-reset, which step 5d itself had already flagged as a latent write-back risk: a later save could write part of that half-cleared state back over whatever the (also-cleared) DB no longer had.
- **New behavior**: `forgetLibrary()` (`src/library/delete.js`) now clears only the `tracks` store, this run's crate-art tiers (`deleteCrateArtTiers`, per album), and thumbnails nothing else still references (`pruneThumbs()`, the exact mechanism `history/clear.js` already used for the same purpose — reused here, not reinvented). `meta`, `sessions`, `overlays`, `achievements` and `collection` are never touched. In memory, only the fields tied to the now-gone tracks are reset (`S.tracks`, `S.index`, `S.queue`/`S.baseQueue`/`S.qpos`, the decoded-audio cache `S.lru`, `S.curPlayed`/`S.curTrackDone`, the shell/platter state) — every stats field, every setting, playlists, album order/sort and `S.sealed` are left exactly as they were, which is both the fix for the write-back risk above (nothing resets, so there's nothing stale to write back) and the mechanism for reconnect (see below). "Clear listening history" (`history/clear.js`) stays the one place that resets stats — the two actions now mean clearly different things, matching the F3 doc's own framing.
- **Playlists and manual album order needed a representation change to support this.** Before this step, `S.playlists[].items` and `S.albumOrder[album]` held track *indices*, valid only for the session's current `S.tracks` shape — `saveMeta()`/`restoreMeta()` (`src/storage/meta.js`) converted them to/from ids at the storage boundary, and `restoreMeta()` silently dropped any entry whose id no longer resolved (`idxOf(id) < 0`) — the exact mechanism of the step 5d regression this doc's own framing calls out by name. Both now hold track **ids** everywhere, in memory and in storage alike: `saveMeta()`/`restoreMeta()` write/read them straight through with no conversion or dropping at all; `src/library/model.js`'s `albumTracks()` resolves `S.albumOrder[album]`'s ids to indices for display (filtered to the album's current membership) without ever removing an id that doesn't currently resolve; `src/library/favorites.js`'s `isFavorite`/`toggleFavorite` read/write `pl.items` by id directly, with no index lookup left in the module at all; and `src/library/view.js`'s playlist rendering, picking-mode "already picked" check, and both it and `renderAlbumDetail`'s manual-reorder buttons (`moverCtl`) all resolve ids to indices for what's shown, moving within the *full* id-based order (not just the currently-visible rows) so a hidden, not-yet-reconnected entry elsewhere in the same list keeps its own relative place. `src/library/delete.js`'s `deleteTracks()` — explicit, intentional removal, unlike Forget — now just `.filter()`s the deleted ids out of `S.playlists`/`S.albumOrder` directly, replacing the old snapshot-to-ids-then-rebuild-to-surviving-indices dance the index-based representation used to require.
- **Result: a forgotten track's playlist membership, manual order, favorite status, edits (`overlays`) and custom cover (`artwork`) all wait exactly where they are and reconnect automatically** the moment a file with the same id (`name|size|lastModified`) is imported again — `library/import.js`'s existing `loadOverlayFor(t)` call, already run for every newly-added track, is what re-applies the overlay/custom-art without any new code. A *partial* re-add (only some of a playlist's tracks come back) shows just the reconnected ones; the rest stay recorded, hidden, not dropped, exactly as the F3 doc's own test list describes.
- **Copy updated** to match: the Library tools "Forget library" row (`miziki.html`) now reads *"Removes every song from Miziki. Your stats, playlists and settings stay, and reconnect if you add the songs again. Files on your device aren't touched — to reset stats instead, use Clear listening history above."* There's no separate confirm *dialog* for Forget — the two-tap arm/confirm button (`#forget`, `src/wiring/library-management.js`) already serves as its own confirmation, unchanged by this step.
- **A real, incidental bug found and fixed along the way, not part of F3's own scope but caught during this step's own Playwright verification**: step 01's Content Security Policy shipped `connect-src 'self'` with no `blob:` — but `src/crate/tiers.js`'s `buildCrateTier()` and `src/library/thumbs.js`'s `makeThumb()` both `fetch()` a track's own `blob:` object URL to re-read its embedded cover art (or a user's custom cover) before resizing it. Every one of those fetches was being silently blocked by the browser and swallowed by its own `catch(_e){return false}`, so crate art and thumbnails never actually built for any file with embedded artwork, a regression invisible in step 01's own verification since its fixture tracks carried no embedded art. Fixed by adding `blob:` to `connect-src` in `miziki.html`'s CSP meta tag (see "Security" above) — caught and fixed before this PR merged, folded into this same PR rather than split out, since it was found mid-verification of this very step.
- No `await` was added, removed or reordered anywhere in `deleteTracks`/`invalidateActiveSessionIfAny`/`persistSessionCounts`/`loadOverlayFor` or any other touched function beyond what the behavior change itself required (the removed `meta.clear()`/`sessions.clear()`/`overlays.clear()`/`artwork.clear()` calls in `forgetLibrary()`, and the simplified id-filter logic in `deleteTracks()`, replacing the old id↔index snapshot/rebuild).
- Added `tests/unit/forget-library.test.js` (5 tests) — see the `tests/unit/` entry above for exactly what it covers and how it's mocked. `library-model.test.js`, `favorites.test.js`, `meta.test.js`, `delete.test.js`, `search.test.js` and `tags.test.js` all needed updates for the id-not-index representation change — see that same entry for the specifics; `delete.test.js`'s own two `forgetLibrary` tests moved to the new file outright, since the behavior they checked (whole-store clearing) no longer exists.
- Preview verification (Playwright, both built targets) used three WAV fixtures sharing an album tag, all with a fixed, pinned `mtime` (`fs.utimesSync`) so a later "re-import the same file" step could feed a byte-identical copy at a *different* OS path and reliably get a `change` event — Chromium's `setInputFiles` does not reliably re-fire `change` for the literal same path a second time, a known headless-testing quirk unrelated to the app, not a regression; the computed track id (`name|size|lastModified`) matches either way, which is what the app itself actually keys reconnect on. The script: imported all three, created a playlist with all three, favorited one track and edited another's title plus set a custom cover (both from the Songs tab, not from inside the playlist's own detail view — see the next paragraph), ran Forget library from Profile → Library tools (two-tap), confirmed the library emptied, re-imported the identical three files from the copied paths, and confirmed the playlist showed all three back in their original order (the edited title included), the Favorites playlist showed the favorited track, and the custom cover's `<img>` was present on the reconnected row. Zero non-baseline console/page errors on either built target, confirming the CSP fix above actually resolved the blob: fetch failures.
- **A second, genuine but out-of-scope bug found during this same verification pass, left unfixed and flagged here rather than silently worked around**: `src/library/favorites.js`'s `ensureFavoritesPlaylist()` creates the system Favorites playlist, the first time it's ever needed, via `S.playlists.unshift(pl)` — but `S.view.playlist` (which playlist is currently open, in `src/library/view.js`) is a plain *index* into `S.playlists`, not an id or a reference. Favoriting a track for the first time while already viewing a *different* playlist's own detail screen shifts every existing playlist's index by one, so `S.view.playlist` ends up pointing at the wrong playlist (the newly-created Favorites, not whatever was actually open) until the user navigates away and back. This predates step 02 entirely (both pieces — `ensureFavoritesPlaylist`'s `unshift`, and `S.view.playlist`'s index-based addressing — existed before this step touched either file) and isn't something F3 asked to fix; the verification script above works around it simply by favoriting/editing from the Songs tab rather than from inside the Trial Playlist's own view, which is also the more common real-world path. Worth a small follow-up at some point (either stop unshifting, or give `S.view.playlist` a different kind of handle), but not this PR's job.
- Verified: `npm run test:unit` passes (260/260 — 257 before this step + 5 new in `forget-library.test.js`, net of 2 removed from `delete.test.js` (257 + 5 − 2 = 260) — see the `tests/unit/` entry above for exactly what changed in each existing file); `npm run lint` passes with 0 errors, 0 warnings; both builds succeed (`npm run build` and `MIZIKI_BASE=/ npm run build`); the Playwright script above ran clean against both built targets once the CSP fix landed.

### Step 03 (B2): backup merge logic

Third of the post-refactor roadmap's numbered documents (see the roadmap README the user pasted in). Pure logic only, per that doc's own framing — no Supabase, no UI, no network, so **no iPhone testing is needed** this step, and the app is unchanged: `main.js`/`miziki.html` aren't touched at all.

- Added `src/sync/merge.js` and `src/sync/backup-doc.js` — see their own repo-layout entries above for the full export list and the reasoning behind each merge rule. In short: `merge.js` holds one small, separately-tested primitive per row of the key-inventory table above (sum/max/union/last-write-wins/history-clear-gate), composed by `mergeRows(rows)`; `backup-doc.js` holds the doc format (`SCHEMA_VERSION`, `toDoc`/`fromDocs`) and the one real piece of data-shape work this step does — converting `playlists`/`albumOrder` items and `overlays` between local file ids (`name|size|lastModified`, meaningless on another device) and the portable `trackIdentityKey`/`albumKey` the app already uses for duplicate detection and play counts.
- **The key-inventory table** (added to "How the app is put together" above, with the new "every new saved key must be added" rule) is this PR's actual first deliverable, per the doc's own instruction to do that *before* writing any merge code — every `meta` key and every store got checked against `src/storage/repo.js`, `src/history/`, `src/library/sealed.js`/`thumbs.js`/`favorites.js`, `src/profile/screens.js` and `src/state.js`'s defaults, not assumed from memory.
- **Three implementation decisions the doc left to this PR's own judgment, each made and explained in the table/repo-layout entry rather than guessed at silently:**
  1. **The `sessions` store (per-album in-progress play-through) is device-only, excluded from backup entirely** — not because it doesn't matter, but because it's resume state tied to one device's own physical timeline (a 4-hour gap, an in-order `tracksCompleted` list), and the only thing that's supposed to survive a session long-term — the completed-session count — is already `sessionCounts`, a backed-up, per-device-summed counter. Merging two devices' *in-progress* sessions could actually corrupt the 95%/in-order completion rule itself (whose `tracksCompleted`/`expectedOrder` would even apply?), so this is the one row where "read the spec, then decide" (the doc's own words) came out to "don't back this up at all."
  2. **Sealed records need a signal the local app doesn't keep yet.** `S.sealed` only ever records "still sealed" — once `breakSeal()` removes an id, there's no durable trace that it was *ever* sealed, so a backup doc built from `S.sealed` alone can't tell "broken on this device" apart from "this device never had the album." The doc's own `sealedAlbums`/`brokenSealIds` split in the key-inventory table is this PR's fix: `brokenSealIds` is new local state (not built yet — flagged in the table's own "doesn't exist locally yet" list) that a later wiring PR must start keeping, the same way `historyClearedAt` and per-field write timestamps are flagged there too.
  3. **"Last write wins" is per *field*, not per whole doc.** Each of `playlists`/`albumOrder`/`albumSort`/`albumDisplay`/`trackDisplay`/`albumEdge`/`albumLookPref`/`prefs`/profile name carries its own `{at, value}` in the doc (`backup-doc.js`'s `lw()` helper), and `merge.js`'s `lastWriteWins()` picks a winner field by field. A single whole-doc timestamp was the simpler option, but it would mean any device's later, completely unrelated change (say, a crate-anchor update) silently overwrites another device's earlier-but-still-newer-than-last-sync playlist edit just because its *doc* happens to be newer overall — exactly the kind of loss this PR exists to prevent. Tested directly (`backup-merge.test.js`'s "an unrelated, later change to one field never clobbers an earlier change to another field").
- **Counters without double counting (point 3)**: `fromDocs(ownLocal, remoteRows)` always rebuilds this device's own doc fresh from `ownLocal` via `toDoc()`, rather than trusting a possibly-stale copy of itself that might be sitting in `remoteRows` — any row sharing `ownLocal`'s own `deviceId` is dropped before merging, and the remaining rows are de-duplicated by `deviceId` too (a cheap safeguard against a server-side duplicate, not something B1's "one row per device" should ever produce, but free to guard against). `mergeRows()` itself only ever sums/maxes/unions across rows, so calling it repeatedly, or feeding it the same rows in a different order, never changes a total. The result's `totals.*` are the cross-device grand totals for *display only* (Top Songs, metal thresholds, Collection progress) — the comments on both `fromDocs` and `mergeRows` say explicitly that a caller must never write these back into `S.trackPlayCounts`/`S.sessionCounts`/etc., which have to keep holding only this device's own contribution, or the next upload would double-count what's already synced.
- **"Clear listening history" wins everywhere (point 4)**: `computeHistoryClearedAt`/`freshRows` in `merge.js` compute the newest clear across every row and exclude any row whose own `historyClearedAt` predates it from the counters/max-timestamps/`rareUnlocked`/`achievements`/`collection` merges — but never from `lastWrite`/`sealed`/`overlays`, none of which are "history" (sealed state is deliberately independent of listening history locally too, per SEALED spec §2). `fromDocs`'s `ownReset` flag tells the caller when *this* device is the stale one, so it knows to zero its own local counters and adopt the newer clear before its next upload — otherwise that device's next doc would still carry pre-clear numbers that every other device has, from that point on, started ignoring anyway.
- **Portability (point 2)**: `toDoc()` converts a playlist/`albumOrder` item's local id to its `trackIdentityKey` whenever the referenced track is currently present; an id that doesn't resolve (forgotten but kept, per F3) is kept as the bare local id rather than dropped — not portable to another device, but it still round-trips on the *same* device after a reinstall, the same file-identity match F3 itself already relies on. An overlay with no resolvable identity key is skipped outright (unlike a playlist item, an overlay can never be reapplied without knowing which track it belongs to, so there's nothing useful to preserve). `resolveIdentityKey(key, tracks)` is the one piece of forward-looking plumbing this PR adds for the *next* PR to use: it isn't called anywhere in `fromDocs` itself, since resolving a pending identity key to a real local id has to happen at apply time, against whatever's actually in the library then — a resolution computed once, at merge time, would go stale the moment the next file is imported.
- **A versioned doc format (point 5)**: `SCHEMA_VERSION = 1`, stamped by `toDoc()`. Preserving a future version's unknown top-level fields across a re-upload is the uploader's job, not `toDoc`'s — a later PR fetches this device's existing row, calls `toDoc(localState)` for the fields this version knows about, and shallow-merges over the existing row before uploading, so nothing this version doesn't recognize is ever dropped. `toDoc`/`fromDocs`/`mergeRows` are all defensive about missing/malformed input by construction (plain `|| {}`/`|| []` fallbacks throughout, no field ever assumed present) — tested directly in `backup-merge.test.js`'s robustness cases.
- Added `tests/unit/backup-merge.test.js` (22 tests) — see the `tests/unit/` entry above for the full list. No existing test file needed any mock-chain fix: nothing in the suite previously touched `src/sync/`, and neither new module imports from `main.js` or anything DOM-dependent.
- Verified: `npm run test:unit` passes (282/282 — 260 before this step + 22 new); `npm run lint` passes with 0 errors, 0 warnings; no build or Playwright verification this step, per the doc's own "no visible change" framing — `main.js`/`miziki.html` have zero diff.

### Step 04: Supabase project setup, then the keys PR

Fourth of the post-refactor roadmap's numbered documents. Two parts, per that doc: Ken set up the real Supabase project by hand (`docs/SUPABASE_SETUP.md`, already merged) and applied migrations 001–004; this PR is the small part that connects the app to it. **This is the step where the social layer stops being inert** — everything 01–03 built (bundled client, the tag-injection/CSP hardening, the backup merge logic) existed in anticipation of this moment.

- `src/state.js`: `SOCIAL_SUPABASE_URL`/`SOCIAL_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY` now hold the real project URL and its publishable key (`sb_publishable_…`), not the legacy JWT-shaped anon key Supabase is retiring at the end of 2026 — the comment above both was rewritten to say so, and to repeat (CLAUDE.md's own long-standing rule) that the **secret key** must never go here or in this repo at all.
- `miziki.html`'s CSP: added the project's `https://<ref>.supabase.co` to both `connect-src` and `img-src`, and `wss://<ref>.supabase.co` to `connect-src` — exactly the three additions step 01's own CSP comment and CLAUDE.md's "Security" section had already flagged as needed once real keys landed. Nothing else in the policy changed.
- **Verified the bundled client activates correctly, not via a CDN, with zero CSP violations** — both via the unit/build checks below and a dedicated Playwright pass against the Netlify-base preview:
  1. Confirmed the lazy `index-*.js` chunk (supabase-js, ~231 kB/~60 kB gzip — exactly the size step 01's own note predicted "once configured") is what gets requested once the profile/social route needs it, and that zero requests ever go to jsDelivr/unpkg or any other CDN.
  2. Confirmed zero `Content-Security-Policy`/"Refused to connect" console messages while loading the app and opening the social route.
  3. **A direct CSP check, not just an absence of errors**: from inside the page, attempted a real `fetch()` to `https://<ref>.supabase.co/auth/v1/health` and a real `WebSocket` to `wss://<ref>.supabase.co/realtime/v1/websocket` — both reached the network layer with zero CSP violations (they failed only on this sandbox's own outbound-proxy restriction for that host, `ERR_TUNNEL_CONNECTION_FAILED`, an environment limitation having nothing to do with the app). A control fetch to a host deliberately *not* in `connect-src` (`https://example.com`) produced the real thing for contrast: two explicit "Refused to connect... Content Security Policy" console messages — confirming the harness can tell the difference, and that the Supabase origins really are allowed, not just untested.
- Updated `docs/SUPABASE_SETUP.md` step 5: marked the keys as now in place for this project, and added a note that repeating this setup for a different project also needs the CSP widening (with a pointer to CLAUDE.md's "Security" section for the exact policy) — the checklist itself hadn't mentioned the CSP step at all before this PR.
- No behavior change to anything *except* the social layer itself switching from inert to live — no refactor, no new module, no touched test file. `main.js` is untouched; the only files this PR changes are `src/state.js`, `miziki.html` and the two docs noted above.
- Verified: `npm run test:unit` passes (282/282, unchanged — nothing here is covered by a unit test, since both changes are literal config values and a meta tag); `npm run lint` passes with 0 errors, 0 warnings; both builds succeed (`npm run build` and `MIZIKI_BASE=/ npm run build`), each confirming the separate ~231 kB supabase-js chunk; the Playwright script above ran clean against the Netlify-base preview — zero CSP violations, zero non-baseline console/page errors.
- **Not done here, per the doc's own "after-merge check"**: Ken doesn't sign in on the real GitHub Pages app yet — that's deferred until step 06 merges, so the first real sign-in also creates the first backup rather than an account with nothing behind it yet. The Netlify preview is where the real sign-in flow (6-digit code, handle claim) gets tried first, by Ken, by hand — not something this PR's own automated verification does, since it would mean sending a real email through the live project.

### Step 05 (B1): account backup table, reserved handles, account deletion — server side only

Fifth of the post-refactor roadmap's numbered documents. A server-side-only feature PR, same shape as step 03's B2: nothing in `main.js`/`miziki.html`/`src/` (outside `src/miziki-social.js`'s own small addition) changes — the deliverable is `supabase/migrations/005_backup.sql` plus the test coverage proving it does what it claims. **No iPhone testing is needed this step**, same reasoning as step 03. **This PR must not be merged until Ken applies `005_backup.sql` to the live Supabase project's SQL editor and re-runs the Security Advisor scan** — see "Before merging (Ken)" at the end of this entry; everything else below is already verified against a local Postgres instance.

- **`public.library_backup`**: one row per `(user_id, device_id)` — `device_label` (≤60 chars), `schema_version`, `doc jsonb` (≤2MB, enforced by a `check` on `octet_length`), `updated_at` (via the existing `touch_updated_at()` trigger). Owner-only RLS on all four operations (`user_id = auth.uid()`), `revoke all ... from anon`. The server never reads inside `doc` — every merge rule from step 03's key-inventory table is enforced entirely client-side, in `src/sync/merge.js`/`backup-doc.js`, and (since step 06, B3) actually called from `src/sync/backup.js`. See "Backup: what gets synced" above, and "What's stored server-side, and who can see it" below, for the full privacy-rule writeup.
- **Reserved handles**: a `before insert or update of handle` trigger on `public.profiles` (`profiles_handle_not_reserved`) rejects `admin`, `administrator`, `support`, `help`, `miziki`, `official`, `security`, `moderator`, `mod`, `staff`, `team`, `root`, `system`, `null`, `undefined` (case-insensitive) with `'That handle is reserved'`. A trigger, not a validated `check` constraint, specifically so only *future* writes are checked — nothing already claimed before this migration is retroactively affected, per the doc's own explicit requirement. `src/miziki-social.js` got a matching `RESERVED_HANDLES` `Set`, wired into `checkHandle` (returns `{ok:true, available:false, reason:'reserved'}`), `createProfile` and `updateProfile` (both: a client-side pre-check plus, as a backstop, catching the server's own `P0001` error code the trigger raises) — the client check is the fast, offline path; the trigger is the real enforcement, since nothing stops a request from skipping the client entirely. The trigger function itself has `set search_path = ''` (the same fix as `touch_updated_at()` below) and `execute` revoked from `public`/`anon`/`authenticated` — a trigger function is invoked by the system, not called directly by any role, so no role ever needs to execute it.
- **`public.delete_my_account()`**: a `security definer` RPC, `set search_path = ''`, that deletes the caller's own `auth.users` row (`where id = auth.uid()`, raising if not signed in) and relies entirely on existing `on delete cascade` foreign keys — `profiles` and everything that cascades from it (`follows`, `blocks`, `crate_items`, `verified_stats`, `now_spinning`, `digging_list`, `feed_events`, `reports`), plus `library_backup`'s own cascade — to remove every other row the account ever wrote. Owned by `postgres` (the role every migration runs as), which already has the grants a hosted Supabase project needs to delete from `auth.users` directly — the standard, documented shape for in-app account deletion. Irreversible; can only ever delete the caller's own account, never anyone else's, since there's no parameter to target a different id. Nothing in the app calls this yet — wiring a "delete my account" button into Settings is a future PR; this migration exists so that PR has an RPC to call.
- **Security Advisor fixes (Oct 12 scan)** — see the new "Security" section bullet above for the full writeup: moved `is_blocked_between`/`item_visibility`/`effective_visibility`/`can_view` into a new, API-invisible `private` schema (RLS policies keep working unchanged, since they bind by OID not name; every function *body* that called one of the four by name — `can_view` itself, `follows_before_insert`, `crate_items_emit_events`, `verified_stats_emit_events`, `set_now_spinning`, `digging_page` — was re-declared with the `private.`-qualified reference); fixed `releases_insert`'s `with check (true)` by adding `releases.created_by default auth.uid()` and changing the policy to `with check (created_by = auth.uid())`; fixed `touch_updated_at()`'s mutable `search_path` with `set search_path = ''`. "Leaked password protection" doesn't apply — no passwords, only emailed one-time codes.
- **No realtime publication changes**: `library_backup` is private, owner-only data, not a social/live-presence signal like `now_spinning` — it was never added to the realtime publication, and the migration's own closing comment says so explicitly rather than leaving it unaddressed.
- Extended `tests/client.test.js` with 14 new tests (36 total, up from 22): `library_backup`'s own CRUD + cross-user isolation + size-limit rejection (5 tests, using new `asUser`/`asAnon` helpers — `set role` plus `set_config('request.jwt.claim.sub', ...)` inside one `psql` invocation, the same "read the last line of multi-statement `-At` output" convention the existing `sb.rpc()` helper already uses); reserved handles rejected on both insert and update, with existing rows (`ken`) confirmed untouched (2 tests); the four moved functions now resolve in `private` not `public` via a `pg_proc`/`pg_namespace` lookup, `anon` gets a real `permission denied` calling any of them directly, `authenticated` keeps direct execute (3 tests — the most honest set of signals available in a harness with no real PostgREST layer in front of it: a real Postgres permission check for `anon` is the exact mechanism production relies on; the schema-location check is the honest stand-in for "not exposed via the Data API," since there's no PostgREST proxy here to prove that against directly); `releases.created_by` attribution, both the happy path (`sync_crate` as the real caller) and the rejected spoofing attempt (3 tests); `delete_my_account` removing the caller's `auth.users`/`profiles`/`follows`/`library_backup`/`crate_items` rows while leaving every other user's rows untouched, and confirming it can only ever target the caller's own account (2 tests, the first using a disposable fixture user with its own `crate_items` row — added a `releases` row and a `crate_items` row for that user specifically so the test's own name, "removes the caller's auth user, profile, crate, follows and backup rows," is actually true of what it checks, not just the stats/profile/follows/backup subset).
- **Bootstrapped a local Postgres 16 instance for this session** (`initdb`/`pg_ctl` under the `postgres` system user, matching `tests/client.test.js`'s own `PGHOST=/var/tmp/pgmz`/`PGPORT=5544` defaults) — the first time in this entire engagement that `npm test` (as opposed to `npm run test:unit`) was actually run, since no step before this one needed a real Postgres. **Lesson for future verification**: `tests/client.test.js` is a stateful script against a persistent database with no automatic reset — recreate it fresh (`dropdb && createdb`, then reapply `tests/supabase_stub.sql` and every migration in order) before trusting any run; re-running against a database a previous run already mutated produces false-looking failures from leftover rows, not real regressions (caught and confirmed harmless once during this step's own verification).
- Verified: `npm test` passes (36/36, against a freshly recreated database with all five migrations applied in order); `npm run test:unit` passes (282/282, unchanged — nothing in `src/` outside `miziki-social.js`'s reserved-handle check changed, and no unit test touches that file); `npm run lint` passes with 0 errors, 0 warnings. No build or Playwright verification this step, same reasoning as step 03 — this is server-side schema and test coverage only; `main.js`/`miziki.html` have zero diff.

**Before merging (Ken):**
1. Apply `supabase/migrations/005_backup.sql` to the live project via the SQL editor (`docs/SUPABASE_SETUP.md`'s own step-by-step, now with a dedicated section for this migration).
2. Re-run the Security Advisor scan and confirm the four `security definer`-in-`public` findings this migration fixes are gone.
3. Only then merge this PR.

### Step 06 (B3): backup in the app — sign-in, sync, Account and Privacy screens

Sixth of the post-refactor roadmap's numbered documents. The first app-side PR to actually read from and write to `library_backup` (migration 005, step 05) — everything steps 01–05 built (the bundled client, the tag-injection/CSP hardening, the pure merge logic, the Supabase project itself, the empty table) existed in anticipation of this moment, the same way step 04 was "the social layer stops being inert." This one changes real app behavior (new screens, a background sync loop) and touches real server data for a signed-in user, so — per the task's own instruction — **this PR must not be merged** until Ken has verified it by hand; the full "Preview focus (Ken)" list is at the end of this entry.

- **The sync engine, `src/sync/backup.js`** (new) — see its own repo-layout entry above for the full export list and the two deliberate "never import this" rules it follows. In short: `scheduleBackupSync()` debounces ~30s (piggybacking `storage/meta.js`'s existing `queueSave` cadence rather than inventing a second one), `flushBackupSync()` pushes immediately (called from `src/wiring/lifecycle.js`'s new `visibilitychange`/`pagehide` handlers on backgrounding), and `pullBackupOnForeground()` pulls-and-merges on launch/foreground — all three are plain synchronous entry points with their own internal `async` work, never awaited by anything, so sync can never block launch, play, or a tap-to-play (point 2 of the spec). Offline-safe with the same backoff shape (`[15000, 30000, 60000, 120000, 300000]`) `src/miziki-social.js`'s own sync engine already uses — a failed push only ever sets `lastError`, never flips the UI's "backed up" state back off for a transient blip.
- **Four local-state prerequisites step 03's own doc flagged as missing now exist** (all added in step 06, three in `src/state.js`, one in `src/library/sealed.js`/`src/library/edit.js`): `S.deviceId`/`S.deviceLabel` (generated once, persisted, the `library_backup` row key), `S.historyClearedAt` (stamped by `src/history/clear.js`'s full-scope clear), `S.sealedBroken` (a `Set` recording every album ever sealed-then-broken on this device, persisted by a new `persistBrokenSeals()` alongside the existing `persistSealedAlbums()`), `S.backupUpdatedAt` (one timestamp per per-field-last-write-wins key, stamped by a new `touchBackupField(name)` at each field's real mutation site), and per-overlay `editedAt`/`revertedAt` timestamps (`src/library/edit.js`'s `applyEdit`/`revertTrack`/`undoLastEdit`, the last two now writing a `{fields:null, reverted:true, revertedAt}` tombstone instead of deleting the overlay row outright — see "Backup: what gets synced" above for why that's safe for local-only use too). `src/library/import.js`'s `addFiles()` calls a new `reconcileImportedTrack(t)` on every newly-added track, resolving any pending cross-device playlist/albumOrder/overlay entry that was waiting on this exact identity key.
- **How it behaves, matching the spec's own point 1**: signed out, nothing is ever uploaded (`scheduleBackupSync`/`flushBackupSync` both resolve a null Supabase client and return before touching `meta` or the network). Signing in and picking a handle (the existing social sign-in flow, `src/profile/settings.js`) now pre-fills the handle field from the local profile's username (`handleFromUsername()`) and runs an immediate debounced availability check, with the status line distinguishing "taken" from "reserved" from "not quite a valid handle" rather than one generic error. First sign-in on a device that already has local data merges via `fromDocs()` — never overwrites — and `src/sync/backup.js`'s `initBackupSync()` builds a restore summary (`buildRestoreSummary()`) the first time a given account's data is actually merged in (guarded by `S.backupRestoredFor` so it only fires once per account per device). Music re-added later reconciles pending entries via `reconcileImportedTrack` above.
- **Never breaks playback (point 2)**: confirmed by what's *absent*, not just what's present — `grep -rn "sync/backup" src/player/ src/audio/` outside this PR's own `library/import.js` touch point returns nothing; `player/transport.js`'s `play()` is untouched by this step, with `bgPrime()` still its literal first statement before any `await`, exactly as every prior step's own iOS-gesture confirmation describes (rule 9).
- **The Account screen** (point 3, in Profile, `src/profile/settings.js`'s rewritten `renderSocialSettingsScreen`, relabeled "Account" from "Settings" in `screens.js`'s `labels` map): @handle + email (`MizikiSocial.getMyProfile()` + `getClient().auth.getUser()`), a "Backed up · X ago" / "Waiting for connection" line (`getBackupStatus()`), a device list (`listBackupDevices()`), Sign out (`{scope:'local'}`, now explicit rather than relying on the library default) and Sign out everywhere (`{scope:'global'}`, a new `signOutEverywhere` on `MizikiSocial`), and Delete account — type-the-handle-to-confirm, calling a new `MizikiSocial.deleteAccount()` (the `delete_my_account()` RPC from migration 005, finally wired to a UI control after sitting unused since step 05), which resets local social state and signs out.
- **The Privacy screen** (point 4, `src/profile/privacy.js`, new — linked from both the profile home and the Account screen): the visibility picker (private/friends/public, default friends), share-now-spinning/share-milestones toggles (default **off** — moved here from the old Settings screen, where point 4 of the spec actually asks for them to live), follow requests, blocked accounts with unblock (a new `listBlocked` on `MizikiSocial`), the backup status line, the Download-my-data button, and the exact required sentence verbatim: *"Your listening history is backed up privately to your account. Your music and your location never leave this device."* Signed out, the social controls are replaced by a calm prompt, matching the existing "not set up" posture the rest of the profile/social screens already use.
  - **One deliberate design choice, made to avoid a real bug**: `wirePrivacyLink(host, renderProfile)` takes `renderProfile` as a parameter rather than importing it from `./screens.js`. `screens.js` already has a real 3-way circular cluster with `profile/storefront.js`/`profile/settings.js`; routing a fourth edge through `privacy.js` as well — on top of `privacy.js`'s own real import of `sync/backup.js` — was, in practice during this step's own test runs, enough additional surface to transitively reach `library/view.js`'s whole seven-circular-pair web through `record-art/looks.js` ↔ `history/sessions.js` ↔ `library/thumbs.js` ↔ `library/edit.js`. Passing the already-imported function in from each of the two call sites (`screens.js`'s own `renderProfileHome`, `settings.js`'s `renderSocialSettingsScreen`) avoids the edge entirely rather than trying to mock around it everywhere it would have been reached.
  - **A second instance of the same class of bug, in `backup.js` itself**: the sync engine's own `applyMerged()` logically wants to re-render the library and refresh the current disc's look after a pull, but importing `library/view.js`'s `renderTracks` or `record-art/looks.js`'s `applyDiscVariant` directly from `backup.js` hit the identical chain-explosion during this step's test runs (traced through `record-art/looks.js` ↔ `history/sessions.js` → `library/thumbs.js` → `library/edit.js` → `library/view.js`). Fixed the same way as every circular-import problem this engagement has hit before, just with an event instead of a direct call this time: `backup.js` exports a tiny `onBackup(evt, fn)` emitter and calls `emit('applied')`; `main.js` — which already safely imports both `renderTracks` and `applyDiscVariant` for its own boot sequence — is the one listener (`onBackup('applied', () => { renderTracks(); const t = current(); if(t) applyDiscVariant(t, true); })`), added right after `MizikiSocial.init()` in `restoreLibrary()`.
- **Download my data** (point 5, Privacy screen): `collectDownloadData()`/`collectLocalDownloadData()` (`src/sync/backup.js`) build a `miziki-data-YYYY-MM-DD.json` export — local stats/settings/playlists/track-edit text always (explicitly no audio, no images, in any case), plus an `account` section (profile, backup status, device list) only when signed in. Works fully signed out, per the spec's own requirement — confirmed by a dedicated test (below) asserting the exported object has no `account` key at all when there's no social client.
- **Docs (point 6)**: added a new top-level "What's stored server-side, and who can see it" section above, right before "Security" — a table mapping every server-side table/column to who can see it, written for the privacy policy and App Store privacy label rather than for a developer, plus the Download-my-data cross-reference. Rewrote "Backup: what gets synced" and its "doesn't exist locally yet" list to say what now exists and where, since this step is the "later PR" both were written to wait for.
- **Tests (point 7)**:
  - `tests/unit/backup-sync.test.js` (new, 7 tests): the debounce collapsing several `scheduleBackupSync()` calls into one push, a no-`MizikiSocial` no-op, `flushBackupSync()`'s immediacy, the backoff retry sequence (confirmed against the real `BACKOFF[0]`/`BACKOFF[1]` values, not hardcoded numbers), two Download-my-data cases (signed-out local-only export — `schemaVersion` present, no `account` key, no `tracks` key, but `overlays` present, confirming the "no audio/images, but text edits are fine" line is actually true of the real output — and the synchronous `collectLocalDownloadData()` variant), and that `deviceId` is generated once and persisted, not regenerated on a later push. Uses `node:test`'s `mock.timers.enable({apis:['setTimeout']})` plus a `flushMicrotasks()` helper (real `setImmediate`, repeated, to let pending `await`s inside the mocked-timer callback actually settle) and a tiny in-memory `meta`/`overlays` stand-in for `storage/repo.js` — the same "stop the chain at the door" reasoning as every other mock-heavy file in this suite, chosen specifically because `backup.js`'s own real imports (`state.js`, `storage/prefs.js`, `record-art/tiers.js`, `library/model.js`, `backup-doc.js`) are all pure, so nothing else needs mocking. Since step 07 (B4), the `storage/repo.js` mock also gained in-memory `artwork`/`profile` stand-ins (needed once `backup.js` itself started importing both for the image download/upload paths), and the file's destructured import gained `orphanedFiles` — confirmed directly, via a plain Node `import()` of `src/sync/backup.js` with no mocking at all, that its whole real import chain (now also `crate/tiers.js` and `sync/images.js`) stays DOM-free, which is why neither of those two needs its own mock here. 5 new tests cover `orphanedFiles(files, referencedHashes, keepNewest)`: an unreferenced file is returned for deletion at `keepNewest=0`; a referenced file never is, even at `keepNewest=0`; `keepNewest=1` spares the single newest *unreferenced* file (one with only a single unreferenced candidate keeps it, matching the real "avatars keep at most one" rule); a referenced file is correctly excluded from consideration even when it would otherwise be the newest; and an empty input is just an empty result.
  - `tests/unit/images.test.js` (step 07, B4, new, 4 tests) — the one pure, DOM-free piece of `src/sync/images.js`: `sha256Hex` produces the identical hex string for identical bytes (twice, and via a Blob-like object's own `arrayBuffer()` method, confirmed to match the plain-`ArrayBuffer` result), a different hash for different bytes, and the well-known SHA-256 hash of an empty input as a literal test-vector check. `prepareCoverUpload`/`prepareAvatarUpload`'s own canvas re-encode isn't tested here, for the same reason `share/styles.js`'s canvas drawing isn't — no DOM in this test suite — exactly as the file's own header comment says.
  - `tests/client.test.js` (extended, 2 new tests, 38 total): a genuine two-simulated-device run against the real Postgres harness — device A signs in and pushes a play (`trackPlayCounts`); device B's first-ever merge (`fromDocs`) picks it up (total: 1); device B pushes its own play; device A's next merge sees both (total: 2); re-merging the same rows again never inflates the total — and a full-scope "Clear listening history" on one device producing `ownReset: true` on the other once merged, correctly excluding the stale pre-clear count from the merged totals. Uses the real `toDoc`/`fromDocs` (`src/sync/backup-doc.js`) and `trackIdentityKey` (`src/record-art/tiers.js`), not a reimplementation, against real `library_backup` rows.
  - **Two real bugs caught by this step's own test-writing, not by a later review pass**: (1) `touchBackupField` was originally placed in `storage/meta.js`, next to `saveMeta` — but every module that calls it (`library/thumbs.js`, `history/sessions.js`, `profile/screens.js`) already reaches wide, DOM-adjacent chains of its own, and importing it from `meta.js` would have added a brand-new edge into `audio/mode.js`/`audio/engine.js`/`sundown/location.js`/`player/controls.js`/`player/background.js`/`launch/intro.js` for a dozen *existing* test files that had nothing to do with backup at all. Moved to `src/state.js` instead, which every one of those six call sites already imports `S` from — zero new edges. (2) a Postgres test fixture wrote `{ SONG_KEY: 1 }` where `SONG_KEY` was meant as a computed key (`trackIdentityKey(...)`'s return value) — plain JavaScript object-literal syntax, so this created a property literally named the four characters `"SONG_KEY"`, not one named by the variable's value, and the merged total silently read back `undefined`. Fixed to `{ [SONG_KEY]: 1 }` (computed-property syntax) in all three occurrences, caught by the test's own assertion failing rather than passing silently — a reminder of why the test was worth writing in the first place.
  - A large number of *existing* `tests/unit/*.test.js` files needed small mock-chasing fixes of the same shape every prior step's extractions have required — adding `touchBackupField(){}` to existing `storage/meta.js` mocks, a new `sync/backup.js` mock (`scheduleBackupSync(){}`/`reconcileImportedTrack(){}`) wherever a real chain now reaches one, and a `getClient(){ return null; }` added to any pre-existing `globalThis.MizikiSocial` test stub that `backup.js`'s defensive `getSocial()`/`getClient()` accessors would otherwise find incomplete. `tests/unit/edit.test.js` got two genuine assertion rewrites (not just mock patches) to match the new overlay-tombstone shape: a reverted/undone-with-no-prior-overlay edit now reads back `{fields: null, reverted: true}` from the `overlays` store instead of the row being gone entirely.
- **Reserved handles' `src/miziki-social.js` note updated**, and three new methods added to its public API for this step: `getClient()` (the small accessor `backup.js` uses instead of constructing a second Supabase client, per the task's own explicit instruction), `signOutEverywhere`, `deleteAccount`, `listBlocked` — see that module's own repo-layout entry above.
- Verified: `npm run lint` passes with 0 errors, 0 warnings; `npm run test:unit` passes (289/289 — 282 before this step + 7 new in `backup-sync.test.js`); `npm test` passes (38/38, against a freshly reseeded Postgres database with all five migrations reapplied in order — see step 05's own "Lesson for future verification" note, re-confirmed necessary again this step). Both builds (`npm run build` and `MIZIKI_BASE=/ npm run build`) and Playwright preview verification are the remaining items before this PR can be marked ready for review — see the PR description itself for their results, since they run after this changelog entry is written.

**Preview focus (Ken) — please verify by hand before merging:**
1. Sign in on a fresh device/browser profile with no local library, then again on one with existing local stats/playlists — confirm the second shows a restore summary and never silently overwrites what was already there.
2. Play a track on two different signed-in sessions (or two tabs) for the same account and confirm play counts sum rather than one overwriting the other (the same invariant `tests/client.test.js`'s new two-device test checks, but against the real client flow, not the harness).
3. Turn off network (airplane mode or devtools offline) mid-session, make a few changes, turn it back on, and confirm sync catches up without an error dialog.
4. Open the Account screen, confirm the device list and "Backed up · X ago" line look right; try Sign out vs. Sign out everywhere from two sessions.
5. Open the Privacy screen, flip visibility and the two share toggles, and confirm Download my data produces a real, readable JSON file both signed in and signed out.
6. **Do not use "Delete account" on a real account you want to keep** — it is irreversible and calls the real RPC against the live Supabase project; verify it only with a disposable test account.
7. Confirm no CSP violations or console errors anywhere in this flow, against both built targets (GitHub Pages base and Netlify's `/` base).

### Step 07 (B4): custom covers and profile pictures

Seventh of the post-refactor roadmap's numbered documents. Builds on B1–B3 (steps 03/05/06), all merged and working. A feature PR, same posture as step 06: real images now travel to and from a real Supabase project for a signed-in user, so — per the task's own instruction — **this PR must not be merged until Ken applies `006_images.sql` to the live Supabase project**; see "Before merging (Ken)" at the end of this entry.

- **Point 1 — confirmed, by reading the code, exactly which images get backed up and which never do.** The local `artwork` store mixes two genuinely different things: a *custom* cover a user picks in the edit sheet (`src/library/edit.js`'s `applyEdit()`, stored keyed by the track's `albumKey`) and *generated* images — crate-view tiers (`src/crate/tiers.js`) and `thumb:*` thumbnails (`src/library/thumbs.js`) — both rebuilt locally from whatever source art (embedded, or a now-synced custom cover) happens to be on the device, never something a user set directly. Only the first kind is backed up. Embedded artwork isn't uploaded either — it already comes back with the file the moment it's re-imported, so backing it up would just be a second, pointless copy of something the file itself already carries.
- **Point 2 — two Storage buckets, different privacy, via `supabase/migrations/006_images.sql`**: `covers` (private, `{user_id}/{sha256}.jpg`, owner-only read/upload/delete, 1 MB cap) and `avatars` (public, `{user_id}/avatar-{sha256}.jpg`, owner-only upload/delete but readable by anyone including signed out — it doubles as the account's public avatar once signed in, per the Account/Privacy screens' existing framing). Both buckets also cap `allowed_mime_types` to `image/jpeg`/`image/webp` at the Storage-API level, which no RLS policy can express — see the migration's own comments and docs/SUPABASE_SETUP.md's manual-check section for why that half needs a human to verify against the real API, not just Postgres RLS. `profiles.avatar_url` (already existing, owner-updatable) gets set to the Storage public URL after a successful avatar upload — no new column needed.
- **Point 3 — `src/sync/images.js` (new, a leaf)**: `prepareCoverUpload`/`prepareAvatarUpload` re-encode through canvas (covers: max 1200px @ ~0.85; avatars: 512px square @ ~0.85) via a module-private `cropSquareStepped`, a miniature, deliberately *duplicated*-not-imported copy of `crate/tiers.js`'s own stepped-downscale crop — kept separate so this file stays import-free, the same reasoning `backup-doc.js`/`merge.js` already follow. Re-encoding strips EXIF (and with it, GPS) by construction — canvas never carries a source image's metadata through to its own output — called out explicitly in the file's own header comment, since it matters most for the avatar, the one publicly-readable image in this whole pipeline. Each output is named by the SHA-256 of its *own re-encoded* bytes, not the source file's — `sha256Hex` (pure, async, Web Crypto) is the one piece of this file unit-tested directly (`tests/unit/images.test.js`), since the canvas/`Image` half needs a DOM this test suite doesn't have (the same accepted gap `share/styles.js`'s canvas drawing already has).
- **Point 4 — schema v2**: `src/sync/backup-doc.js`'s `SCHEMA_VERSION` bumped 1 → 2. A new `convertCovers`/`covers` doc field (per-track-identity-key, `{identityKey, at, hash}` — `hash: null` is the tombstone, written by a whole-overlay revert alongside the existing `fields: null, reverted: true`) and a new `lastWrite.picture` field (the exact `lw()` shape every other last-write-wins field already uses). `src/sync/merge.js` gained `mergeCovers` (the same per-identity-key last-write-wins primitive as `mergeOverlays`, kept separate on purpose — a cover change and a tag change are independent edits with their own timestamps) and folded `picture` into the existing `lastWriteWins` plumbing. A v1 doc — missing both fields entirely — still merges without throwing: every reader already treats a missing field as "no claim" by construction (`row.covers || []`, etc.), confirmed by two dedicated `tests/unit/backup-merge.test.js` schema-v1-compatibility tests rather than just assumed.
- **Point 5 — upload/download/cleanup, all in `src/sync/backup.js`**:
  - **Upload** rides the exact same debounced/offline-safe queue B3 already built (`scheduleBackupSync`/`runPush`'s existing backoff) — nothing new to invent. `queueCoverUpload(trackId, blob)` (called fire-and-forget from `library/edit.js`'s `applyEdit()`) and `queueAvatarUpload(blob)` (from `profile/screens.js`'s `persistProfile()`) each re-encode/hash via `images.js`, stamp the claim locally, and schedule a sync — neither is ever awaited by its caller, matching the existing fire-and-forget style already used elsewhere in this codebase (e.g. `buildCrateTier(...).catch(() => {})`), so a save can never be blocked waiting on a network upload. The real network step, `uploadOne`/`flushPendingImages`, re-derives the canonical re-encoded blob+hash fresh from the locally-stored *source* blob on every sync cycle rather than keeping a second copy of pre-encoded bytes around — deduped only by a small persisted `Set` of confirmed `'bucket:hash'` tags (`loadUploadedHashes`/`persistUploadedHashes`), so an unchanged cover or picture is never re-uploaded just because a sync cycle ran.
  - **Download** happens only when a matching local track already exists (`queueCoverDownload`) — otherwise the claim waits in `B.pending.covers`, resolved later by `reconcileImportedTrack(t)` the same way a pending overlay/playlist/albumOrder entry already does. A downloaded cover is written into the local `artwork` store and triggers `deleteCrateArtTiers`/`buildCrateTier` so the crate view picks it up without any new rendering code; a downloaded avatar updates `S.profile.art`/`artBlob`/`pictureHash` directly. Both fire `emit('applied')` so `main.js`'s existing listener (see its own repo-layout entry, extended this step to also call `renderProfile()` when the profile tab is open) can refresh what's on screen — `backup.js` itself still never imports `library/view.js`/`record-art/looks.js` (see below).
  - **Cleanup**: `orphanedFiles(files, referencedHashes, keepNewest)` is the one pure decision in this whole area, exported and unit-tested directly — covers keep zero unreferenced files (an old cover that's been fully replaced has nothing left pointing at it), avatars keep the single most recently updated unreferenced one (a brief grace window, never more than one spare file per user). `cleanupOrphanedImages`/`listBucketFiles` do the actual Storage-side removal, wrapped in try/catch as best-effort — a cleanup failure must never block the push itself.
- **A second chain-explosion bug, same shape as step 06's two, caught and fixed before it shipped**: a first draft had `queueAvatarUpload` flush `S.backupUpdatedAt` to IndexedDB via the normal `storage/meta.js`'s `queueSave()`/`saveMeta()` path — but `storage/meta.js` real-chains into `player/background.js` → `player/transport.js` → `library/view.js`, exactly the hub this file has twice already been designed around avoiding. Fixed by calling `meta.put('backupUpdatedAt', S.backupUpdatedAt).catch(() => {})` directly against the already-imported, true-leaf `meta` from `storage/repo.js` instead — `backup.js`'s own header comment now lists `storage/meta.js` alongside `library/view.js`/`record-art/looks.js` as a module this file must never import, with the same reasoning for each.
- **Point 2 (account deletion) — `src/miziki-social.js`'s `deleteAccount()`** now lists and removes every file under the caller's own `covers/<uid>/` and `avatars/<uid>/` folders (a new module-private `removeStorageFolder(bucket, uid)`, best-effort, wrapped in its own try/catch) *before* calling the `delete_my_account()` RPC — Storage objects have no foreign key to `auth.users`, so migration 005's existing cascades can't reach either bucket, and this has to happen while the account's own owner-only RLS policies (migration 006) still apply, i.e. before the RPC deletes the account out from under it.
- **Point 6 — tests**: `tests/unit/images.test.js` (new, 4 tests — `sha256Hex` only, per the file's own documented DOM gap); `tests/unit/backup-sync.test.js` extended (5 new tests, all for `orphanedFiles`, plus `artwork`/`profile` added to its existing `storage/repo.js` mock); `tests/unit/backup-merge.test.js` extended (9 new tests — cover last-write-wins/tombstone, picture last-write-wins/field-isolation, schema-v1-compatibility). `tests/supabase_stub.sql` gained a minimal `storage.buckets`/`storage.objects`/`storage.foldername()` stand-in (the same "real Postgres role/`auth.uid()` semantics, not a real Storage API" approach the existing `auth.users`/`auth.uid()` stub already takes) so `tests/client.test.js` could add 4 real-RLS tests against it: owner CRUD on `covers`; a second user's read/write/delete against the first user's folder all correctly failing or matching zero rows; `avatars`' public-read-but-owner-write split (confirmed for both an authenticated second user and a fully signed-out `asAnon()` request); and `deleteAccount()`'s own Storage-cleanup calls, exercised against the file's existing fake `sb` client's new `storage.from(bucket).list()/.remove()` stub. What the Postgres stand-in can't reach — the Storage API's own `file_size_limit`/`allowed_mime_types` enforcement, and actual byte storage/retrieval — is flagged in the stub's own comment and covered instead by a manual-check section added to docs/SUPABASE_SETUP.md (point 6's own documented fallback for what isn't testable locally).
- **Point 7 — docs**: CLAUDE.md's "Backup: what gets synced" table gained two new rows (a custom cover's own image bytes; the profile picture's own image bytes) with the opening paragraph's "never leaves the device" claim corrected to carve out the one exception, plus `S.profile.pictureHash` and `backupUpdatedAt.picture`/the cover-tombstone addition folded into the existing "local state the merge logic depends on" list. "What's stored server-side, and who can see it" gained two new table rows (`covers`, `avatars`) and its own opening claim was corrected the same way. docs/SUPABASE_SETUP.md's migration list and the step 05 "Applying" pattern both gained a `006_images.sql` entry, including the Storage-dashboard bucket-settings check and the manual cross-user/public-read policy check noted above.
- Verified: `npm run lint` passes with 0 errors, 0 warnings; `npm run test:unit` passes (307/307 — 289 before this step + 4 new in `images.test.js` + 5 new in `backup-sync.test.js` + 9 new in `backup-merge.test.js`); `npm test` passes (42/42, against a freshly reseeded Postgres database with all six migrations reapplied in order, same "Lesson for future verification" procedure every prior step needing Postgres has followed). Two existing unit test files needed a mock-chain fix of the exact shape every prior step's extractions have required, once `library/edit.js` started importing real names from `sync/backup.js`: `tests/unit/forget-library.test.js`'s existing `crate/tiers.js` mock gained `buildCrateTier` (needed once `backup.js`'s own real import of it, reached transitively through `library/edit.js`, joined the chain this test already mocks `deleteCrateArtTiers` to stop); `tests/unit/history.test.js`'s existing `sync/backup.js` mock gained `queueCoverUpload` (reached the same way, via the real `cropSquareImage` → `library/edit.js` chain its own header comment already documents).

**Before merging (Ken):**
1. Apply `supabase/migrations/006_images.sql` to the live Supabase project via the SQL editor (`docs/SUPABASE_SETUP.md`'s own step-by-step, now with a dedicated section for this migration).
2. Verify the two Storage buckets exist with the right settings (private/public, size caps, allowed types) in the dashboard's Storage tab.
3. Run through docs/SUPABASE_SETUP.md's manual cross-user/public-read policy check for both buckets — the one thing a real Supabase project can confirm that the local Postgres harness's `storage.objects` stand-in can't (its own `file_size_limit`/`allowed_mime_types` enforcement included).
4. Only then merge this PR.

**Preview focus (Ken) — please verify by hand before merging:**
1. Set a custom cover on a track from the edit sheet, confirm it still shows after a reload, and confirm (via the dashboard's Storage tab, or a second signed-in device) that a file appears under `covers/<your user id>/`.
2. Set or change a profile picture, confirm the Account/Privacy screens still work as expected, and confirm a file appears under `avatars/<your user id>/` — and that it's visible from a signed-out tab or a different account (the one bucket meant to be public).
3. Revert a custom cover back to the embedded/default art and confirm the old cover file is eventually removed from Storage (cleanup runs on the next sync cycle, not instantly).
4. Change the profile picture a second time and confirm at most one old, unreferenced avatar file lingers in Storage afterward, never more.
5. On a second device signed into the same account, confirm a cover/avatar set on the first device eventually appears there too, without having to re-pick either image.
6. **Do not use "Delete account" on a real account you want to keep.** On a disposable test account instead, confirm that after deletion, its `covers/`/`avatars/` folders are actually empty in the Storage dashboard (not just the database rows).
7. Confirm no CSP violations or console errors anywhere in this flow, against both built targets, and that with Supabase not configured the app behaves exactly as it did before this PR.

### Step 08 (P3, part A): Privacy Policy and Terms of use screens

Eighth of the post-refactor roadmap's numbered documents, part A only (see the task doc's own framing: "Part A is one Claude Code PR; parts B–D are mostly you, in dashboards" — B is `docs/MODERATION.md`, covered separately below; C/D/E are Ken's own setup/domain/App Store work, out of scope for Claude Code). A content PR, not a refactor — new screens, new docs, zero behavior change to anything that existed before this step. **This PR must not be merged until Ken has the final text reviewed** (the task doc's own words: "I'm not a lawyer. Before the App Store, have the final text reviewed"). The contact address in all four files (`docs/PRIVACY_POLICY.md`, `docs/TERMS_OF_USE.md`, and their two in-app mirrors inside `src/profile/legal.js`) is filled in as `sckhitokiri@gmail.com`, noted inline as temporary — to be swapped for an admin@ address on Miziki's own domain once one is purchased — see "Before merging (Ken)" at the end of this entry. The minimum age was 13 throughout at first, per the task doc's own instruction — raising it was flagged as Ken's own later call to make, not something this PR decided on its own, and Ken has since made that call: the minimum age is now **16** in all four files, to avoid GDPR's under-16 parental-consent rules for EU users.

- **The five required content areas**, all written from facts already established elsewhere in this file rather than generic boilerplate — every sentence in `docs/PRIVACY_POLICY.md` traces back to the "Backup: what gets synced" and "What's stored server-side, and who can see it" tables above, not invented: what's collected (email for sign-in only; handle/display name/bio/avatar; social activity, now-spinning/milestones off by default; the private listening backup — play/session counts, achievements/Collection, sealed state, track-edit text, playlists, settings; uploaded custom covers and the profile picture, both re-encoded and EXIF/GPS-stripped per step 07); what's never collected (music files, embedded art, location — on-device only for Sundown/Car Mode — and "no requests to any third party at all, other than your own Supabase project"); who can see what (backup/covers private; handle/name/avatar visible to signed-in users except anyone blocked; shared activity follows the visibility setting, friends by default); where it lives and how long (Supabase US East as processor; kept until deleted; Download my data / Delete account, both already-shipped features from steps 06/05 this document just explains); and who can use it (16+, must own/have the right to play the music) plus community rules (no impersonation/harassment/illegal content; reports reviewed, consequences up to account deletion) — all five appear in both `docs/PRIVACY_POLICY.md` and, condensed, in `src/profile/legal.js`'s `renderPrivacyPolicyScreen`.
- **`docs/TERMS_OF_USE.md`** (not separately asked for by name, but the task doc's own five bullets split naturally into privacy-facing and usage-facing halves, and the Account/Privacy screens already distinguish the two concepts) covers what Miziki is, who can use it, how an account works (no password, single-use emailed codes, sign out one/all devices, delete anytime), content ownership (the user owns their music/photos/text; Miziki claims none of it), the same community rules as the bulleted list above, "provided as is" with no uptime guarantee (pointing at Download my data as the mitigation), and a changes clause.
- **Content-addressed canonical source, mirrored by hand**: `docs/PRIVACY_POLICY.md`/`docs/TERMS_OF_USE.md` are the reviewable markdown (so wording changes go through a normal PR diff); `src/profile/legal.js`'s `renderPrivacyPolicyScreen`/`renderTermsScreen` render the same words in-app, since there's no markdown renderer anywhere in this codebase and adding one for two static pages would be more moving parts than keeping the two in sync by discipline — the same two-sources-reviewed-together pattern this very file (CLAUDE.md) already uses for its own prose vs. the code it describes. Each of the four files opens with a comment/note pointing at its sibling.
- **Three link points, exactly as asked** ("linked from the sign-in screen, the Privacy screen and the account screen"): a `legalLinksRow(renderProfile, before)` helper in `legal.js`, reused at all three. The Account screen (`src/profile/settings.js`'s `renderSocialSettingsScreen`) and the Privacy screen (`src/profile/privacy.js`'s `renderPrivacyScreen`) each get the row appended from `src/profile/screens.js`'s own dispatch in `renderProfile()` — right after calling each screen's render function — rather than either module importing `legal.js` directly, the same `wirePrivacyLink(host, renderProfile)`-style parameter-passing shape `privacy.js` already established in step 06 specifically to avoid adding a new edge to `screens.js`'s already-dense circular-import cluster. The sign-in wizard's first step (`renderSocialAuthEmail`, in `settings.js`) gets a one-line "By continuing, you agree to our Privacy Policy and Terms of use" note plus the same row, but with `before` set to `closeSocialAuth` — the one call site that needs it, since tapping either link there has to close the sign-in modal first or it would stay open on top of the profile screen underneath it once `renderProfile()` runs. `renderProfile()`'s `labels` map and dispatch gained two new leaf screens, `privacyPolicy`/`termsOfUse` (`'Privacy Policy'`/`'Terms of use'`), reached the same way every other profile sub-screen already is: `S.profileView.screen = '…'; renderProfile();`.
- **Styling**: a new, correctly-numbered `src/styles/21-legal.css`, appended after `20-friend-store.css` in both the cascade and `miziki.html`'s `<link>` order — scoped `#profileBody h2` styling (no generic element reset exists anywhere else in this app's CSS, confirmed by audit before adding one), `.legal-list` for the bullet lists, and `.legal-links`/`.link-btn` for the inline footer row.
- **Why `el()`/`textContent`, not `innerHTML`, for static text that could never carry a tag-injection payload**: consistency with every other screen in the app, not a security requirement unique to this content — called out explicitly in `legal.js`'s own header comment, since a reader familiar with the step 01 `no-restricted-syntax` rule (which exists for untrusted *file-tag* text, not developer-authored copy) might otherwise wonder why this screen bothers.
- No `await` touched — this step adds two pure-DOM render functions and one small helper function; nothing here is on the playback or sync path.
- Verified: `npm run lint` passes with 0 errors, 0 warnings; `npm run test:unit` passes (307/307, unchanged — per the same precedent step 04's config-only PR set, nothing in this step is pure/stateful logic worth a unit test: two render functions over static strings and a link-row helper, exercised instead by the Playwright pass below); both builds succeed (`npm run build` and `MIZIKI_BASE=/ npm run build`, 166 modules transformed each, bundle sizes unchanged beyond the new CSS file); a Playwright script against both built targets opened the Privacy screen and followed its Privacy Policy/Terms of use links (confirming the crumb label, the "Last updated" line, and the contact line are all present and that navigating back lands on the right prior screen), confirmed the same two links appear and work from the Account screen, and opened the sign-in modal's first step to confirm its new legal footer renders, that tapping either link closes the modal first, and that the app then shows the corresponding screen — zero non-baseline console/page errors on either built target.

**Before merging (Ken):**

Replace it with an admin@ address on Miziki's own domain once one is purchased (Part D).

3. ~~Decide whether to raise the minimum age from 13 to 16 for GDPR~~ **Done** — Ken asked for 16, applied in all four files (both docs plus both `src/profile/legal.js` renderers). ### Step 08 (P3, part B): `docs/MODERATION.md`

Part B of the same roadmap document as part A (Privacy Policy/Terms of use, above — already merged). Per the task doc's own framing — "Docs only, so no preview needed" — this is the lowest-ceremony step in the whole engagement so far: one new file, no app code, no migration, nothing to build, lint, or test.

- Three sections: how to review reports (the dashboard SQL query, run weekly, ordered oldest-first, plus the convention this doc picks for `reports.status` — `open`/`actioned`/`dismissed`, since the column itself is a plain `text` with no enum); how to find a user's id from a handle when a report doesn't already carry it; and the three actions, each with the exact SQL and the equivalent Table Editor click-path.
- **Clear an avatar or bio**: null the `profiles` columns; separately flagged that this doesn't delete the underlying Storage object, with the dashboard steps to remove the `avatars` bucket file too when the image itself (not just the bio) is the problem.
- **Remove a sleeve note**: find the `(user_id, release_id)` pair via `crate_items`/`releases`, null `sleeve_note`.
- **Delete an account**: the one action that can't go through the app's own path — `delete_my_account()` (migration `005_backup.sql`) is written `where id = auth.uid()`, so it only ever works for someone acting on their own account, including from the SQL editor (which isn't signed in as anyone). A moderator instead deletes the `auth.users` row directly, relying on the same `on delete cascade` chain `delete_my_account()` itself uses — confirmed against the real schema in `001_schema.sql`/`005_backup.sql`, not assumed.
