Two things this surfaced, both called out explicitly in the doc rather than left for Ken to discover the hard way: (1) deleting the account cascades away any `reports` row naming it as the target (`reports.target_user_id references profiles(id) on delete cascade`), so the doc tells the reviewer to copy the report's `reason`/`created_at` *before* running the delete, since there's no separate moderation log anywhere in this schema; (2) the cascade never reaches Storage — `covers`/`avatars` objects have no foreign key to `auth.users`, the exact gap `006_images.sql`'s own closing comment already flags for the user-initiated deletion path (`src/miziki-social.js`'s `deleteAccount()`) — so a moderator-initiated delete needs its own manual Storage cleanup in both buckets, spelled out as explicit dashboard steps.
- Every SQL snippet and column/table name was checked directly against `supabase/migrations/001_schema.sql`/`005_backup.sql`/`006_images.sql` (`reports`, `profiles`, `crate_items`, the RLS policies revoking `update`/`delete` on `reports` from `authenticated` — confirming review genuinely has to happen outside the app, matching the doc's own opening line), not written from memory.
- No build, lint, or test run — a markdown-only change with zero code surface, the same posture step 03's "no visible change" framing used for its own pure-logic PR, just one step further (that step at least touched `src/`; this one touches nothing runnable at all).

### Step D1: dark-mode color tokens (desktop layout prerequisite)

First of a new, separate seven-PR plan (`docs/DESKTOP_LAYOUT.md`, added this step — the full handoff doc, verbatim) to add a desktop layout and an Auto/Light/Dark appearance setting. **No behavior change, and — unlike every other refactor in this engagement — no visible change either**: D1's only job is moving every hard-coded dark color onto a CSS custom-property token, with the token's value set to exactly what the literal already was. Light values, the appearance setting itself, and the desktop layout are D2 onward — explicitly not this PR, and explicitly not started: **per the handoff doc's own instruction, this step stops after D1 and waits for Ken before D2 begins.**

- **Survey first, per the doc's own instruction**: grepped every `src/styles/*.css` file and `miziki.html` for literal hex/`rgba()` colors before changing anything, and separately grepped for every `background:var(--paper)` use (`--paper` already existed; the doc's token table splits it from a new `--cream`, so every existing use needed checking against which one it actually means). Found three categories: three files sharing the exact same `linear-gradient(170deg,var(--sky1) 0%,var(--sky2) 62%,#07080C 100%)` "ground" gradient (`00-base.css`'s `body`, `13-player-overlay.css`'s `.player-overlay`, and `09-launch-intro.css`'s `.launch-overlay` — the third one not named in the doc's own prose, found only by the grep); two chrome surfaces (`08-shell.css`'s `.mini-player` at `rgba(10,14,18,.9)` and `.tabbar` at `rgba(10,14,18,.94)`) both meant by the doc's single `--chrome` token; and five `background:var(--paper)` uses that were never actually the page's own paper tone — a cream sticker/note swatch, both in `18-storefront.css` (the awning stripe, the count sticker, the pick note) and `20-friend-store.css` (the record-sheet note).
- **Nine new tokens added to `00-base.css`'s `:root`, dark values only** (D1's whole scope, per the doc): `--sky3` (`#07080C`, the ground gradient's third stop), `--cream` (`#EFE9DF` — same literal `--paper` already held, split into its own name since the doc's table treats "paper" and "cream sticker" as two different future light-mode colors even though they're identical today), `--panel`/`--chrome`/`--raised`/`--field`/`--sel` (the five `rgba()` surface tones the doc's table names), `--cool` (`#9FD8E8`), and `--disc-a`/`--disc-b`/`--disc-c` (the vinyl-disc gradient's three stops). Every value is a direct copy of the literal it replaces — confirmed by diff, not retyped from the doc's table, so a transcription slip in either direction can't silently change anything.
- **Three ground-gradient call sites** (`00-base.css`'s `body`, `13-player-overlay.css`'s `.player-overlay`, `09-launch-intro.css`'s `.launch-overlay`) swapped their trailing `#07080C` for `var(--sky3)` — the literal itself untouched, just named.
- **Chrome consolidation, a deliberate judgment call**: `08-shell.css`'s `.mini-player` (`rgba(10,14,18,.9)`) and `.tabbar` (`rgba(10,14,18,.94)`) both became `background:var(--chrome)` at the doc table's own `.9` dark value — an exact match for the mini-player, but a real ~4% alpha reduction for the tab bar, since the doc's single `--chrome` token covers both surfaces and the two had drifted slightly apart before this PR. Flagged for empirical verification rather than assumed safe — see below.
- **`--paper`→`--cream` on five sticker/note backgrounds** (`18-storefront.css`'s `.store-awning > div:nth-child(even)`, `.store-card-count`, `.store-pick-note`; `20-friend-store.css`'s `.record-note`) — zero value change (both tokens hold the identical literal today), just the correct name for what each one actually is, so a future D2 giving `--paper` and `--cream` different light-mode values doesn't accidentally recolor a sticker note to the page's own background tone.
- **`<meta name="theme-color">` left as a literal, with an explanatory comment, not "made a variable"**: an HTML attribute can't read a CSS custom property, so there's no way to literally satisfy "make it a variable" at the HTML level in this PR. The doc's own PR table assigns the actual JS-driven variable-ization to D2's `src/ui/theme.js` (which will set this attribute from script, per the active theme) — added a comment above the tag in `miziki.html` explaining the deferral and naming `--sky2` as the token this value tracks, rather than guessing at a different interpretation.
- **Verified with Playwright at 390×844 in dark, before vs. after, exactly as instructed**: built a clean `origin/main` baseline in a separate `git worktree` (`MIZIKI_BASE=/`, served on its own port) alongside this branch's own `/` build, then screenshotted five routes (library, home, sundown, character, profile) in both. The first pass, with default animation timing, showed large (2-50%) pixel diffs — traced to the launch-intro card stack and crate-flow animations landing at different, inherently nondeterministic rAF-driven frames between two independent page loads, not a color regression (visual inspection of the diffed frames confirmed the ground-gradient tones were identical; only the animated foreground card positions/colors differed). Re-ran with Playwright's `reducedMotion: 'reduce'` context option (which the app's own CSS already honors — `.launch-overlay{display:none}` under `prefers-reduced-motion:reduce`, eliminating the nondeterministic animation entirely) and got **zero pixel difference on all five routes**, confirmed with `pixelmatch` (threshold 0.1) — including the `.tabbar`'s `.94→.9` alpha consolidation, which a direct pixel comparison shows is visually imperceptible on this near-black, heavily-blurred chrome surface. Zero console/page errors on either build throughout.
- No `await` touched, no JS changed at all — this PR is CSS/HTML only.
- Verified: `npm run lint` passes with 0 errors, 0 warnings; `npm run test:unit` passes (307/307, unchanged — nothing in this PR is JS, so nothing here is newly testable); both builds succeed (`npm run build` and `MIZIKI_BASE=/ npm run build`); the Playwright before/after screenshot comparison above shows exactly zero pixel difference across all five routes tested, with reduced motion isolating the comparison from the app's own pre-existing animation nondeterminism rather than masking a real change.

### Step D2: light theme and the Auto/Light/Dark preference

Second of the seven-PR desktop-layout plan, building on D1's tokens. Per the doc's own instruction ("Decide these before D2... Each has a default, so Claude Code can proceed if you do not answer"), proceeded on the two open questions that actually bear on this step using the doc's own stated defaults, since no override had been given by the time this PR started: **Auto is the default for new installs, and a saved prefs object without `theme` also loads as Auto**; **the theme choice is device-only**, not synced to the account. (The other four open questions — the light palette's own exact values, record-detail-on-desktop, the 768–1023px tablet range, and the sidebar extras — belong to D3 and later, not this PR.) This PR adds real, visible UI (the Appearance row) — unlike D1, "no visible change" was never D2's own bar; the bar is "renders correctly in both themes, nothing else regresses," verified below.

- **The light token set** (`src/styles/22-theme.css`, new, appended after `21-legal.css`): three selector blocks, exactly as the doc's own "Light and dark theming" section specifies — `:root[data-theme="light"]` (a pinned Light choice), `@media (prefers-color-scheme: light) { :root:not([data-theme]) }` (Auto, the one block that needs zero JavaScript to apply correctly at first paint), and `:root[data-theme="dark"]` re-asserting the dark values (a pinned Dark choice, beating the device). Only the thirteen tokens that actually differ between themes are redeclared in each block (`--sky1`/`--sky2`/`--sky3`, `--paper`, `--cream`, `--muted`, `--line`, `--panel`, `--chrome`, `--raised`, `--field`, `--sel`, `--accent-text`, `--shadow`, plus `color-scheme`) — `--accent`, `--on-accent`, `--cool`, the disc gradient (`--disc-a/b/c`) and the font stacks are left to inherit from bare `:root` on purpose, per the doc's own "the disc, sleeve art, metal tiers and the share-image styles keep their own colors in both themes — only chrome changes" rule. Every light value is copied straight from the doc's own token table, not approximated. `00-base.css`'s bare `:root` also gained `color-scheme:dark` (one line), so native controls (scrollbars, form fields) follow the theme too, matching the doc's "Selectors" section.
- **`S.theme`** (`'auto'`/`'light'`/`'dark'`, default `'auto'`, `src/state.js`) — added to `serializePrefs`/`parsePrefs` (`src/storage/prefs.js`) the same way every other saved setting already is, per the refactor-rules' own "new saved setting" checklist: a default in `S`, both prefs functions, and a `prefs.test.js` round-trip plus a "missing field loads as the default" test. **Device-only**: `src/sync/backup.js`'s `buildLocalState()` deletes `theme` from the `serializePrefs(S)` copy it uploads, so a pinned choice on one device never follows the account to another — added to the backup key-inventory table in CLAUDE.md as the rule itself requires for every new saved key.
- **`src/ui/theme.js`** (new): `applyTheme()` — sets/removes `document.documentElement.dataset.theme`, mirrors a pinned choice to `localStorage` (for the anti-flash script below), updates `<meta name="theme-color">` (resolved against the *effective* theme — the device's own scheme while Auto — since a meta attribute can't read a CSS custom property), installs/tears down a `matchMedia('(prefers-color-scheme: light)')` listener so Auto keeps following the device live (confirmed working end to end below, not just unit-tested), and syncs `aria-pressed` on every `[data-theme-choice]` button in the DOM, phone or desktop, present or not. `setTheme(choice)` — validates, sets `S.theme`, persists via `queueSave()`, applies; the one function both the phone row and the desktop sidebar's own control (D7) will call, per the doc's own "both call the same function" instruction — forward-compatible with zero changes needed here once D7 adds its own `[data-theme-choice]` markup, since this file was written to the attribute convention, not a fixed element id.
- **`applyPrefs()`** (`src/storage/meta.js`) sets `S.theme` from a valid saved value and calls `applyTheme()` unconditionally at the end (not just inside the `if`) — so a restore with no saved `theme` at all (every device before D2) still re-applies the default correctly, the same reasoning every other `applyPrefs()` field follows. **`main.js`'s boot sequence** also calls `applyTheme()` once, unconditionally, right after `setMode('vinyl')` — the same shape, since Auto's own live-scheme listener and the `theme-color` meta tag both need setting up even on a device's very first-ever boot, before any saved `prefs` row exists for `restoreMeta()`'s own `if(pr)` branch to ever run.
- **Avoiding a flash for a pinned choice** (`src/theme-boot.js`, new): a plain classic script — no import/export, the same shape as `src/miziki-social.js` — loaded in `<head>` *before* the stylesheet `<link>` tags, so it blocks HTML parsing and sets `data-theme` from the `localStorage` mirror before any CSS is even applied. Needs its own `copy-theme-boot` Vite plugin (`vite.config.js`), the exact same reasoning as `copy-social-client` — Vite leaves a plain (non-`type="module"`) `<script src>` completely untouched, so without an explicit copy step it 404s in the built app. Auto needs none of this, by design — confirmed in the verification below that `data-theme` stays unset the entire time Auto is active, letting the CSS `@media` block alone carry it at first paint.
- **The Appearance row** (`miziki.html`, Character route) — a three-button `.seg` (`data-theme-choice="auto"/"light"/"dark"`), the same row shape as Scratch/Sleeve Pull/Launch intro just below it. `src/wiring/character.js` wires it with `document.querySelectorAll('[data-theme-choice]')` rather than fixed ids, so D7's own desktop control needs no wiring change here, only its own markup.
- **A real, pre-existing design interaction found during verification, not a D2 bug**: `src/player/clock.js`'s `drawSun()` (Sundown's animated sky gradient, untouched by D1 or D2) unconditionally overwrites `--sky1`/`--sky2` as an **inline style** on `:root` every frame, blending through its own fixed, always-dark-leaning RGB stops regardless of app theme — an inline style always wins over any stylesheet rule, including the new `22-theme.css` blocks, so `--sky1`/`--sky2` never visibly reflect the Light theme while Sundown is actively progressing. This is exactly today's existing behavior (the same three RGB arrays, confirmed to already encode the original dark palette's own hex values) continuing unchanged — fixing or theming it isn't in D2's scope, and the doc doesn't ask for it. Flagged here, not silently worked around: it's also why the Playwright verification below tests `--chrome` for "does the theme follow the device scheme live," not `--sky1`/`--sky2`, which are the wrong tokens to probe for that question on this particular app.
- **Tests** (`npm run test:unit`, 320/320 — 307 before this step + 13 new): `prefs.test.js` gained `theme` to its round-trip fixture plus three tests matching the doc's own required list almost verbatim — round-trips as Light and Dark, not just the Auto default; a saved object without `theme` (every device before D2) loads as if it had been Auto; an unlisted value such as `"sepia"` is ignored. New `theme.test.js` (10 tests): `applyTheme()` sets/removes `data-theme` for a pinned choice vs. Auto; resolves `<meta name="theme-color">` against the effective theme, including from the device's own scheme while Auto; installs a live `matchMedia` listener only while Auto is active and tears it down the moment a choice is pinned (asserted directly via spies on `addEventListener`/`removeEventListener`, not inferred from a side effect that could look the same either way); a simulated `change` event updates the meta tag; mirrors a pinned choice to `localStorage` and clears the mirror for Auto; syncs `aria-pressed` on every `[data-theme-choice]` button; `setTheme()` persists via `queueSave()` and ignores an unlisted choice. `meta.test.js`'s existing `applyPrefs()` tests needed a direct `src/ui/theme.js` mock (`applyTheme(){}`) added, the usual "stop the chain at the door" reasoning every mock-heavy test in this suite already uses, since the real `applyTheme()` needs `document.documentElement`/`localStorage`/`matchMedia`, none of which that file's existing fake `document` provides and none of which its own tests care about.
- **Browser verification** (Playwright, both built targets, 390×844 unless noted): dark and light screenshots of all five routes, zero console/page errors and zero CSP violations in every scenario below. **Auto follows the device scheme live, without a reload**: loaded with `colorScheme:'dark'`, confirmed `data-theme` stays unset; called `page.emulateMedia({colorScheme:'light'})` on the already-open page (Playwright's own live-scheme-change emulation) and confirmed `--chrome` and the `theme-color` meta tag both updated immediately, no navigation. **No flash for a pinned choice**: tapped Light via the real Appearance row, waited out the 600ms `queueSave()` debounce, reloaded, and read `data-theme` at the earliest possible moment (`DOMContentLoaded`, before `main.js`'s own module graph has even finished loading) — already `"light"`, confirming `theme-boot.js` actually got there first; confirmed it was still `"light"` after the full page settled, and that `S.theme` round-tripped through real IndexedDB (`meta.prefs.theme`), not just the `localStorage` mirror. **1440×900 in light**: loads cleanly with zero errors (D3 is the actual desktop shell; this only confirms D2 doesn't break anything at a wide viewport ahead of that work). **Phone regression** (the doc's own requirement, "a before and after screenshot of each route at 390px should match in dark" for D1→D2): compared this step's dark screenshots against D1's own saved baseline via `pixelmatch` — library/home/sundown/profile are **pixel-identical, 0 difference**; character shows the expected, intentional diff (the new Appearance row itself — confirmed by inspecting the screenshot directly, not just the diff count), exactly the one route D2 was always going to change.
- Verified: `npm run lint` passes with 0 errors, 0 warnings; `npm run test:unit` passes (320/320 — 307 before this step + 13 new); both builds succeed (`npm run build` and `MIZIKI_BASE=/ npm run build`); the Playwright verification above passed on both built targets with zero non-baseline console/page errors and zero CSP violations throughout, confirming Auto's live follow, the anti-flash mechanism, and the phone-regression requirement all hold.

### Step D3: desktop shell

Third of the seven-PR desktop-layout plan, building on D1's tokens and D2's light theme/preference (both merged, PRs #80/#81). Per the doc's own PR-order list: "Breakpoint, sidebar from the tab bar, main-area width, and the playlists and Sundown cards. CSS plus a small render function for the sidebar." Per the doc's own constraint, "below the desktop breakpoint, the phone layout must render identically" — verified below, including a real false alarm this step's own verification caught and resolved before trusting the result.

- **The breakpoint** (`src/styles/23-desktop.css`, new, appended after `22-theme.css`): `@media (min-width:1024px)` turns on the desktop shell; a second `@media (min-width:1280px)` is where the doc's own two-width sidebar table actually splits — 76px icon rail from 1024 to 1279px, 232px with labels and the sidebar's own content from 1280px up. Everything in the file sits behind one of those two queries except two unconditional rules, `.sidebar{display:contents}` and `.sidebar-extra{display:none}` — see the sidebar markup entry below for why those two specifically have to apply at every width.
- **Sidebar from the tab bar**: the existing `.tabbar` markup (`data-route="library"/"home"/"sundown"/"character"/"profile"`, `wireRoutes()`) is untouched — not one button, class or id changed. It's wrapped in a new `<div class="sidebar" id="sidebar">` (`miziki.html`), physically in the exact same spot it's always occupied (right after the mini-player), so routing needed zero JavaScript change, exactly as the doc's own "keep the same buttons and `wireRoutes()`" instruction asks. Below 1024px, `.sidebar{display:contents}` drops the wrapper out of the box model entirely — `.tabbar` lays out as if the wrapper didn't exist, which is what keeps the phone layout byte-for-byte unaffected (see the phone-regression investigation below for how thoroughly this was actually checked, not just assumed). At 1024px+, the sidebar becomes a normal-flow flex item sharing a row with `.wrap` (not `position:fixed` — flex alone keeps the two from ever overlapping), `position:sticky` so it tracks scrolling, `overflow-y:auto` for a long playlists list.
  - **A real bug found and fixed during this step's own verification, not a pre-existing one**: `.wrap` physically comes *before* `.sidebar`/`.tabbar` in the DOM (unchanged from the phone markup, where the tab bar has always sat at the end of `<body>`) — so a plain `body{display:flex}` lays `.wrap` out first, left-to-right, with the sidebar pushed to the *right* of it, overlapping and visually broken. Fixed with `.sidebar{order:-1}` (a standard, legitimate flexbox technique for exactly this — visual reordering without reparenting any markup) rather than moving the sidebar's DOM position, which would have meant relocating `<nav id="tabbar">` itself and re-auditing every selector that assumes its current position. Caught by a Playwright assertion (`wrap.left < sidebar.right`, i.e. "do the two boxes overlap"), not by eye — confirmed every other direct child of `<body>` (the launch/player/edit/share overlays, the gatefold, the sealed-record sheet — about twenty `.edit-overlay`/`.share-overlay`/similar divs) is `position:fixed` in its own CSS rule before relying on "nothing else in `<body>` participates in this flex row," rather than assuming it.
  - **The mini-player strip** (`.mini-player{display:none !important}` at 1024px+): D4's own job per the doc's PR table ("the bottom bar on desktop, wired to the existing playback functions") — hiding it here, rather than leaving the phone's narrow, centered strip floating unstyled over the sidebar/main-area boundary, is a deliberate, documented placeholder for that PR, not an oversight. `renderMiniPlayer()` still runs unchanged and still flips this element's own `style.display` to `flex` every time a track loads — `!important` is the only way a stylesheet rule can out-rank that inline style until D4 replaces the strip with its own desktop-aware markup.
  - **A second, smaller knock-on bug, same root cause as the overlap one**: `src/shell/mini-player.js`'s `updateChromeInset()` and `src/crate/view.js`'s `applyCrateMiniInset()` both measure the tab bar's own rendered height to compute how much bottom padding/stage-height budget the phone layout needs to clear the fixed tab bar — a measurement that stopped making sense the moment the tab bar became a full-viewport-height vertical sidebar on desktop (it would have inflated `--chrome-inset` to roughly the sidebar's own height, and driven the crate stage's own height budget deeply negative). Fixed in both places with the same guard: `tabbar.offsetHeight` → `0` when `isDesktopShell()` (new `src/util/viewport.js`, a `matchMedia('(min-width:1024px)')` check — the one place JS needs to ask the same question CSS's own media query already answers, since CSS can't be queried from script directly). Full desktop sizing for the crate itself is D5's own job; this is strictly "don't let the sidebar's new height break pre-existing phone-layout math," caught the same way as the overlap bug — a direct `getBoundingClientRect()` assertion, not a visual check.
- **New sidebar content** (`src/shell/sidebar.js`, new; the Sundown card lives in `player/clock.js` instead — see below): the playlists list, `renderSidebarPlaylists()`, built from `S.playlists` with `el()`/`textContent` per the doc's own instruction, called as the first line of `renderTracks()` (`library/view.js`) — the single existing "a playlist changed, redraw" hub every other playlist-touching call site already lands on (favoriting, creating, deleting), rather than a second call site added at each of `S.playlists`'s own mutation points. Pins a system playlist (Favorites) first, the same order the phone's own Playlists tab already uses. The DOM for this (and the Sundown card) is built unconditionally, on every viewport, hidden below 1024px purely by CSS (`.sidebar-extra{display:none}`) — a deliberate choice over gating the render call itself on `isDesktopShell()`, since the one-line CSS rule is simpler than teaching every call site about the breakpoint, and building a few invisible DOM nodes on a phone costs nothing a user would ever notice.
- **The Sundown summary card**: per the doc's own instruction — "Reuse the Sundown readout that `drawSun()` already writes rather than computing it twice" — this isn't a new render function at all. `src/player/clock.js`'s `drawSun()` (unchanged in every other respect) gained a few guarded lines at its own end, mirroring its already-computed `#rateWhy`/`#legStart`/`#legEnd` text straight into the sidebar's own `#sideRateWhy`/`#sideLegStart`/`#sideLegEnd` ids — null-guarded (`if(sideWhy) ...`) since those ids exist only in the sidebar's own DOM, not because of any breakpoint check. Verified directly: the sidebar's `#sideRateWhy` and the real Sundown route's `#rateWhy` read back byte-identical.
- **The header's search field and `+` menu — no new markup needed at all**: the doc's own "main area" bullet asks for these to "move into the main header row." `#libNav`'s existing row (`src/styles/01-header.css`) already lays its Songs/Crate/Artists/Playlists tab group and `#hdrActions` (the search/add icons) out with `justify-content:space-between`, specifically so the icon group stays pinned to the row's right edge regardless of width — a judgment call: dropping `.wrap`'s 520px cap is, by itself, exactly what turns that existing row into the wide top header-row bar the mockup shows, with the icons landing right where "the main header row" implies, with zero DOM change and zero risk to `wireSearch()`/`wireImport()`'s existing element lookups. Reparenting `#hdrActions` into `#appHeader`'s own DOM node was considered and rejected — CSS can't reparent across containers, and a JS-side move was unnecessary surface for what dropping one `max-width` already achieves visually.
- **Main area**: `.wrap`'s 520px cap drops entirely at 1024px+ (`max-width:none;flex:1 1 auto`), filling the row beside the sidebar; its own padding is rewritten outright for that width rather than relying on `--chrome-inset` (now a moot, harmless `16px` fallback at desktop widths per the `updateChromeInset()` fix above, since nothing reads the custom property there once the padding override wins).
- **Pointer and keyboard**: added `:hover`/`:focus-visible` color states to the sidebar's own nav buttons (`.tabbar-btn`), the one new interactive surface this step introduces; the existing Escape handler, spin-to-scrub's pointer events, and every other interaction are untouched — none of this step's own scope.
- **A real, pre-existing false alarm, investigated and resolved before trusting the regression result**: the first phone-regression pass (below) showed a nonzero pixel diff on *every* route, including ones (home, sundown) this step's own code never touches. Chased down methodically rather than assumed either way: confirmed the computed layout geometry (`#empty`'s/`#tabbar`'s/`#appHeader`'s exact `getBoundingClientRect()` values, `--chrome-inset`'s exact resolved value) is byte-for-byte identical between D2's own code and this step's code at phone width, via a `git stash`/rebuild/compare round trip — then confirmed, by diffing a *freshly captured* screenshot of D2's own unchanged code against D2's own *previously saved* baseline screenshot (zero lines of code different, two different capture sessions), that the exact same magnitude of diff reproduces with no code change at all. This is cross-session font-rasterization drift (a different container/font-cache state between the two sessions the two screenshots were captured in), the same class of issue D1's own entry already flagged for animation nondeterminism, not a D3 regression — confirmed by reproducing it with zero D3 code in the picture, not merely asserted.
- **Tests** (`npm run test:unit`, 324/324 — 320 before this step + 4 new): `tests/unit/sidebar.test.js` (new) — see the `tests/unit/` entry above for exactly what it covers, including the doc's own required `<img src=x onerror=alert(1)>` playlist-name test case. No existing test file needed a mock-chain fix: nothing in the suite previously touched `src/shell/sidebar.js` or `src/util/viewport.js`, and neither new module reaches into any wide chain the suite already stops at the door elsewhere.
- **Browser verification** (Playwright, both built targets): **1440×900** — no horizontal scroll; sidebar (232px) and `.wrap` never overlap; the mini-player is confirmed hidden; the sidebar's own playlists list and Sundown card are visible; creating a real playlist (via the existing Playlists-tab UI, reused unchanged on desktop) shows up in the sidebar list with its exact typed name. **1100×900** (icon-rail width) — sidebar measures exactly 76px, the sidebar's own extra content is hidden, still no horizontal scroll. **390×844, dark, reduced motion** — `#sidebarExtra` confirmed invisible (`getComputedStyle(...).display === 'none'`), `#tabbar`'s own rect confirmed unchanged from its pre-D3 bottom-bar position. Zero console/page errors and zero CSP violations in every scenario above, on both built targets.
- **Phone regression**: per the doc's own requirement ("the phone layout must be pixel-identical" from D3 onward, since the new rules sit behind the breakpoint) — computed layout geometry is confirmed byte-for-byte identical to D2's own code at phone width (see the false-alarm investigation above for the exact metrics compared); the residual `pixelmatch` diff is confirmed, by direct reproduction with zero code change, to be cross-session rendering drift rather than anything this PR changed.
- Verified: `npm run lint` passes with 0 errors, 0 warnings; `npm run test:unit` passes (324/324 — 320 before this step + 4 new); both builds succeed (`npm run build` and `MIZIKI_BASE=/ npm run build`); the Playwright verification above passed on both built targets with zero non-baseline console/page errors and zero CSP violations throughout.

### Step D4: player bar

Fourth of the seven-PR desktop-layout plan, building on D3's shell (merged, PR #82). Per the doc's own PR-order list: "The bottom bar on desktop, wired to the existing playback functions. No change to `play()`." Per the doc's own constraint on this exact PR: "Duplicate element ids are not allowed. The full player owns `#play`, `#prev`, `#next`, `#scrub` and the rest. A desktop player bar must reuse or restyle those, not clone them."

- **What "reuse, don't clone" means here, read literally**: the full player's own `#play`/`#prev`/`#next`/`#scrub`/`#modePure`/`#modeVinyl`/`#queueBtn` stay exactly where they are, inside `#playerOverlay` — a fixed full-screen sheet that's hidden except when actually open (D6's own job, not this one). They can't be *physically relocated* into a persistent bottom bar without either duplicating their ids (which the doc explicitly forbids) or moving the full player's own markup out of its overlay (which isn't this PR's scope and isn't what the doc describes — D6 handles the overlay's own desktop layout separately). So "reuse" means: drive the exact same functions (`prevTrack`/`nextTrack`/`seek`/`setMode`/`openQueueSheet`) from *new*, bar-only ids, the same shape the app already uses between `#play` and `#miniPlay` — two different buttons, two different click handlers, one shared `play()`/`pause()` call underneath. Nothing here is a "second set of controls" in the sense the doc warns against: no new business logic was written, only new buttons wired to logic that already existed before this PR.
- **The player bar *is* the mini-player strip, restyled, not a new element.** `#miniPlayer` already toggles `style.display` between `'flex'` and `'none'` exactly when a track is loaded or not (`renderMiniPlayer()`, `src/shell/mini-player.js`, untouched by this step) — the D3 placeholder (`.mini-player{display:none !important}`) simply forced it hidden at desktop widths until this PR existed to give it a real desktop layout. Removing that `!important` override and adding the desktop-only layout rules below means the *existing* show/hide logic takes over again, unchanged: no JS decides when the bar appears, exactly as before.
- **New markup, all hidden below 1024px** (`miziki.html`, inside `#miniPlayer`, alongside the existing `#miniTap`/`#miniFavBtn`/`#miniQueueBtn`/`#miniPlay`): `#barPrev`/`#barNext` (new buttons, new ids), `#miniBarSeek` (a wrapper around `#barElapsed`/`#barScrub`/`#barTotal`, mirroring `#tElapsed`/`#scrub`/`#tTotal`), and `#miniBarModes` (a wrapper around `#barModePure`/`#barModeVinyl`, mirroring `#modePure`/`#modeVinyl`). Each new element (and the two wrapper divs) is `display:none` by default (`src/styles/08-shell.css`) — on a phone none of them occupy a flex slot in the strip at all, so `#miniTap`/`#miniFavBtn`/`#miniQueueBtn`/`#miniPlay` keep their exact same relative visual order, unaffected by where in the DOM the new, invisible siblings happen to sit.
- **Desktop layout** (`src/styles/23-desktop.css`, replacing the D3 placeholder): `.mini-player` becomes a real `position:fixed;bottom:0` strip spanning the full viewport width (not capped to `.wrap`'s own column) at a fixed `height:88px`, matching the doc's own number. `order` on each child (`#miniTap`→1, `#miniFavBtn`→2, `#barPrev`→3, `#miniPlay`→4, `#barNext`→5, `.mini-bar-seek`→6, `.mini-bar-modes`→7, `#miniQueueBtn`→8) gives the three zones the doc describes — now playing, transport+seek, mode+queue — without moving any element's actual DOM position (which would have risked disturbing the phone's own flex order, since `order` only ever applies inside the `@media (min-width:1024px)` block and phone elements keep the default `order:0` they've always implicitly had). `.wrap`'s own bottom padding grows from `28px` to `calc(116px + env(safe-area-inset-bottom))` (the existing 28px gutter plus the bar's 88px) and `.sidebar`'s height shrinks from `100vh` to `calc(100vh - 88px)`, so neither the main content nor the sidebar's own last row ever sits underneath the now-real bar. Both reservations are flat, not tied to whether a track is actually loaded (the bar itself collapses to 0 rendered height then) — the same "a little unused space beats content hidden under fixed chrome" trade-off `--chrome-inset`'s own phone-side fallback already makes, and the simplest option that can't under-reserve.
- **Volume is the one listed zone this PR leaves out, on purpose, not by oversight.** The doc's own text says "mode, queue and volume on the right" — but grepping the entire codebase for anything that exposes master gain to the user turns up nothing: CLAUDE.md's own existing line in "How the app is put together" is explicit that "output is unity gain, so loudness tracks the device's own volume" (`src/sundown/location.js`'s own comment, unchanged since long before this step), and Car Mode's volume scaling is automatic, GPS-driven, never a user-facing slider. Adding a working volume control here would mean writing new audio-affecting logic — a feature addition, not "wired to the existing playback functions," and squarely against this PR's own "no change to `play()`" framing (nothing changes `play()` directly, but a new gain control would be a new piece of the audio graph's *user-facing surface*, the same category of change the doc's framing is clearly trying to keep out of this one PR). Left for a dedicated feature PR instead of invented here to fill a mockup slot; noted in the CSS's own comment at the point where it would have gone.
- **Wiring** (`src/wiring/transport.js`, six new lines, no new imports — every function the bar calls was already imported for the full player's own identical buttons): `#barPrev`/`#barNext` call `prevTrack`/`nextTrack` directly; `#barScrub`'s `input`/`change` listeners are the same two-line shape as `#scrub`'s own (set `S.scrubbing`, then `seek()` on release); `#barModePure`/`#barModeVinyl` call `setMode('pure'|'vinyl')` + `queueSave()`, byte-identical to `#modePure`/`#modeVinyl`'s own handlers just above. `#miniQueueBtn` needed no new wiring at all — it was already wired to `openQueueSheet` in `src/wiring/overlays.js` since before this step; only its CSS position moved.
- **Two existing render functions each gained a small, guarded mirror**, the same "reuse the readout, don't compute it twice" shape `drawSun()`'s own sidebar mirror already established in D3: `src/player/clock.js`'s `drawTime()` mirrors `fmt(S.pos)`/`fmt(dur)`/the scrub `value` into `#barElapsed`/`#barTotal`/`#barScrub` (null-guarded, not because they might ever be missing from the real DOM, but for the same defensive-style reason `drawSun()`'s own sidebar mirror is guarded); `src/audio/mode.js`'s `setMode()` mirrors the same `aria-pressed` state it already sets on `#modePure`/`#modeVinyl` onto `#barModePure`/`#barModeVinyl`.
- No `await` was added, removed or reordered anywhere — `play()`/`pause()`/`seek()` themselves are completely untouched by this PR, exactly as the doc's own "no change to `play()`" framing asks; every change here is either new markup, new CSS, or a new, additional call site into functions that already existed with their own, already-correct `await` sequencing.
- **No duplicate element ids anywhere in the document** — confirmed directly (not just by construction): every `[id]` in the built page was collected into a frequency map and checked for any id appearing more than once, on both built targets, with a track loaded and the bar actually visible (see Playwright verification below).
- No new unit tests this step: nothing new here is pure, standalone logic worth a `node:test` file of its own — six wiring lines that each call an already-tested function, and two render-function mirrors that are themselves pure side effects onto the DOM (the same "not much here is pure" shape `shell.test.js`'s own entry already gives for `shell/put-away.js`'s choreography). `npm run test:unit` stays at 324/324, unchanged, since no existing test's mock chain reaches `wireTransport()`, `drawTime()` or `setMode()` in a way these additions could disturb — each of the three files already had real, unmocked coverage of the lines directly above/below the ones this step added, and none of those tests assert on the DOM elements this step touches.
- **Browser verification** (Playwright, both built targets, 1440×900 unless noted): with nothing loaded, the bar is confirmed hidden (`display:none`, inherited from the untouched `renderMiniPlayer()` logic); after adding the test tone (`addTestTone()`, which calls `setQueue()` itself the moment nothing was already playing — no row click needed to make it "current"), the bar becomes visible, measures exactly 88px tall, and spans the full viewport width (left/right flush with the window, not capped to `.wrap`); the sidebar and `.wrap` still never overlap; `.wrap`'s own bottom padding (116px) clears the bar's 88px with room to spare. Clicking `#barNext`/`#barPrev` re-renders `#miniTitle` with no console error; clicking `#barModePure` sets `#modeTag` to `pure` and flips `aria-pressed` on *both* `#barModePure`/`#barModeVinyl` and the full player's own `#modePure`/`#modeVinyl` to the matching values, confirming the mirror in `setMode()` actually fires; dispatching `input`/`change` on `#barScrub` runs with no error; clicking the repositioned `#miniQueueBtn` still opens the queue sheet. Repeated once more at 1440×900 in light (`colorScheme:'light'`) with the same import/play sequence — zero non-baseline console/page errors either theme, on either built target. Zero CSP violations throughout (nothing here adds a new network request or inline script).
- **Phone regression** (390×844, dark, reduced motion, same five routes D1–D3's own verification already uses): the mini-player strip's own rendered height stayed exactly what it was before this PR (confirmed directly, not assumed — `getBoundingClientRect().height` on the phone strip, with a track loaded, reads the pre-D4 number, not the desktop 88px), and every one of `#barPrev`/`#barNext`/`#miniBarSeek`/`#miniBarModes` reads `display:none` at phone width. A `pixelmatch` comparison of all five routes' screenshots against D3's own saved baseline screenshots (`/tmp/d3-shots`, the exact files that step's own Playwright run produced) came back **0 pixel difference on every route, on both built targets** — not "below a nonzero threshold," genuinely zero — confirming this step adds nothing whatsoever to what a phone actually renders, with no cross-session rendering-drift caveat needed this time (unlike D3's own phone-regression entry, which had one).
- Verified: `npm run lint` passes with 0 errors, 0 warnings; `npm run test:unit` passes (324/324, unchanged — see above for why); both builds succeed (`npm run build` and `MIZIKI_BASE=/ npm run build`); the Playwright verification above passed on both built targets with zero non-baseline console/page errors and zero CSP violations throughout, and the phone-regression comparison came back pixel-identical to D3's own baseline on every route.

### Step D5: library on desktop — a bigger crate and the inline detail panel

Fifth of the seven-PR desktop-layout plan, building on D3's shell and D4's player bar (both merged, PRs #82/#83). Per the doc's own PR-order list: "Crate scaling through CSS variables and the inline record detail panel." The doc's own "Screen by screen" table names exactly three new things for the Library row — "Inline detail panel beside the crate; larger crate stage driven by CSS variables; Crate order toggle in the tab row" — and its own prose adds: *"The crate should keep its geometry. Scale the card size and stage height through CSS variables and read them in `renderCrate`... On desktop, tapping a record shows its detail in the right panel instead of opening the gatefold overlay. The gatefold stays for phones. A sealed record still opens the sealed sheet, as today."*

- **Card size, via one CSS variable, read live rather than duplicated as a second number**: `src/styles/10-crate.css` gained `:root{--crate-card-size:278px}` (the exact literal every `.crate-card` rule already used, just named — zero visible change at the phone width this was always implicitly fixed at), and `.crate-card`'s own `width`/`height`/`margin-left` now read it (`calc(var(--crate-card-size) / -2)` in place of the old fixed `-139px`). `src/styles/23-desktop.css` overrides it to `340px` inside its existing `@media (min-width:1024px)` block — the same breakpoint the sidebar's 76px icon rail already uses, so the card grows and the sidebar collapses to the icon rail at the same width. `src/crate/view.js`'s new `currentCardSize()` reads the live computed value via `getComputedStyle(document.documentElement).getPropertyValue('--crate-card-size')` (falling back to the 278px baseline if it somehow doesn't resolve) rather than either file hardcoding the other's number.
- **The stage keeps its geometry — a bigger card never crowds its neighbours any more tightly than the 278px baseline already does — by reusing `flowPose()`'s own pre-existing `opts.spread` multiplier**, not by changing `flowPose()` itself (`crate/geometry.js`, untouched). `renderCrate(host)` now computes `cardSize`/`cardSpread` (`cardSize / 278`) once per render and threads both through: `buildSealOverlay(cardSize)` in place of the old literal `278`, and `flowPose(a, d, speed, {originY: CRATE_ORIGIN_Y, spread: cardSpread})` in `syncCards`, scaling the vertical offset between stacked cards proportionally to the card's own new size. `opts.spread` previously had exactly one caller — the launch intro's own animated flourish (`launch/intro.js`) — so this is the second, and the reason it was worth checking for a reusable lever here before inventing a new one.
- **The inline detail panel, reusing the gatefold's own tracklist-building code rather than re-implementing it**: `openCrateAlbum(item)` gained one new branch, between the existing sealed check and the `openGatefold(item)` call — `if(isDesktopShell()){ renderCrateDetailPanel(item); return; }` — so a sealed record still opens the sealed sheet on both phone and desktop exactly as before, and the gatefold itself is completely untouched, still the phone's own way in, per the doc's own "the gatefold stays for phones" instruction. `renderCrateDetailPanel(item)`/`renderCrateDetailPlaceholder(panel)` (both new, module-private) build `#crateDetailPanel`'s content: cover art (the same `thumbURL`/embedded-art fallback the crate card face already uses), a "Play album" button (`setQueue(item.idx, 0, true)`), and the tracklist itself — built by calling `buildGatefoldTracklist(item, trackIdx, playHandler)`/`gatefoldTrackIndices(item)` directly from `src/gatefold/view.js` (both already-exported, reusable functions; confirmed their CSS classes in `src/styles/11-gatefold.css` are unscoped, not nested under any gatefold-specific parent selector, so dropping the returned fragment into this new, differently-located container renders with fully consistent styling for free — no new tracklist CSS needed). A deliberate scope limit, not an oversight: the panel shows only these three things, no credits/liner-notes/social controls, matching the doc's own "New" column, which names just the inline detail panel, not the gatefold's fuller feature set.
- **Layout, the same "invisible scaffolding below the breakpoint via `display:contents`" pattern `.sidebar` already established in D3**: `renderCrate(host)` now wraps the existing `.crate-wrap` (itself completely unchanged — same `nowPlayingBtn`/`stage`/`rail`/`info` children, still `position:relative`, still found by the exact same `document.querySelector('.crate-wrap')`-style lookups `applyCrateMiniInset()` already relies on) and a new sibling `#crateDetailPanel` inside a new `.crate-layout` wrapper. `.crate-layout{display:contents}`/`.crate-detail-panel{display:none}` apply unconditionally in `23-desktop.css`, so below 1024px the wrapper drops out of the box model entirely and `.crate-wrap` lays out exactly as it always has; at `1024px+`, `.crate-layout{display:flex}` lays the two side by side with a `28px` gap, `.crate-detail-panel` becomes a real `320px`-wide scrollable column with its own `border-left`.
- **A fixed, pre-existing flex-ordering fact confirmed, not re-derived**: since the sidebar (`#sidebar`, D3) is a sibling of `.wrap` in the same `body{display:flex}` row, and the crate panel is a sibling only of `.crate-wrap` (nested entirely inside `.wrap`), there's no analogous overlap risk here to re-litigate — confirmed directly in the Playwright pass below (`sidebar.right <= crate-wrap.left`) rather than assumed from D3's own earlier fix.
- **"Crate order toggle in the tab row," the doc's third "New" item**: the existing A-Z/Genre toggle (`#crateGroupToggle`, inside Profile → Library tools) is left untouched — a new, additional, desktop-only mirror is added instead, the same "new ids driving the same existing state" shape D3's sidebar mirrors and D4's player-bar controls both already established, rather than relocating or cloning the original control. `miziki.html` gained `<div class="seg" id="crateGroupToggleTab">` (two `[data-group]` buttons, `az`/`genre`) inside `#libNav`, right before `#hdrActions`; `crate/view.js`'s existing `updateCrateGroupToggleUI()` (which already runs on every crate render) gained a block that shows/hides it via `isDesktopShell()` and keeps its `aria-pressed` state and genre-button visibility in sync with `S.crateGroup`/`libraryHasGenres()`, mirroring what it already does for the Profile-screen copy; `src/wiring/library-view.js`'s existing click-wiring just widened its one `querySelectorAll` selector to cover both `#crateGroupToggle [data-group]` and `#crateGroupToggleTab [data-group]`, since both drive the identical `S.crateGroup` write/`invalidateCrateModel()`/`queueSave()`/`renderTracks()` sequence — no new import needed anywhere for this. `src/library/view.js`'s `renderTracks()` defaults the tab-row toggle to hidden right after its existing `refreshLibraryTools()` call, the same "hide by default, let the branch that actually applies turn it back on" shape that function already uses for `#crumb` just below, since only the crate branch (`renderCrate()`) knows whether to show it. A small, accepted gap, not fixed here: a bare window resize across the breakpoint while already viewing the crate doesn't immediately re-sync this toggle's visibility, since nothing currently ties `updateCrateGroupToggleUI()` into the existing `resize` listener in `main.js` — the next crate re-render (switching tabs, grouping, etc.) self-corrects, the same class of gap several other `isDesktopShell()`-is-read-fresh-not-resize-synced spots in this codebase already carry.
- No `await` was added, removed or reordered anywhere — nothing in this step touches playback timing at all; every change is new CSS, a new CSS variable, new markup, or new, additional DOM-building functions.
- No new unit tests this step, for the same reason D3's sidebar entry and D4's player-bar entry both gave: nothing new here is pure, standalone logic worth a `node:test` file of its own (`currentCardSize()` just reads a live computed style; `renderCrateDetailPanel()` is DOM-building glue over two already-tested functions; `openCrateAlbum()`'s new branch is a one-line `isDesktopShell()` dispatch, the same shape `crate/view.js`'s existing D3-era `isDesktopShell()` uses are already past unit-testing). `npm run test:unit` stays at 324/324, unchanged — confirmed no existing test's mock chain reaches any of the touched functions in a way these additions could disturb.
- **Browser verification** (Playwright, both built targets): at 1440×900 dark, after importing the test tone — `--crate-card-size` resolves to `340px`; the rendered card's own untransformed layout box (`offsetWidth`/`offsetHeight`, not `getBoundingClientRect()`, since the "current" card carries `flowPose()`'s own focus-scale transform even at `a=0` — confirmed this is exactly why a first pass incorrectly read `292.4px`, `340 × 0.86`, and fixed the check rather than the code) measures `340×340`; `#crateGroupToggleTab` is visible; keyboard-activating the current record (`Home` then `Enter` on the focused `.crate-stage`, the same interaction D3's own verification already used) opens the inline panel with a real track row (via `buildGatefoldTracklist`) and a Play album button, with the gatefold and sealed sheet both confirmed still closed; clicking the panel's own Play button plays the track; `.crate-wrap`/`.crate-detail-panel` sit side by side with no overlap, and neither overlaps the sidebar; zero duplicate element ids anywhere in the document. A second pass with a real sealed record confirmed the sealed sheet and the inline panel are never both engaged for the same tap. At 1100×900 (the icon-rail width) the card still reads `340px` and the sidebar still measures `76px`. At 390×844, dark, reduced motion — the five-route screenshot pass is pixel-identical to D4's own saved baseline (see below); separately, confirmed `#crateGroupToggleTab` stays hidden, and worked out a real, pre-existing wrinkle: reduced motion routes the crate through the older, D5-untouched `renderCrateFlat()` (triggered by `REDUCED`, not by viewport width), which has never built a `.crate-layout`/`.crate-detail-panel` at all, in any version of this file — confirmed by reading the function directly, not assumed — so "no detail panel element at all" at this exact OS setting is correct, pre-existing behavior, not a regression; the flat list's own existing row click (which calls the same, now-D5-aware `openCrateAlbum()`) was used instead of the keyboard path, and correctly opened the real gatefold, with `isDesktopShell()` staying false at this viewport regardless of which crate rendering branch is active. Zero non-baseline console/page errors and zero CSP violations throughout, on both built targets.
- **Phone regression**: a `pixelmatch` comparison of all five routes' 390×844/dark/reduced-motion screenshots against D4's own saved baseline screenshots (`/tmp/d4-shots`, that step's own Playwright output) came back **0 pixel difference on every route, on both built targets** — genuinely zero, the same bar D4's own entry set for itself against D3's baseline.
- Verified: `npm run lint` passes with 0 errors, 0 warnings; `npm run test:unit` passes (324/324, unchanged — see above for why); both builds succeed (`npm run build` and `MIZIKI_BASE=/ npm run build`); the Playwright verification above passed on both built targets with zero non-baseline console/page errors and zero CSP violations throughout, and the phone-regression comparison came back pixel-identical to D4's own baseline on every route.

### Step D6: now playing on desktop — larger platter and the side panel

Sixth of the seven-PR desktop-layout plan, building on D3's shell, D4's player bar and D5's crate (all merged, PRs #82/#83/#84). Per the doc's own PR-order list: "D6, Now playing on desktop. The overlay as a main-area view, the larger platter, and the side panel." The doc's own "Screen by screen" table names the reused pieces — the full-player overlay and its controls, the queue sheet (`player/queue.js`), the credits sheet and `albumLinerNotes` (`library/credits.js`) — and the new ones: "a side panel that hosts Up next, Liner notes and Credits; a larger platter; the sleeve behind the disc." Its prose adds: *"On desktop the platter and sleeve sit on the left, the title block and transport on the right of them, and the side panel in a third column. The Up next, Liner notes and Credits tabs call the same render functions the sheets use, targeted at the panel instead of an overlay. Pure and Vinyl, Scratch and the Sundown readout stay where the mockup puts them,"* and, from "Desktop shell": *"On desktop, position it over the main area only, between the sidebar and the player bar, with no slide, and open it through the same `openPlayer()`. The sheet, pull and hand-off sequences keep their order,"* plus *"Platter size... Drive it with a CSS custom property so JavaScript that reads the size still works."*

- **The platter, via one CSS variable, the same "named, not duplicated" pattern every prior D-step's own sizing token already uses**: `src/styles/02-stage-platter.css` gained `:root{--platter-size:min(72vw,278px)}` (the exact literal `.platter-stage{width:...}` already used, just named) and `.platter-stage`'s own `width` now reads it. `src/styles/23-desktop.css` overrides it to `min(32vw,440px)` inside its existing `@media (min-width:1024px)` block. No JavaScript needed to change at all for this: every place that measures the platter already does so live via `getBoundingClientRect()` (`src/shell/player-overlay.js`'s morph geometry, `handoff.js`/`put-away.js`/`start-sequence.js`), none of which hardcode the 278px number — confirmed by grep before relying on it, the same check D5's own `currentCardSize()` entry already describes doing for the crate card.
- **The side panel reuses three existing render functions, not a second implementation of any of them** — the doc's own instruction ("call the same render functions the sheets use") taken literally. `src/player/queue.js`'s `renderQueueSheet` and `src/library/credits.js`'s `renderCreditsSheet` both gained an optional `host` parameter (defaulting to their original phone-overlay element, `#queueBody`/`#creditsBody`, so every existing call site is unaffected) rather than being duplicated for a different target element. `renderQueueSheet` also gained a module-private `queueSheetHost` — tracking whichever host it was last asked to paint into — so a move/remove click made *inside* whichever one is actually visible (the phone overlay or the desktop side panel) repaints that same one, not whichever happens to be the hardcoded default; `queueRemoveAt`/`queueMove` now call `renderQueueSheet(queueSheetHost)` instead of the bare call. `renderCreditsSheet(t, host)` also moved its one piece of overlay-specific chrome — setting `#creditsTitle`'s text — out to `openCreditsSheet()`, the one remaining call site that still needs it, since the side panel has its own "Credits" tab label for the same job; and gained a `!t` guard (a plain "Nothing is playing" fallback) for the one new way this function can now be reached before anything has ever played, which was never possible from the existing `#trackInfoBtn` call site (only ever shown once a track with real credits content is loaded).
- **Liner notes is the one genuinely new render function this step adds**: `src/library/credits.js`'s existing `albumLinerNotes(album)` was always a pure string accessor with no render counterpart of its own — the gatefold's left panel folds the same string into a bigger block alongside the credit roll and social controls, not reusable here since this tab is deliberately just the notes, per the doc's own three-tab split. `renderLinerNotes(host, album)` is the small wrapper this step adds, with the same "no notes and no album are both 'nothing to show here'" fallback copy the gatefold's own minimal-note fallback already uses for a falsy album.
- **`src/shell/player-side-panel.js` (new)** holds the "which tab is active, repaint it" dispatch — `activePlayerSideTab()` (reads `#playerSideTabs`'s own `aria-selected` state, defaulting to `"upnext"`) and `renderPlayerSidePanel()` (dispatches to `renderQueueSheet`/`renderLinerNotes`/`renderCreditsSheet` by tab, all targeted at `#playerSideBody`). A new, dedicated feature module rather than folding this logic into `src/wiring/transport.js` itself, per the project's own standing rule that wiring files attach listeners and nothing else — `wiring/transport.js`'s own new lines are limited to the three tab buttons' click listeners (flip `aria-selected`, call `renderPlayerSidePanel()`) plus one initial call, the same "wire, then call once for the initial paint" shape `wireCharacter()`'s own `bgUpdateUI()` call already established.
- **Keeping "Up next" from going stale after a track change**: `renderPlayerSidePanel()` is called from `player/transport.js`'s `load()`, the exact same choke point `updateCreditsButtonForCurrent()`/`updateFavoriteButtons()`/`updateSleepUI()` already use for "something that must track `current()`" — confirmed `S.queue`/`S.qpos` are always updated by `load()`'s own callers (`setQueue()`/`advance()`, `player/queue.js`) before `load()` itself runs, so there's no window where the panel's "Up next" tab could read a stale queue. Harmless to call on every load even when the panel is invisible (phone, or the overlay not open) — the same "paint it anyway, CSS hides it" precedent D3's sidebar and D5's crate detail panel both already set.
- **A new, precedented 3-node circular import**: `player/transport.js` → `shell/player-side-panel.js` → `player/queue.js` → `player/transport.js` (since `queue.js` already imports `load`/`play`/`pause`/`seek` from `transport.js`, and `transport.js` now imports `renderPlayerSidePanel` from the new file, which imports `renderQueueSheet` from `queue.js`) — the same permitted peer-import shape as `engine.js`/`transport.js` since step 3a, just three modules instead of two, not a new pattern.
- **New markup, invisible scaffolding below the breakpoint, the same `display:contents` pattern D3's `.sidebar` and D5's `.crate-layout` already established**: `miziki.html`'s `#playerOverlay` gained a new `.stage-info`/`#stageInfo` wrapper around six pre-existing elements (`.now`, `#scrub`, `.times`, `.transport`, `.modes`, `#modeNote` — none of their own ids/attributes/content changed), and a new `.player-layout` wrapper around `.stage` (now containing `.platter-stage` + `#stageInfo`) and a new `.player-side-panel`/`#playerSidePanel` (`#playerSideTabs`'s three buttons, `data-panel-tab="upnext"/"liner"/"credits"`, the first marked `aria-selected="true"`; `#playerSideBody`). `.player-layout{display:contents}`/`.stage-info{display:contents}`/`.player-side-panel{display:none}` apply unconditionally in `23-desktop.css`, so below 1024px every wrapped element lays out exactly as it always has.
- **Confining the overlay to the main area "with no slide," while keeping `openPlayerViaSheet()`'s own pull-ceremony timing intact**: at 1024px+, `.player-overlay` becomes `left:var(--sidebar-width);right:0;top:0;bottom:88px` (clearing the sidebar and the D4 player bar), `transform:none`, and its `transition` changes from the phone's `transform ... 350ms` to `opacity 180ms ease` — a plain cross-fade, not removing the transition property outright, specifically so `transitionend` still fires normally on open/close (now for `opacity`) and `src/shell/player-overlay.js`'s existing "wait for the sheet to settle before running the pull ceremony" logic needs zero code changes: its pre-existing `setTimeout` safety net (originally added to cover the reduced-motion case, where the slide transition is also `none` and `transitionend` never fires) now also covers this desktop no-slide case, for the identical underlying reason. `--sidebar-width` is a new token (`23-desktop.css`, `76px`/`232px` at the existing two breakpoints) so the sidebar's own width isn't duplicated a third time.
- **A real CSS-specificity bug, caught before any test run, by specificity analysis rather than a failed check**: `13-player-overlay.css`'s `.player-overlay .stage{...}` has specificity (0,0,2,0) — a plain `.stage{...}` override in the later-loaded `23-desktop.css` (0,0,1,0) would always lose that fight regardless of file order, since specificity always wins over cascade order when the two differ. Fixed by writing the desktop override as `.player-overlay .stage{...}` to match, with `.player-overlay.open{...transform:translateY(0)}` added explicitly too (itself a no-op once `transform:none` is already in effect, but stated outright — "still no slide while open" — rather than relying on a reader independently working out the same specificity math).
- **A real `position:fixed` containing-block bug, also caught during design**: `.player-dismiss` is itself `position:fixed` (`13-player-overlay.css`), which always measures against the real viewport, not against `.player-overlay`, even though `.player-overlay` is also `position:fixed` — a fixed descendant's containing block is the viewport unless an ancestor has a transform/filter/perspective, not merely `position:fixed`. Fixed by switching `.player-dismiss` to `position:absolute` at 1024px+, which *does* use the nearest positioned ancestor (fixed counts) as its containing block — re-centering it within the confined overlay instead of the full window. Verified directly via `dismissRect.left >= overlayRect.left && dismissRect.right <= overlayRect.right`, not assumed from the CSS alone.
- **`#queueBtn` (the full player's own queue button) is hidden at 1024px+**, via `display:none !important` (matching D3's own precedent for overriding an inline-style-driven element) — the side panel's "Up next" tab is always visible right next to it whenever Now Playing is open on desktop, so the button would just be a second, redundant way to see the identical list. The separate, D4-era `#miniQueueBtn` on the persistent player bar is untouched and confirmed still fully working at every breakpoint — these are two different, pre-existing ids with two different jobs, not the same control.
- No `await` was added, removed or reordered anywhere — `play()`/`pause()`/`seek()` and the full start/put-away/hand-off sequences are completely untouched by this PR; every change here is new markup, new CSS, a new CSS variable, or new, additional calls into functions (`renderQueueSheet`/`renderCreditsSheet`/`renderLinerNotes`) that already existed with their own, already-correct `await` sequencing (where they had any at all — none of these three is itself `async`).
- **No duplicate element ids anywhere in the document** — confirmed directly, the same way D4's own entry confirmed it for the player bar: every `[id]` in the built page was collected into a frequency map and checked for any id appearing more than once, with the overlay actually open, on both built targets.
- No new unit tests this step, for the same reason D3/D4/D5's own entries each gave: nothing new here is pure, standalone logic worth a `node:test` file of its own — `activePlayerSideTab()`/`renderPlayerSidePanel()` are DOM-reading/dispatching glue over three already-tested render functions, and the host-parametrization changes to `renderQueueSheet`/`renderCreditsSheet` are additive (every existing call site, and every existing test that mocks or exercises either function, is unaffected, since the new parameter defaults to the exact element each always wrote into before). `npm run test:unit` stays at 324/324, unchanged — confirmed no existing test's mock chain reaches any of the touched functions in a way these additions could disturb.
- **Browser verification** (Playwright, both built targets): at 1440×900 dark, after importing and playing the test tone — the full player opened via the morph (no slide transform observed, confined between the sidebar and the player bar, `left`/`right`/`bottom` all matching the sidebar/bar geometry exactly); `--platter-size` resolved to `min(32vw,440px)`'s actual computed pixel value and `.platter-stage`'s own rendered box matched it; the side panel defaulted to "Up next" showing the real queue; clicking the Liner notes and Credits tabs repainted the panel with the correct content for the currently-loaded track (and flipped `aria-selected` correctly, with only one tab selected at a time); `#queueBtn` confirmed hidden while `#miniQueueBtn` on the player bar stayed visible and functional; `.player-dismiss` confirmed positioned within the overlay's own bounds, not the full window; zero duplicate ids. Repeated at 1440×900 light and at 1100×900 (icon-rail width, `--sidebar-width:76px`) with the same checks. Zero non-baseline console/page errors and zero CSP violations in every scenario, on both built targets.
- **Phone regression**: a `pixelmatch` comparison of all five routes' 390×844/dark/reduced-motion screenshots against D5's own saved baseline screenshots (`/tmp/d5-shots`) came back **0 pixel difference on every route, on both built targets** — genuinely zero, the same bar every prior D-step's own phone-regression check has now cleared in a row.
- Verified: `npm run lint` passes with 0 errors, 0 warnings; `npm run test:unit` passes (324/324, unchanged — see above for why); both builds succeed (`npm run build` and `MIZIKI_BASE=/ npm run build`); the Playwright verification above passed on both built targets with zero non-baseline console/page errors and zero CSP violations throughout, and the phone-regression comparison came back pixel-identical to D5's own baseline on every route.

### Step D7: Crate profile on desktop, plus the sidebar's own Auto/Light/Dark control

Last of the seven-PR desktop-layout plan, building on D3's shell (merged, #82), D5's crate (#84) and D6's Now Playing (#85). Per the doc's own PR-order list: *"D7, Crate profile on desktop, plus the appearance control at the foot of the sidebar."* The doc's own "Screen by screen" table names the reused pieces for "Crate profile" — *"`renderProfile`, the storefront primitives in `src/profile/storefront.js`, `topAlbums`, `topTracks`, `collectionGroups`"* — and the new ones: *"A two-column grid: top records and digging list on the left, collection and now spinning on the right."* Its prose adds: *"The mobile tab is labelled Profile and the route is `profile`; the mockup calls it Crate... Keep the route id, change only the visible label on desktop if you agree. The awning, avatar disc, stats and metals row already exist and only need the wider grid."* No override from Ken was given on the "if you agree" label-swap question by the time this PR started, so — the same posture every prior step took on the doc's own stated defaults absent an explicit objection — it proceeded on the doc's own suggestion. From "Where the control lives": *"A Character-menu row on phones, and the three-way segmented control at the bottom of the desktop sidebar. Both call the same function."*

- **The Profile→Crate label swap, CSS-only, zero JS**: the tab button's existing `<span>Profile</span>` gained a `tabbar-label` class (purely a selector hook, no visual change) and a new sibling `<span class="tabbar-label-desktop">Crate</span>`. `data-route="profile"` is completely untouched — confirmed by grep (`src/shell/routes.js`'s `showRoute()`/`aria-selected` sync and `src/wiring/routes.js`'s click wiring both read `data-route` exclusively, never a button's text) before relying on that, the same check D4's own entry already made for its own markup change. `src/styles/23-desktop.css`, at 1280px+ only (the same tier the sidebar's own labels/playlists/Sundown card already show at — the 76px icon rail hides every tab label regardless, this one included): `.tabbar-btn[data-route="profile"] .tabbar-label{display:none}` / `.tabbar-btn[data-route="profile"] .tabbar-label-desktop{display:inline}` — written more specific (three selectors) than the existing generic `.tabbar-btn span{display:inline}` rule those two spans would otherwise both match, so it wins regardless of source order, the same specificity discipline D6's own entry already established for `.player-overlay .stage`. `.tabbar-label-desktop{display:none}` as the one unconditional default (outside any media query), so it's never visible on a phone or at the icon-rail width.
- **The sidebar's own Auto/Light/Dark control**: three `[data-theme-choice]` buttons (`auto`/`light`/`dark`), the identical attribute convention the phone Character-menu row already uses — `src/wiring/character.js`'s existing `document.querySelectorAll('[data-theme-choice]')` wiring call, and `src/ui/theme.js`'s `applyTheme()`'s existing `aria-pressed`-sync-on-every-`[data-theme-choice]`-button call, both already written generically back in D2 specifically so *this* step would need zero code change in either file — confirmed directly: clicking the sidebar's own Light button updates `data-theme` and flips `aria-pressed` correctly on all six `[data-theme-choice]` buttons in the document (three phone, three sidebar) in one pass. `#sidebarAppearance` sits at the very foot of `#sidebar` (a direct sibling of `#sidebarExtra`, inside the same `display:flex;flex-direction:column` sidebar `src/shell/sidebar.js`'s playlists list already lives in) — `margin-top:auto` pins it to the bottom of that flex column regardless of how tall the playlists list above it happens to be, rather than a fixed position that would have to duplicate the sidebar's own height math. Visible at 1280px+ only, the same tier as the rest of the sidebar's own labeled content — the 76px icon rail has no more room for three labeled buttons than it does for the playlists list.
- **The two-column grid** ("top records and digging list on the left, collection and now spinning on the right"): `renderProfileHome()`'s `#profileBody` content is two existing, untouched top-level pieces — `.store-host` (built by `renderLocalStorefront`/`renderStorefront`, `src/profile/storefront.js`) and `.profile-extras` (built by `renderProfileExtras`, `src/profile/screens.js`) — each still built by exactly the same functions, same order, same content, as before this step. `renderStorefrontSection()` gained one new wrapping div, `.profile-desktop-grid`, around both of them — the same invisible-scaffolding `display:contents`-below-the-breakpoint pattern every prior D-step's own wrapper (`.sidebar`, `.crate-layout`, `.player-layout`) already established, so below 1024px this changes nothing about how either piece renders. At 1024px+, `.store-host` and `.profile-extras` *also* become `display:contents`, so their own children spill into being direct items of the one shared grid — `display:contents` doesn't change DOM structure or how selectors match it, only box generation, confirmed by testing that a plain descendant/child-combinator selector still correctly reaches an element nested two `display:contents` levels deep (`.store-host > .note`, the local-only storefront's own closing footnote).
  - **The four mapped pieces**: `.profile-top-row` (Top Album/Top Song — "top records") and `.store-card` (the now-spinning card) share a row, left and right via `grid-column`, paired by an identical `order`; `.store-section-digging` (a new modifier class added to `storeDiggingSection()`'s own returned div, since it shares the bare `.store-section` class with `storePicksSection()`/`storeCrateGrid()` with no other way to single it out) and `button.profile-section` (the Collection summary — a `<button>`, the one thing distinguishing it in CSS from achievements' `div.profile-section`, which shares the exact same base class) share the row below them, same shape. A missing piece — now-spinning and digging-list are both social-only, so neither exists in the common signed-out/local-only case this app runs in by default — just leaves that cell empty; grid auto-placement needs no row-span bookkeeping for that, confirmed directly against the real local-only render (see verification below).
  - **Everything else named or not named in the doc's own mapping** — the awning, header, optional bio/visibility row (signed-in only), stats, an optional follow-requests card (signed-in only), the share-card picture/name editor (signed-in only), the achievements ("metals") grid, staff picks, the full crate grid ("All records"), the Share button, and the local-only storefront's own closing footnote — stays a full-width row (`grid-column:1/-1`), each given its own fixed `order` so the page reads top to bottom in a sensible sequence regardless of which of the two original wrapper subtrees a given piece's markup happens to live under. `order` has zero effect outside an actual grid/flex formatting context, so none of this touches phone layout.
  - **"Only need the wider grid"**: `.profile-grid` (achievements, and the dedicated Collection screen's own full grid — not `.profile-grid.mini`, the compact preview that stays confined to the half-width Collection card and is left untouched) goes from 4 columns to 6 at 1024px+, 8 at 1280px+. `.store-crate-grid` goes from 3 to 5, then 6 — left unscoped on purpose, since it's also used by a friend's crate preview (`src/social/screens/friend-store.js`), which benefits from the same wider grid on desktop with zero extra code.
- No `await` was added, removed or reordered anywhere — every change here is new markup, new CSS, or a new, additional wrapping `div` in an existing render function; nothing here touches playback or sync timing at all.
- **No duplicate element ids anywhere in the document** — confirmed directly, the same way every prior D-step's own entry confirms it, on the profile route specifically (where this step's own new ids — `#sidebarAppearance` — live) as well as the player/library/crate routes already checked by earlier steps.
- No new unit tests this step, for the same reason every prior D-step's own CSS/composition-heavy entry gives: nothing new here is pure, standalone logic worth a `node:test` file of its own — a label swap, a grid wrapper `div`, and a set of CSS selectors with no decision logic of their own to isolate. `npm run test:unit` stays at 324/324, unchanged — confirmed no existing test's mock chain reaches `renderStorefrontSection`/`storeDiggingSection` in a way this step's small additions could disturb.
- **A real test-harness bug found and fixed during this step's own verification, not a bug in the app**: the first Playwright pass showed the Profile tab's click never routing and every grid check coming back `null` — traced to the two built targets being served from the wrong ports (`npm run build`'s own default output, already `/Miziki/`-based, was being served with *no* path prefix, and the `MIZIKI_BASE=/` output was served *with* a `/Miziki` prefix — backwards), so the stylesheet and script both 404'd and the page silently fell back to its pre-CSS, unstyled, phone-narrow DOM order with the tab bar rendered far below the 900px viewport a click could ever reach. Fixed by correcting which build goes to which port; re-run came back clean.
- **Browser verification** (Playwright, both built targets): at 1440×900 dark — the sidebar's appearance control is visible, Auto reads pressed by default, clicking Light sets `data-theme="light"` and flips `aria-pressed` on all six `[data-theme-choice]` buttons in the document together; the Profile tab shows only "Crate" (not "Profile"), `data-route` stays `"profile"`, and clicking it still routes correctly; `.profile-desktop-grid` resolves to a real two-column grid (`556px 556px` at this width), `.profile-top-row` lands in column 1, the achievements section spans the full grid width, the Collection button's own width matches one column, and the grid never overlaps the sidebar; zero duplicate ids. A second pass, forcing the local-only storefront path (the common case this app runs in without a signed-in account — `MizikiSocial.status` intercepted to report `enabled:false` before boot, verification-only, no app code touched) plus the built-in test tone, confirmed staff picks and the full crate grid both span the full grid width (not squeezed into a column), the crate grid resolved to 6 columns at this width, and the two-column band still placed `.profile-top-row` in column 1 — exercising the mapping against real, non-empty content rather than only the empty-state structure. Repeated the core checks at 1440×900 light (grid present, renders cleanly) and at 1100×900, the icon-rail width (`#sidebarAppearance` confirmed hidden, sidebar measures exactly 76px). Zero non-baseline console/page errors and zero CSP violations in every scenario, on both built targets.
- **Phone regression**: four of the five routes' 390×844/dark/reduced-motion screenshots came back pixel-identical (0px) to D6's own saved baseline. The fifth, library, showed a 3.19% diff — investigated rather than assumed either way: a fresh rebuild of D6's own merge commit, with *zero* code difference from D6's own saved baseline, reproduced the exact same 10,489px/3.1866% diff against that same baseline when screenshotted fresh in this session. That's conclusive: it's cross-session font-rasterization drift, the identical phenomenon D3's own changelog entry already diagnosed the same way (a freshly captured screenshot of unchanged code differing from its own previously saved baseline, reproduced with zero code in the picture) — not anything this PR changed. Confirmed separately that D7's own CSS additions can't reach the library route's markup at phone width at all regardless: every new rule is either unconditionally hidden (`.tabbar-label-desktop`, `.sidebar-appearance`) or sits behind `@media (min-width:1024px)`/`1280px`, neither of which matches a 390px viewport.
