- Verified: `npm run lint` passes with 0 errors, 0 warnings; `npm run test:unit` passes (324/324, unchanged — see above for why); both builds succeed (`npm run build` and `MIZIKI_BASE=/ npm run build`); the Playwright verification above passed on both built targets with zero non-baseline console/page errors and zero CSP violations throughout; the phone-regression comparison came back pixel-identical to D6's own baseline on four of five routes, with the fifth's apparent diff independently confirmed to be cross-session rendering drift unrelated to this PR's own code, not a regression.

### After step D7

All seven PRs in the desktop-layout plan (`docs/DESKTOP_LAYOUT.md`) are now complete and merged: D1 (dark-mode tokens), D2 (light theme + Auto/Light/Dark preference), D3 (desktop shell — sidebar, breakpoint, main-area width), D4 (the persistent player bar), D5 (the crate's own desktop layout), D6 (Now Playing's own desktop layout), D7 (Crate profile's own desktop layout + the sidebar's appearance control). The three mockup screens (Library, Now Playing, Crate profile) and the Auto/Light/Dark appearance setting are all shipped; the phone layout is confirmed pixel-identical to its pre-D1 baseline at every step along the way. Per the doc's own "Device checklist" instruction, the two new checks (toggling the iPhone between Light and Dark with the app on Auto, and the status bar color in each) are the one item still outstanding — a real-device check, not something any of these PRs' own Playwright verification could stand in for.

### Fix: drawSun()'s --sky1/--sky2 inline override clobbering Light theme (D2 gap, now closed)

A real bug report, not a refactor or a planned step: Light theme on desktop showed a dark diagonal gradient bleeding through the main content area (visible behind the Crate/profile route's cards and sections) instead of the flat, consistent cream background every other part of the page correctly showed.

- **Root cause, already named — but left unfixed on purpose — in step D2's own changelog entry above**: `src/player/clock.js`'s `drawSun()` (Sundown's sky-color readout, called once at boot from `main.js` and then every ~220ms from the render loop's `tick()` while the page is visible) has always unconditionally written `--sky1`/`--sky2` as **inline styles** on `document.documentElement`, animating them through a fixed dark palette (daylight-teal → dusk → near-black) tied to the sun's progress toward dusk. Since D2 gave `--sky1`/`--sky2`/`--sky3` real Light/Dark theme values in `src/styles/22-theme.css`, and since an inline style always wins over any stylesheet rule regardless of theme, this meant `--sky1`/`--sky2` stayed stuck on the dark animated palette even in Light theme, while `--sky3` (never touched by `drawSun()`) correctly followed Light — producing exactly the two-tone, mismatched ground gradient (`linear-gradient(170deg,var(--sky1) 0%,var(--sky2) 62%,var(--sky3) 100%)`, used on `body`/`.player-overlay`/`.launch-overlay`) the screenshot showed. `--sky2` is also what `html,body`'s own background-color and three sheet overlays (`16-edit-sheet.css`'s edit sheet, `20-friend-store.css`'s social-nav overlay and record sheet) read, so the same stuck-dark value was quietly wrong there too, just less visible behind their own opaque content.
- **The fix**: `drawSun()` now checks the *effective* theme (`effectiveTheme()`, newly exported from `src/ui/theme.js` — the same light/dark/auto resolution `applyTheme()`'s own meta-tag/live-listener logic already uses, not a second implementation of it) before touching either property. In Light theme, it calls `removeProperty('--sky1'/'--sky2')` instead of `setProperty(...)`, clearing any previously-set inline value so the Light tokens `22-theme.css` already declares take over cleanly — no inline value left to fight them. In Dark theme (pinned, or Auto resolving to dark), the exact same animated dusk palette applies exactly as before this fix, byte-for-byte — the math itself (now `skyGradientStops(p)`, pulled into its own exported pure function purely for testability, same precedent as `angleDelta`/`gatefoldSideSplits`/`computeFlickPosition`) is untouched. Switching theme live self-corrects within one `drawSun()` tick (≤220ms while visible), the same "next render self-corrects" tolerance several other `isDesktopShell()`-style checks in this codebase already accept.
- **Why not invent a light-appropriate animated dusk palette instead of simply not animating in Light**: Sundown's whole visual premise — a sunset settling into night — is the dark theme's own chrome language to begin with; per D2's own "only chrome changes" framing (the disc, sleeve art, metal tiers and share-image colors are deliberately the same in both themes, since they're not chrome), the sky gradient is chrome, so it follows the theme the same way everything else chrome-shaped already does. A bright, static Light-theme background is the correct, expected look for anyone who explicitly chose Light.
- No `await` was added, removed or reordered — `drawSun()` is a synchronous render-loop function with no promises in it before or after this change.
- Added 7 new tests to `tests/unit/clock.test.js`: `skyGradientStops(0)`/`skyGradientStops(0.5)`/`skyGradientStops(1)` match the exact A/B/C palette stops; no seam discontinuity straddling the `p=0.5` blend boundary; `drawSun()` sets an animated `rgb(...)` inline value for both `--sky1`/`--sky2` in Dark theme; sets neither in Light theme; and clears a previously-set inline override when the effective theme switches from Dark to Light mid-session. A thin override on top of the real `effectiveTheme()` (not a full mock) gives each test deterministic control over which branch `drawSun()` takes, while the real light/dark/auto resolution logic itself stays covered by `tests/unit/theme.test.js`; a small `util/dom.js` mock (one reusable stub element) and a `document.documentElement.style` stub (plain object tracking `setProperty`/`removeProperty` calls) are what let `drawSun()` run in this file at all, since nothing before this fix ever called it here.
- **Browser verification** (Playwright, both built targets, isolated `dist` copies so each target's own build never clobbers the other's running server): confirmed, directly reading `document.documentElement.style.getPropertyValue`/`getComputedStyle`, that Light theme leaves `--sky1`/`--sky2` with no inline value at all and all three sky tokens resolve to the identical light hex triple (`#F6F1E7`/`#ECE5D8`/`#E0D8C7`) — before the fix this would have shown `--sky1`/`--sky2` stuck on an `rgb(...)` inline value while `--sky3` alone read the light hex; Dark theme (OS-dark, default Auto) still shows both `--sky1`/`--sky2` as live animated `rgb(...)` inline values, unchanged; the Sundown route's own readout (`#rateWhy`/`#rateOut`/`#rpmText`) is byte-identical to its pre-fix text, confirming nothing about Sundown's actual rate/position logic moved. A full-page screenshot of the Crate route in Light theme shows a single, consistent cream background throughout — the dark diagonal bleed from the bug report is gone — while the same route in Dark theme is visually unchanged from before. Zero page errors, zero CSP violations, on both built targets.
- **Phone regression**: pixel-diffed this fix's own phone screenshots (390×844, dark, reduced motion, all five routes) against D7's own saved baseline screenshots — **0 pixel difference on every route, on both built targets**, confirming this fix changes nothing about the phone experience, which was already correct (the bug was Light-theme-only, and the phone's own default/most-common state is Dark or Auto-resolving-dark). Also screenshotted the phone layout in Light theme directly (not a regression check, since D1–D7 never had a Light-theme phone baseline to diff against, but worth confirming given the bug itself) — consistent light background, no bleed, same as desktop.
- Verified: `npm run lint` passes with 0 errors, 0 warnings; `npm run test:unit` passes (331/331 — 324 before this fix + 7 new); both builds succeed (`npm run build` and `MIZIKI_BASE=/ npm run build`); the Playwright verification above passed on both built targets with zero non-baseline console/page errors and zero CSP violations, and the phone-regression comparison came back pixel-identical to D7's baseline on every route.

### DA1: day model and Sunrise (platter speed)

First of the six-PR day-arc plan (`docs/DAY_ARC.md`, added alongside `docs/design/day-arc/` in a prior docs-only PR — see that doc's own repo-layout entry above). A feature PR, not a refactor — it changes real app behavior: the platter now eases back up to full speed across Sunrise, where before DA1 it simply held the night rate from midnight straight through until the next sunset. Per the doc's own gating, **each DA PR stops and waits for Ken's go-ahead before the next one starts** — this PR is not to be merged without that.

- **The gap this closes, named by the doc itself**: before DA1, Sundown's "night" was midnight-to-midnight-of-settings, not dawn-to-dusk by the actual sun — a session open past midnight played the full night rate straight through sunrise and into the morning, with no code path that ever eased it back up; `S.sun`'s stale-overnight problem (no recompute trigger once dusk had passed) made this worse, not better; and there was no dawn/morning behavior of any kind to begin with, since nothing in the old `sunProgress()` ever asked what happens before noon.
- **`src/sundown/day.js`** (new) is the actual day model: `dayEvents(date,lat,lon)` (dawn/sunrise/solar-noon/sunset/dusk, by calling into `solar.js`'s now-dual-sided `solarEvent`) and `dayPosition(now,events)` (`{phase, phaseProgress, nightness, rateProgress}`, one of seven named phases — Night/Dawn/Morning/Midday/Afternoon/Golden hour/Twilight — bounding the whole 24-hour cycle). See that module's own repo-layout entry above for the full ladder and both of its fallback branches.
- **The key design decision, made and documented rather than following the doc's own suggested shape literally**: the doc's prose implies tracking yesterday's dusk and tomorrow's dawn (`prevDusk`/`nextDawn`) to handle continuity across midnight. Neither field exists anywhere in `S.sun` or `day.js`'s own output. Instead, `solarEvent`'s pre-existing Julian-day rounding (`Math.round(J - 2451545.0 - ...)` in the newly-factored-out `solarTransit()`) always resolves to the astronomically-nearest calendar day for whatever `date` is passed in — so a single `dayEvents(now, lat, lon)` call, evaluated at 2am, already correctly represents "today" in local solar terms, with no separate yesterday/tomorrow computation needed at all. The one discrete internal day-rollover this produces lands deep inside the flat Night phase, where adjacent calendar days' events agree on "it's night," making it invisible to `dayPosition`'s own output — proven, not just argued, by `tests/unit/day.test.js`'s 48-hour, per-minute continuity walk (no jump anywhere, including the literal midnight crossing). This is simpler than the doc's own suggested shape and was worth deviating for.
- **`src/sundown/solar.js`** extended, not rewritten wholesale: `solarEvent(date, lat, lon, altitude, rising = false)` gained the one new parameter, defaulting to `false` so every pre-DA1 call site (three or four arguments, no `rising`) is byte-for-byte unchanged — `rising` just flips which side of solar noon the same hour-angle equation (`Jt ± w/360`) resolves to. Two new exports, `solarNoon()` (never `null` — the sun transits the local meridian every day regardless of latitude) and `solarAltitudeAtNoon()` (the one thing that can tell polar day from polar night apart once `solarEvent()` itself collapses that distinction to a bare `null`), both factored out of a new private `solarTransit()` that `solarEvent` itself now also calls, rather than duplicating the Julian-date/declination math a third time.
- **`computeRate()` is untouched, on purpose, satisfying the doc's own "stays the single place speed is decided" requirement by construction**: it still only ever reads `S.mode`/`S.auto`/`S.manualRate`/`S.target`/`easedProgress()`, in the exact same four-line body as before DA1. Everything DA1 changes is *underneath* `easedProgress()` — what `rateProgress` means across the whole day, and how pacing (Window/Moment) shapes it — never the function that turns a progress value into an actual playback rate.
- **Pacing applies symmetrically to both halves**: `easedProgress()`'s existing `pace()` helper (`smooth(p) = p*p*(3-2*p)`, Moment compressing the back 38% of its own input) is unchanged in shape; it's now also applied to the dawn side, via `1 - pace(1 - rateProgress)` — "risen" (0 at dawn, 1 at sunrise) stands in for the dusk side's own raw progress, run through the identical pacing curve, then mapped back to "how much night-rate remains." The result is a *felt* rise near sunrise under Moment (held back through most of dawn, same as the dusk side holds back through most of twilight), not a separate, unmirrored dawn behavior invented from scratch.
- **`S.sun` gained `dawn`/`rise`/`noon` (plus `altitudeAtNoon`)** — `computeSun()` and `fallbackSun()` both fill all five events now, not just `set`/`dusk`; `currentDayPosition()` (new, `src/sundown/solar.js`) is the one place outside `day.js` that reads `phase`/`phaseProgress` directly, for `drawSun()`'s own two new readout branches. None of the three new `S.sun` fields are separately persisted — `S.sun` was never a saved pref before DA1 and still isn't; it's recomputed fresh every session.
- **Staleness, the doc's second named gap, closed two ways**: `src/sundown/location.js`'s new `scheduleMidnightRecompute()` (a recursive `setTimeout`, not `setInterval`, so the daily cadence never drifts) recomputes the day model a few seconds past local midnight for a session left open and visible overnight; `src/wiring/lifecycle.js`'s existing `visibilitychange` handler gained the same `computeSun()`/`drawSun()` pair on the visible-again branch, for a session that was *backgrounded* across midnight and never had the timer fire while hidden. Both are new, additional calls alongside work that already existed there (`pullBackupOnForeground()`), not replacements for it.
- **The readout** (`src/player/clock.js`'s `drawSun()`) gained exactly two new `#rateWhy` branches, ahead of the pre-existing day/night/twilight ones: Dawn ("Dawn — rising, N% of the way up," reading `phaseProgress` directly — "percent risen" by construction, so the percentage always counts up toward sunrise, matching what a user watching the number would expect) and Morning ("Morning — full speed"). Every other phase (midday/afternoon/golden hour) still falls through to the pre-existing "Daylight — full speed" line — DA1 only asks for Dawn's and Morning's own text, not a distinct line per phase (DA3's own richer split). Every pre-existing branch's wording is byte-for-byte unchanged, confirmed both by direct diff and by the `legStart`/`legEnd`/manual-mode/preview-slider checks in the Playwright verification below.
- No `await` was added, removed or reordered anywhere — `computeSun()`/`dayEvents()`/`dayPosition()`/`drawSun()` are all synchronous; the two new `scheduleMidnightRecompute()`/`visibilitychange` call sites are fire-and-forget the same way the existing backup-sync calls already are in both of those places.
- Added `tests/unit/day.test.js` (14 tests) — see that file's own entry in the `tests/unit/` list above for exactly what it covers, including the two real-world fixtures (Columbia, MD and Sydney, Australia, checked against published sunrise/sunset times the same way `solar.test.js` already does) and the two polar fixtures (Svalbard, June and December). `tests/unit/solar.test.js`'s own pre-existing fixtures (`pastDusk`, `midTwilight` — both missing the three new `S.sun` fields) needed no changes at all: `dayPosition()`'s "legacy partial data" fallback branch exists specifically so they keep passing unchanged, confirmed by running the full suite rather than assumed.
- One lint fix along the way: `tests/unit/day.test.js`'s first draft imported `solarEvent` for a comment-only mention with no actual call site — dropped from the import line once `npm run lint --max-warnings=0` caught it.
- **Browser verification** (Playwright, both built targets, each served from its own stable, separate static-file directory — not a shared `dist/` a second build could clobber out from under a running server, the exact mistake flagged in several earlier D-series entries): used Playwright's `page.clock.install()`/fixed-time API to land a faked UTC "now" (the host's own real timezone) inside each of five phases in turn — dawn (06:35, mid-dawn), morning (07:30, just after sunrise), midday (13:00), night (02:00) and twilight (20:23, mid sunset→dusk) — against the fallback stand-in times (geolocation denied by default in a fresh Playwright context, so every run genuinely exercises `fallbackSun()`'s path, not a live-location one). `#rateWhy` read exactly "Dawn — rising, 50% of the way up," "Morning — full speed," "Daylight — full speed," "Night — settled at 0.80×" and "Twilight — 50% of the way down" respectively — all five matching expectations exactly, on both built targets, zero console/page errors in every run. Separately confirmed Manual mode still shows "Set by hand" (`#autoOff`/`#autoOn`), and the pre-existing dusk-preview slider (`#preview`/`#previewOut`) still moves off "Live" and back exactly as before DA1 — nothing about either pre-existing control changed.
- No phone-regression pixel diff this step, unlike the D-series/sky-fix entries above: DA1 touches no CSS and no markup at all (the only visible change is text content in two specific, newly-reachable readout states) — the pixel-diff discipline in this changelog has only ever been applied to PRs that touched `src/styles/`, which this one doesn't.
- Verified: `npm run lint` passes with 0 errors, 0 warnings; `npm run test:unit` passes (345/345 — 331 before this step + 14 new); both builds succeed (`npm run build` and `MIZIKI_BASE=/ npm run build`, 173 modules transformed — 166 before DA1 — the new `day.js` plus its new circular pair with `solar.js`); the Playwright verification above passed on both built targets with zero non-baseline console/page errors across all five simulated phases plus the manual-mode/preview-slider regression checks.

**Preview focus (Ken, iPhone) — please verify by hand before merging:**
1. Leave the app open and playing overnight (or jump a device's clock forward across sunrise, if that's easier to arrange) and confirm the platter actually eases back up to full speed across Sunrise, rather than holding the night rate until the next sunset.
2. Open the Sundown panel sometime after sunrise but before the readout would call it "Daylight" and confirm the new Dawn/Morning text reads sensibly against the real sun outside.
3. Leave a tab open and visible across real local midnight (or background the app across midnight and bring it back to the foreground) and confirm the Sundown panel's sunset/dusk times for "today" update rather than staying stuck on yesterday's.
4. Confirm Pure mode still always plays at exactly 1.0× regardless of time of day, and that Window vs. Moment pacing still feels the same way during a sunset you can watch in real time as it always has.
5. Confirm no console errors or regressions anywhere else in Sundown/Character while this PR is active.

### DA2: character drift

Second of the six-PR day-arc plan (`docs/DAY_ARC.md`), building on DA1's day model (merged). A feature PR, not a refactor — it changes real app behavior: Vinyl's character (wow, saturation, top-end softening, compression) now drifts a little off the user's own sliders depending on the time of day — warmer and softer at night, brighter and lighter in the morning — rather than staying pinned to whatever the sliders say regardless of when it's playing. Pure is untouched, per F1's own "Pure means untouched" rule; the doc's own gating applies here too — **this PR is not to be merged without Ken's go-ahead**.

- **`src/sundown/drift.js`** (new, pure, no DOM/`S` import — the same "testable without a browser" shape as `solar.js`/`day.js`) is the whole DA2 deliverable's math: `driftOffsets(dayPos, intensity)` → `{wow, sat, soft, comp}`, and `effectiveChar(userChar, dayPos, intensity)` → the same four keys, each `clamp(user + offset, 0, 1)` — the small decision function pulled out and exported for its own test, same precedent as `gatefoldSideSplits`/`skyGradientStops`/`angleDelta` elsewhere in this codebase. `ANCHORS` holds the doc's own four-phase table at Full intensity (Morning −0.20/−0.10/−0.10/−0.05 soft/sat/comp/wow; Midday 0,0,0,0; Golden hour +0.08/+0.05/+0.03/0; Night +0.20/+0.10/+0.10/+0.05); Subtle is exactly half, Off exactly zero (`INTENSITY_SCALE`).
- **Four anchors, seven phases — a judgment call, since the doc's own table names only four of the seven phases `dayPosition()` (DA1) can return.** Dawn/afternoon/twilight are each read as the doc's own implied transition between their two neighboring anchors (dawn: Night→Morning; afternoon: Midday→Golden; twilight: Golden→Night), smoothstep-blended (`smooth(p) = p*p*(3-2*p)`, the same curve `solar.js`/`day.js` already use for Sundown's own pacing) across that phase's own `phaseProgress`. Morning and Golden hour are flat holds at their own anchor value for their *whole* phase, not a single instant — this is what makes "peak ~1-2h after sunrise" true in the way a listener would actually notice it, without inventing a second ramp the doc's table never asked for.
- **Morning is the one phase that has to do two jobs, not one, and this took a second pass to get right (see Problem Solving below)**: it sits between Dawn (which already ramps Night→Morning, reaching the peak exactly at sunrise) and Midday (flat at zero), with no third ramp phase of its own to spend on the way back down — every other named anchor (Night, Golden) gets exactly one neighboring flat phase and one ramp phase from the opposite direction, but Morning would otherwise have to jump straight from its own flat peak to Midday's flat zero with nothing in between. Fixed by splitting Morning internally: flat at the peak through the first half of the phase (sunrise to roughly the middle of the doc's own "peak ~1-2h after sunrise" window), then ramped down to Midday across the second half — closing the one internal boundary none of dawn/afternoon/twilight's own ramps already cover, and keeping every phase boundary exactly continuous (a flat hold's own edge value is always identical to the smoothstep ramp's 0 or 1 endpoint next to it).
- **The single Pure/Vinyl gate, the same one-place shape as F1's own `computeRate()` Pure-rate rule**: `src/audio/engine.js`'s `applyCharacter()` computes `const intensity = S.mode === 'pure' ? 'off' : S.charDrift;` before `effectiveChar()` is even called — forced to `'off'` regardless of `S.charDrift`, not merely left unused because Pure's own routing bypasses these nodes. This matters because a slider moved (or the intensity toggle clicked) while in Pure should never leave a drifted value sitting on a node Pure doesn't currently route through, waiting to surprise the very next switch back to Vinyl — `src/audio/mode.js`'s `setMode()` gained a trailing `applyCharacter()` call for exactly this reason, so a mode switch re-gates immediately rather than waiting up to 30s for the next periodic recompute to catch up.
- **Two ramp time constants, a judgment call the doc's own "ramping with a longer time constant, so the drift is never audible as a change" line left open**: `applyCharacter(ramp = 0.05)` keeps the exact same fast 50ms default every pre-DA2 call site already used (a slider drag, a preset click, a mode switch, restoring saved prefs) — none of those should suddenly feel sluggish just because drift now rides along with them. Only the two genuinely new call sites pass a slow `2` (seconds): `src/audio/engine.js`'s own new `scheduleDriftRecompute()` (a plain `setInterval(() => applyCharacter(2), 30000)`, called once from `main.js`'s boot sequence, the same "set it up once, it re-arms on its own" shape `sundown/location.js`'s `scheduleMidnightRecompute()` already has — "about every 30s" tolerates ordinary `setInterval` drift fine, unlike midnight's own wall-clock anchor) and `src/wiring/character.js`'s intensity-toggle click handler — both are the drift mechanism moving on its own, not a direct user edit, which is exactly what "never audible as a change" is actually about.
- **Only rebuild `satCurve` when it actually moved enough to matter**, per the doc's own instruction: `applyCharacter()` keeps a module-private `lastAppliedSat` and only calls `satCurve()` (which allocates a brand-new `Float32Array` every time) when the effective sat value has moved by more than `0.005` since the last call — `satTrim`'s own gain still glides on every call regardless, since that's a cheap `setTargetAtTime`, not a curve rebuild.
- **`S.charDrift`** (`'off'`/`'subtle'`/`'full'`, default `'subtle'` per the doc's own instruction, `src/state.js`) — added to `serializePrefs`/`parsePrefs` (`src/storage/prefs.js`) the same way every other saved setting already is, per the refactor rules' own "new saved setting" checklist: a default in `S`, both prefs functions, and three new `prefs.test.js` tests (round-trips as Off and Full, not just the default; a saved object from before DA2 with no such field loads as Subtle; an unlisted value is ignored) — the same shape `theme`'s own D2 addition already established. **No new row in the backup key-inventory table** (CLAUDE.md, "Backup: what gets synced") — the decision this table's own rule calls for, made explicitly rather than skipped: unlike `theme`, `charDrift` rides inside `prefs` with no carve-out, since a day-arc intensity choice has no reason to stay device-only — it's an ordinary character preference, the same class as `S.char`'s four sliders or `S.char.scratch`, neither of which needed a row of its own either.
- **The UI**: a new segmented control (`[data-char-drift="off"/"subtle"/"full"]`) in the Character route, between the Pure-mode note and the existing D2 Appearance row, with the doc's own copy verbatim: *"Your settings are midday. Mornings drift brighter, nights warmer."* Wired with `document.querySelectorAll('[data-char-drift]')` (`src/wiring/character.js`), the same attribute convention `data-theme-choice` already established in D2 specifically so a later control sharing it needs no wiring change — forward-compatible with DA3's own planned move of this control into Sundown's Sound view. `src/storage/meta.js`'s `applyPrefs()` restores `S.charDrift` and syncs the three buttons' `aria-pressed` inline, the same shape `sleevePullEnabled`'s own button sync already uses there, rather than a whole extra function for three buttons.
- No `await` was added, removed or reordered anywhere — `applyCharacter()`/`driftOffsets()`/`effectiveChar()` are all synchronous; the new `scheduleDriftRecompute()` call site is fire-and-forget the same way `scheduleMidnightRecompute()` already is.
- Added `tests/unit/drift.test.js` (9 tests): zero offset at Midday and at Off, at every phase; an unrecognized intensity value is treated as Off; Subtle is exactly half of Full at every phase; the four named anchors match the doc's own table at Full; no offset ever pushes an effective value outside 0–1 (checked from both rails, in both directions — Night pushes up, Morning pushes down); Off leaves the effective value exactly equal to the user's own; and two continuity tests — the real correctness property the doc's "offsets continuous across 24h" line actually calls for.
  - **A real structural bug this testing caught, not just confirmed absent**: an early version of `anchorOffsets()` left `'morning'` as a single flat case, which produced a hard ~0.2-magnitude jump exactly at the morning/midday boundary — every other boundary was already correctly continuous, but this one wasn't, since the original shape gave Morning no ramp of its own on its midday-facing side. Fixed by the two-part Morning split described above; re-running the continuity test after the fix came back clean.
  - **A second test-design bug, found and fixed after the first fix, not a code bug**: the first version of the 24-hour continuity test used a fixed per-two-minute-step cap (`< 0.01`) across the whole day. Re-running it against the real `dayPosition()` (not synthetic phase objects) surfaced a second apparent "failure" — a real ~0.037 delta during dawn — that turned out to be the test's own design flaw, not a discontinuity: dawn and twilight on the test's own date/location (Columbia, MD, summer solstice) each last only ~32 minutes, and the soft knob swings its full 0.40-wide range (Night's +0.20 to Morning's −0.20) across that one ramp — a real, continuous, *legitimately steep* change that a fixed slew-rate cap can't tell apart from an actual jump without being fragile to exactly how long dawn/twilight happen to last on whatever date the test runs against. Replaced with two tests that measure the right property instead: one samples ±1 second on either side of each of the six real phase-boundary instants and asserts the value barely moves there (continuity is a boundary property, independent of how fast the ramp on either side is moving), and a separate, looser whole-day sweep that only checks nothing ever exceeds the largest documented anchor magnitude (0.20) — catching a real overshoot bug without flagging a legitimate steep-but-continuous ramp.
  - A third, much smaller issue in `engine.test.js`'s own new assertions (not `drift.js` itself): comparing a drifted value against a literal float expression (`-9 * 0.60`) against the actual value computed via `effectiveChar()`'s `clamp(0.40 + 0.20, 0, 1)` arithmetic hit an ordinary floating-point ULP mismatch (`-5.3999999999999995` vs `-5.4`) — fixed by switching to an epsilon-tolerant comparison, the same `EPS = 0.005`-style precedent that file's own `satCurve` tests already established, rather than comparing against an exact literal.
- Added three new tests to `tests/unit/engine.test.js` (Pure never applies drift even at Full intensity and full night, confirming the gate actually does something rather than vacuously passing; Vinyl applies the Night drift at Full intensity; Vinyl with intensity Off matches the user's own raw sliders, same as Pure) — using a real "night" `S.sun` fixture (the same legacy-partial-data shape `solar.test.js`'s own `pastDusk` fixture already uses) so Full-intensity drift would be nonzero if the gate weren't actually working, plus a `FakeParam.setTargetAtTime` that now records `lastTarget` instead of being a pure no-op, so these new tests can assert on what `applyCharacter()` actually set.
- **Browser verification** (Playwright, both built targets, each served from its own stable, separate static-file directory — the same isolation DA1's own entry already established): added the built-in test tone, opened Character, confirmed the drift control defaults to Subtle pressed, clicked Off/Full/Subtle and confirmed `aria-pressed` toggles correctly and mutually exclusively on all three buttons every time; swept the Sundown preview slider (`#preview`) across night↔daylight↔night with Full intensity and Vinyl active, confirming no error and a sane `#rateOut` reading at the end; switched to Pure via the persistent desktop player bar (`#barModePure`) and back to Vinyl (`#barModeVinyl`), confirming no error on either switch; set intensity to Full, waited past the 600ms `queueSave()` debounce, reloaded, and confirmed Full was still the pressed button (the test tone itself is transient audio, not a saved library track, so the player bar is correctly gone after this particular reload — expected, not a bug, and not needed for this check). Zero non-baseline console/page errors on either built target — the one 404 that appeared is the same long-documented baseline 404 this changelog has noted since step 01 (confirmed absent at page load alone, only appearing during interaction, matching every prior step's own note on it).
- Verified: `npm run lint` passes with 0 errors, 0 warnings; `npm run test:unit` passes (360/360 — 345 before this step + 15 new: 9 in `drift.test.js`, 3 in `engine.test.js`, 3 in `prefs.test.js`); both builds succeed (`npm run build` and `MIZIKI_BASE=/ npm run build`, 174 modules transformed — 173 before DA2, the new `drift.js` plus its new real import of `currentDayPosition` into `engine.js`); the Playwright verification above passed on both built targets with zero non-baseline console/page errors.

**Preview focus (Ken, iPhone, with headphones) — please verify by hand before merging:**
1. Set intensity to Full, then use the Sundown preview slider to jump between night and daylight — confirm Vinyl's character audibly warms/softens at night and feels brighter/lighter in the morning, not just that the numbers move.
2. Set intensity to Subtle and repeat — confirm the same direction of change, gentler.
3. Set intensity to Off and confirm Vinyl sounds exactly like your own sliders say, regardless of time of day.
4. Switch to Pure at any intensity, at any time of day, and confirm it sounds exactly the same as it always has — no drift, ever, in Pure.
5. Leave the app open and playing for a while in Vinyl with Full intensity and confirm the character shifts gradually as real time passes (the ~30s recompute), never as an audible jump.
6. Confirm no console errors or regressions anywhere else in Character/Sundown while this PR is active.

### DA3: Sundown/Character merge, the day arc, and the sky across the whole day

Third of the six-PR day-arc plan (`docs/DAY_ARC.md`), building on DA1's day model and DA2's character drift (both merged). A feature PR, not a refactor — it changes real app structure (the tab bar drops from five buttons to four, the Character route is gone) and real visible behavior (a day-arc replaces the old sunset-only progress bar; the sky now follows the whole day, in both themes). The doc's own gating applies here too — **this PR is not to be merged without Ken's go-ahead**.

- **The tab bar is four equal tabs, not five.** `data-route="character"` and its button are gone from `miziki.html` entirely. `#route-sundown` now holds a two-tab `role="tablist"` switch (`.sd-tabs`, `[data-sundown-view="day"/"sound"]`) over two sibling views inside the same route: `#sundownDayView` (everything described below) and `#sundownSoundView` (the old Character route's content, moved here verbatim except for one row — see Appearance below). `src/shell/routes.js`'s `showRoute()` gained a defensive first line, `if(name === 'character'){ name = 'sundown'; selectSundownView('sound'); }`, so anything that still asks for the now-gone route — a stale saved last-tab value, an old link, a future typo — lands on Sundown's own Sound view instead of matching no `.route` element at all and leaving the screen blank; `tests/unit/routes.test.js` (new) is the test the doc's own "add a test for that" line asked for.
- **`selectSundownView(view)`/`lastSundownView()` live in `src/shell/routes.js`, not `src/wiring/sundown.js`** — a judgment call, made so `showRoute()`'s own redirect above could call `selectSundownView` directly without a wiring file ever being imported from a shell/ module (this codebase's imports always run wiring → shell/, never the reverse). `selectSundownView()` toggles the tablist's own `aria-selected`, shows/hides the two view `<div>`s, and mirrors the choice to `localStorage` (try/catch-wrapped — device-only, never part of `prefs`/backup, the same posture `S.theme`'s own mirror, `src/theme-boot.js`, already established) so it's remembered per device across reloads; `lastSundownView()` reads it back, defaulting to Day. `src/wiring/sundown.js` wires the two tab buttons' clicks to it and calls it once at boot to restore whichever view this device last had open — a real, one-directional import, not a circular pair.
- **The Appearance control moved off Character entirely, onto a new Profile → Settings screen** (`src/profile/screens.js`'s `renderAppSettingsScreen`, artboard `ProfileSettings`) — not into Sound. The doc's own spec is explicit that Sound is "today's Character content (the four sliders... and Scratch)" plus the Day-drift control, and separately that Appearance belongs in Settings; **everything else that used to live in the Character route — presets, Scratch, Sleeve Pull, Launch intro, Background playback, Car Mode — moved into Sound as-is**, a judgment call the doc's own prose doesn't settle explicitly (it names only the headline mockup-visible items for Sound), but the only one consistent with "nothing that used to open Character should become unreachable": there is no other screen in this plan that would otherwise house sleeve-pull/launch-intro/background-playback/Car-Mode settings, so Sound keeps the whole old route's body minus the one row the doc *does* explicitly relocate.
  - **The Settings screen's own Appearance control can't reuse `wireCharacter()`'s existing boot-time wiring.** `document.querySelectorAll('[data-theme-choice]')` (`src/wiring/character.js`) only ever finds elements physically present in the DOM at the moment it runs, once, at boot — which is exactly why the D7 desktop sidebar's own static `#sidebarAppearance` control already works with zero code change there. A screen built fresh every time `renderProfile()` runs is never present at boot, so `renderAppSettingsScreen()` gives each of its three `[data-theme-choice]` buttons its own inline `click` listener calling `setTheme()` (imported directly from `ui/theme.js`) instead — `setTheme()`'s own existing `aria-pressed` sync, which re-queries every `[data-theme-choice]` button in the document on every call, still picks these up correctly regardless of which screen happens to be open when it runs. Only Auto/Light/Dark exist yet — Nychthemeron (DA4) adds the fourth choice and, with it, the mockup's own 2×2 grid; three choices read naturally in one row, so that layout isn't anticipated here.
  - **Reaching Settings needed a new, universal entry point, not a reuse of the existing one.** Before this step, only the signed-in `renderStorefront()` branch of `renderStorefrontSection()` (`src/profile/storefront.js`) had a settings button at all (a gear, going straight to Account) — the other two profile-home states (`renderLocalStorefront`, the common default; `renderOpenStoreCard`, "social enabled but not signed in yet," a real, common case in this actual deployment since social config has been live since step 04) had no settings entry point whatsoever. Moving Appearance behind a screen only the signed-in gear could reach would have made it unreachable in both of the other two states. Fixed with one new `.profile-settings-btn` ("Settings ›") added once, at the very top of `renderStorefrontSection()`, before any of its three branches, rather than three separate additions — the old signed-in-only gear is removed as redundant (Settings → Account is one tap further, the same destination).
  - **"Library tools" in Settings opens the existing disclosure panel in place, not a new screen.** `#libToolsPanel` physically lives as a sibling of `#profileBody`, not inside it (so its listeners survive the `innerHTML` resets every profile re-render does — S1 spec §5, unchanged by this step). Settings' own "Library tools" row reuses the exact `classList`/`aria-expanded` toggle `src/wiring/library-view.js`'s `#libToolsToggle` handler already performs, then calls `scrollIntoView()` on the panel — reusing the existing mechanism rather than inventing a second one.
- **The day arc** (`#sundownDayView`'s own SVG, `src/player/clock.js`) replaces the old sunset→dusk `.sun-bar`/`.sun-fill`/`.sun-legend` progress bar entirely (removed from `miziki.html` and, since nothing else used them, their now-dead CSS rules pruned from `16-edit-sheet.css` too) — a schematic (not-to-scale, per the design README's own note on the artboard) sun path with five time labels (Dawn/Sunrise/Noon/Sunset/Dusk, from `S.sun`) and a sun-or-moon dot. `dayArcPosition(dayPos)` (new, pure, exported for its own test — same precedent as `angleDelta`/`skyGradientStops`) places the dot: day phases walk a fixed, evenly-spaced schematic span left to right, rising from the horizon at dawn to a peak around midday and back down by the end of twilight; night has no finer-grained progress of its own to track (`dayPosition()`'s `phaseProgress` is always 0 for `'night'`, by design — see `day.js`'s own entry), so the moon sits at one fixed, representative point under the horizon rather than animating across the night — a deliberate, documented simplification, not a bug, the same "not every decorative mockup detail needs a backing data model" judgment this engagement has made before (e.g. 6d's own skipped canvas-rendering tests, DA2's own skipped drift sparkline). `updateDayArc()` (the DOM-touching half, module-private) moves the SVG dot, highlights whichever of the five labels the current phase maps to (`DAY_ARC_ACTIVE_LABEL`, a schematic seven-phases-to-five-labels mapping — Night highlights none of them), and writes the five times.
- **The phase block** (`#phaseName`/`#phaseSub`, mirrored into the desktop sidebar's own new `#sidePhaseName`/`#sidePhaseSub`) is a second, separate piece of UI from the existing `#rateWhy` row just below it, not a replacement — the doc's own "keep the existing 'Now playing at' readout and add a new phase line" instruction, taken literally: `#rateWhy`/`#rateOut`/`#rpmText` are byte-for-byte untouched by this step. `phaseText(dayPos, p, e)` (new, pure, exported for its own test) is the small decision function behind the big heading ("Twilight") plus muted sub-line ("60% of the way down" for twilight, "N% of the way up" for dawn — both reusing `phaseProgress`/`easedProgress()`'s existing "percent risen"/"percent down" construction DA1 already established — and a flat "Full speed"/"Settled in for the night" for every other phase).
- **Sky across the whole day, in both themes — `skyGradientStops(p)` became `skyGradientStops(dayPos, theme)`.** Eight named anchors (`docs/design/day-arc/README.md`'s own sky-token table: Night/Dawn/Morning/Midday/Afternoon/Golden/Twilight/Dusk, each with a dark and a light hex pair), smoothstep-blended between neighbours — the same architecture DA2's own `drift.js` already uses, and for the same reason: every phase boundary is continuous by construction, with no separate continuity mechanism to keep in sync.
  - **Which anchor blends toward which was the one genuinely ambiguous design call in this whole step, made and documented rather than guessed at silently.** The design README names a "when" for all eight rows, but four of them (Night "dusk → dawn," Dawn "dawn → sunrise," Afternoon "noon → golden," Twilight "sunset → dusk") describe a span matching a `dayPosition()` phase exactly, while the other four (Morning "sunrise + 1.5h," Midday "solar noon," Golden "sunset − 1h," Dusk "dusk + 30 min") describe a single instant. Read together with DA2's own precedent (where Morning/Midday/Golden/Night are flat-for-their-whole-phase anchors and Dawn/Afternoon/Twilight are pure ramps between two of them), the chosen scheme keeps Midday and Golden flat for their whole phase (the same judgment call DA2 already made for them), gives Morning the same two-stage DA2-style split (flat at its own anchor through the first half of the phase, ramping to Midday across the second half — "sunrise + 1.5h" being exactly the midpoint of Morning's own 3-hour span makes this read correct, not arbitrary), and has Dawn/Afternoon ramp between their two flanking flat anchors (Night→Morning, Midday→Golden) exactly as DA2's own dawn/afternoon cases do. **Twilight and Night are the one place this step diverges from a literal "ramp toward your own same-named anchor" reading**: Twilight ramps Golden → **Dusk** (not toward its own "Twilight" table row), reaching the Dusk anchor exactly at the dusk instant; the first 30 minutes of Night (a new `duskRampProgress` field, see `day.js`'s own entry) then ramps Dusk → **Night**, continuous with Twilight's own ending value, before settling flat at the Night anchor for the rest of the night. This is what makes the "Dusk" anchor (`dusk + 30 min`, a moment with no `dayPosition()` phase of its own) earn a real, load-bearing place in the algorithm rather than sitting unused — the one true interior waypoint this scheme has, everywhere else a "when" column entry is read as a flat-hold's own representative instant rather than a second blend target. Dawn then ramps Night → Morning the same way DA2's own dawn branch already does, closing the loop.
  - **`day.js`'s `dayPosition()` gained `duskRampProgress`** specifically to make the Dusk waypoint above possible: a second, independent 0..1 progress value, meaningful only while `phase === 'night'` — 0 right at dusk, 1 once 30 minutes have passed, 1 for the rest of the night including the pre-dawn tail. A new field, not a repurposed `phaseProgress` (which stays always 0 for `'night'`, exactly as before this step, since nothing currently reading it — `drift.js`'s own flat `'night'` case chief among them — expects anything different); confirmed safe by checking every existing consumer and test (`tests/unit/day.test.js`/`tests/unit/drift.test.js` use no `deepEqual` against a whole `dayPosition()` result anywhere, only per-field checks) before adding it.
  - **`drawSun()` now always animates `--sky1`/`--sky2`, in both themes** — the D2-era "Light keeps its static tokens, no inline `setProperty` override" behavior (and the later sky-fix entry's own restatement of the same rule) is superseded on purpose, not forgotten: both were an explicit, documented stand-in for "we haven't designed a light sky arc yet," and the design README now gives Light a full, contrast-checked anchor table of its own, so there's no longer a reason to skip animating it.
  - **A WCAG contrast check, run directly in the unit test, not just asserted by eyeballing the design README's own numbers.** `tests/unit/clock.test.js` implements the standard relative-luminance/contrast-ratio formula once and checks every one of the eight anchors, in both themes, against all three text tokens the README names (paper/muted/accent-text) — and, per the doc's own "verify that in the unit test by sampling every 10 minutes" instruction, also walks a real 24-hour day at Columbia, MD (reusing `day.test.js`'s own fixture location) sampling every 10 minutes and checking every *blended* value along the way, not just the eight named anchors. Both pass at ≥4.5:1 everywhere — the design README's own table is confirmed correct against the actual blending code, not just trusted.
- **The Day-drift shortcut row** (`#dayDriftSummary`/`#dayDriftLink`, Day view) is a one-line summary of what Sound's own Day-drift control is doing right now, with a button that jumps straight to Sound — `driftSummaryText(dayPos, intensity)` (new, pure, `src/sundown/drift.js`) reads the sign of `driftOffsets()`'s own already-computed `soft` offset (every anchor in that file's own table is uniformly signed one way or another, so reading just one of the four is never misleading) to say "A touch warmer"/"A touch brighter"/"Right where you set it," suffixed with the intensity label, or "Off — your own settings, no drift today" at Off. Computed fresh in `drawSun()` alongside everything else it already writes there, not on a separate timer.
- **Each Sound slider gained a "now" badge** (`#wowNow`/`#satNow`/`#softNow`/`#compNow`, next to the existing user-value label) rather than rebuilding all four native `<input type=range>` sliders as the mockup's own custom thumb/tick/dual-label design — a deliberate, documented scope trim, the same "escape valve" this engagement has used before for a decorative mockup detail that would need disproportionate new surface for a purely cosmetic gain (6d's own skipped canvas-rendering tests; DA2's own skipped drift sparkline). `drawSun()` computes `effectiveChar()` once (the exact same call `applyCharacter()`'s own audio-graph update already makes, never a second computation) and only shows a badge once it would actually read differently from the slider's own value — `#charNowLegend`'s explanatory note is shown/hidden the same way, based on whether Day-drift intensity is currently Off.
- **"Preview the day" is now a direct 24-hour scrubber, not a sunset-window-relative formula** — `<input id="preview" min="0" max="1440">`'s own value *is* `S.previewMin` (minutes from now), with no conversion at all. This needed no backend change whatsoever: `nowClock()`, `currentDayPosition()`, `sunProgress()`, `easedProgress()`, `computeRate()`, and (since DA2) `effectiveChar()` via `currentDayPosition()` already transparently honor `S.previewMin` across the *whole* day, not just the dusk window — confirmed by reading every one of those call sites before writing a single line of this feature, since the old formula's own `(S.sun.dusk - S.sun.set)/60000`-style span math was the only thing actually scoped to sunset, not the computation underneath it. The header eyebrow (`#sundownEyebrow`) reads "Now · 6:49 PM" while live (updated from `drawSun()`'s own ~220ms tick, since it's not time-critical the way a drag needs to feel) and flips to accent-colored "Previewing · <time>" the instant the slider moves (owned by the slider's own `input` handler instead, so that one genuinely does need to feel immediate) — `#liveBtn` and a `v === 0` slider value both still return to Live exactly as before this step.
- No `await` was added, removed or reordered anywhere — every change in this step is new markup, new CSS, a new pure helper, or an additional DOM-writing line inside an already-synchronous function (`drawSun()`, `showRoute()`, `renderStorefrontSection()`); nothing here touches playback or sync timing at all.
- **A real mock-chain bug, caught by the test suite itself, not missed silently.** `src/profile/screens.js`'s one new import (`setTheme`, for the Settings screen) is real, from `src/ui/theme.js` — whose own real `storage/meta.js` import reaches `player/background.js` → `player/transport.js` → `library/view.js`'s own seven-circular-pair web → `shell/header.js` → `shell/routes.js` → `social/screens/home.js` → `social/screens/friend-store.js`, whose own real `storeAwning` import then failed against `tests/unit/profile.test.js`'s already-narrow `storefront.js` mock (`SyntaxError: ... does not provide an export named 'storeAwning'`) — the exact `mock.module` behavior (replacing a module's exports *entirely*, not patching specific names) that every prior step's own "stop the chain at the door" mock-chasing note in this file already describes, just the first time it's been triggered by a one-line import addition this far downstream. Fixed the same way every prior instance was: added a direct `src/ui/theme.js` mock (`setTheme(){}`) to `profile.test.js`, rather than trying to patch the five modules in between. No other existing test file needed the same fix — `clock.test.js`/`forget-library.test.js`/`history.test.js`/`queue.test.js` all already mock the *whole* `profile/screens.js` module (so the new import inside it is never reached), and `profile.test.js` is the one file in the suite that loads the real file.
- Added `tests/unit/routes.test.js` (new, 6 tests): a normal route still just toggles the matching `.route` element; `showRoute('character')` redirects to Sundown and selects Sound, confirmed via `lastSundownView()` rather than by reaching into `showRoute`'s own internals (the one externally-observable side effect of the redirect); `selectSundownView`/`lastSundownView` persist and restore correctly, including across an unrecognized saved value (treated as Day) and a missing `localStorage` (never throws, the same device-only-and-defensive posture `S.theme`'s own mirror already has); `isLibraryRouteVisible`/`isProfileRouteVisible` read the real `"on"` class off whichever route is actually showing. Mocks `profile/screens.js`/`social/screens/home.js`/`shell/header.js`/`gatefold/view.js` directly, the usual "stop the chain at the door" reasoning — `routes.js`'s own real imports of the first two would otherwise reach the exact same wide chain the bug above describes, for no benefit to what this file is actually testing.
- Extended `tests/unit/day.test.js` (2 new tests) for `duskRampProgress`: 0 right at dusk, ~0.5 at the 15-minute mark, 1 at 30 minutes and for the rest of the night (including the pre-dawn tail, and the legacy-partial-data/polar-night fallback branches, each checked against their own already-existing fixtures). Extended `tests/unit/clock.test.js` substantially: the four old `skyGradientStops(p)` tests (byte-identical-to-the-old-A/B/C-triple checks, the p=0.5 seam check) are gone, replaced by tests against the new `(dayPos, theme)` signature — the midday/night-settled/golden-hour anchors still byte-match the exact old dark-theme triples (confirming this is a true generalization, not a redesign), light never matches dark for any phase, an unknown `dayPos` falls back to midday, every one of the seven phase-to-phase boundaries described above is exactly continuous in both themes, and the two contrast-ratio tests described above. The three old light-vs-dark `drawSun()` tests (which asserted Light *never* sets an inline sky value) are replaced with three new ones asserting the opposite on purpose: Light now sets one too, and it differs from Dark's. New `phaseText`/`dayArcPosition` tests cover the wording/positioning rules described above. The existing `fakeEl()` stub this file already builds for `$()`/`el()` gained `classList.add`/`classList.remove` (previously only `toggle`) and `setAttribute`, since `updateDayArc()`/the new eyebrow-toggling code call them directly.
- **Browser verification** (Playwright, both built targets, each served from its own stable, separate static-file directory — the same isolation DA1's/DA2's own entries already established): confirmed the tab bar shows exactly four buttons (Library/Home/Sundown/Profile), no fifth Character button anywhere, on both phone width and desktop width; opened Sundown, confirmed the Day view renders the arc/phase block/reorganized controls/Day-drift row/24h preview slider with no console error; tapped the Sound tab, confirmed every old Character control (sliders, presets, Scratch, Sleeve Pull, Launch intro, Background playback, Car Mode) is present and still saves correctly (toggled Scratch off, reloaded, confirmed it read back off); confirmed Appearance is gone from Sound and reachable instead via the new Profile → Settings screen, with its three buttons correctly syncing `aria-pressed` and applying the chosen theme; dragged "Preview the day" across a full 24 hours and confirmed the sun/moon dot moves along the arc, the sky changes smoothly with no visible jump at any phase boundary, the phase name/sub-line update, and the platter's own rate readout tracks the previewed time instead of the real clock, in both Light and Dark theme; confirmed `showRoute('character')` (fired directly, since nothing in the real UI can trigger it any more) lands cleanly on Sundown/Sound with no console error. Zero non-baseline console/page errors on either built target throughout — the one 404 that appears only during interaction is the same long-documented baseline 404 every prior step since 01 has already noted.
- Verified: `npm run lint` passes with 0 errors, 0 warnings; `npm run test:unit` passes (375/375 — 360 before this step + 15 new: 6 in `routes.test.js`, 2 in `day.test.js`, 7 in `clock.test.js`); both builds succeed (`npm run build` and `MIZIKI_BASE=/ npm run build`); the Playwright verification above passed on both built targets with zero non-baseline console/page errors across the full phase×theme×mobile/desktop matrix the doc's own verification bar asks for.

**Preview focus (Ken, iPhone and desktop) — please verify by hand before merging:**
1. Confirm the tab bar shows four tabs, not five, and that the Day/Sound switch under Sundown works — tap Sound and confirm every old Character setting is still there and still saves correctly (try Scratch, a preset, Sleeve Pull).
2. Confirm the Appearance/theme picker is now reached via Profile → Settings, not Character, and that choosing Light/Dark/Auto still works exactly as before from its new home.
3. Drag "Preview the day" through a full 24 hours and confirm: the sun (or moon, at night) moves smoothly along the arc with no jumps; the times under the arc match Columbia's real dawn/sunrise/noon/sunset/dusk; the sky changes smoothly through every phase with no visible seam, in both Light and Dark theme — pay particular attention to golden hour and dusk, where the design doc's own contrast numbers are tightest; the phase name/sub-line and the "Now playing at" rate both update to match wherever the slider is; and the platter's own audible speed follows the previewed time, not the real clock, while dragging.
4. Confirm the Day-drift shortcut row on the Day view reads sensibly against whatever Sound's own Day-drift intensity is currently set to, and that tapping it actually opens Sound.
5. Confirm no console errors or regressions anywhere else while this PR is active.

**After-merge check:** glance at the real app at a few different times of day over the next day or two — morning, midday, golden hour, night — and confirm the sky and phase readout look right for each, in whichever theme you happen to be using.

### DA4: theme follows the sun ("Nychthemeron")

Fourth of the six-PR day-arc plan (`docs/DAY_ARC.md`), building on DA1's day model (merged, PR #90) and DA3's own Settings screen/sidebar control (merged, PR #91). A feature PR — it adds a fourth appearance choice: light from sunrise to sunset, dark from sunset to sunrise, resolved from the real (or previewed) day position rather than a device-level `prefers-color-scheme` signal. The doc's own gating applies here too — **this PR is not to be merged without Ken's go-ahead**.

- **`S.theme` gains a fourth value, `'nychthemeron'`** — no new field, the same existing enum (`storage/prefs.js`'s `parsePrefs()`, already updated for `'auto'`/`'light'`/`'dark'` since D2) just accepting one more string. `effectiveTheme()` (`src/ui/theme.js`) resolves it by asking `sundown/solar.js`'s `currentDayPosition()` whether the *current* (or previewed, via `S.previewMin` — the same transparent honoring every other day-position reader already has since DA1) phase is one of the four daylight phases — morning, midday, afternoon, golden hour — light otherwise dark; dawn and twilight read dark, since both sit outside the literal sunrise-to-sunset span even though Sundown's own rate curve treats them as transitional. This cut of the phase list is exported as `isDaylightPhase(phase)`, specifically so `profile/screens.js`'s own switch-time caption can ask the identical question without duplicating the set a second time.
- **`applyTheme()`'s Nychthemeron branch sets `document.documentElement.dataset.theme` to the *resolved* light/dark value, not the literal choice** — Nychthemeron was never meant to be a third palette (the design README's own framing: "light from sunrise to sunset, dark from sunset to sunrise," not a distinct look), so it reuses `22-theme.css`'s existing `[data-theme="light"]`/`[data-theme="dark"]` token blocks verbatim. The literal `'nychthemeron'` itself is what gets mirrored to `localStorage` (`THEME_STORAGE_KEY`), since `theme-boot.js` needs to know *which rule* to apply at first paint, not just last session's resolved color.
- **A live light↔dark watcher, piggybacking an already-running loop instead of a second timer.** `syncNychthemeronTheme()` (new, `src/ui/theme.js`) is called once per tick from `player/clock.js`'s `drawSun()` — the one place in the app that already recomputes the day position on a steady ~220ms cadence while visible, the same reasoning DA2's own `scheduleDriftRecompute()` entry already used for not inventing a parallel polling mechanism. It's a no-op whenever `S.theme !== 'nychthemeron'` (and resets its own "last known side" tracking then, so switching away and back later never animates a stale, no-longer-live difference) and a no-op when the resolved side hasn't actually changed since the last call, so it never re-triggers anything on every tick while nothing's different. The very first call after switching onto Nychthemeron never animates either — nothing was visibly different a moment ago to transition away from, the same reasoning every other theme choice's own direct `applyTheme()` call has never needed to animate.
- **"Switch with a ~1.5s colour transition (none under reduced motion)"** — a `.theme-transition` class (`22-theme.css`, appended after DA3's own `.theme-transition`-adjacent additions... actually appended as the file's own newest block, right after the existing three selector blocks) briefly toggled on `document.documentElement` by a new `beginThemeTransition()`, declared on every element (`.theme-transition, .theme-transition *`) since a CSS transition has to be declared on whichever element actually paints the changed custom property, not on `:root` itself, which has no visible background/color of its own. "None under reduced motion" is a single JS-side check (`REDUCED`, `util/motion.js` — already real-imported into `ui/theme.js` for this) rather than a second `@media (prefers-reduced-motion)` guard in the CSS, so there's exactly one place that decision lives. Every other theme change in the app — a manual Auto/Light/Dark tap, Auto following a live OS scheme change — stays an instant snap, unchanged; only a *live* Nychthemeron crossing gets this treatment.
- **The device-only sun-times cache, `miziki-sun-times`** (new, `localStorage`, `{date, rise, set}` — never coordinates): `src/sundown/solar.js`'s new `persistSunTimesCache(rise, set)`, called from `computeSun()` and (since location is never stored, CLAUDE.md's own long-standing rule) also from `sundown/location.js`'s `fallbackSun()` with its own fixed stand-in times — "written whenever events are computed," per the doc, and a fixed stand-in still counts, keeping the cache's own freshness stamp current even on a device that never grants location. `date` (today's `toDateString()`) is the cache's one freshness check — never used for anything else. This is the one fact `theme-boot.js`'s pre-paint `<head>` script is allowed to remember about the sun, specifically so a pinned Nychthemeron choice can resolve light/dark *before* `S.sun`, or any module, even exists.
- **`theme-boot.js` reads that cache for Nychthemeron, inline, duplicated by hand** — same "stay import-free to run as a plain classic script" constraint `THEME_STORAGE_KEY` already forced on this file since D2. A missing cache, a malformed one, or one dated anything other than today falls back to the exact fixed stand-in times `fallbackSun()` uses (6:50am rise, 8:10pm set). The *testable* twin of this exact decision is a new, exported pure function in `src/ui/theme.js`, `resolveBootThemeFromSunCache(cache, now)` — kept in sync with the inline copy by hand, the same "two sources reviewed together" discipline CLAUDE.md's own `docs/PRIVACY_POLICY.md` vs. `src/profile/legal.js` entry already describes. The real app corrects this best-guess within moments either way, the instant `S.sun` is actually populated and `applyTheme()` runs for real via `restoreMeta()`.
- **The fourth button, a 2×2 grid, and the narrow-sidebar letter-spacing tightening — all per the design README's own `ProfileSettings`/`DesktopSidebar` artboards, read directly rather than guessed at.** Profile › Settings' `renderAppSettingsScreen()` (`src/profile/screens.js`) gains a fourth `[data-theme-choice="nychthemeron"]` button in its existing segmented control, now laid out as a 2×2 CSS grid (`.settings-appearance`, `src/styles/17-profile.css` — overriding `.seg`'s own `display:flex` the same way this rule's pre-DA4 `width:100%` already did, later in the cascade, equal specificity) rather than the three-across row the control used before — "the grid gives the long word room at 320-390px," so no letter-spacing tightening is needed here, only at the desktop sidebar's own narrower control (`#sidebarAppearance`, D7): `.sidebar-appearance .seg` becomes the same 2×2 grid, and the Nychthemeron button alone gets `letter-spacing:.02em;font-size:9px` (`23-desktop.css`), matching the `DesktopSidebar.dc.html` artboard's own inline style on that one button exactly; the other three keep the row's existing 10px size. Only the Nychthemeron button gets an `aria-label` ("Nychthemeron: follows sunrise and sunset") in both places — the other three's own visible text already says everything their `aria-pressed` state needs to convey.
- **The caption's own "today's switch time" sentence, `nychthemeronSwitchCaption()` (`profile/screens.js`), reads correctly in every phase without guessing at tomorrow's sunrise.** If the real (or previewed) phase is currently a daylight one, the next switch is unambiguously "Turns dark at `<S.sun.set>` today." If it's dark and today's own `S.sun.rise` hasn't happened yet (the dawn phase, or the night phase's own pre-dawn tail — both cases where `dayEvents()`'s own Julian-day-nearest rounding, DA1's "continuity across midnight" trick, still gives an upcoming rise), it's "Turns light at `<S.sun.rise>` today." Otherwise — twilight, or the post-dusk portion of night — today's own `S.sun.rise` is already a *past* time (the same calendar day's morning rise, by the identical Julian-day convention), so showing it would be a confusing already-happened time; the caption instead says the plainly honest "Turns light at sunrise tomorrow," rather than attempting to compute tomorrow's exact rise (which would need a second `dayEvents()` call against `S.sun.lat`/`lon`, unavailable in fallback mode in the first place). Deliberately not unit-tested on its own — it's presentation text built from already-tested primitives (`isDaylightPhase`, `S.sun`'s own fields), the same "not much here is pure" escape valve this engagement has used before for decorative-but-derived text (DA3's own `driftSummaryText`) — and is instead confirmed correct by the Playwright verification below, which reads the real rendered sentence against the real resolved theme.
- **A real bug, caught by browser verification, not by any unit test — `applyPrefs()` (`src/storage/meta.js`) keeps its own, separate copy of the theme-enum check, independent of `storage/prefs.js`'s `parsePrefs()`.** `parsePrefs()` already accepted `'nychthemeron'` correctly (confirmed by `prefs.test.js`'s own round-trip test, which passed from the start); `applyPrefs()`'s *own* `if(p.theme === 'auto' || ...)` line had simply been missed. The practical effect: picking Nychthemeron worked instantly (the direct `setTheme()` call applies and saves correctly), but reloading the app silently reverted it to Auto every single time — `main.js`'s own unconditional, synchronous boot-time `applyTheme()` call (before `restoreLibrary()`/`restoreMeta()` has even started) runs with `S.theme` still at its module default, and `applyPrefs()`'s theme-restoring line is the *only* thing that corrects it once the real saved value comes back from IndexedDB; with Nychthemeron silently dropped from that one check, nothing ever did. Fixed by adding `'nychthemeron'` to `applyPrefs()`'s own check, matching `parsePrefs()`'s already-correct one. A dedicated regression test (`tests/unit/meta.test.js`, below) now exercises exactly this path, which the suite's pre-existing round-trip test never had reason to touch (it only ever checked `playlists`/`albumOrder`/`albumSort`).
- **A separate timing question, investigated and found to be a pre-existing characteristic, not a regression**: a per-30ms poll of `document.documentElement.dataset.theme` across a real reload (a throwaway debug script, not part of the shipped test suite) showed the attribute briefly reads unset (Auto's own "no attribute" state) for roughly 30-80ms between `theme-boot.js`'s own correct early application and `restoreMeta()`'s async completion — the exact gap the bug above's own root-cause explanation implies, confirmed directly rather than only inferred. This is identical, in kind and in cause, to what a pinned *Light* or *Dark* choice already does on every reload since D2 (the same unconditional early `applyTheme()` call, same async `restoreMeta()` correction) — DA4 doesn't make it any better or any worse, and fixing that shared, pre-existing boot-sequencing characteristic (reordering `applyTheme()`'s first call to after `restoreMeta()`, or gating it) is out of this PR's own scope; noted here rather than silently worked around, in case a future PR wants to take it on for all four choices at once.
- No `await` was added, removed or reordered anywhere — `effectiveTheme()`/`applyTheme()`/`syncNychthemeronTheme()`/`persistSunTimesCache()` are all synchronous; `computeSun()`/`fallbackSun()`'s own existing `await`-free bodies are unchanged except for the one new trailing call each.
- Added 14 new tests: `tests/unit/theme.test.js` gained 12 — `setTheme('nychthemeron')` as a real, accepted choice; `effectiveTheme()` light between sunrise/sunset, dark either side of it (dawn and twilight, checked as two separate cases), following `S.previewMin` rather than the real clock, and the polar-day/polar-night fallback (reusing the exact `day.test.js`-style fixture shape); `applyTheme()` resolving Nychthemeron to the correct light/dark `dataset.theme` while still mirroring the literal choice to `localStorage`; `syncNychthemeronTheme()`'s two behaviors (a genuine flip animates and re-applies, with staying-the-same checked inline as a no-op in the same test; switching off and back on resets the baseline so a stale difference is never animated); and 4 for `resolveBootThemeFromSunCache()` (a fresh today-dated cache, a missing one, one dated a previous day — deliberately given a wide, clearly-wrong-if-trusted window so the test can tell "correctly fell back" apart from "accidentally read something close enough" — and a malformed one missing `rise`/`set`). A small `sunAt(dawnH, riseH, noonH, setH, duskH)` fixture builder (hour offsets from a captured `Date.now()`, the same relative-to-now shape `solar.test.js`'s own fixtures already use) keeps every one of these fully ordered (`dawn < rise < noon < set < dusk`) rather than risking `dayPosition()`'s own clamping quietly papering over an inconsistent hand-rolled fixture — the first draft of two of these tests used ad hoc `Date` arithmetic with `noon` placed after `set`, which still happened to pass by coincidence (collapsing into a degenerate but still-correctly-signed golden-hour sliver) and was rewritten once noticed, not left in place just because it passed. `tests/unit/prefs.test.js` gained 1 (`theme` round-trips as `'nychthemeron'`). `tests/unit/meta.test.js` gained 1, the dedicated regression test for the `applyPrefs()` bug above, bringing it to 5 (3 existing + this + the 650ms debounce test, unchanged).
- **A real mock-chain bug, caught by the test suite itself, the same shape this engagement has hit before**: `profile/screens.js`'s two new imports (`isDaylightPhase` from `ui/theme.js`, `currentDayPosition` from `sundown/solar.js`) broke `tests/unit/profile.test.js`'s existing, already-narrow `src/ui/theme.js` mock (`node:test`'s `mock.module()` replaces a module's exports *entirely*, not just the names a given importer happens to use) with a `SyntaxError: ... does not provide an export named 'isDaylightPhase'`. Fixed by adding `isDaylightPhase(){ return false; }` to that existing mock and a new, equally narrow `src/sundown/solar.js` mock (`currentDayPosition(){ return { phase: 'midday' }; }`) — the usual "stop the chain at the door" reasoning, since `profile.test.js`'s own fake DOM has no reason to also support whatever a real `solar.js`/`day.js` call site might eventually need. No other existing test file needed the same fix.
- **Browser verification** (Playwright, both built targets, each served from its own stable, separate static-file directory — the GitHub Pages target nested under a `Miziki/` subfolder so its own absolute `/Miziki/assets/...` references resolve correctly, the same isolation every prior DA step's own entry already established): navigated to Profile › Settings and confirmed all four `[data-theme-choice]` buttons render, the Nychthemeron one carrying the exact `aria-label` the doc specifies; confirmed the caption includes both the explanation sentence and a "Turns dark/light at ... today"-or-"...sunrise tomorrow" sentence; confirmed `.settings-appearance` never overflows its own container at 390px width; clicked Nychthemeron and confirmed `data-theme` resolved to a real light/dark value immediately; reloaded and confirmed, after the app settled, the theme was still correctly resolved **and** the literal `'nychthemeron'` choice had survived in `localStorage` (the regression this step's own fix targets, now passing); confirmed the `miziki-sun-times` cache was written with exactly `{date, rise, set}` and no `lat`/`lon` fields at all; dragged "Preview the day" +12 hours and confirmed the theme flipped to the opposite of its live value, then back to 0 and confirmed it returned to the live value; switched directly to Light, then Auto (confirming the attribute is removed), then Dark, confirming each overrides Nychthemeron cleanly with no leftover state. Zero non-baseline console/page errors on either built target — the one 404 that appears is the same long-documented baseline 404 every prior step since 01 has already noted.
- Verified: `npm run lint` passes with 0 errors, 0 warnings; `npm run test:unit` passes (389/389 — 375 before this step + 14 new); both builds succeed (`npm run build` and `MIZIKI_BASE=/ npm run build`, 175 modules transformed — unchanged from before this step, since no new file was added, just one new real import edge, `ui/theme.js` → `sundown/solar.js`); the Playwright verification above passed on both built targets with zero non-baseline console/page errors.

**Preview focus (Ken, iPhone) — please verify by hand before merging:**
1. Choose **Nychthemeron** (Profile › Settings on phone, the sidebar's own control on desktop). Drag Preview the day past sunset: the app turns dark, smoothly (not an instant snap). Past sunrise: it turns light, the same way.
2. Close and reopen the app (or reload the tab): it opens in the right theme, with no flash — and if you check right away, Nychthemeron is still the selected choice, not reverted to Auto.
3. Read the caption under the Appearance control against the real time of day where you are — it should say something sensible ("Turns dark at 8:10 PM today," or "Turns light at sunrise tomorrow" late at night).
4. Check Light, Dark and Auto still behave exactly as before — picking any of them should override Nychthemeron immediately and cleanly.
5. Confirm no console errors or regressions anywhere else while this PR is active.

**After-merge check:** set Nychthemeron on the real app, and look at it once after sunset and once after sunrise.

### DA5: your listening rhythm on the profile

Fifth of the six-PR day-arc plan (`docs/DAY_ARC.md`), building on DA1's day model (merged, PR #90). A feature PR — it adds a new per-phase listening-history counter (device-only, like every other history counter), a profile card that visualizes it once there's enough data, and an opt-in (default off) sharing switch that publishes only the aggregate verdict+percentages to a friend's own crate page. The doc's own gating applies here too — **this PR is not to be merged until Ken applies migration `007_rhythm.sql` to the live Supabase project** — see "Before merging (Ken)" at the end of this entry.

- **Counting the real clock, never the previewed one.** `src/sundown/solar.js` gained `realDayPosition()` — `dayPosition(new Date(), S.sun)`, computed directly against the real clock rather than `S.previewMin`, the one deliberate difference from the existing `currentDayPosition()` (every Sundown UI readout, by design, follows "Preview the day" when it's active; a durable stat never should). `src/history/played.js`'s `incrementTrackPlay(t)` — already the one place a qualifying play is counted (CLAUDE.md's own long-standing "`src/history/` is the only place that writes listening counters" rule) — calls `realDayPosition().phase` once per play and tallies both `S.phasePlayCounts[phase]` (a flat `{phase: count}` map) and `S.albumPhaseCounts[albumKey(t)][phase]` (one such map per album, for the card's own "best record per phase" list), then calls two new sibling persist functions, `persistPhasePlayCounts()`/`persistAlbumPhaseCounts()` (`src/history/sessions.js`, right alongside `persistTrackPlayCounts()` — the same per-device-counter shape, restored the same way in `restoreSessions()`). `src/history/clear.js`'s `clearListeningHistory(orphanedOnly)` treats both exactly like every sibling counter: a full-scope clear zeroes both outright (`S.phasePlayCounts = {}; S.albumPhaseCounts = {}`) and persists the reset; the orphaned-only scope prunes `albumPhaseCounts` by the same `filterKeep(..., albumIds)` call `albumLastPlayed`/`sessionCounts` already get, but leaves `phasePlayCounts` completely untouched — it has no per-identity shape at all to prune by (a phase total isn't "about" any one album), so only a full reset can ever touch it, the doc's own implied behavior made explicit rather than assumed. `src/library/delete.js`'s `forgetLibrary()` needed **zero changes** — it was already, and remains, the one function in this app that never touches a listening-history counter at all (F3's own "Forget library keeps your stats" rule) — confirmed by a dedicated regression test (below) rather than left to the existing suite's broader forgetLibrary coverage to happen to catch.
