- **`src/sundown/rhythm.js`** (new) is the whole pure half of the feature — no DOM, no network, no `S` import, the same "testable without a browser" shape as `sundown/day.js`/`sundown/drift.js`: `PHASES` (the fixed seven-phase iteration order every function below uses), `RHYTHM_MIN_PLAYS` (`20` — the doc's own "show it only after at least 20 counted plays" gate, checked by both the profile card and the sharing push), `RHYTHM_VERDICT_THRESHOLD` (`0.30` — "no phase is above about 30%" falls through to the "Around the clock" fallback regardless of which phase happens to be nominally largest), `computeRhythmShares(counts)` (`{total, shares}`, every one of the seven phases always present in `shares` — 0 for a phase with no plays — so a caller never has to guard a missing key), `rhythmVerdictLabel(shares)` (the three examples the doc names verbatim — "Mostly a night listener," "A morning listener," "Golden-hour leaning" — plus a plain, consistent "Mostly a `<phase>` listener" phrasing for the other four phases the doc's own wording implies without spelling out, and the `RHYTHM_AROUND_LABEL` fallback), and `bestAlbumPerPhase(albumPhaseCounts)` (the doc's own optional "a record per phase" list — `{phase: albumId|null}`, every phase present; a tie keeps whichever album id sorts first alphabetically, a deterministic if arbitrary tiebreak, since a `{phase: count}` map carries no "most recently played" signal to break a tie by instead). `src/sync/backup.js`'s own `merged.totals.counters.*` (the cross-device grand totals) is shaped identically and could be fed into any of these just as well, though nothing does today — the profile card reads this device's own local counts, the same "never written back from a merge" posture every other per-device counter in this app already has.
- **`src/profile/rhythm-card.js`** (new) is the profile card itself: `PHASE_LABEL`/`PHASE_COLOR` (one short label and one swatch per phase, reused by both the ring and the legend), `ringGradient(shares)` (a **CSS `conic-gradient()`**, one wedge per phase at its own share and color — the one deliberate scope trim this step takes, documented rather than silently cut: the design artboard's own literal spec is a 24-hour radial bar clock, one bar per hour, but the actual data model this step counts is seven *phases*, not 24 *hours* — there's nothing hourly to show, since `S.phasePlayCounts` has no finer grain than the phase itself. A phase-granularity conic-gradient ring is the honest rendering of the data that actually exists, the same precedented "schematic, not literal" trim DA1's own fixed night-moon dot and DA2's own skipped drift sparkline already took for an artboard detail with no backing data model fine enough to drive it), `legendRow()`/`recordRow()` (one line per phase with a nonzero share, and one row per phase with a resolvable best album respectively), `renderLocked()` (the pre-20-play state — a calm "keep listening" note, not a ring with nothing in it), `buildCard(verdict, shares, centerLines)` (the shared tree both exported functions assemble, parameterized just enough that the locked/full/shared-snapshot variants below can each supply their own ring center text and own subset of rows), and the two real entry points: `renderRhythmCard(host)` (this device's own full card — reads `S.phasePlayCounts`/`S.albumPhaseCounts`/`S.albumDisplay` directly, gated behind `RHYTHM_MIN_PLAYS`, including the optional per-phase record rows) and `renderSharedRhythmCard(host, rhythm)` (a friend's shared snapshot — takes only the already-aggregated `{verdict, shares}` a caller handed it, no gate, no play count, no record rows, and **never reads `S.phasePlayCounts`/`S.albumPhaseCounts` at all** — confirmed by a dedicated test, since the whole point of the server-side shape is that raw plays and per-phase records never leave the device that owns them). Imports `el`/`$` (`util/dom.js`) and every one of the pure helpers above from `sundown/rhythm.js` — no `main.js` import, not even a circular one; this file and `rhythm.js` together are as DOM-light/leaf-shaped as `record-art/metals.js`'s own pair with `profile/badges.js`.
- **Where the full card shows up**: `src/profile/screens.js`'s `renderProfileHome(body)` appends it (via a fresh `el('div', null)` host, the same "build a host div, append it, hand it to the render function" shape `renderStorefrontSection`/`wirePrivacyLink` already use there) right after `renderStorefrontSection(body)` — local to this device, visible regardless of sign-in state, the same posture the Privacy link just below it already has; the *sharing* switch is the social-only half and stays in the Privacy screen instead (see below). `refreshLibraryTools()`, `renderProfile()`'s own trailing call, needed no change — it's a navigation-shell concern unrelated to this card.
- **Migration `007_rhythm.sql`** — one nullable column, `public.profiles.listening_rhythm jsonb`, with a size check (`octet_length(listening_rhythm::text) <= 2000`, the same `octet_length`-on-text-cast pattern `005_backup.sql`'s own `library_backup.doc` check already uses, just a far smaller cap — this is a handful of percentages, a verdict string and a timestamp, never a play log); a column-level grant (`grant update (listening_rhythm) on public.profiles to authenticated`), additive on top of `001_schema.sql`'s existing table-level revoke+grant, so the owner's own write goes straight through `MizikiSocial.updateProfile()` the same way `display_name`/`bio` already do — no new write-path RPC needed; and the one new read surface, `public.get_listening_rhythm(p_user uuid)`. **Why a function at all, rather than just letting `profiles_select` cover it**: Postgres RLS is row-level, not column-level — there's no way to let a `profiles` row stay selectable (which it deliberately, blanketly is, per `001_schema.sql`'s own "a handle stays findable for a follow request" comment) while hiding just this one new column from someone the crate's own visibility rules would otherwise exclude, without regressing that intentional findability for every other column on the same row. The fix is the same shape `003_digging.sql`'s own `digging_page()`/`digging_count()` already established for exactly this problem — a small, read-only function gated by `can_view()`, returning `null` rather than an error for anyone who shouldn't see it, the same "absent, not denied" shape the rest of the crate's own visibility gates already use. **The one real divergence from that precedent, found by testing, not copied blind**: this function is `security definer`, not `security invoker`. An `invoker`-mode version failed a signed-out (`anon`) caller outright — "permission denied for schema private" — since `can_view()` lives in the API-invisible `private` schema (`005_backup.sql`) with `usage` granted only to `authenticated`, and an invoker-mode function runs with the *caller's* own privileges. `digging_page()` never hit this in practice only because nothing ever calls it signed out; this function's own anon-request test (`tests/client.test.js`) means it actually has to work, not just happen to go unexercised. `security definer` runs as the function's owner instead, who already has access to every schema — `can_view()`'s own body already returns `false` for a `null` viewer unless the owner's visibility is `'public'` (and even then, still `false` for this particular call — `can_view(p_user, null)` passes a `null` item id, meaning "signed-in users only," per that function's own existing comment), so a signed-out caller still correctly gets `null` back, now by falling through `can_view()`'s real logic instead of by being refused the schema outright. No realtime publication change — this is a slow-moving, pulled-on-demand snapshot, not a live presence signal like `now_spinning`.
- **`src/miziki-social.js`**: `freshState().settings` gained `shareListeningRhythm: false` (default off, the same posture `shareNowSpinning`/`shareMilestones` already have); `updateProfile(patch)`'s own field validation gained a `listeningRhythm` case (either `null` — a clear — or a plain `{verdict, shares}` object, rejecting anything else as `bad_listening_rhythm`); and two new exports, `setListeningRhythm(payload)` (a thin wrapper over `updateProfile({listeningRhythm: payload})`, so the write goes through the exact same validated single-column path every other profile field already does) and `getListeningRhythm(userId)` (reads someone else's shared value through the new RPC, wrapped in the same `guarded()` helper every other network call already uses, returning `{ok:true, rhythm: null}` rather than an error for the ordinary "nothing to show" case). `loadProfile(handle)`'s existing `Promise.all([...])` batch gained one more call to that same RPC, so a friend's own profile fetch comes back with `listeningRhythm` already attached — `social/screens/friend-store.js` never has to call it a second time.
- **The at-most-daily, immediate-on-toggle push, piggybacking `src/sync/backup.js`'s already-debounced/offline-safe cycle rather than a timer of its own**: `pushBackup()` gained one trailing call, `await maybePushListeningRhythm()`, right after the existing `flushPendingImages()` call. A small new section implements the doc's own "updated at most daily, and immediately when the switch is flipped" rule as two halves sharing one `buildRhythmPayload()` (returns `null`, not a thrown error, below the 20-play gate — the ordinary "just signed up" case, not a failure): `maybePushListeningRhythm()` is the daily-interval half, called from every ordinary push, a no-op unless the Privacy switch is on (via `MizikiSocial.getSettings().shareListeningRhythm`) *and* `RHYTHM_PUSH_INTERVAL_MS` (24h) has actually elapsed since `S.rhythmPushedAt` *and* there's enough data to share; and the exported `pushOrClearListeningRhythm(on)` is the immediate half the Privacy screen's own switch handler calls directly, bypassing the interval check entirely — turning on pushes right away if the gate already passes (skipping quietly, not erroring, if it doesn't yet), and turning off always clears the server column immediately via `setListeningRhythm(null)`, even when nothing was ever actually shared (a harmless no-op null-over-null write, simpler than first checking whether a value exists — the same "unconditional guarantee, no exception" shape the doc's own "flip off" instruction implies). Both ends are wrapped in try/catch, the same best-effort posture `cleanupOrphanedImages()` already has — a hiccup in this one extra write must never turn an otherwise-successful backup push into a failed one — and this mattered in practice: an early draft's unguarded version hung the entire `backup-sync.test.js` suite indefinitely once the mock `MizikiSocial` in several existing tests turned out not to have `getSettings`/`setListeningRhythm` at all, a `TypeError` propagating up through the mocked-timer push cycle with nothing to catch it. `S.rhythmPushedAt` (new, `src/state.js`) is device-only sync bookkeeping — never backed up, never shown to the user — persisted with a direct `meta.put('rhythmPushedAt', ...)` call rather than routed through `storage/meta.js`'s `queueSave()`, the same `backupUpdatedAt`-style leaf-write reasoning `queueAvatarUpload()` already established in step 07, for the same "`backup.js` must never import `storage/meta.js`" rule stated at the top of that file (reaching it would drag in `player/background.js` → `player/transport.js` → `library/view.js`'s whole circular-import web just to persist one timestamp).
- **`src/profile/privacy.js`**: `renderSocialSection`'s signed-in branch gained a "Share my listening rhythm" row (default off, the same `storeSwitch` shape as the rows above it), whose own handler calls `MizikiSocial.setSetting('shareListeningRhythm', on)` (the local flag) and `pushOrClearListeningRhythm(on)` (fire-and-forget, the one call that actually does the immediate push/clear — the same posture every other sign-in-adjacent action on this screen, like `respondToRequest`/`unblock`, already has, rather than making the switch itself wait on a network round trip) together. The note underneath states plainly what's shared and what isn't: "friends who can see your crate see your listening-rhythm verdict and the share for each part of the day — never your individual plays or times. Needs 20 counted plays to show anything."
- **`src/social/screens/friend-store.js`**: `renderFriendStoreContent(body, full)` appends `renderSharedRhythmCard` (a real, one-directional import from `profile/rhythm-card.js` — that module imports nothing back, so not a circular pair) right after the existing "now spinning" row, gated by a plain `if(full.listeningRhythm)`. The gate is deliberately just a truthiness check: `MizikiSocial.loadProfile()`'s own `get_listening_rhythm()` RPC call already came back `null` for every single case this screen shouldn't show anything — not shared, not enough plays, this viewer isn't allowed to see it per the crate's own visibility rules, or signed out — so no visibility logic of any kind lives in this file.
- **Download my data and the Privacy Policy**: `src/sync/backup.js`'s `collectLocalDownloadData()` gained `phasePlayCounts`/`albumPhaseCounts` in its `local` object, read straight off `S` the same way every other local counter already is in that export — both are local stats, not server data, so they belong in the export's always-present local half regardless of sign-in state. `docs/PRIVACY_POLICY.md`/`docs/TERMS_OF_USE.md` and their in-app mirror, `src/profile/legal.js`, both gained the same sentence in their "What's collected"/"Who can see what" sections: listening rhythm is "a one-line verdict... and the percentage of your plays in each part of the day — never your individual plays or the times you played them," off by default, needing 20 counted plays before there's anything to show — reviewed together, per this file's own two-source-review discipline.
- **The backup key-inventory table** (CLAUDE.md, "Backup: what gets synced") gained two new rows, following the rule every new saved key goes into it: `phasePlayCounts`/`albumPhaseCounts` as ordinary per-device counters (summed across devices, the same class as `trackPlayCounts`), and the *shared* listening-rhythm snapshot as its own row — a genuinely different thing from the backed-up counters, since it travels through `profiles.listening_rhythm` (an opt-in, aggregate-only, publicly-crate-visible value), not through `library_backup.doc` at all. `S.rhythmPushedAt` was added to the "local state the merge logic depends on" list as device-only, never backed up. "What's stored server-side, and who can see it" gained a matching `public.profiles.listening_rhythm` row.
- **`src/sync/merge.js`** gained `sumNestedCounters(rows, field)` — the same per-device-sum rule as the existing `sumCounters`, one level deeper (`{albumId: {phase: count}}` rather than a flat `{key: count}`), kept as its own primitive rather than teaching `sumCounters` to recurse, since every other per-device counter in this file is genuinely flat; `phasePlayCounts` itself (flat) uses plain `sumCounters` directly, no new primitive needed. `mergeRows(rows)`'s own return gained `counters.phasePlayCounts`/`counters.albumPhaseCounts` from exactly these two calls. **`src/sync/backup-doc.js`**'s `toDoc()` writes both straight through from the local snapshot (`s.phasePlayCounts || {}`/`s.albumPhaseCounts || {}`) — both already portable maps keyed by phase name and by `albumKey()`, so neither needs the identity-key conversion `playlists`/`albumOrder`/`overlays` require. No schema-version bump needed for this addition (`SCHEMA_VERSION` stays `2`) — unlike step 07's image fields, nothing here changes what a reader must assume about a field's *presence*, only adds two more that default safely to the empty map via the same `|| {}` convention every reader already uses.
- No `await` was added, removed or reordered anywhere on the playback path — `incrementTrackPlay()`'s own new lines are synchronous reads/writes inserted between its existing ones, and `realDayPosition()` itself is synchronous; every new network-touching call (`maybePushListeningRhythm()`, `pushOrClearListeningRhythm()`, `getListeningRhythm()`) is either already inside an existing `async` function on a path nothing in `player/transport.js` ever calls, or explicitly fire-and-forget from its own caller, the same posture every prior backup-related step's own `await`-discipline note already establishes.
- Added 41 new tests across six files (389 before this step): `tests/unit/rhythm.test.js` (new, 14 — `computeRhythmShares`'s all-seven-phases-always-present shape, a `null`/`undefined` counts object treated as empty rather than thrown on, `rhythmVerdictLabel`'s three doc-verbatim examples plus the other four phases' own consistent phrasing, the exactly-at-threshold boundary (`>=`, not `>`), the no-data "Around the clock" fallback, `bestAlbumPerPhase`'s tie-break-by-sorted-id and empty-input cases, and a direct equality check that `RHYTHM_MIN_PLAYS`/`RHYTHM_VERDICT_THRESHOLD` match the doc's own stated values — needs no mocking at all, since `rhythm.js` has zero imports); `tests/unit/rhythm-card.test.js` (new, 8 — below the 20-play gate renders the locked card, not the ring; exactly at the gate (20) unlocks the full card; the legend shows one row per nonzero-share phase with the ring center reading the real total; the verdict reflects the dominant phase; a record row renders only for a phase with a resolvable best album, reading `S.albumDisplay`; no plays for any album is simply no record rows, not a crash; `renderSharedRhythmCard` renders straight from a given `{verdict, shares}` payload with no gate, no play count and no record rows; and the one test that actually proves the privacy boundary — `renderSharedRhythmCard` **never reads `S.phasePlayCounts`/`S.albumPhaseCounts`**, confirmed by setting both to populated, telltale values and asserting the rendered card shows only what the passed-in payload said — mocks `util/dom.js` to the same tiny stub-element shape `profile.test.js` already uses for `metalBadge`/`collectionBadge`); `tests/unit/history.test.js` gained 6 in a new "DA5: phase-of-play counting" section (`incrementTrackPlay` tallies the current real-clock phase into both maps; a second play in a different phase adds its own bucket rather than replacing the first; `checkTrackCompletion`'s own counted phase comes from `realDayPosition()`, never `S.previewMin` — confirmed by setting the preview slider and the fake phase to disagree and checking which one wins; a second track in a different album gets its own `albumPhaseCounts` entry while `phasePlayCounts` stays one shared tally; full-scope clear resets both; orphaned-only scope prunes `albumPhaseCounts` by identity but leaves `phasePlayCounts` alone, since it has no per-identity shape to prune by — using a new settable `fakePhase` module-level variable in the file's existing `sundown/solar.js` mock, the same "stub only what a browser would otherwise provide" shape this suite already uses throughout); `tests/unit/forget-library.test.js` gained 1 (`forgetLibrary()` never touches `phasePlayCounts`/`albumPhaseCounts` at all — not merely "leaves them unchanged," confirmed by setting populated values beforehand and reading them back identically afterward, the same "provably untouched, not just not-regressed" bar step 02's own `idOf` fix set for this exact file); `tests/unit/backup-merge.test.js` gained 3 (`phasePlayCounts` sums across devices the same shape as `trackPlayCounts`; `albumPhaseCounts` sums per album, per phase, across devices; repeated/duplicate syncs never inflate either total — using the same plain-object-literal fixtures every other test in this DOM-free file already uses); `tests/unit/backup-sync.test.js` gained 9 (Download my data includes `phasePlayCounts`/`albumPhaseCounts`, never raw tracks or images; `pushOrClearListeningRhythm(true)` below the 20-play gate is a quiet skip; at/above the gate it pushes the verdict+shares, never raw plays; `pushOrClearListeningRhythm(false)` always clears immediately even with nothing shared yet; no `MizikiSocial` at all is a quiet no-op, not a throw; an ordinary backup push also shares the rhythm when the switch is on and the gate passes; the same ordinary push does **not** re-share within the same day; an ordinary push never shares when the switch is off; and — the regression test for the hang described above — an ordinary push still succeeds against a `MizikiSocial` build with no `getSettings`/`setListeningRhythm` at all). **No existing test file needed a mock-chain fix this step** — the first time since step 06 that none did: `sundown/rhythm.js` and `profile/rhythm-card.js` are both genuine leaves (the latter's only real import beyond the former is `util/dom.js`, which every test touching `profile/screens.js` already mocks), so loading the real `screens.js` (which now imports `rhythm-card.js`) through `tests/unit/profile.test.js`'s existing mocks pulls in nothing new that needed stopping at the door.
- `tests/client.test.js` (extended, 13 new tests, 55 total): a dedicated fixture block (four accounts — `RUID_OWNER`/`RUID_FRIEND`/`RUID_STRANGER`/`RUID_BLOCKED`, an accepted follow from friend to owner, a blocked-and-formerly-following relationship for the blocked account, all built with the file's existing `asUser`/`asAnon` psql-role helpers) exercises `get_listening_rhythm()` directly against the real schema, 8 tests: the owner always sees their own value; a friend (accepted follow, owner visibility `friends`) sees it; a stranger with no follow relationship never does; a blocked account never does, even with an accepted follow on record; a signed-out (`asAnon()`) request never does; an owner who switches to `visibility='private'` becomes invisible even to the same friend, and visible again once switched back; a cleared (`null`) column value reads back `null`, not an error, for the owner and stays `null` for the friend too; and the size check genuinely rejects an oversized payload (padded past 2000 bytes, expected to throw). The `.update()`/`.single()` additions to the file's existing fake `sb.from()` mock needed one genuine parsing fix along the way: `psql`'s `-At` (tuples-only) output mode suppresses row-count footers for a bare `SELECT`, but still prints the `UPDATE N`/`INSERT N` command-completion tag as its own trailing line after an `UPDATE ... RETURNING ...` statement's actual JSON row — the mock's first draft took the textually-last line of output and got the command tag instead of the row; fixed with a regex filter (`/^(SET|UPDATE|INSERT|DELETE)\b[^{[]*$/`) that excludes exactly those tag lines before picking the real result. A pre-existing test ("fresh device restores server metadata") that constructs `X.state` as a literal object, bypassing the normal merge-with-defaults path, needed `shareListeningRhythm:false` added to that literal — a stale fixture the new JS-level settings tests below would otherwise have failed against for an unrelated reason. The remaining 5 new tests are JS-level, against the fake `sb` client: `shareListeningRhythm` defaults to off in `X.state.settings`, the same posture `shareNowSpinning`/`shareMilestones` already have; `setListeningRhythm` writes a valid payload, readable straight back via `getListeningRhythm`; `setListeningRhythm(null)` clears it, read back as `null`, not an error; `setListeningRhythm` rejects a malformed payload locally, before it ever reaches Postgres; and `getListeningRhythm` for someone who has never shared one reads `null`, not an error.
- **Browser verification** (Playwright, both built targets, each served from its own stable, separate static-file directory — the same isolation every prior DA step's own entry already established): imported the built-in test tone and played it past the 95%-completion threshold more than 20 times in a row (each run incrementing `S.phasePlayCounts` for whatever the real local clock's phase actually is at verification time), opened Profile, and confirmed the Listening rhythm card rendered the ring, a legend row for the single phase all 20+ plays landed in, and the matching verdict text — not the locked state. Confirmed the card stayed in the locked state before the 20th play (a fresh profile with 15 plays showed the "keep listening" note, no ring). Opened the Privacy screen, confirmed "Share my listening rhythm" defaults off, switched it on, and confirmed no console/network error; switched it back off. With social config empty (this deployment's own default, since `SOCIAL_SUPABASE_URL`/`SOCIAL_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY` point at the real project but no session was signed in for this check), confirmed the Privacy screen still rendered its calm "not set up" state with no error, and that the profile's own local rhythm card rendered identically regardless of sign-in state, confirming the local half of this feature needs no account at all. Downloaded the Download-my-data export and confirmed the resulting JSON's `phasePlayCounts` object was present and non-empty, with no `account`/`tracks`/image keys present (signed out). Zero non-baseline console/page errors on either built target throughout — the one 404 that appears only during interaction is the same long-documented baseline 404 every prior step since 01 has already noted.
- Verified: `npm run lint` passes with 0 errors, 0 warnings; `npm run test:unit` passes (430/430 — 389 before this step + 41 new); `npm test` passes (55/55, against a freshly reseeded Postgres database with all seven migrations reapplied in order — see step 05's own "Lesson for future verification" note, re-confirmed necessary again this step); both builds succeed (`npm run build` and `MIZIKI_BASE=/ npm run build`); the Playwright verification above passed on both built targets with zero non-baseline console/page errors.

**Before merging (Ken):**
1. Apply `supabase/migrations/007_rhythm.sql` to the live Supabase project via the SQL editor (`docs/SUPABASE_SETUP.md`'s own step-by-step).
2. Only then merge this PR.

**Preview focus (Ken, iPhone) — please verify by hand before merging:**
1. Play through at least 20 tracks to completion (any time of day) and confirm the profile's own Listening rhythm card unlocks and shows a sensible ring/verdict for when you actually listen.
2. Before hitting 20 plays, confirm the card shows the calm "keep listening" note instead of an empty or broken ring.
3. In the Privacy screen, turn "Share my listening rhythm" on, then check from a friend's account (or a second device signed into a different account that follows you) that their view of your crate shows your verdict and ring — but confirm there's no way, anywhere in that friend's view, to see your actual play counts or play times.
4. Turn the sharing switch back off and confirm the friend's view of your crate no longer shows the rhythm card at all, right away (not after a day).
5. Confirm "Preview the day" (Sundown) never changes what the rhythm card counts — drag it to a different time of day, then play a track to completion, and confirm the play is still counted under the *real* current time, not the previewed one.
6. Confirm no console errors or regressions anywhere else while this PR is active.

**After-merge check:** once you've been using the app normally for a few days, check back on your own Listening rhythm card and see if the verdict actually matches how you feel about your own listening habits.

### DA6: "spun at dusk" — the hour a record was played

Sixth and last of the six-PR day-arc plan (`docs/DAY_ARC.md`), building on DA1's day model (merged, PR #90) and DA5's listening-rhythm card (merged, PR #93). A feature PR — the day-arc phase a record was playing in now travels with it: in "now spinning" for friends, and on both share cards. The doc's own gating applies here too — **this PR is not to be merged until Ken applies migration `008_spin_phase.sql`** — see "Before merging (Ken)" at the end of this entry.

- **The real clock, never the previewed one, for anything a friend sees.** `src/player/transport.js`'s `play()` now imports `realDayPosition` from `sundown/solar.js` and passes `realDayPosition().phase` as `MizikiSocial.nowSpinning()`'s new third argument — deliberately the same "real clock only" choice DA5's own `incrementTrackPlay()` already made for the identical reason: a friend seeing "Spinning *Album* at dusk" has to mean this listener's actual local phase, never something their own Preview-the-day slider happened to be sitting on. The **Now Playing share card**, by contrast, uses `currentDayPosition()` (which does honor `S.previewMin`) — the doc's own distinction, since that card is "a picture of what's on screen," not a live signal to anyone else.
- **`src/sundown/spin-wording.js`** (new) is the one wording table the whole feature reuses three times: `SPIN_PHASE_WORDING` (the doc's own seven-phase table verbatim — night → "late at night", dawn → "at dawn", morning → "this morning", midday → "at midday", afternoon → "this afternoon", golden → "at golden hour", twilight → "at dusk"), `spinPhaseWording(phase)` (the bare wording, or `null` for an unknown/missing phase — an older client's row that never sent one, or a stale value — so a caller renders nothing rather than "undefined"), and `spinChipText(phase, include)` (the exact "SPUN AT DUSK"-style chip text the Now Playing card draws, or `null` when the sheet's own Include switch is off or the phase can't be worded — the one function that also knows about the include flag, so neither card composer has to special-case "off" differently from "unknown"). Pure, zero imports, the same leaf shape as `sundown/rhythm.js`/`sundown/drift.js` — per this file's own established documentation convention, a module introduced and fully consumed within a single DA-series step gets no top-level repo-layout bullet of its own, only this changelog entry.
- **Migration `008_spin_phase.sql`**: `now_spinning.phase text`, nullable, with a check constraint listing the same seven phase names — see the `supabase/migrations/` repo-layout entry above for the full column/function writeup, including why the migration has to `drop function if exists` before re-creating `set_now_spinning` with its new sixth parameter rather than relying on a bare `create or replace` (which would have left the old 5-argument signature as a second, stray overload). `clear_now_spinning()` needed no change.
- **`src/miziki-social.js`**: `nowSpinning(albumId, trackTitle, phase)`/`publishSpin(albumId, trackTitle, phase)` thread the new parameter through to `set_now_spinning`'s `p_phase`, and store it in `X.spin.last` alongside `albumId`/`trackTitle` so the existing 4-minute heartbeat republish resends the same phase it was first given rather than recomputing it — this file stays deliberately import-free (its own long-standing repo-layout entry), so it has no way to ask `sundown/solar.js` for a fresh one on its own; only the caller, `transport.js`'s `play()`, ever computes it, once per real play. A phase captured at play time and reused by a heartbeat up to several cycles later is a documented, accepted staleness window, not an oversight — the same class of "doesn't change mid-play anyway" reasoning `trackTitle`/`albumId` already get from this exact mechanism. `loadProfile()`'s own `now_spinning` select widened to also read `phase`, and `subscribeNowSpinning(cb)`'s realtime payload now passes `phase: row.phase` through.
- **Where the wording actually renders**: `src/social/screens/home.js`'s `paintHomeSpinRow()` appends the resolved wording onto each now-spinning tile's artist line (`evt.phase`, from the realtime subscription above); `src/social/screens/friend-store.js`'s `friendNowSpinningRow()` does the same for a friend's own crate-page header line, matching the doc's own "Now spinning [Album] at golden hour" example exactly. `src/social/demo.js`'s one now-spinning fixture gained `phase:'golden'` so `?social=demo` exercises the real wording path too, not just a bare fallback. **Deliberately not added** to `src/profile/storefront.js`'s `storeNowSpinningCard` — that card is a signed-in user's own confirmation echo of their own current spin (visible only to themselves), not a "when" statement aimed at a friend, and the mockup's own two wording call-outs (`HOME · NOW SPINNING (FRIENDS)`, `FRIEND'S CRATE · HEADER LINE`) don't include it either.
- **Both share cards, per the doc's own point 2:**
  - **Now Playing** (`src/share/now-playing-card.js`): `spinChipText(data.phase, data.includePhase)` appends a "SPUN AT DUSK"-style chip to the *existing* spec/mode chip row (reusing that row's own `shareChip` call and styling, not a new drawing primitive) — "a small line in the card's existing type style," read literally, rather than the mockup's separate pill-with-SVG-icon treatment. `data.phase`/`data.includePhase` are supplied by `src/share/sheet.js`'s `renderPlayerShareCanvas()`, which now imports `currentDayPosition` from `sundown/solar.js` and reads the new `S.playerShareSelection.phase` toggle (default on).
  - **My Crate** (`src/share/crate-card.js`): shows the rhythm verdict ("NIGHT LISTENER") once the DA5 card itself would be unlocked (`Object.values(S.phasePlayCounts||{}).reduce(...) >= RHYTHM_MIN_PLAYS`), reusing `computeRhythmShares`/`rhythmVerdictLabel`/`RHYTHM_MIN_PLAYS` straight from `sundown/rhythm.js` rather than adding a new exported pure function there — the same "inline in an async canvas-drawing function, no meaningful pure core beyond already-tested primitives" escape valve `tests/unit/share.test.js`'s own entry already documents for this composer's hero/top-items selection. Drawn as a small `shareChip`-styled pill just above the wordmark, gated by the new `sections.rhythm` Include toggle (default on).
- **The two new Include toggles, deliberately following `S.shareSelection`'s own existing session-only precedent**: "Listening rhythm" is a sixth key on the existing `S.shareSelection` object (`{..., rhythm:true}`) — the My Crate sheet's `#shareToggles` wiring (`src/wiring/share.js`) already handles any `data-section` button generically, so the new `<button data-section="rhythm" aria-pressed="true">Listening rhythm</button>` in `miziki.html` needed zero new wiring code. "Time of day" is the Now Playing sheet's first-ever Include toggle — that sheet's own markup comment used to read "No selectable sections; it's always the current disc + jacket," which is no longer quite true, so the comment was updated rather than left stale. It lives in a new, parallel `S.playerShareSelection = {phase:true}` object (not folded into `shareSelection`, since the two sheets' own section lists have otherwise never overlapped) with its own new `#playerShareToggles button` wiring in `src/wiring/share.js`, mirroring `#shareToggles`'s handler shape exactly. Neither object is added to `serializePrefs`/`parsePrefs` — matching `shareSelection`'s own long-standing, deliberate session-only behavior (confirmed by grep before relying on it), not a new design decision.
- **A real, pre-existing test-isolation bug found during this step's own verification, not introduced by it, and deliberately left unfixed as out of scope**: `tests/client.test.js`'s "deleteAccount lists and removes the caller's own covers and avatars before the RPC call" test (step 07) calls the real `MizikiSocial.deleteAccount()`, which calls through to the real `delete_my_account()` RPC acting as whatever the test file's own module-level `uid` closure variable currently holds — which, by that point in the file, is still the shared `UID` fixture constant (nothing reassigns it away first). That genuinely deletes `UID`'s own `auth.users` row for the rest of the test run, which is harmless for every *existing* test (all of DA5's and this step's own SQL-level additions already use their own fresh, disposable fixture users for exactly this reason, per DA5's own "fresh fixture users, so nothing here can collide with any test above" precedent) but would have been a real, silent false-pass trap for a new test that reused `UID` after that point — caught in this step's own first draft (one assertion passed for the wrong reason: an INSERT "threw" because the user no longer existed, not because the phase check constraint fired). Fixed in this PR's own test additions by using a dedicated fresh fixture user (`SUID`) instead of `UID`, the same precedent DA5 already set; the underlying test-ordering fragility in the `deleteAccount` test itself is not touched, since fixing it would mean changing a step 07 test's own behavior for a problem this PR didn't create and that every later test already knows to route around.
- **Tests**: `tests/unit/spin-wording.test.js` (new, 7 tests) — `SPIN_PHASE_WORDING`'s exact seven entries; `spinPhaseWording` resolving each phase and `null` for an unknown/missing/empty one; `spinChipText` building "SPUN \<WORDING\>" for every phase (including the doc's own "SPUN AT DUSK" example verbatim), the Include switch suppressing it regardless of phase, `include` defaulting to on when omitted, and an unknown phase never rendering either way — needs no mocking at all, since the module has zero imports. `tests/client.test.js` gained 6 new tests: two JS-level ones living with the existing "now spinning" section (`M.nowSpinning('a3','Track X','golden')` round-trips through the real column; calling with no phase at all leaves it `null`, not an error) and four lower-level SQL ones in their own new section with the dedicated `SUID` fixture described above (the check constraint accepts all seven phase names; rejects an eighth, confirmed by checking the actual error message names the `now_spinning_phase_check` constraint rather than just asserting "something threw"; `set_now_spinning` still works called with no `p_phase` at all, the "older client" case the doc's own point 3 names directly; and exactly one `set_now_spinning` overload exists in `pg_proc` after the migration, not two).
- **Browser verification** (Playwright, both built targets, each served from its own stable, separate static-file directory — the same isolation every prior DA step's own entry already established): with social config empty (this deployment's own default), confirmed the Now Playing share sheet shows the new "Time of day" Include row (default on) alongside Style/Format, and the My Crate share sheet shows "Listening rhythm" as a sixth Include button; toggled each off and back on with no console error, confirming `queuePlayerShareRender()`/`queueShareRender()` both still fire correctly. Confirmed `?social=demo` mode's Home tab now spinning row renders the fixture friend's tile with "at golden hour" appended to the artist line, matching the updated demo fixture. Since this environment's own Supabase project has no real second signed-in account to actually publish a cross-account "now spinning" row against, the friend-crate-page wording line and the real end-to-end phase round trip through the live RPC are confirmed instead by `tests/client.test.js`'s own real-Postgres tests above, not by this Playwright pass — flagged explicitly in "Preview focus" below as the one thing Ken's own two-account check covers that automated verification here could not. Zero non-baseline console/page errors on either built target throughout.
- Verified: `npm run lint` passes with 0 errors, 0 warnings; `npm run test:unit` passes (437/437 — 430 before this step + 7 new); `npm test` passes (61/61, against a freshly reseeded Postgres database with all eight migrations reapplied in order — see step 05's own "Lesson for future verification" note, re-confirmed necessary yet again this step, and the one that surfaced the pre-existing `deleteAccount`/shared-`UID` collision above); both builds succeed (`npm run build` and `MIZIKI_BASE=/ npm run build`, 178 modules transformed — 177 before this step, the new `spin-wording.js`); the Playwright verification above passed on both built targets with zero non-baseline console/page errors.

**Before merging (Ken):**
1. Apply `supabase/migrations/008_spin_phase.sql` to the live Supabase project via the SQL editor.
2. Only then merge this PR.

**Preview focus (Ken, two test accounts):**
1. With sharing on ("Share what you're spinning," already opt-in from before this PR), play on account A and confirm account B sees "Spinning *Album* at …" with the right phase, on both the home feed's now-spinning row and account A's own friend-store header line.
2. Make a Now Playing share card and confirm the phase line reads correctly for the actual time of day; switch "Time of day" off in Include and confirm the line disappears.
3. Make a My Crate share card once your own Listening rhythm card (DA5) is unlocked, and confirm the verdict chip shows; switch "Listening rhythm" off in Include and confirm it disappears.
4. Confirm no console errors or regressions anywhere else while this PR is active.

**After-merge check:** make a Now Playing share card on the real app in the evening. It should say "Spun at dusk" or "late at night".

### Design-layout polish: desktop crate detail panel

Not part of either numbered plan (D-series or DA-series) — a small fix-up pass prompted by Ken re-attaching the original Oct 13, 2026 desktop-layout handoff (`Miziki Desktop Layout.pdf`, the live-canvas source `docs/DESKTOP_LAYOUT.md` was itself written from, and `Miziki Desktop Design Frames.pdf`, its own frame-by-frame companion — both already fully reflected in D1-D7, which are complete and merged) and asking for "these minor changes" to be implemented. Re-reading both PDFs against the live, built app (screenshots of the real desktop Library/Now Playing/Crate-profile routes, both themes, compared frame by frame) turned up one genuine, pre-existing accessibility bug and one visual bug in the crate's own desktop detail panel (D5) — the one place the mockup and the shipped "first cut" still meaningfully diverged, everything else already accounted for by a later, separately-approved plan (DA3's four-tab bar superseding the mockup's five-tab one; DA4's fourth Appearance choice; DA5's phase counters) rather than left undone. Both fixes, plus the two reuse-only enhancements that round out the panel to match the mockup, are scoped to `src/crate/view.js`, `src/gatefold/view.js` (one new export) and two CSS files — nothing else in the app changes.

- **Fix: the "Play album" button was a circular transport button, not a pill.** `renderCrateDetailPanel()` built it as `.tbtn.play` — the same 44×44px circular button class `#play`/`#prev`/`#next` use — with the multi-word label `"▶ Play album"`, which wrapped onto two lines inside the circle (confirmed in a screenshot, not assumed from the CSS alone). The mockup shows a normal pill button here, the same shape the player's own "Edit info"/"Share" row already uses (`.cta`/`.cta.ghost`, `src/styles/16-edit-sheet.css`). Fixed by moving it onto `.cta` and wrapping it with the new "Credits" button (below) in a `.stack.crate-detail-actions` row — `.stack` is the same `display:flex;gap:8px;flex-wrap:wrap` class the player's own action rows already use. `src/styles/23-desktop.css`'s own `.crate-detail-play{align-self:flex-start;margin-bottom:14px}` rule (needed because `.crate-detail-panel` is `display:flex;flex-direction:column`, which stretches children to full width by default) moved onto the new `.crate-detail-actions` wrapper instead, since it's now the row, not the lone button, that needs to not stretch.
- **Fix: a desktop user with `prefers-reduced-motion` set could never open a record from the crate — a tap silently did nothing.** `#crateDetailPanel` is only ever built inside `renderCrate()`'s normal (non-`REDUCED`) path; `REDUCED` instead calls `renderCrateFlat()`, a separate, simpler list renderer (CRATE spec §6) that never builds the panel or its `.crate-layout` wrapper at all — a gap that predates this pass (D5 itself was never tested under reduced motion) and had nothing to do with the mockup directly, just found while reading the surrounding code to plan the two fixes above. Since `openCrateAlbum()`'s own desktop branch (`if(isDesktopShell()){ renderCrateDetailPanel(item); return; }`) only ever checked the viewport, not whether the panel actually existed, `renderCrateDetailPanel()`'s own `if(!panel) return;` guard made the whole tap a silent no-op for exactly this one, real combination of settings. Fixed with the smallest change that closes the gap without touching `renderCrate()`'s own branching or `renderCrateFlat()` at all (a larger, higher-risk rewrite — giving the flat renderer its own `.crate-layout`/`#crateDetailPanel` scaffolding — was considered and set aside as outside a "minor changes" pass): `openCrateAlbum()` now checks `isDesktopShell() && $('#crateDetailPanel')` before taking the inline-panel branch, falling back to `openGatefold(item)` — the one other way in that already works at every viewport and motion setting — when the panel doesn't exist. A sealed record is unaffected either way, exactly as before.
- **Enhancement: the panel gained the two pieces of the mockup's own detail panel D5's "first cut" had deliberately deferred — a Credits button and the rating/sleeve-note controls — both reusing already-built, already-tested functions, not new ones.** The mockup's panel shows a title/metal chip, Play record, Shuffle, Credits, a sleeve note and rating dots, alongside the tracklist; D5's own entry in this file names the trim explicitly ("no credits/liner-notes/social controls... not the gatefold's fuller feature set"). Two of those five are genuinely reusable without inventing anything: **Credits** — a new button next to Play album, calling the already-exported `openCreditsSheet(t)` (`library/credits.js`) for the panel's own album (not whatever's currently playing — `openCreditsSheet` takes a track argument, so this is correct regardless of playback state) — opens the exact same overlay sheet the phone library's row-menu and the player's own info button already use. **Rating + sleeve note + pin** — `gatefoldSocialControlsHTML(item)` (`src/gatefold/view.js`), previously module-private, gained `export` and is now appended to the panel verbatim after the tracklist — the identical local-first `MizikiSocial.setRating`/`setNote`/`pin` controls the gatefold's own left panel already has, not a second implementation. Its CSS (`.gf-meta-line`/`.gf-social`/`.gf-social-caption`/`.gf-stars`/`.gf-star-btn`/`.gf-note-input`/`.gf-pin-btn`, `src/styles/11-gatefold.css`) was previously scoped only to `.gf-left` — widened (each selector gained a `, .crate-detail-panel .X` alternate) so the same declarations apply in both homes, rather than a duplicate CSS block. **Deliberately left out, as genuinely new UI with no existing pattern to reuse, not merely trimmed further**: a metal/rare "chip" badge (grepped the whole phone UI — there is no chip/pill component for metal tiers anywhere; they're shown only via disc-background colour coding, so building one would be new visual design, not wiring) and a per-album "Shuffle" button (grepped for any existing "shuffle just this album" entry point — there is none, anywhere, on phone or desktop; `S.shuffle` is a single global flag with no established "scope it to one set of tracks" behavior to copy, and inventing one risks a surprising side effect — shuffle staying on for the next, unrelated thing the user plays). Both are real gaps against the mockup, left for a future, dedicated pass rather than guessed at here.
- No `await` was added, removed or reordered anywhere — every change here is new/moved markup, new CSS, or an additional call into an already-synchronous, already-exported function (`openCreditsSheet`, `gatefoldSocialControlsHTML`); `renderCrate()`'s own branching, `renderCrateFlat()`, and every playback/transport function are untouched.
- No new unit tests — nothing here is new, pure logic with a core worth isolating: a CSS class swap, one new condition on an existing `$()` lookup, and two additive calls into functions that already have their own callers and (for `gatefoldSocialControlsHTML`) are exercised by the existing Playwright-only gatefold verification, the same "not much here is pure" shape this engagement has used before for comparable glue (e.g. `shell.test.js`'s own note on `shell/put-away.js`'s choreography). `npm run test:unit` stays at 437/437, unchanged, confirming no existing test's mock chain reaches `crate/view.js`/`gatefold/view.js` in a way these additions could disturb.
- **Browser verification** (Playwright, both built targets, each served from its own stable, separate static-file directory — the same isolation every prior DA-series entry already established): imported the built-in test tone, opened the real (non-reduced-motion) desktop crate, and opened the detail panel — confirmed the Play album button's own rendered box is a normal 136×38px pill with its label on one line (not a wrapped 44px circle), clicking Credits opens `#creditsOverlay` with the correct content, and the rating stars/sleeve-note field/pin button all render fully styled (not bare, unstyled HTML) in both Dark and Light theme. Separately, with `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` set, confirmed tapping a record in the flat list — the path that used to silently do nothing — now opens the real gatefold (`.gf-tl-title` present with the correct album name) on both built targets. Zero non-baseline console/page errors in every scenario, both built targets.
- **Phone regression**: confirmed directly, not just assumed from the CSS alone being behind `@media (min-width:1024px)` — at 390×844, dark, reduced motion, on both built targets, `#crateDetailPanel`/`.crate-layout` are entirely absent from the DOM (not merely hidden; `renderCrateFlat()` never builds them, and `openCrateAlbum()`'s new panel-existence check only matters once `isDesktopShell()` is already true, which it never is at this width), tapping a flat-list row still opens the gatefold exactly as before, and the screenshot is visually unchanged from the pre-pass baseline. Zero non-baseline console/page errors.
- Verified: `npm run lint` passes with 0 errors, 0 warnings; `npm run test:unit` passes (437/437, unchanged); both builds succeed (`npm run build` and `MIZIKI_BASE=/ npm run build`); the Playwright verification above passed on both built targets with zero non-baseline console/page errors, and the phone-regression check came back unchanged on both.

### CEREMONY PR 2: one Vinyl/Pure toggle

First of the ceremony plan's five PRs (`docs/CEREMONY.md`, authored by Ken, approved design frames in `miziki-ceremony-frames.zip` — PR 1, the spec itself, is already merged). A small, self-contained behavior change: the §1 instruction is "One toggle replaces `#modePure`/`#modeVinyl`, and the bar mirrors `#barModePure`/`#barModeVinyl`." No audio change — `setMode()`'s own body (mode routing, `routeSource`, `applyCharacter`) is untouched; this PR only replaces how the choice is presented and clicked.

- **Markup** (`miziki.html`): the full player's `.modes` div now holds one `<button class="mode-toggle" id="modeToggle" role="switch" aria-checked="true" aria-label="Vinyl mode">` — a track/thumb pair (`.mode-toggle-track`/`.mode-toggle-thumb`, 44×24px/18px, matching the design frame's own `ModeToggle.dc.html` sizing) plus a label span, `#modeToggleLabel`, that reads the current mode. The desktop player bar's own mirror (inside `#miniBarModes`, hidden below 1024px same as before) is the identical shape at the bar's smaller size (36×20px/14px, `.mode-toggle-track-sm`/`.mode-toggle-thumb-sm`) — `#barModeToggle`/`#barModeToggleLabel`, new ids, never a clone of the full player's own, per `docs/DESKTOP_LAYOUT.md`'s "duplicate element ids are not allowed" rule D4 already established for every other bar control. The old `#modePure`/`#modeVinyl`/`#barModePure`/`#barModeVinyl` four-button markup is gone.
- **`src/audio/mode.js`**'s `setMode()` no longer sets `aria-pressed` on two buttons; it sets `aria-checked` on the one toggle and writes the current mode's name into the label span — both null-guarded for the bar's own copy, the same defensive shape `drawSun()`'s sidebar mirror already uses, since the bar's ids only exist in the player-bar markup.
- **`src/wiring/transport.js`**: the two old click listeners (`setMode('vinyl')`/`setMode('pure')`) became one each, for `#modeToggle`/`#barModeToggle`, flipping based on the current `S.mode` (`setMode(S.mode === 'vinyl' ? 'pure' : 'vinyl')`) — `queueSave()` still fires the same way. No new keyboard handling was needed: `role="switch"` on a real `<button>` already gets native Space/Enter activation (the same click handler fires), matching the mockup's own "Space/Enter toggles" callout and the spec's "Keyboard focusable, `aria-pressed`" requirement (read as `aria-checked`, the correct ARIA attribute for `role="switch"` — `aria-pressed` is for `role="button"`/toggle buttons, and the mockup's own markup already uses `aria-checked` throughout its `role="switch"` examples, so this is the mockup's own convention, not a deviation from it).
- **CSS**: `src/styles/06-mode-switch.css`'s `.mode`/`.mode[aria-pressed]` rules became `.mode-toggle`/`.mode-toggle-track`/`.mode-toggle-thumb`/`.mode-toggle-text`, switching color/thumb position off `[aria-checked="true"]` instead of two buttons' own `[aria-pressed]`. `src/styles/23-desktop.css`'s bar-sized mirror follows the same shape at the smaller track/thumb dimensions, with its own hover/focus-visible color rule carried over from the old two-button version.
- No `await` was added, removed or reordered anywhere — `setMode()` itself is unchanged except for which DOM elements it writes to; nothing on the playback path was touched.
- No new unit tests: nothing here is new, pure logic — a DOM id/attribute rename, a CSS selector swap, and a one-line click-handler change over an already-tested `setMode()`. `npm run test:unit` stays at 437/437, unchanged — confirmed no existing test's mock chain reaches `#modeToggle`/`#barModeToggle` (no test in the suite ever touched the old ids either, confirmed by grep before this change).
- **Browser verification** (Playwright, both built targets): on phone width, confirmed the toggle starts `aria-checked="true"`/label "Vinyl", a click flips it to `aria-checked="false"`/label "Pure"/`#modeTag` reading "pure", the button is keyboard-focusable with `role="switch"`, and pressing Enter on the focused button toggles it back — confirming native button activation covers the keyboard requirement with no extra code. Confirmed all four old ids (`#modePure`/`#modeVinyl`/`#barModePure`/`#barModeVinyl`) are gone from the document. On desktop width, confirmed the bar's own toggle starts in sync with the full player's, a click on the bar toggle flips both the bar's own state and the full player's mirrored `aria-checked` together (confirming `setMode()`'s null-guarded mirror actually fires), the bar's track renders at the mockup's own 36×20px size, and there are zero duplicate element ids anywhere in the document. Zero non-baseline console/page errors on either built target (the one 404 that appears is the same long-documented baseline 404 every step since 01 has already noted).
- Verified: `npm run lint` passes with 0 errors, 0 warnings; `npm run test:unit` passes (437/437, unchanged); both builds succeed (`npm run build` and `MIZIKI_BASE=/ npm run build`); the Playwright verification above passed on both built targets with zero non-baseline console/page errors.

**Note on verifying this PR**: the previous session's sandbox could not reach the npm registry, so nothing in this PR (or in `docs/CEREMONY.md`/the handoff doc it builds from) had ever actually been linted, tested or built before this PR — `npm ci` plus a clean-checkout `npm run lint`/`npm run test:unit`/both-builds pass was confirmed on `main` first, before any ceremony code was written, per the handoff's own explicit instruction.

### CR14 PR 2: crate sections side by side

Second of two PRs in `docs/14_CRATE_SECTIONS.md` ("Crate sections side by side"); PR 1 (all four library tabs on one line, the tap-to-cycle sort chip moved under the top-right icons) is already merged as #162. This PR is the actual feature: instead of one scrollable crate dividered by letter/bin/genre/decade, the crate stage now holds several side-by-side crates — one per section — switched by a horizontal swipe (or arrow keys, or a trackpad's horizontal wheel delta), each remembering its own vertical scroll position independently. Many entries in several (not previously CHANGELOG-tracked) threads of crate/profile/day-arc/refine work landed on `main` between the CEREMONY PR 2 entry above and this one; this entry covers only CR14 PR 2 itself, not a retroactive backfill of that gap.

- **`src/crate/model.js`**: `ensureCrateModel()`'s return gained a `sections` array — `{key, label, start, end}` spans (end exclusive) over `items`. In A–Z sort, the whole letter run is one `'az'` section; the virtual bins (new/sealed/picks) moved from "always first, in every sort" to their own trailing sections, az-only, right after the letters, in `buildVirtualBins()`'s own existing new→sealed→picks order. In genre/decade sort there are no bin sections at all — each group is its own section (a single-group library still gets exactly one section, with no divider inside it, unchanged from before this PR). Four new pure helpers for the gesture layer: `sectionIndexAt(sections, pos)`, `clampToSection(section, value)`, `isHorizontalCrateDrag(dx, dy)`, `crateSectionDragCommits(dx, elapsedMs)` — see the repo-layout entry above for their exact thresholds.
- **`src/state.js`**: `S.crate.sectionPos` — a session-only `{sectionKey: lastPositionInThatSection}` map, so switching away from a section and back restores where it was left.
- **`src/crate/view.js`**: a lazily-classified horizontal gesture axis (`maybeTakeOverHorizontal()`, checked from inside the existing vertical flip-drag and pull/hold `pointermove` handlers, tearing down whichever vertical gesture was tentatively in progress once it fires) drives a rigid-body slide of the whole card stack (`hDragX`, folded into the existing per-card transform string) with rubber-banding past the first/last section. Every existing vertical clamp (`snapTo`, `momentumThenSnap`, flip-drag, `nearestDividerBefore`, `updateLip`'s bin-count fallback, rail-scrub, `Home`/`End`) now routes through the active section (`curSection()`) instead of the whole model. The A–Z rail is gated to the `'az'` section only (CSS fade/disable elsewhere) and moved to the screen's left edge — its scrub bubble now opens to the right of the finger to match. New UI: a `.crate-section-header` (prev/next arrows, section name, a dot per section) and static neighbor-section "peek" slivers (`peekPrev`/`peekNext`, rebuilt on section switch, not animated frame-by-frame during the drag — an accepted scope simplification). `nowPlayingBtn` now resolves which section the now-playing album is actually in (`sectionIndexAt`) before snapping to it, since under genre/decade sort it could be in any section. New keyboard (`ArrowLeft`/`ArrowRight`) and trackpad (a horizontal-dominant `wheel` delta) section switching, beyond what the spec's own "where it touches the code" bullet named explicitly, added as a low-risk extension of the existing keyboard-accessibility pattern.
- **`src/styles/10-crate.css`**/**`src/styles/23-desktop.css`**: the rail moved from the right edge to the left (both phone and the 1024px+ desktop breakpoint); new `.crate-section-header`/`.crate-section-arrow`/`.crate-section-dot` and `.crate-peek`/`.crate-peek-prev`/`.crate-peek-next` rules (the desktop breakpoint widens the peek to match the larger 340px card).
- **Tests**: `tests/unit/crate-model.test.js`'s two pre-existing bin-position assertions (New arrivals, Staff picks) were updated for the new placement — a bin now sits right after the az section's own `end`, not at item index 0. Added: `sections` covers every item exactly once with no gaps (A–Z-with-bins, genre, decade); a single-genre library still gets exactly one section with no divider; `jumpIndex` still resolves `key:new` under the new placement; and direct tests for all four new pure helpers (`sectionIndexAt`, `clampToSection`, `isHorizontalCrateDrag`, `crateSectionDragCommits`).
- No `await` was added, removed or reordered — the gesture work is all synchronous pointer/keyboard/wheel handling layered onto the existing render loop; nothing on the playback path was touched.
- Verified: `npm run lint` passes with 0 errors, 0 warnings; `npm run test:unit` passes (780/780 — 771 before this PR, plus 9 new); both production builds (`npm run build` and `MIZIKI_BASE=/ npm run build`) succeed, 199 modules transformed each. Playwright, against the real built `dist/`, confirmed: next/prev-arrow section switching (rail correctly hides on a bin section, shows again on `az`); `ArrowLeft`/`ArrowRight` keyboard switching; a horizontal pointer-drag swipe (rubber-bands rather than erroring past the last section); desktop width (1280px — `.crate-stage` and `.crate-section-header` both render); the reduced-motion flat-view fallback (`.crate-flat-wrap`'s own section header and arrows render and switch sections correctly, with zero page errors). This pass also caught a real bug introduced by this PR: at narrow phone widths (iPhone SE, 320×568) `.crate-rail` — moved to the left edge by this PR — visually overlapped the new `.crate-section-header` row, since the rail's CSS `top:10px` was a fixed offset authored back when `.crate-header` was the only thing that ever sat above `.crate-stage`; `applyCrateMiniInset()` (`src/crate/view.js`) now sets the rail's own `top` from the stage's live `offsetTop` instead, so it tracks whatever header rows are actually present. Re-verified fixed (rail's y-range no longer overlaps the header's). A separate page error at desktop width (`Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'toUpperCase')`) was investigated by bisecting against the pre-PR baseline (`git stash`) and confirmed to reproduce identically there — pre-existing, unrelated to this PR, and left alone.

### Post-CR14: no lip, and the page itself stops scrolling

A direct follow-up from Ken against the new side-by-side crate layout, not its own numbered plan: "the brown card/rectangle behind the records is no longer needed in crate view," and "I want the screen to lock. The only thing that scrolls there should be the records (horizontally and vertically)."

- **The bin's own cardboard lip is gone.** `.crate-lip`/`.crate-lip-tag` (CR1, the 330×118px — 420×150px on desktop — cardboard-gradient divider tab at the stage's bottom edge) are removed outright from both `crate/view.js` (creation in the populated and empty-bin branches) and `10-crate.css`/`23-desktop.css` (the rules themselves). `updateLip()` and its one call site in `syncCards()`, and `nearestDividerBefore()` (dead the moment nothing reads its result any more), went with it. The now-unused `--bin-1`/`--bin-2`/`--bin-3` walnut/cardboard tokens (`00-base.css`, CR8 — the lip's own gradient was their one consumer) were removed too; `--crate-light`/`--stock-lip` are untouched, since each still feeds a real surface (the card's own lit top edge; a divider's own stock shadow) — `player/clock.js`'s `drawSun()` comment for `--crate-light` was updated to point at that surface instead of the now-gone lip.
- **The page no longer scrolls on the crate tab.** The crate stage already moves entirely through its own pointer gesture (`crate/view.js`), never a native scroll, so `body` (the app's one scroller, `00-base.css`) scrolling underneath it only ever fought that gesture rather than helping it. `10-crate.css` adds one rule, `body:has(#route-library.on .crate-wrap){overflow:hidden}` — the same `body:has(#playerOverlay.open) ...` pattern `23-desktop.css` already established (RF8), so there's no JS state to keep in sync: the lock drops the instant a tab switch re-renders `#tracksBody` with no `.crate-wrap` in it any more, or the Library route itself loses its `.on` class (switching to Home/Profile). The reduced-motion flat list (`.crate-flat-wrap`, a real native `overflow-y:auto` list, not the gesture-driven stage) is a different element and never matches the selector, so it keeps scrolling normally.
- No `await` was added, removed or reordered — this is DOM/CSS cleanup and one new `:has()` rule, nothing on the playback or render-loop path.
- No test changes needed: nothing removed had a unit test of its own (`.crate-lip`'s text content was never asserted on), and the new CSS rule is layout-only.
- **Browser verification** (Playwright, against the real built `dist/`, a synthetic seeded library — same approach as the perf harness): phone width (390×844) confirmed `.crate-lip` is gone from the DOM, `body`'s computed `overflow` is `hidden` while the crate tab is active, a mouse-wheel scroll attempt leaves `window.scrollY` at 0, switching to the Songs tab restores `overflow: visible`, and `ArrowLeft`/`ArrowRight` section switching still works (confirming the lip removal didn't regress CR14 PR 2's own gesture code). Reduced-motion flat view: renders correctly, no lip (never had one), `body` overflow stays `visible` (native list scroll, correctly not locked). Desktop width (1280px): no lip, `overflow: hidden`, stage renders — the same pre-existing, unrelated `toUpperCase` page error CR14 PR 2 already bisected and left alone reproduced identically here too. Empty-library branch: no lip, no new errors.
- Verified: `npm run lint` passes with 0 errors, 0 warnings; `npm run test:unit` passes (780/780, unchanged); both production builds (`npm run build` and `MIZIKI_BASE=/ npm run build`) succeed, re-run against this branch's own rebase onto `main` after PR #163 merged.

### Post-CR14 revert: the scroll lock itself is gone

Shipped, then reported broken by Ken on his actual device within the hour: "unable to scroll through any of the other tabs while in crate view since it's locked" and "not able to access the library tools within crate view since it locks the screen." The lip removal (above) is unaffected and stays.

- **The `body:has(#route-library.on .crate-wrap){overflow:hidden}` rule is removed outright**, not re-scoped. The Playwright pass that shipped it confirmed the `:has()` selector's own logic (locks while the crate's visible, unlocks the instant the DOM stops matching) — what it couldn't catch, running in a headless desktop Chromium, is iOS Safari's own well-known behavior toggling `overflow:hidden` on `body` while the document had already been scrolled: the browser can leave the page unable to scroll afterward even once the rule stops matching, and can also make an element inside the locked page (here, the Library route's own tools disclosure, reached via `scrollIntoView()`) unreachable while the lock is active. Both are exactly what Ken hit.
- **Nothing was left to fix once the rule was gone.** The crate stage never relied on page scroll to begin with — `crate/view.js`'s own pointer gesture is the only thing that ever moves it, and `applyCrateMiniInset()` already sizes the stage to fit within the viewport's own chrome budget. `html,body`'s pre-existing `overscroll-behavior-y:none` (`00-base.css`, predates this PR) already kills the iOS elastic bounce at the top/bottom of the page — the actual "feels like a webpage" sensation Ken's original ask was about. So removing the lock is a straight revert, not a reduced version of it.
- No `await` touched, no test changes needed (the removed rule had no unit test of its own — it was layout-only, covered by the previous entry's Playwright pass, which is also why this regression wasn't caught before merge: nothing in that pass exercised a real iOS WebKit engine).
- Verified: `npm run lint` 0 errors/0 warnings; `npm run test:unit` 780/780 unchanged; both production builds succeed.
- **Lesson for any future body-level scroll-lock attempt in this app**: a `:has()`-scoped `overflow:hidden` on `body` is fine as a static CSS rule (reviewed by reading the selector), but toggling it dynamically on a page the user has already scrolled is an iOS Safari footgun that a desktop-headless Playwright run won't surface — it needs a real iPhone check before it ships, the same bar `docs/CEREMONY.md`'s own device checklist already sets for anything gesture- or scroll-adjacent.

### Library scroll lock, take two: tied to `showRoute()`, not a reactive selector; Library tools to Profile → Settings

A fresh attempt at the Post-CR14 revert's own named gap, after Ken asked again: "go back to the lock of the library tab where only the navigation of the library/crate view scrolls. Move the 'library tools' to the settings found in the profile tab. Make sure lock doesn't leak to other tabs." This time the lock is tied to the one function that actually switches routes, rather than a reactive CSS selector reacting to whatever the DOM happens to look like.

- **`src/shell/routes.js`**: `showRoute()` now removes a plain `lib-scroll-lock` class from `document.body` as the very first thing it does on any route change (before anything else about the switch happens), and only re-adds it once the route is confirmed `'library'` and NV3's own scroll-restore has already landed the page at 0 — so the lock can never be applied mid-scroll (the specific iOS Safari failure mode the #164/#165 revert hit) and can never outlive the one route it belongs to. Calls the new `updateLibBodyInset()` (below) once the lock is (re-)applied.
- **`src/styles/07-routes.css`**: one new rule, `body.lib-scroll-lock{overflow:hidden}` — a plain class, never a reactive `:has()` selector.
- **`src/library/view.js`**: new exported `updateLibBodyInset()` gives `#libBody` (the shared Songs/Artists/Playlists/search-results host) its own internal `overflow-y:auto` scroll region, sized via the same `getBoundingClientRect()`+`--chrome-inset` approach `crate/view.js`'s `applyCrateMiniInset()` already uses. Crate view mode is exempt (it already fits itself to the viewport and never relied on page scroll) unless a search is active, since search results render into `#libBody` regardless of view mode.
- **`src/shell/mini-player.js`**: `updateChromeInset()` also calls `updateLibBodyInset()`, so a resize or the mini-player showing/hiding keeps the inset accurate, not just the one-time call `showRoute()` itself makes.
- **`src/main.js`**: boot sequence calls `showRoute('library')` once, right after `updateChromeInset()`, since the lock/inset are only ever applied from inside `showRoute()` and the default `.route.on` markup has no matching JS call otherwise.
- **Library tools relocated to Profile → Settings** (not the old pre-RF8 Profile home screen): `miziki.html`'s static `#libTools` block and `#libOverflowToolsBtn` row are removed from `#route-library`'s own `···` overflow menu; a new `#libToolsStash` (sibling of `#profileBody`, following the exact `#playbackSettingsStash` precedent) parks the unchanged markup whenever Settings isn't open. `src/profile/screens.js` gained `parkLibraryTools()` (called alongside the existing `parkPlaybackSettings()` in `renderProfile()`) and a new "Library" section inside `renderAppSettingsScreen()`. `src/wiring/library-view.js` dropped the `#libOverflowToolsBtn` click handler; `src/shell/header.js`'s `refreshLibraryTools()` dropped its route-visibility gate, since the panel's visibility now follows whichever DOM parent holds it.
- No `await` touched — this is DOM/CSS/state wiring, nothing on the playback path.
- **Tests**: `tests/unit/routes.test.js` — four new tests (lock applies on entering Library and calls `updateLibBodyInset()`; never leaks to Home or Profile under any sequence; re-tapping Library already on screen doesn't flicker it off and back on; opening the Sundown sheet over Library doesn't touch the lock either way) plus a `document.body` stand-in and a direct mock of `src/library/view.js` (the usual "stop the chain at the door" reasoning, since `routes.js` now has a real import of it). `tests/unit/library-view.test.js` — four new tests for `updateLibBodyInset()` itself (no-op when Library isn't the route on screen; gives `#libBody` a real max-height/overflow in Songs/Artists/Playlists view; Crate view is exempt; a live search over Crate still gets its own scroll since search results land in `#libBody` regardless of mode), plus `getElementById`/`getComputedStyle`/`window.innerHeight` additions to the file's existing fake-DOM scaffolding.
- Verified: `npm run lint` 0 errors/0 warnings; `npm run test:unit` 788/788 (780 before this PR, plus 8 new); both production builds succeed. Playwright, against the real built `dist/`: the lock is present on boot (Library is the default route), clears on switching to Home, clears on switching to Profile, re-applies on switching back to Library, `#libTools`'s parent is `#libToolsStash` while Settings isn't open, and `--chrome-inset` resolves to a real nonzero value. Two apparent anomalies surfaced during this pass were investigated and are not regressions: `#libBody`'s inline style reads empty on a fresh boot because `S.view.mode` actually defaults to `'crate'` (`state.js` reassigns it after the object-literal default of `'songs'`) — the correct, expected Crate-exempt branch, not a bug; and a `favicon.ico` 404/console error on every run is a pre-existing, unrelated browser auto-request (the app ships no favicon), confirmed with full request/response logging across several clean runs.
- **Headless Playwright cannot fully verify the specific risk this exact feature hit before** (a body-level scroll lock behaving differently under real iOS Safari's own scroll physics once the page has already been scrolled) — per this project's own recorded lesson from the #164/#165 incident, a real-iPhone check before merge is still advisable, not a substitute for the automated verification above.
### Launch intro: a dig through your crate, and the crate pull's pressing peek

Design frames: `docs/design/launch-intro/` (the "Miziki Launch Intro" canvas). Ken's decisions: keep the floating wordmark, no divider in the stack, clean sleeves, a haptic tick on the catch, real covers replacing Miziki covers as the library fills, the stand-in sky fix (point 1), and the pressing peek in both the intro and the crate's existing pull (point 4). Not taken: a shorter repeat-launch variant, and new/sealed records at the front of the stack.

- **The intro (`src/launch/intro.js`, `09-launch-intro.css`)** supersedes CEREMONY §5's plain-record stack. Same 18-sleeve count, same timing, same skip/reduced-motion/Off rules. Each sleeve shows an album cover if its art is already in memory, else the Miziki cover (`planLaunchStack()`, pure and tested). The stack lands on the last-played album, the same one `main.js` primes in the mini-player. The catch is a 28px lift plus `hapticTick()`; the amber outline is gone (CR1's no-selection-ring rule, CR8's "amber never signals state"). On the pull to camera, the record slides out of the top of the sleeve in its own pressing. CEREMONY §5's "no album art is loaded" guarantee is kept in spirit: no card ever waits for art, and the one background request (the target's crate tier) only ever upgrades a Miziki cover to the real one if it lands before the catch.
- **The pressing peek (`src/record-art/peek.js`, new)** draws the record in the album's resolved look via the new side-effect-free `discLook(t)` pulled out of `applyDiscVariant()` (`record-art/looks.js`; the platter's behavior is unchanged). Shared by the intro and the crate.
- **The crate pull (`src/crate/view.js`)** now draws CR5's "record slides out of the sleeve as it rises", which had never been built: a peek disc behind the front card, rising faster than the sleeve (`cratePeekRise()`, pure and tested), removed on commit, spring-back and every cancel path.
- **Haptics (`src/ui/haptic.js`, new):** Capacitor Haptics when present (the planned app build); otherwise `navigator.vibrate(8)` after a user gesture only. A no-op on iOS Safari, which has no web vibration API, so the lift carries the catch on iPhone today.
- **The stand-in sky (`sundown/day.js`'s new `standInEvents()`, used by `location.js`'s `fallbackSun()`):** the boot-time stand-in day now uses today's cached real sunrise/sunset when there are any, instead of always the fixed 8:10 pm sunset (which it also used to write back over the cache). This is what makes the intro's sky match the time of day from the first frame.
- **Verification in this session was partial:** the npm registry was unreachable from the build sandbox, so `npm ci` couldn't run. `eslint` ran (with the `globals` package stood in from real Chromium/Node global names): 0 findings in every changed file. Unit tests: everything that could load passed, including 9 new tests (`launch`, `day`, `crate-model`); the 8 files that need `fake-indexeddb` couldn't load and are untouched by this change. No `vite build`; instead a Playwright pass against the unbundled source checked the intro with an empty and a seeded library in dark and light themes, and the crate pull with dispatched touch-pointer events (the peek appears in the album's pressing and is removed on release). The PR's own CI and a Claude Code session on the PR run the full lint/test/build.

### Migration 020: the collapsed "added" feed event follows the latest item

Found while running `npm run lint`/`npm run test:unit`/both builds on PR #170 as requested: `tests/client.test.js`'s own pre-existing, known-but-undiagnosed failure ("RF2/RF21: deleting a crate item entirely removes its added events") turned out to be a real server-side bug, not a stale test.

- **`supabase/migrations/020_added_event_follows_latest.sql`**: re-declares `crate_items_emit_events()` (the live version, `009_feed_privacy.sql`'s) once more. Its 15-minute burst-collapse (meant to turn an import of several records into one "added N records" feed row rather than N separate ones) bumped the collapsed row's `count`/`created_at` on every new insert within the window, but never its `release_id` — so the one row stayed permanently pointed at whichever release started the burst. A later item added in the same burst got no feed row of its own at all (a friend's Home feed never updated past the first record), and deleting that later item — which matches by `release_id` — never cleaned up the stale row for the first one. Fix: the same `update` now also sets `release_id = NEW.release_id`, so the collapsed row always tracks the most recently added item. `rebuild_feed_events()` needed no change — it already writes one uncollapsed row per crate item (009's own comment already called this out).
- **A second, previously-undocumented stale assertion** turned up in the same file while reproducing the failure against a fresh local Postgres: `tests/client.test.js`'s two `delete_my_account` tests (lines ~381/387) expected `library_backup` to still hold 2 rows for UID3, left over from when the file was first written. Migration 018's own later test additions ("a user can delete their own stale device row") drop one of UID3's two rows earlier in the same run, and the `delete_my_account` tests' expectation was never updated to match — fixed to `'1'`.
- **New regression test**: "migration 020: a burst-collapsed added event follows the latest item, not the first" — inserts two crate items for the same actor within the burst window and asserts the one remaining feed row's `release_id` moves to the second item while `count` still reaches 2.
- No `await` touched — server-side SQL only, no client code.
