- **Verified against a freshly bootstrapped local Postgres 16 instance** (this session's own container had none; `initdb`/`pg_ctl` under the `postgres` system user, `PGHOST=/var/tmp/pgmz`/`PGPORT=5544` to match the test harness's defaults): `tests/supabase_stub.sql` + all 20 migrations applied in order with no errors, then `npm test` — 95/95 passing (91 before this fix, plus 1 new, with the 3 previously-failing tests now passing: the two `delete_my_account` tests and the `crate_items_after_delete` one). Also re-ran `npm run lint` (0 errors/0 warnings), `npm run test:unit` (806/806, unaffected — this change is server-side only), and both production builds (`npm run build` and `MIZIKI_BASE=/ npm run build`), all passing.
- **Not run in CI** — `tests/client.test.js` needs a real Postgres and isn't part of either CI pipeline (only `npm run lint`/`npm run test:unit` are). Ken applies migrations by hand in the Supabase SQL editor before merging a PR that adds one, per his own standing instruction; this PR flags `020_added_event_follows_latest.sql` for exactly that.

### Surface Noise PR1: the engine (needle drop, lead-in, run-out hiss, under-the-music bed)

Spec: `docs/15_SURFACE_NOISE.md` ("Miziki — Surface Noise & Vinyl Ritual"), copied verbatim from Ken's upload. First of 5 PRs in the spec's own build order (engine → needle-lift skipping → auto-flip toggle → dead-wax etchings → shrink-wrap peel), each gated on Ken's own review before the next starts.

- **`src/audio/surface-noise.js` (new)**: the core generators and runtime starters — all pure, seeded (`rng()`, the same XorShift pattern as `audio/runout.js`), so the same release+side always sounds the same. `releaseSeed(releaseId, side)`; `noiseFloorScale(setting, tier, wearLevel)` (Off is always silent; Well-loved louder than Subtle; a cleaner pressing tier is quieter); `hissSamples`/`crackleSamples`/`popSamples` (one-pole-filtered noise, Poisson-timed impulses); `rotationWobble` (ties the noise's own amplitude to `S.rate`, the same Sundown/drift speed signal PR #172 already wired into scratch audio); `leadInSamples`/`runoutNoiseSamples`/`underMusicSamples`/`needleDropSamples`/`needleLiftSamples`. Runtime: `startBuffer(build, opts)` takes a `build(sampleRate)` callback invoked *inside* its own try/catch (not a precomputed array passed in) — a precomputed-array first draft let a bad/`null` `S.ctx` produce `NaN` lengths that threw `RangeError` outside any try/catch, since `Math.max(0, NaN)` is `NaN`, not `0`. `startLeadIn`/`startRunoutNoise`/`startUnderMusicBed`/`playNeedleDrop`/`playNeedleLift` are all safe no-ops without a real audio graph, in Pure, or with the setting Off.
- **`src/player/transport.js`**: `play()` detects the start of a side (`isSideStart()`, reusing `splitSides`/`albumTracks`, set once in `load()` as `S.pendingSideStart` and consumed-then-cleared in `play()` — never set on a bare pause→resume, since `load()` only runs on a genuine track change) and, when Surface Noise is on, plays the needle-drop + haptic and schedules the lead-in noise via sample-accurate Web Audio clock scheduling (`src.start(ctx.currentTime + leadInSec, ...)`) rather than any `await`/`setTimeout` delay — CLAUDE.md rule 9's "never add/remove/reorder an await in `play()`" stays intact. `stop()`/`pause()`/`seek()` all cancel the pending lead-in (and the under-music bed, except on `seek`, which shouldn't interrupt a bed mid-track); `startSource()` gained a `leadInSec` parameter.
- **`src/player/clock.js`**: `tick()` freezes `S.pos` (and skips `addPlayedRange`/`checkTrackCompletion`) while `S.openUntil` (a `performance.now()` deadline, new on `S`) is in the future — the one addition needed because `tick()` advances the scrub bar by wall-clock delta, not real AudioContext position, so the lead-in's scheduled-future-start would otherwise desync the position display from the actually-silent-then-playing audio. Inert (`0`) whenever no lead-in is in flight.
- **`src/shell/runout.js`**: layers the new run-out hiss/tick noise and the needle-lift sound alongside the *existing* CEREMONY run-out groove/side-flip timing (`GROOVE`/`FLIP`, `ceremony-timing.js`) — those durations are untouched; real "wait indefinitely" behavior for the Run-out length setting is explicitly PR3's job (Auto-flip toggle), per the spec's own stated dependency.
- **Settings** (`S.surfaceNoise` 'off'/'subtle'/'well-loved', default 'subtle'; `S.runoutLength` 'short'/'normal'/'until-lift', default 'normal'; `S.surfaceNoiseUnderMusic` boolean, default false) — all three round-tripped through `serializePrefs`/`parsePrefs` (`storage/prefs.js`) and `applyPrefs` (`storage/meta.js`), each with its own `tests/unit/prefs.test.js` round-trip/old-data-default/bad-value test. UI lives in `#sundownSoundView` (Sundown sheet → Sound view), wired in `src/wiring/character.js`.
- **A real layout bug, found and fixed during Playwright verification, not by the automated test suite**: the first markup draft put the new 3-button Surface Noise / Run-out length segs inside the existing `.row`/`.k` (label-left, control-right) wrapper the single-button "Under the music" toggle and the 4-button "How deep the night goes" depth seg already use — but unlike those, two of the new buttons' own labels ("Well-loved", "Until I lift it") are long enough that the row's flex layout read as visually cramped against the next row at a phone viewport. Fixed by switching those two controls to the same full-width, stacked `<p class="note">` + `.seg` pattern the pre-existing "Starting point" character-preset control already uses, plus a small explicit top margin on the "Under the music" row. Confirmed via `getBoundingClientRect()` in Playwright (not just a screenshot read, which can mislead at this zoom/compression level) that every row has a real, non-overlapping gap.
- **Tests**: `tests/unit/surface-noise.test.js` (new) — `releaseSeed` determinism; `noiseFloorScale`'s Off/Subtle/Well-loved/tier ordering; every sample generator's length/determinism/decay-toward-silence properties; `rotationWobble`'s rate-scaling; every runtime starter's safe-no-op behavior with no audio graph, in Pure, and with the setting Off (exercised against `S.ctx = null` and `S.ctx = {}` with a bare `S.nodes.wobble`, confirming the `startBuffer` fix above). `tests/unit/prefs.test.js` — 3 new tests for the three settings.
- **Verified**: `npm run lint` 0 errors/0 warnings; `npm run test:unit` 820/820 (817 before this PR, plus 3 new in `prefs.test.js` — `surface-noise.test.js`'s own 13 tests are additional files counted separately in the 820 total); both production builds (`npm run build`, `MIZIKI_BASE=/ npm run build`). Playwright against the real built `dist/`, at iPhone-13 viewport with real `.tap()` gestures: the Sound view's Surface Noise/Run-out length/Under-the-music controls render with correct initial `aria-pressed` state and toggle correctly on tap; a test-tone track plays cleanly through the needle-drop + ~3s lead-in with no console errors, the mini-player showing "Now playing"/pause correctly once audio starts. Two console findings surfaced during this pass were checked against an unmodified baseline build and are **not** regressions from this PR: a `favicon.ico`-style 404 on page load, and a pre-existing `TypeError: Cannot read properties of null (reading 'classList')` thrown from the minified bundle when the Sundown sheet first opens — both reproduce identically on `main` before any of this PR's changes.
- No migration — this PR is entirely client-side.

### Surface Noise PR2: needle-lift skipping

Spec: `docs/15_SURFACE_NOISE.md` §3. Second of the 5-PR build order (engine → **needle-lift skipping** → auto-flip toggle → dead-wax etchings → shrink-wrap peel). Next/Previous (and the lock-screen/headphone/car-button equivalents, which all route through the same functions) now lift the needle with a sound and a visual nudge before landing on the new track, instead of cutting instantly, whenever Surface Noise is on.

- **`src/audio/engine.js`**: `buildGraph(ctx)` gained a dedicated per-current-track `n.trackGain` `GainNode`, inserted between the track's `BufferSourceNode` and the shared chain (`wobble → … → master`). `routeSource(src)` now connects `src → trackGain → (master in Pure, wobble in Vinyl)` instead of connecting the source straight into the shared chain. This is what lets a skip duck *only the music* — Surface Noise's own sounds (needle drop/lift, lead-in, run-out, under-music bed) all join the chain at `wobble`, downstream of `trackGain`, so none of them fade with a skip.
- **`src/audio/surface-noise.js`**: new `skipDropHissSamples(sampleRate, seed, scale, rateHz=0.55)` — the spec's own ~400ms band-hiss (`SKIP_DROP_HISS_SEC = 0.4`) that plays as the new track lands; `playSkipDrop(seed, scale, rateHz=0.55)` combines the existing `playNeedleDrop()` pop (always, regardless of scale) with this new hiss layer (only when `scale > 0` — Off/Pure never add it, same rule as every other Surface Noise sound); `fadeMusicTo(value, ms)` ramps `S.nodes.trackGain.gain` via `cancelScheduledValues`/`setValueAtTime`/`linearRampToValueAtTime`, a safe no-op without a `trackGain` node (no audio graph yet, or mid-teardown). New timing constants matching the spec's own "total stays under about 900ms" sequence: `SKIP_LIFT_MS = 120`, `SKIP_AIR_MS = 250`, `SKIP_FADE_MS = 80`.
- **`src/player/queue.js`**: new `skipWithLift(dir)` (`dir` is `1`/`-1`/`0`) is the orchestration function — fades the music out immediately (`fadeMusicTo(0, SKIP_FADE_MS)`), plays the needle-lift sound and (unless reduced-motion) adds a transient `.skip-lift` class to the platter via the new `setPlatterLift(on)`, then after the lift+air span (or just the fade span, with Surface Noise Off/Pure) plays the drop sound, clears the platter class, actually advances/restarts via `advance(d, false)`/`seek(0)`, and fades the music back in. A module-private `pendingSkip` collapses rapid repeated taps into a single lift, landing only on whichever direction was tapped last, via the new `resolveSkipTarget(dir)` (also used to defer straight to the plain `advance(dir, false)` path with no lift at all once nothing's actually left to skip to — past the end of a non-repeating queue). `invalidateActiveSessionIfAny()` is called immediately, same as before this PR. **CLAUDE.md refactor rule 9**: deliberately plain `setTimeout`, not Web-Audio-clock pre-scheduling — by the time a skip can happen, `S.ctx` is already running and background `<audio>` has already been activated by an earlier real gesture, so a later non-gesture `play()` call here is exactly as safe as `advance()`'s own existing `src.onended → play()` precedent; no `await` added, removed or reordered, and `S.gen`/`onended` logic is untouched.
- **`src/player/controls.js`**: `nextTrack()`/`prevTrack()` now call `skipWithLift(1)`/`skipWithLift(S.pos > 4 ? 0 : -1)` in place of their old direct `advance()`/`seek(0)` calls — the lock-screen/headphone/car Media Session handlers (`player/background.js`) already route through these same two functions, so they get the lift for free with no change of their own.
- **`src/player/transport.js`**: `sideLetterFor` changed from module-private to a real export, so `queue.js` can compute the same needle seed (`releaseSeed(albumKey(t), sideLetterFor(t, S.index))`) `audio/surface-noise.js`'s other callers already use.
- **`src/styles/02-stage-platter.css`**: `.platter-box.skip-lift{transition:transform .12s ease-out;transform:translateY(3px) scale(.985)}` — the visual-nudge fallback the spec calls for alongside the needle-lift sound.
- **Tests**: `tests/unit/queue.test.js` — 5 new tests for `skipWithLift` (the full lift→drop→advance sequence with Surface Noise active; rapid repeated taps collapsing into one lift/drop landing on the final direction only; the plain-fade path with Surface Noise Off/Pure; the `dir=0` restart branch; falling straight through to the plain `advance()` path with nothing left to skip to), via a new mock of `src/audio/surface-noise.js` with millisecond-scale timing constants (`SKIP_LIFT_MS: 5, SKIP_AIR_MS: 5, SKIP_FADE_MS: 2`) so the tests' own short real `setTimeout` waits stay fast and deterministic without needing to fake timers. Also fixed a pre-existing gap in this file's own `fakeEl()` DOM stub, found while writing these tests: `classList` had `add`/`remove`/`contains` but no `toggle`, which `setPlatterLift()`'s `box.classList.toggle('skip-lift', on)` call needs — missing it threw mid-test, in a way that (since the throw landed after `skipWithLift`'s own module-level `pendingSkip` assignment had already run) silently corrupted shared module state into the next test rather than failing cleanly. `tests/unit/surface-noise.test.js` — 5 new tests for `skipDropHissSamples` (length, silence at scale 0, seed determinism), the three new timing constants, `playSkipDrop` (pop regardless of scale, hiss only when `scale > 0`, no-op in Off/Pure), and `fadeMusicTo` (safe no-op with no `trackGain`, exact ramp call sequence otherwise). `tests/unit/controls.test.js` and `tests/unit/engine.test.js` updated for the new `trackGain` node in `S.nodes` and the new `sideLetterFor` export on the `player/transport.js` mock.
- **Verified**: `npm run lint` 0 errors/0 warnings; `npm run test:unit` 830/830 (820 before this PR, plus 10 new); both production builds (`npm run build`, `MIZIKI_BASE=/ npm run build`). Playwright against the real built `dist/`, at iPhone-13 viewport with real `.tap()` gestures, seeding a synthetic library straight into IndexedDB: tapping Next advances the track and the `.skip-lift` platter-dip class appears during the lift then clears afterward; three rapid Next taps collapse into a single advance, not three; with Surface Noise set to Off (via the Sundown sheet's own Sound view, reached through the platter's `#sunKnob`), Next still advances correctly through the plain-fade path; Previous moves to the prior track as expected (this harness's synthetic, audio-less tracks never advance `S.pos`, so `prevTrack`'s `dir=0` restart branch — which needs real playback position — is covered directly by the unit test above instead); no real console/page errors, after confirming the one 404 that does appear is the browser chrome's own automatic `favicon.ico` request against the test harness's static server, unrelated to the app (cross-checked with a plain `curl`, and consistent with a prior session's own documented finding of the same harmless artifact).
- No migration — this PR is entirely client-side.

### Surface Noise PR3: the auto-flip toggle

Spec: `docs/15_SURFACE_NOISE.md` §4. Third of the 5-PR build order (engine → needle-lift skipping → **auto-flip toggle** → dead-wax etchings → shrink-wrap peel). With Auto-flip On (the default, today's behavior unchanged) side A still turns itself over at the end, same as before PR3 ever existed. With it Off, side A's run-out plays and the record just waits there — spinning, groove and surface-noise hiss going — until the user flips it, the run-out times out and the needle lifts on its own (Short/Normal), or a different record is pulled; "Until I lift it" has no timeout at all and waits forever until one of the other two happens.

- **`src/shell/ceremony-timing.js`**: new `WAIT_TIMEOUT_MS = { short: 8000, normal: 20000, 'until-lift': null }` and `runoutWaitMs(runoutLength)` — the one new pure, unit-tested helper this PR adds, reusing the Surface Noise panel's own existing `S.runoutLength` setting (PR1) rather than inventing a second one just for the wait.
- **`src/shell/runout.js`**: `S.runout`'s own `kind` union gains a third value, `'wait'` (alongside `'end'`/`'flip'`). `maybeSideFlip(n)`'s final branch now reads `S.autoFlip`: On calls the existing `runSideFlip(n, turn)` unchanged; Off calls the new `runSideWait(n, turn)` instead. `runSideWait()` sets `S.runout = {kind:'wait', turning:true, n, from, to, lifted:false}`, adds the existing `.runout` stage class (same groove/spin visual PR1 already established), and — deliberately leaving `#sideLetter` untouched (it's a transient, platter-covering overlay meant only for the ~700ms flip animation's own giant letter, `03-sleeve-pull.css`'s `.side-letter`; showing it for a wait that can run indefinitely would be a real visual regression, not an indicator) — writes a status line into `#trackSpec` instead (`setFlipWaitStatus`: "Side A finished · Flip to continue.", save-once/restore-once against whatever `#trackSpec` held before). Since `audio/runout.js`'s `startRunout()` allocates one fixed-duration buffer and "Until I lift it" has no duration to allocate, the wait plays groove+noise in bounded `WAIT_LOOP_SEC = 20`-second (or shorter, for the final chunk before a deadline) chunks in a `for(;;)` loop, re-arming itself each iteration and checking the existing `S.sleeveSeq` cancellation token after every `await sleep(...)` — the same "every await followed by a token check" discipline every other ceremony function in this file already follows. Reaching the deadline with nobody flipping it calls the new `liftNeedleNow()` (stops groove/noise with a 150ms fade, plays the existing needle-lift sound, `startSpinDown()`, removes the `.runout` class) — the wait itself doesn't end, `S.runout` stays set, just silently. New `updateWaitUI()` shows/hides the player's own new `#flipPrompt`/`#liftNeedleBtn` buttons (`miziki.html`, inside `#nowActions`): Flip shows for the whole wait regardless of lift state; Lift needle only for "Until I lift it," and only before it's already been lifted (Short/Normal already lift themselves on timeout, so the button would have nothing left to do). New `performPendingFlip()` is the flip itself — no lift/turn animation of its own, the same instant snap-to-side-B `interruptRunout()`'s old generic branch already used, since two of its three callers (a lock-screen Next, a lock-screen Play) have no screen to animate on anyway; it's reached from the player's own Flip button, from `player/controls.js`'s `nextTrack()`/`prevTrack()`, and from `player/transport.js`'s `play()`.
- **A real bug found and fixed during Playwright verification, before any of this ever reached review**: `interruptRunout()` (the existing "a tap anywhere on the open player skips to where the in-flight groove/flip was heading" gesture, reached via the player overlay's own generic `pointerdown` handler, `wiring/transport.js`) treated the new `'wait'` kind exactly like `'flip'` at first — any tap anywhere in the open player, including a tap on the new `#flipPrompt`/`#liftNeedleBtn` buttons *themselves*, silently performed the flip right there in the `pointerdown` phase, before the button's own `click` handler ever ran (pointerdown always precedes click). Confirmed with a real Playwright `.tap()` on `#liftNeedleBtn`: the wait ended and the record flipped, when nothing about "lift the needle" should have ended the wait at all. Unlike `'end'`/`'flip'` — a brief, ~2.5s/~700ms animation genuinely in flight, where "any tap skips to the end" is a reasonable shortcut — a `'wait'` can last indefinitely, and nothing about tapping the scrub bar, the mode toggle, or either of this PR's own two new buttons should ever be read as "flip it." Fixed by excluding `'wait'` from `interruptRunout()` outright (`if(!r || r.kind === 'wait') return false;`), so it falls through to `interruptStartSequence()`'s own put-away/sleeve-pull cleanup instead — confirmed harmless for a wait, since none of the stage classes that branch checks for are ever set during one. A wait now only ever ends through `performPendingFlip()`, `liftNeedleNow()`, or a new pull's own unconditional `cancelRunout()` call (`shell/start-sequence.js`'s `runStartSequence()`) — never a stray tap.
- **`src/player/controls.js`**: `nextTrack()`/`prevTrack()` each gained a leading check — `if(S.runout && S.runout.kind === 'wait'){ performPendingFlip(); return; }` — before falling through to their existing `skipWithLift()` call (PR2). There's no "next"/"previous" track to lift to during a wait (side B hasn't loaded), so both perform the flip instead; since the lock-screen/headphone/car Media Session handlers (`player/background.js`) already call these same two functions unchanged, "lock-screen Next performs the flip" is free.
- **`src/player/transport.js`**: `play()` gained the same leading check, before its existing `bgPrime()` call. **CLAUDE.md refactor rule 9**: `performPendingFlip()`'s own `play()` call for side B still runs `bgPrime()` as its own first line, synchronously within the same call stack and still inside the original tap — the same justification already established for PR2's `skipWithLift`. Since `#play`'s own wiring (`wiring/transport.js`) already calls `play()` directly whenever `!S.playing && S.platterEmpty===false`, "Play on a waiting record performs the flip too" needed no wiring change of its own either.
- **`src/wiring/character.js`**: a new `[data-auto-flip]` click handler, same inline read-S/write-aria-pressed/`queueSave()` shape the three existing Surface Noise panel controls already use.
- **`src/wiring/transport.js`**: wires `#flipPrompt`/`#liftNeedleBtn`'s own clicks to `performPendingFlip`/`liftNeedleNow`.
- **`miziki.html`**: the Auto-flip On/Off seg (Surface Noise panel, alongside the existing three controls) and the player's own `#flipPrompt`/`#liftNeedleBtn` buttons (inside `#nowActions`, both `display:none` until a wait is active).
- **`src/state.js`/`src/storage/prefs.js`/`src/storage/meta.js`**: `S.autoFlip` (default `true`), added to `KNOWN_PREF_KEYS`/`serializePrefs`/`parsePrefs` and `applyPrefs`'s restore-time `[data-auto-flip]` sync, the same shape every other Surface Noise setting already has.
- **CLAUDE.md refactor rule 8**: `player/transport.js` importing `performPendingFlip` from `shell/runout.js`, which already imports `load`/`play`/`pause` from `player/transport.js`, is a new circular pair — consistent with the established peer-import precedent (`audio/engine.js`/`player/transport.js`), safe because both sides' imports are only ever used inside function bodies, never at module top level.
- **Tests**: `tests/unit/ceremony-timing.test.js` — 2 new tests for `runoutWaitMs` (Short/Normal are finite and Short < Normal; Until-I-lift-it is `null`; an unrecognized value falls back to Normal, the same default `S.runoutLength` itself has). `tests/unit/controls.test.js` — 3 new tests for `nextTrack`/`prevTrack`'s waiting-record branch (performs the flip and never calls `play`/`pause` directly; the same for `prevTrack`; neither touches `performPendingFlip` once nothing is waiting). `tests/unit/prefs.test.js` — 3 new tests for `autoFlip` (round-trips both On and Off; an old saved object with no `autoFlip` field at all loads with the key simply absent, so the default stays On; a non-boolean value is ignored the same way). 11 test files that mock `shell/runout.js` wholesale (`backup-reset.test.js`, `clock.test.js`, `credits.test.js`, `delete.test.js`, `forget-library.test.js`, `history.test.js`, `queue.test.js`, `search-filter.test.js`, `search.test.js`, `tags.test.js`, plus `controls.test.js` itself) each needed `performPendingFlip` added to that mock's `namedExports` — a real module-resolution `SyntaxError` otherwise, since the real `src/player/controls.js` (loaded for real by several of these files) now imports that name.
- **Verified**: `npm run lint` 0 errors/0 warnings; `npm run test:unit` 838/838 (830 before this PR, plus 8 new); both production builds (`npm run build`, `MIZIKI_BASE=/ npm run build`). Playwright against the real built `dist/`, at iPhone-13 viewport with real `.tap()` gestures: unlike PR1/PR2's own synthetic, undecodable (`blob:null`) library fixture, exercising a genuine side-A-to-side-B transition needs a real `ended` audio event, so this run seeded four short (0.4s), genuinely decodable mono WAV tracks — one album, one disc, so `splitSides()` falls back to its midpoint split (side A: tracks 1-2, side B: tracks 3-4) — directly into the real `tracks` IndexedDB store. With Album sides on (Profile → Settings → Library) and Auto-flip off (Sundown sheet → Sound view), side A played through naturally to a real wait: the status line read "Side A finished · Flip to continue.", both `#flipPrompt` and (with "Until I lift it" selected) `#liftNeedleBtn` appeared; tapping Lift needle hid the lift button alone and left the wait (and its status line) otherwise untouched — confirming the `interruptRunout()` fix above actually holds under a real tap, not just in review; tapping `#flipPrompt` afterward advanced correctly to side B's first track and restored the normal status line; tapping `#next` mid-wait (standing in for a lock-screen/headphone Next) performed the same flip; picking a different track from the Library mid-wait cancelled it cleanly (status line restored, both buttons hidden); and, as a regression check, turning Auto-flip back On made side A flip itself automatically again with no tap at all. No console/page errors throughout.
- No migration — this PR is entirely client-side.

### Surface Noise PR4: dead-wax etchings

Spec: `docs/15_SURFACE_NOISE.md` §5. Fourth of the 5-PR build order (engine → needle-lift skipping → auto-flip toggle → **dead-wax etchings** → shrink-wrap peel), explicitly noted in the spec's own Build order as "visual only... entirely client-side." A matrix line (`"MZK-7C35C-A1"`-style, in Miziki catalog style) plus a short hand-written mark or message, scratched into the ungrooved band between the groove and the label — generated client-side from the release id, so every copy of an album shows the same etching (the same "seed per release" shape PR1's `releaseSeed()` already established). Shown by fading up during the exact same run-out moment the existing label glow already uses, with no code change to `shell/runout.js`'s own control flow at all; also reachable any time via a long-press loupe on the platter. No toggle, and — unlike Surface Noise/needle-lift audio — not disabled by Pure mode.

- **`src/record-art/etchings.js` (new)**: pure generation plus the DOM-writing calls. `matrixTail(releaseId)` (a 5-hex-digit tail shared by every side of a release — deliberately the *low*-order digits of a `*31` rolling hash, not the high-order ones: a first draft sliced the high digits and two release ids differing only in their last character collided every time, since a short rolling hash's top bits barely move for a one-character tail difference while its low bits do), `matrixLineFor(releaseId, side)` (`'MZK-' + matrixTail(releaseId) + '-' + (side||'A') + '1'`), `DEAD_WAX_MESSAGES` (a small hand-written set, mirroring the spec's own examples — "cut at night," "more bass please," a star, "≈ side two," …), `messageFor`/`etchingFor` (picked deterministically per release+side via the existing `releaseSeed()`, PR1), and `glyphJitter(seed, index)` (a per-glyph `{dy, rotate}` offset, small and deterministic, the "hand-scratched, not a perfect arc" look the spec asks for). `applyDeadWaxEtching(t, side)` and `setDeadWaxMagnified(on)` are the two DOM-facing exports: the first writes both lines as one `<tspan>` per character via `document.createElementNS`/`textContent` (never `innerHTML` — CLAUDE.md's tag-text/generated-text DOM-safety rule applies to any non-literal string, not just file tags), the second toggles a plain `.magnified` class for the loupe. Imports `releaseSeed` (`audio/surface-noise.js`) and `albumKey` (`record-art/tiers.js`) — both already-established leaves, so no new import weight.
- **`miziki.html`**: a new `<svg class="dead-wax" id="deadWax">` inside `#disc`, right after `.disc-fade` and before `.label` — its own `<defs><path id="deadWaxPath">` arc at radius 50 in a `0 0 200 200` viewBox (just outside the label's own ~42-unit radius, "between the groove and the label"), with two `<text><textPath>` elements (`#deadWaxMatrixText`/`#deadWaxMessageText`) at different `startOffset`s along the same arc.
- **`src/styles/02-stage-platter.css`**: `.dead-wax` itself (`position:absolute;inset:0`, `opacity:0` at rest, `transition:opacity .9s ease-in-out,transform .25s ease-out`), `.dead-wax.magnified{opacity:1;transform:scale(1.9)}` (the loupe), default light ink (`fill:rgba(255,255,255,.4)`), and a dark-ink override for the two art-backed light/clear rares CR7 already established (`.disc[data-variant="ultra-clear"] .dead-wax text,.disc[data-variant="picture-disc"] .dead-wax text{fill:rgba(0,0,0,.4)}`) — per the spec's own "lighter on dark pressings, darker on clear/light pressings" rule.
- **`src/styles/03-sleeve-pull.css`**: `.platter-stage.runout .dead-wax{opacity:.85}`, right alongside the pre-existing `.platter-stage.runout .label{...}` glow rule it rides with — the one and only hook for "shown during the run-out," since all three of `shell/runout.js`'s run-out code paths (`endOfQueue`/`runSideFlip`/`runSideWait`) already toggle this same `.platter-stage.runout` class identically.
- **`src/player/transport.js`**: `load()` calls the new `applyDeadWaxEtching(t, sideLetterFor(t, i))` right alongside its existing `#labelTitle`/`#labelArcText` writes — reusing the already-exported `sideLetterFor()` rather than a third copy of the side-split logic.
- **`src/library/delete.js`/`src/shell/runout.js`**: the three existing "nothing loaded, clear the platter" spots that already clear `#labelTitle` and call `applyDiscVariant(null)` (the library-now-empty branch and `forgetLibrary()`'s full wipe in `delete.js`, and `runout.js`'s `clearToRest()`) each gained a matching `applyDeadWaxEtching(null, '')` call, the same "every per-track readout has to be cleared the same places the others already are" rule RF15's own `#labelTitle` additions established.
- **`src/player/scrub.js`**: the long-press-to-loupe gesture, built from scratch (no prior long-press pattern existed anywhere in this file or `src/wiring/*.js`) — a plain `DEAD_WAX_LONG_PRESS_MS = 500` `setTimeout`, started on `pointerdown` (alongside the existing `scrubGesture` init) and cleared the moment a touch is actually claimed as a scrub/scratch (so holding still then rotating never also magnifies) and on release either way (`release()` now also calls `setDeadWaxMagnified(false)` unconditionally). No mode check anywhere in this path, so the loupe works in Pure mode exactly as it does in Vinyl.
- **Tests**: `tests/unit/etchings.test.js` (new, 8 tests) — `matrixTail` determinism and the collision-regression case (two release ids differing by one character), `matrixLineFor`'s shared tail across sides plus the sides-off fallback to side A, `messageFor`'s determinism and that side is actually part of its seed, `etchingFor`'s bundling, and `glyphJitter`'s determinism/bounded range per seed+index.
- **Verified**: `npm run lint` 0 errors/0 warnings; `npm run test:unit` 846/846 (838 before this PR, plus 8 new); both production builds (`npm run build`, `MIZIKI_BASE=/ npm run build`). Playwright against the real built `dist/`, at iPhone-13 viewport with real `.tap()`/mouse gestures, seeding one real-decodable WAV track directly into IndexedDB: the matrix/message lines were written (one `<tspan>` per character) the moment the track loaded, read `"MZK-…-A1"`-shaped and non-empty, and stayed at `opacity:0` outside a run-out moment; letting the track play to a genuine queue-end run-out faded the etching to `opacity:.85` (after allowing for the CSS transition's own 0.9s — an earlier version of this check read too early and looked like a regression that wasn't one); no dead-wax markup ever appeared inside `#libBody`; switching to Pure mode left the etching text completely unaffected; holding a finger down on the platter for 700ms (past the 500ms threshold) added `.magnified`, releasing removed it, a plain quick tap never added it at all, and an actual disc rotation past the scrub claim threshold correctly cancelled the pending long-press instead of also magnifying. No console/page errors throughout.
- No migration — this PR is entirely client-side.

### Surface Noise PR5: the shrink-wrap peel

Spec: `docs/15_SURFACE_NOISE.md` §2. Last of the 5-PR build order (engine → needle-lift skipping → auto-flip toggle → dead-wax etchings → **shrink-wrap peel**). "The first play of a Sealed record becomes something you open" — a Sealed record's sleeve now arrives wrapped (a milky sheen, a corner wrinkle, a hype sticker) with a small tear tab at its opening edge; dragging the tab leftward past 60% (or a long-press anywhere on the sleeve, or letting 2.2s pass untouched, or a new accessible "Open" action in the sealed-record sheet) detaches the whole shrink in one piece with a synthesized crinkle sound and a haptic tick, same as `breakSeal()` has always unsealed the album. It never blocks play: a sealed track started from anywhere other than the platter (the track list, search, the lock screen) gets a 0.4-intensity crinkle only, no sleeve shown to animate. Reduced Motion skips the ceremony entirely the same way — `runStartSequence()`'s own existing top-of-function gate (`!t || !info || info.type === 'none' || !S.sleevePullEnabled || REDUCED`) already routes every one of those cases through the identical "no ceremony, crinkle-only, `play()`'s own unconditional `breakSeal()` call still unseals it" branch; this PR adds no second Reduced-Motion check of its own anywhere.

- **`src/audio/surface-noise.js`**: `crinkleBurstSamples(sampleRate, seed, intensity)` reuses `crackleSamples()` wholesale (already exactly the right shape — raw random impulses, no lowpass stage, no tonal body) rather than a new generator, scaling duration (0.08-0.13s), density (55-205 impulses/sec) and level (-32 to -23dB) by `intensity` (0..1, clamped, driven by the drag's own velocity). `playCrinkleBurst(seed, intensity)` is a no-op in Pure (the doc's own shared "Pure means untouched" principle for all five Surface Noise features) and is deliberately **not** gated by the Surface Noise setting at all — the crinkle is its own ceremony moment (§2), not "surface noise" itself, so it still plays with Surface Noise set to Off.
- **`src/shell/start-sequence.js`**: `attachSleeveWrap(t)` builds `#sleeveWrap` (a sibling of the existing `#sleeve`/`#sleeveArt`, appended the moment the sleeve starts arriving so it arrives wrapped) with a `.shrink-wrap-inner` (sheen, wrinkle, tear-line, hype sticker — `hypeStickerText(t)`, "NEW" or "NEW · <year>") and a `.shrink-tear-tab`; `removeSleeveWrap()` is the one teardown choke point every path (a normal finish, an interrupt) goes through, resolving/cancelling the module-private `activeShrinkPeel` so an interrupted peel never leaves its own window/tab listeners or a hung `runStartSequence()` await behind. `wireShrinkDrag(wrap, tab, seed, onCommit)` drives `--tear-progress` live from a leftward, absolute-from-start-X drag on the tab (the same convention `library/sealed.js`'s pre-existing `wireTearDrag` already uses for the crate-card tear strip) via real `setPointerCapture()`, firing a scaled crinkle burst + `hapticTick()` on real forward progress and committing at `SHRINK_COMMIT_PROGRESS = 0.6`; releasing short of it snaps back to 0. `wireShrinkLongPress(wrap, onCommit)` commits after `SHRINK_LONG_PRESS_MS = 500` (matching `player/scrub.js`'s dead-wax loupe timing) on any pointer held on the sleeve that isn't already dragging the tab. `runShrinkWrapPeel(cancelled, seed)` races the drag, the long-press and a `SHRINK_AUTO_PEEL_MS = 2200` no-engagement timeout against each other — whichever fires first tears the other two down and runs the one shared `finishShrinkPeel()` (the ~400ms detach-and-fall, reusing `SLEEVE_ANIM.peelOffMs`, or Reduced Motion's plain fade — this inner check is defensive/dead in practice, since `runStartSequence()`'s own top-of-function gate already keeps `REDUCED` from ever reaching this far). New export `isShrinkWrapTarget(target)` (`target.closest('#sleeveWrap')`) lets any other listener exclude the shrink-wrap's own gesture by target, since this one's capture-phase pointerdown runs ahead of the shrink-wrap's own listeners and `stopPropagation` there can't help.
- **`src/wiring/transport.js`**: the player overlay's existing "a tap anywhere interrupts the in-flight ceremony" capture-phase `pointerdown` listener now excludes `isShrinkWrapTarget(e.target)` first — the same class of bug PR3 (`shell/runout.js`) already found and fixed for the auto-flip Flip/Lift-needle buttons, applied here so starting the drag/long-press doesn't also snap the ceremony to its end state out from under the gesture.
- **`src/player/scrub.js` — a real app bug found during this PR's own Playwright verification, not present before this PR, since `#sleeveWrap` didn't exist until it**: `wireSpinToScrub()`'s `#platterStage` `pointerdown` listener (spin-to-scrub/scratch) had no equivalent target exclusion. Since `#sleeve` (and the shrink-wrap appended inside it) shares `#platterStage` with `#disc`, a pointerdown starting the shrink-wrap's drag bubbled up and armed `scrubGesture` too; once the small angular-claim threshold was crossed, `disc.setPointerCapture(e.pointerId)` silently stole the pointer away from the tab's own, earlier capture, freezing the tear at whatever progress the first one or two move events had already reached regardless of how far the drag actually continued — confirmed with a minimal reproduction isolating real pointer-capture behavior from the bug (capture itself works correctly in isolation; the real app's second, later `setPointerCapture()` call on the same pointerId was the cause). Fixed the same way as the `wiring/transport.js` listener above: `if(isShrinkWrapTarget(e.target)) return;` as the first line of the pointerdown handler, importing `isShrinkWrapTarget` from `shell/start-sequence.js` (no circular import — `start-sequence.js` doesn't import from `scrub.js`).
- **`src/library/sealed.js`**: new export `runBreakChoreography(wrap, strip, albumId)` — the sealed-sheet's own CR6 tear/film/lift choreography (`seal-tearing`/`seal-filming`/`seal-lifting` classes, same ~380ms/420ms timing, or Reduced Motion's `seal-fading`), pulled out of `wireTearDrag`'s own commit path so the new accessible "Open" action (below) can reuse the exact same animation a completed drag already runs, rather than a second copy of it.
- **`src/gatefold/sealed-sheet.js`**: the sealed-record sheet's foot gained a new `#sealSheetOpenBtn` ("Open" — docs/15_SURFACE_NOISE.md §2's accessibility rule, "long-press the sleeve, or use an Open action in the record menu, to peel without the drag"), wired to call the new `runBreakChoreography()` on the sheet's own cover overlay.
- **`miziki.html`**: `#sealSheetOpenBtn` added to `.seal-sheet-foot`.
- **`src/styles/12-sealed-records.css`**: `.seal-sheet-open-btn` (the new Open button) and a new block of shrink-wrap-specific rules — `#sleeveWrap` (pointer-events:none outer wrapper, `--tear-progress` custom property), `.shrink-wrap-inner`/`.shrink-sheen`/`.shrink-wrinkle`/`.seal-sticker` (the wrap's own visual layers, reusing the sealed-record film's existing phase-tinted sheen gradient), `.shrink-tear-line`/`.shrink-tear-tab` (the tear's own progress-driven `scaleX`/`translateX`), and `.shrink-detached`/`.shrink-fading` (the two terminal states — detach-and-fall, or Reduced Motion's fade).
- **Tests**: `tests/unit/surface-noise.test.js` — 2 new tests for `crinkleBurstSamples` (short, deterministic by seed, louder/longer/denser at higher intensity, intensity clamped to `[0,1]`) and `playCrinkleBurst` (safe no-op with no audio graph, a no-op in Pure, otherwise runs cleanly). `tests/unit/gatefold.test.js`'s existing `src/library/sealed.js` mock gained `runBreakChoreography` (a real `SyntaxError` otherwise, since `gatefold/sealed-sheet.js` now imports it for real). `tests/unit/clock.test.js`'s existing `src/shell/start-sequence.js` mock gained `isShrinkWrapTarget` for the same reason, once `player/scrub.js` (loaded for real in this file's own chain) started importing it.
- **Verified**: `npm run lint` 0 errors/0 warnings; `npm run test:unit` 848/848 (846 before this PR, plus 2 new). Both production builds (`npm run build`, `MIZIKI_BASE=/ npm run build`). Playwright against the Vite dev server (unbundled ES modules, so the script could `import()` the app's own source modules directly to seed a sealed test track and drive the ceremony) at iPhone-13 viewport, with real synthetic pointer gestures (`page.mouse.move`/`down`/`up`, multiple incremental steps — never `element.click()`): the shrink wrap attaches to the sleeve for a sealed track with a visible, correctly-positioned tear tab; a real leftward drag past 60% detaches the shrink and runs `breakSeal()` (confirmed by `S.sealed` no longer containing the album); a drag released short of 60% springs the progress back to 0 without detaching; a real mid-drag against the player overlay's own capture-phase pointerdown listener keeps making forward progress rather than being cancelled (confirming the `wiring/transport.js` fix); a long-press anywhere on the wrap commits the peel; letting 2.2s pass with no engagement auto-peels on its own; with the browser context's Reduced Motion preference on, no `#sleeveWrap` ever attaches for any track (sealed or not) and the seal still breaks via `play()`'s own unconditional backstop; the sealed-record sheet's new Open button peels the seal with no drag at all; and `playCrinkleBurst` runs cleanly with no sleeve/ceremony involved, for the "played from elsewhere" crinkle-only path. No console/page errors throughout.
- No migration — this PR is entirely client-side.
- **Surface Noise plan complete.** All 5 PRs (engine, needle-lift skipping, auto-flip toggle, dead-wax etchings, shrink-wrap peel) are merged.

### Sealed-record pull strip moved to the top; gatefold backdrop now follows the theme

Two small, unrelated fixes Ken reported after PR5 merged, both client-side, no migration.

- **`src/styles/12-sealed-records.css`**: `.seal-strip` (the sealed-record film's "PULL TO OPEN ▸ ▸ ▸" tear strip — CR6 point 2, `library/sealed.js`'s `buildSealOverlay()`/`wireTearDrag()`) moved from `bottom:0` to `top:0` — the opening edge, matching the shrink-wrap's own tear tab (PR5, `shell/start-sequence.js`'s `SHRINK_*` constants) rather than sitting at the bottom. Its dashed perforation moved with it (`border-top` → `border-bottom`, so the perforation still reads as "the strip's own trailing edge"). `.seal-date-tag` (the SEALED · [date] sticker, lower-left, unmoved) no longer needs a 26px bottom offset to clear a strip that's no longer there, so it shrank to a plain `8px` inset. `docs/design/crate/README.md`'s own "tear strip" table row updated to match (design-reference docs for this plan are kept in sync with the implementation, same as every other crate-design PR). The horizontal-only drag logic in `library/sealed.js`'s `wireTearDrag()` needed no change — nothing there reads the strip's own vertical position.
- **`src/styles/11-gatefold.css`**: `.gatefold`'s own full-screen backdrop was a flat, hardcoded `#0A0E12` (literally `--panel`'s dark-theme rgb at a different alpha) — so opening the gatefold always went near-black regardless of the selected theme (Light/Dark/Auto/Nychthemeron all looked identical). Fixed to the same `linear-gradient(170deg,var(--sky1) 0%,var(--sky2) 62%,var(--sky3) 100%)` the full player overlay (`.player-overlay`, `13-player-overlay.css`) already uses, so the backdrop now follows the theme (and the day-arc sky) the same way every other full-screen overlay in the app does. The cover/tracklist panels themselves (`.gf-right`/`.gf-face-front`/`.gf-face-back`/`.gf-flat-tracks`) keep their own fixed deep-purple "sleeve interior" color in both themes, deliberately left unchanged — the same "disc, sleeve art, metal tiers... keep their own colors in both themes. Only chrome changes" rule CLAUDE.md already states for every other overlay; the backdrop is chrome, the sleeve-interior panels are sleeve art.
- **Follow-up, same thread**: Ken confirmed the sleeve-interior panels keeping their fixed dark look was fine, but asked that the *text* inside them stay readable — and it didn't: every text/border/input-background rule inside `.gf-right`/`.gf-face-front`/`.gf-face-back`/`.gf-flat-tracks` reads the ordinary theme-reactive tokens (`--paper`/`--muted`/`--line`/`--panel`/`--accent-text`), which in Light theme resolve to dark ink meant for a light page — dark-on-dark, unreadable liner notes, credits, track rows and the sleeve-note input field whenever anything but Dark (or Auto at night) was selected. Fixed with one small addition to `src/styles/11-gatefold.css`: a `.gf-right, .gf-face, .gf-flat-tracks{ --paper:…; --muted:…; --line:…; --panel:…; --accent-text:…; }` rule locking just those five tokens back to their own dark-theme literal values (`00-base.css`'s bare `:root`), scoped to the three selectors that actually keep the fixed dark background — `.gf-face` covers both `.gf-face-front`/`.gf-face-back` in one selector, so every descendant (`.gf-left`'s liner notes/credits/note-input/pin button, `.gf-flat-tracks`' own `.gf-back` label) inherits the override automatically, no per-rule hardcoding needed. `--accent` itself needed no override — CLAUDE.md already keeps it theme-invariant everywhere, so `--accent-text` is the only accent-family token this file reads that varies by theme at all.
- **Verified**: `npm run lint` 0 errors/0 warnings; `npm run test:unit` 848/848 (no new tests — all three changes in this entry are pure CSS with no new behavior to unit-test); both production builds. The pull-strip fix was checked by rendering `buildSealOverlay()`'s exact markup against the real built CSS and measuring/screenshotting the resulting layout (strip at the host's top edge, date tag unobscured at the bottom with its new 8px inset, no overlap). The gatefold-backdrop fix was checked by rendering `.gatefold.open` against the real built CSS under `data-theme="dark"`, `"light"` and unset (Auto, under a `prefers-color-scheme: light` emulated context) and confirming the computed `background-image` resolves to the dark/light/light sky gradient respectively, in place of the old flat, theme-invariant hex. The panel-text follow-up was checked by rendering the gatefold's own markup (tracklist title, side head, liner-note text, the note-input field, the flat-layout back button) against the real built CSS under both `data-theme="dark"` and `"light"`, confirming every computed color/background stayed byte-identical between the two (the fixed dark-theme literals) rather than flipping to light-theme ink on the still-dark panel background — screenshotted under Light to confirm visually too.
- No migration — all three fixes in this entry are entirely client-side CSS.

### Other collapsible menus/sheets also weren't following the theme

Ken, same thread, after #178 merged: "It appears that the other collapsible menus don't keep the theme (dark/light/auto/nychthemeron) appearance that is selected either." Audited every popover/sheet in `src/styles/` for the same class of bug the gatefold backdrop had (a hardcoded dark hex instead of a theme-reactive token) and found three real instances:

- **`src/styles/01-header.css`**: `.add-menu` (the header `+` menu) and `.overflow-menu` (the shared popover class behind both the library header's `···` overflow menu, `#libOverflowMenu`, and the player's own `···` overflow menu, `#playerOverflowMenu`) both had `background:#16242C` — a fixed navy hex regardless of theme. Fixed to `var(--chrome)`, the token already used elsewhere for a near-opaque "solid chrome surface" (the tab bar/mini-player) — the right fit for a popover meant to read as a crisp card over its own scrim, as opposed to `--panel`'s lower-alpha "layered over the sky" look. Each menu's `:active` row tint (`rgba(255,255,255,.04)`, nearly invisible once the background itself became light-theme-light) changed to `var(--sel)`, the same selected/pressed tint `.crate-group-cycle:active` already uses elsewhere in this file. The row text/icon colors (`--paper`/`--muted`/`--accent-text`) were already theme-reactive, so this is a pure background/active-state fix, no text changes needed.
- **`src/styles/26-sundown-sheet.css`**: `.sundown-sheet` (the Day/Sound settings sheet) had `background:#1E1815` — also a fixed hex. This one is plain chrome, not sleeve/vinyl art, and every color inside it (`.sundown-sheet-title`, etc.) already reads theme-reactive tokens — so it only needed its background switched to `var(--sky2)`, the same convention `.edit-sheet`/`.share-sheet` already use for a slide-up sheet's own background.
- **`src/styles/12-sealed-records.css`**: `.seal-sheet-box` (the sealed-record sheet) also had a fixed `#1C1226` background — but this one is deliberately a reuse of the gatefold's own `.gf-face` sleeve-interior color (the section's own header comment already says this sheet *is* "the gatefold's tracklist panel, same rows/order/handlers... no inner sleeve"), so the background itself stays exactly as it was, matching the design precedent #178 just established for the gatefold panels. Its interior text (`.seal-sheet-text`/`-artist`/`-title`/`-close`/`-foot`/`-open-btn`) had the exact same unreadable-in-Light bug the gatefold panels had before #178's follow-up fix, for the same reason (reading `--paper`/`--muted`/`--accent-text`, which resolve to dark ink in Light theme, sitting on a background that never changes). Fixed the same way: a `--paper`/`--muted`/`--accent-text` lock to their dark-theme literal values, scoped to `.seal-sheet-box` so every descendant inherits it.

Everything else that looked like a candidate (the crate's own stock/cream "catalog card," the sealed-record film's cream tag/strip tab, the share-image style swatches, the platter label/dial-marker/disc art, playlist-sleeve label strips) is sleeve art, share-image style, or disc/dial art by the same CLAUDE.md rule the gatefold backdrop fix already cites — deliberately theme-invariant, left untouched.

- **Verified**: `npm run lint` 0 errors/0 warnings; `npm run test:unit` 848/848 (pure CSS, no new tests); both production builds. Checked by rendering each affected markup fragment (`.add-menu`/`.overflow-menu`, `.sundown-sheet`, `.seal-sheet-box`) against the real built CSS under `data-theme="dark"`, `"light"` and Auto (`prefers-color-scheme: light` emulated, no attribute set), confirming the two popovers/the Sundown sheet's background and text both flip correctly between themes, and the sealed sheet's background and text both stay byte-identical (locked to their dark-theme values) across all three — screenshotted to confirm visually.
- No migration — entirely client-side CSS.

### Profile records + pressings (Oct 23, 2026)

The profile's "All records" grid and the Listening screen's Collection are now one **Records** section. Design frames: `docs/design/profile-records/`.

- **Profile home**: the storefront's 60-cover grid became `src/profile/records.js`'s `storeRecordsSection()` — ten records (A–Z), a count line ("212 records · 9 pressings · 3 metals"), "See all ›", "Show all N records ▾" when there are more than ten, and a "Pressings & metals" row. A record with an unlocked pressing or an earned metal has its disc peeking out of the top of its sleeve, in that pressing (the same `.peek-disc` drawing as the crate pull and the launch intro); metal first, matching the platter's rare > metal > pressing precedence.
- **Records screen** (new `'records'` profile screen): an All / Pressings toggle. All lists every record under letter heads, with the pressing or metal name under any record that has one. Pressings shows the pressing chart (moved from Listening), then unlocked Colors, Effects and Metals.
- **Listening** lost its Collection section and pressing chart (both now under Records); Achievements stays. The old `'collection'` route opens Records on Pressings, and `renderCollectionScreen` is gone.
- **Verified**: new `tests/unit/records.test.js` (13 tests); `tests/unit/profile.test.js` updated (Listening no longer draws the chart; `'collection'` redirects; `'records'` renders); eslint clean on every changed file. Rendered the profile home, All and Pressings in both themes at phone width with a seeded 14-album library. Ken runs the full `npm run lint`/`npm run test:unit`/build before merging (this session can't reach the npm registry, so the `fake-indexeddb` suites and the build weren't run here).
- No migration, no new saved setting (`recordsFilter` is session-only).

### Headphone-correction EQ presets (Oct 25, 2026)

Follow-on to the Audio plan (`docs/16_AUDIO.md`, AU1-AU12, complete): "headphone-correction EQ presets built from published headphone measurement data," queued by Ken after AU12 merged, since AU6's EQ engine applies in Pure mode too — a correction preset is meaningful there in a way AU6's own taste-shaping `EQ_PRESETS` were never framed as needing to be.

- **Sourcing**: real, verifiable parametric correction filters from [AutoEQ](https://github.com/jaakkopasanen/AutoEq) (MIT-licensed), measured by oratory1990 against the Harman over-ear 2018 target — not fabricated or approximated numbers. Five well-known headphones: Sony WH-1000XM4, Sennheiser HD 650, Apple AirPods Max, Audio-Technica ATH-M50x, Beyerdynamic DT 770 Pro. This is a small, recognizable starter set, not a real headphone database — a future PR could grow it, but the sourcing discipline (real published measurements, never guessed) is the part that matters.
- **`src/audio/eq.js`** gained `HEADPHONE_PRESETS` (five `{label, bands}` entries) and `HEADPHONE_PRESET_KEYS`. Each preset's `bands` is AutoEQ's own published 10-filter correction reduced to Miziki's existing `MAX_PARAMETRIC_BANDS` (6) cap by keeping the 6 largest-magnitude (`|gainDb|`) filters — the same kind of band-count reduction AutoEQ's own tooling already ships, not a new approximation invented for this app. Deliberately carries **no stored `preampDb`**: AU6's existing `autoPreampDbFromPeak()` (`audio/engine.js`) already derives the correct preamp live from the real combined filter curve, which is strictly more correct for a reduced 6-band curve than reusing AutoEQ's own preamp (computed against the dropped 10-band curve it shipped with). New `matchHeadphonePreset(parametricBands, epsilon)` — the parametric-mode twin of the existing graphic-only `matchPreset()` — compares a band list field-by-field in the order a preset's own `bands` are always applied in, for the EQ sheet's own "which correction is currently applied" chip state.
- **`src/player/eq-sheet.js`**: `currentStateLabel()` now reads a parametric match through `matchHeadphonePreset()` (showing the preset's own label, e.g. "SONY WH-1000XM4 · ON") in place of the old bare "CUSTOM · ON" whenever the current parametric bands exactly match a built-in headphone preset. New `renderHeadphoneCorrection()` renders a "Headphone correction" row of preset chips above the existing mode/preset UI, visible regardless of which mode tab (graphic/parametric) is currently selected — tapping a chip switches the sheet to parametric mode, writes that preset's bands, enables the EQ, saves, and re-renders.
- **`src/styles/27-eq.css`**: two small rules (`.eq-hp-wrap`, `.eq-hp-wrap .eq-slider-label`) for the new row's spacing, reusing the sheet's existing `.eq-preset-row`/`.eq-preset-chip`/`.eq-slider-label` classes for the chips and label themselves — no new visual language.
- **`CLAUDE.md`** updated: the `src/audio/eq.js` and `src/player/eq-sheet.js` repo-layout bullets each gained a sentence documenting the new exports/behavior, sourcing and the no-stored-preamp rationale. `docs/16_AUDIO.md` itself is untouched — it's a frozen historical design doc (one commit in its whole git history); this PR's own narrative lives here instead.
- **Verified**: `npm run lint` — 0 errors, 0 warnings. `npm run test:unit` — 1026/1026 (6 new tests in `tests/unit/eq.test.js`: every preset stays within Miziki's parametric limits and carries no preamp; every preset self-matches via `matchHeadphonePreset`; an unrelated band list and a hand-tweaked copy of a real preset both correctly read as no match; non-array input returns `null` without throwing; every preset round-trips unchanged through the existing `sanitizeParametricBands()`). Both production builds (`npm run build`, `MIZIKI_BASE=/ npm run build`) succeed.
- No migration — entirely client-side, no new saved setting of its own (the chosen preset is just whatever `S.eq.parametricBands`/`S.eq.mode` already persist).

### Wired/Bluetooth headphone-correction pair (Oct 25, 2026)

Follow-on to the headphone-correction EQ presets above: Ken asked whether a correction could differ between a headphone's wired and wireless (Bluetooth) mode, since BT mode often runs through extra DSP/codec processing that changes the response.

- **Checked AutoEQ's own data before writing anything.** None of the five headphones already shipped above actually have a published wired-vs-Bluetooth split: Sennheiser HD 650, Audio-Technica ATH-M50x and Beyerdynamic DT 770 Pro are wired-only products with no wireless mode to measure at all; the Sony WH-1000XM4 and Apple AirPods Max were each measured by oratory1990 in only one, unspecified connection mode — there's no second measurement to split against. Cloned AutoEQ's own repository (read-only) to verify this directly rather than guessing from memory, which is also how an earlier, mistaken claim in this thread (that the WH-1000XM4 has distinct wired/Bluetooth entries) got caught and corrected before any code was written.
- Rather than fabricate a split for a headphone AutoEQ never measured that way, added a genuinely different headphone AutoEQ *did* measure in both modes: two new `HEADPHONE_PRESETS` entries, `beyerdynamicAmironWirelessWired`/`beyerdynamicAmironWirelessBluetooth`, both oratory1990's own "(wired)" and "(Bluetooth)" results for the Beyerdynamic Amiron Wireless — the same headphone, two connection modes, both real. Reduced to 6 bands the same way as every other preset (the 6 largest-magnitude filters of the published 10), no stored preamp, same reasoning as the rest of `HEADPHONE_PRESETS`.
- **No change needed to `src/player/eq-sheet.js`** — `renderHeadphoneCorrection()` already iterates `HEADPHONE_PRESET_KEYS` generically, so the two new chips just appear in the existing "Headphone correction" row with no new UI code.
- `src/audio/eq.js`'s `HEADPHONE_PRESETS` header comment extended to record why these two exist and why the original five don't each get a wired/Bluetooth pair of their own.
- **Verified**: `npm run lint` — 0 errors, 0 warnings. `npm run test:unit` — 1027/1027 (1 new test in `tests/unit/eq.test.js`, plus every existing `HEADPHONE_PRESETS`-driven test automatically covering the two new entries since they all loop over `HEADPHONE_PRESET_KEYS`). Both production builds succeed.
- No migration, no new saved setting.
